# Decisions

Every analysis choice that the study brief does not fix is recorded here in full sentences, with its reason, before the runs it affects. D1 to D12 were written before any kinetics were integrated along an archived temperature history of the two-cell study.

## Kinetics

D1. The kinetics are the B-CRNN model of Koenig, Zhao and Deng, Chemical Engineering Journal 507 (2025) 160402, the brief's first preference, because its trained parameters are public: the article's supplementary material (`inputs/kinetics/SOURCE.md`) holds every SVGD particle of its three submodels (500 for the NCM811 cathode, 100 for the anode-electrolyte-SEI-binder network, 100 for the separator) with a key to their layout. The paper's data statement says data are available on request, and no public code repository of the group for this paper was found, so the supplementary files are the only source used. No parameter from any other paper is mixed in; the brief's second and third options (Feng et al. 2015, Ren et al. 2018, Kim et al. 2007) are not used. Chemistry: the cathode submodel was trained on NCM811 DSC data and the anode and electrolyte submodels on a graphite anode with an EC, DEC and DMC electrolyte (Lenz et al. 2023), so the cathode matches the LG M50's NMC811, while the M50's silicon-graphite negative and its electrolyte are not represented. The reactant masses come from the paper's own cell model, Feng et al.'s 25 Ah NCM cell (D4, D7).

D2. The brief's driver, `onset_kinetics.py`, is kept with its FILL markers resolved: as the brief allows for B-CRNN kinetics, its Hatchard-type `rates()` and five-variable state are replaced by the B-CRNN network of `src/bcrnn.py` (13 species, 11 reactions); everything else is kept, with the two numerical changes of D8.

D3. The kinetic model is the posterior mean: the arithmetic mean of each parameter over its SVGD particles (`inputs/kinetics/bcrnn_mean_model.json`). This is what the paper's Fig. A.1(a) uses ("mean kinetic parameters from current study"). The paper's reported statistics, such as the main-cluster mean T_initial of 183.5 C, are over 2500 samples of a kernel density estimate of the particles; this study propagates one model, not the distribution, so the kinetic uncertainty that the paper quantifies is not carried here. Because ln k = ln A + b ln T - Ea/(RT) is linear in the parameters, the mean model's ln k at any temperature equals the mean of the particles' ln k.

D4. Each reaction's specific heat release is multiplied by a component mass of the paper's 720 g cell (its Eq. 20 and Table 1): the cathode mass, 179.12 g, for the three cathode reactions; the electrolyte mass, 108 g, for evaporation and decomposition; the anode mass including binder, 100.58 g, for the anode-binder reaction; the SEI mass, 100.58 g, for SEI decomposition; the IV and V mass, 104.3 g, for reactions IV and V; the separator mass, 17.6 g, for melting. Table 1 gives no mass for the anode-electrolyte reaction. The paper's Fig. A.1(a) plots that reaction's self-heating rate in its ARC test of the mean model, and below 168 C, where its reactants are essentially unconsumed, the curve divided by the mean model's rate gives the mass directly: 203.2 g as read (156 points, interquartile range 202.1 to 204.1 g), 205.5 g after removing the 1.2 % reading bias measured on the SEI curve of the same figure, whose mass Table 1 gives (`inputs/kinetics/figA1a_mass_basis.json`). This matches the anode plus electrolyte mass, 208.58 g, and not the anode alone (100.58 g) or the electrolyte alone (108 g), so 208.58 g is used. V1 is also reported with the anode alone.

D5. The separator file stores the melting enthalpy as a positive number, but the paper's Fig. 3(e) shows melting as a negative heat release rate, about -0.080 W/g at 139 C at 5 C/min. Taking the heat as minus dH times the rate reproduces that curve (-0.079 W/g at 138.5 C, `results/v0_dsc_check.json`); taking it as dH times the separator's rate of change gives -0.165 W/g. Melting is therefore endothermic with heat -dH r.

## Verification against the source paper (V0 and V1)

D6. V1's acceptance test is the paper's own ARC test, because its T_initial is defined there (Section 2.7): heat-wait-seek from 25 C in 5 C steps, a 30 min wait, an exotherm trigger of 0.15 C/min, the cell of Table 1 (720 g, 1100 J/(kg K), 0.04 m2, 25 W/(m2 K)). Details the paper leaves open are fixed here: in the heat phase the chamber ramps at 1 C/min from the battery temperature until the battery reaches the target, which gives the battery heating rates of about 1e-2 C/s that Fig. A.1(a) shows; the seek lasts 10 min, and a self-heating rate already above the trigger when a seek opens counts as a trigger. T_in is the battery temperature at the trigger. T_on is reported at the paper's stated ISC trigger, 1 C/min, and also at 1 C/s. Acceptance: T_in within the two-sigma band of the main-cluster mean, 169.0 to 198.0 C (the published study's D1 and D2). Sensitivity runs vary the ramp (0.5, 1 and 2 C/min) and the seek (5, 10 and 20 min). V0 reproduces the paper's DSC curves at 5 C/min before V1.

## The neighbour cell

D7. The neighbour's density and heat capacity are those of the published study: RHO = 2938.7 kg/m3 (jelly-roll effective density, its `inputs/oregan2022_properties.txt`) and CP = 950 J/(kg K) (the constant cell-average value both its models use, its D3). The reactant contents per unit volume are the paper's reactant mass fractions (each mass of D4 divided by 720 g) times RHO, so the kinetic self-heating rate, the reaction heat per unit volume divided by RHO times CP, equals the sum of q_j w_j over CP and does not depend on RHO. The mass fractions are those of the 25 Ah cell, not measured for the M50. The reactants are fresh at t = 0, as in the paper's ARC simulation, which starts from fresh reactants at 25 C.

D8. Numerics of the passive integration: the temperature is interpolated linearly between the archived 0.5 s samples (brief); the kinetics are integrated with BDF, an analytic Jacobian, rtol 1e-8, atol 1e-12 and a largest step of 0.25 s, half the sampling interval; the self-heating rate is evaluated on a 0.01 s grid from the solver's dense output and the crossing times are interpolated linearly between grid points. The brief's driver read the crossings off a 0.1 s grid, which would quantise a crossing near 4 s by 2.5 %, more than V2 allows.

D9. The three histories are the archived series the brief names: `Tmax` (the hottest node), `Tmean` (the heat-capacity-weighted mean) and `T2_be` with `t_be` (the lumped node integrated with the resolved model's scheme). `Tmax` is the maximum over the nodes at each time, so the material point it follows can change (`imax`); the passive integration treats it as one point's history, an upper envelope of the local histories.

D10. Before the predictions, V2 uses synthetic histories only (holds, ramps from 0.3 to 30 K/s, and smooth pulses), so that no kinetics touch an archived history before the predictions are public. After Stage 1, V2 is repeated on the headline cases.

D11. Predictions P2 to P4 are scored on the 42 two-strip cases. The 7 one-strip cases are run and reported, not scored.

D12. Stage 2 cases: 33.2 kJ at 500 and 200 mW/K with 10 and 30 s releases (brief), and as the median-energy case 21.5 kJ at 500 mW/K with a 30 s release, the median energy with copper busbars, whose passive hottest node peaks at 236.7 C, near the temperature at which the cathode reaction begins to outrun conduction. Stage 2 definitions: the hottest region is the hottest node of the reactive resolved cell; it enters the internal-short band when it first reaches 255.7 C; the reactive lumped cell is the lumped node of the published `lumped_one_way` scheme with the reaction source of D7 for its whole 65 g; cell 1's history is the archived one (one-way). The numerical scheme of Stage 2 is recorded here before Stage 2 runs.

## Added after the predictions were public, before Stage 2 ran

D13. Stage 2 scheme (`src/reactive.py`). Each step of length dt is split in two (first-order splitting). First, every node integrates its own adiabatic kinetics over dt: dc/dt = S r(T, c) and dT/dt = Q/C_i, with Q the node's reaction heat and C_i its heat capacity; a node whose amounts, temperature-driven rates and temperature change by less than 2e-3, 2e-3 and 0.02 K per sub-step takes explicit Heun sub-steps (1 to 64 of them, in powers of two), and a node that would need more is integrated with BDF on a block-diagonal analytic Jacobian (rtol 1e-7, atol 1e-6 K and 1e-11 for the amounts). Second, the published backward-Euler conduction step, with its boundary paths, lumped capacity and lagged room radiation, runs from the post-reaction temperatures. The step is dt = 0.1 s / 2^k, the largest for which the largest local reaction heating rate times dt stays at or below 1 K, at most one level coarser than the previous step and aligned with the coarser grids; a step whose reaction part heats any node by more than 4 K is repeated at half the length. The brief asked for an adaptive step once the local rate exceeds about 1 K/min; this rule keeps the published 0.1 s step until the local reaction heating reaches 10 K/s (600 K/min), because below that a node changes by less than 1 K per step through the reaction and the splitting error scales with that change. The reaction heat enters the energy account as the sum over nodes of C_i times the temperature change of the reaction part, so the account closes exactly as the published one does. The lumped node uses the published `lumped_one_way` update with the same reaction part and the same step rule.

D14. Where the reactants sit. The neighbour's reactant inventory (65 g of cell at the mass fractions of D7) is spread over the jelly-roll volume of the resolved model in proportion to each node's roll volume; the 0.25 mm can shell holds none and keeps its 4.5 J/K. A roll node therefore self-heats 61.75/57.25 = 1.079 times as fast as the lumped node at the same temperature, and a surface node more slowly; the totals of reactant, reaction heat and heat capacity are the same in both models, as the published V1a requires of the heat capacity.

D15. Stage 2 checks. V3 as the brief states it: the energy account of every Stage 2 run closes to within 1e-9 of the heat taken in (paths plus reaction heat), and with the roll's conductivity raised to 1e4 W/mK the reactive resolved cell matches the reactive lumped node within 1 K, on the published check's case (33.2 kJ, 30 s, 500 mW/K) and on 33.2 kJ, 30 s, 200 mW/K. Two further checks: the Stage 2 loops run without the source on the headline case must reproduce the archived passive series of the resolved model and of the lumped node; and the headline case is rerun with the 1 K and 4 K limits of D13 halved, which must move no band-entry time by more than 1 % of itself.

## Added after Stage 2 ran (presentation only; no result depends on these)

D16. The figure (brief step 6). Panel a shows the median energy (21.5 kJ, hollow markers, dashed) beside the maximum (33.2 kJ), because at 33.2 kJ and 30 s no case has only one history reaching 0.15 K/min, and the brief asks for such cases to be marked: they are ringed, and at 21.5 kJ the 100 mW/K case is one. No case in the study has only the lumped node or only the resolved mean starting, so ringing the only-hottest cases marks every only-one-history case. Panel b is Stage 1 (passive) on a logarithmic axis, so the stretches where the net kinetic rate is endothermic (separator melting) or below 1e-3 K/min are gaps. Panel c adds the lumped node without the source (dashed) to the lumped reactive cell the brief names, so that the reaction heat's effect on both models can be read on one axis. Colours follow the published two-cell figure.

D17. The Databank rows. The two-cell study's `inputs/databank/` files (the M50 rows, the 17 rows used and the Databank's notice) are copied unchanged into this repository, so that the credit sentence the brief asks the note to carry, which names that folder, is true here too and the notice accompanies the rows, as it requires. No script reads them.

D18. The V0 to V2 records committed before the predictions (commit 78c1321) were written with a loop-based `rates()`; Stage 1 and Stage 2 use the vectorised `rates()` of commit b7ba0f5, which agrees with it to 4e-14. The committed records are kept unchanged, and a re-run of the three scripts with the final code is compared with them in `results/rerun_final_code.json`.

## Added after an independent review of the results (exploratory or records only; no registered result changes)

D19. Exploratory, not pre-registered: how much of Stage 1 rests on a fresh SEI. Stage 1 starts every history from the fresh state of the paper's Fig. 2, as the paper's own ARC simulation does, but the mean model's SEI decomposition (Ea 43.8 kJ/mol, order 0.16) runs even at 25 C (0.0049 K/min of self-heating, known from V1's isothermal check). `src/explore_sei_storage.py` stores the neighbour at 25 C for 1, 3, 7 and 30 days under the same kinetics and repeats the Stage 1 integration from each stored state on five archived cases (33.2 kJ at 500 mW/K with both releases; 21.5 kJ at 100 mW/K, 33.2 kJ at 200 mW/K and 21.5 kJ at 500 mW/K with the 30 s release), with a 1 K/s ramp for reference (`results/explore_sei_storage.json`). No kinetic parameter changes; only the initial state does. The one change to the driver is an optional initial state for `integrate_along`, which defaults to the fresh state, so no registered result moves.

D20. Exploratory: the source's own internal-short trigger. The paper switches its internal short on when the rate of the battery's temperature rise surpasses 1 C/min (its Eq. 19, Table 1 and Section 3.2.2), which in its adiabatic calorimeter is the self-heating rate. A neighbour heated by conduction rises far faster than that from the busbar alone, so the trigger is applied here to the kinetic self-heating only, which is the 1 K/min level of Stage 1; the README reports, beside the brief's temperature band (D12), which histories reach it and when. P5 stays scored on the band, as registered.

D21. Records. Page numbers for every value taken from the paper are added to `inputs/kinetics/SOURCE.md`; before, values were cited by table, equation or figure. V1's T_on at the paper's stated trigger (1 C/min) is 199.8 C, below the paper's band of 255.7 to 302.1 C; D6 promised it alongside the 1 C/s value, and the README now gives both. The brief's V1 acceptance is on T_in alone, so V1 still passes, but the T_on check fails at the stated trigger and its cause is not resolved here.

## Stage 3: uncertainty in the kinetics and the thermophysical inputs (brief of 12 October)

D22. Kinetic draws (`src/stage3_draws.py`). The supplementary files were obtained again on 12 October through the author's browser (this machine cannot reach the publisher's server) and match the recorded SHA-256 of the zip and of every file; `inputs/kinetics/extract_mean_model.py`, run on them for the first time here, reproduces `bcrnn_mean_model.json` to 1.1e-15 (`results/extract_mean_model_check.txt`). A draw is one column index into each of the three particle files, sampled independently and uniformly with replacement (anode-electrolyte file 1 to 100, separator 1 to 100, cathode 1 to 500), because the three submodels were inferred from separate DSC datasets and the paper samples "the randomly sampled uncertain effects of all kinetic models" (its Section 3.2.2, p. 11); column j of one file has nothing to do with column j of another. N = 200, seed 20261012, one numpy generator drawing the anode-electrolyte, separator and cathode indices in that order (`results/stage3_draw_indices.csv`). Each draw's parameters go through `bcrnn.rows_to_model`, the same conversion as the mean model (ln A, Ea times 1e5, b, dH, orders and stoichiometry from the same rows), with the masses, initial amounts and separator sign of D3 to D5; the conversion was split out of `load_mean_model` without changing a bit of the mean model. No particle is scaled, trimmed, reweighted or corrected. Every draw's parameter vector (its 220, 17 and 6 rows) is written to `results/stage3_kinetic_draw_parameters.csv`; that file reproduces the publisher's supplementary data, which this repository does not redistribute, so it is not committed, and its SHA-256 is recorded in `results/stage3_draws.json`. Unit test (`src/test_stage3_draws.py`): the particle means recomputed through the loader equal the recorded mean rows to 1.1e-15 (requirement 1e-12).

D23. V4 (`src/v4_arc_draws.py`), recorded before it runs. Every draw goes through V1's emulation of the paper's ARC test with D6's settings unchanged (`v1_arc_check.arc_test`: the cell of the paper's Table 1, heat-wait-seek from 25 C in 5 C steps, 30 min wait, 10 min seek, exotherm trigger 0.15 C/min, then adiabatic tracking). Recorded per draw: T_in and the time of the trigger, T_on at the paper's internal-short trigger (1 C/min of self-heating) and the time it is reached, the temperature at 1 C/s, and the SEI conversion at T_in. The classes follow the paper's Appendix A, which calls a short late when it is "significantly outside of the main cluster seen in Fig. 6(d)" (p. 14), notes that ending the experiment at 4000 min, "as appears reasonable in Fig. 6(d)", would have kept the main cluster and turned many late shorts into no shorts (p. 15), and runs its tests over about 8500 min (p. 15). A draw whose self-heating reaches 1 C/min no later than 4000 min after the test starts is in the main cluster; between 4000 and 8500 min, a delayed short; not by 8500 min, no short; a draw whose seek never triggers below 400 C has no exotherm, a class the paper does not have, reported if it occurs. The paper's fractions are those of its Fig. 6 caption (p. 12) and Appendix A (p. 14): 76 % main cluster, 7 % late short, 17 % without a short. The acceptance is the brief's, over the main-cluster draws: the 2.5 and 97.5 percentiles of T_in (numpy's default linear interpolation) within 5 C of 169.0 and 198.0 C, and their mean within 5 C of 183.5 C. T_max is not reported, because the emulation stops at the triggers and models neither the internal short's energy release (342 kJ over 10 s in the paper's Table 1) nor the chamber's cooling, so T_max is reached by no draw here. T_on is reported beside the paper's main-cluster mean of 278.9 C and band of 255.7 to 302.1 C, with D21's caveat that the mean model's T_on at the stated trigger, 199.8 C, lies below that band; the acceptance does not use it. The paper's statistics are over kernel density samples, which spread wider than the particles (its Fig. 5), so the particle draws here may show a narrower spread of T_in than the paper's; that is reported as found. If V4 fails, the study stops and is reported, and nothing is adjusted.

D24. V4's first run stopped before it finished, and its partial output was not summarised: on 13 of the 200 draws the emulation broke down in the long adiabatic tracking after the exotherm trigger, where scipy's finite-difference Jacobian overflowed once most reactants were spent (on 7 draws before the tracking towards 1 C/min ended, on 6 between 1 C/min and 1 C/s), and the solver met non-finite values or ran for more than four minutes on one draw. The fault is numerical, in the emulation, not in the particles: no draw has a reactant with a positive stoichiometric coefficient or a negative order. `arc_test` therefore gains an option, off by default so that V1 is unchanged, that gives the solver the exact Jacobian of every stage (heat, wait, seek and tracking), built from `bcrnn.Kinetics.jacobian`, the derivative Stage 2 uses; nothing else in the emulation changes. With it the posterior-mean model reproduces V1's record (T_in, T_on, the temperature at 1 C/s and their times) to 4e-12, as the finite-difference run does (5e-12), and all 13 draws run to the end in about 10 s each (this diagnosis showed their T_in, T_on and how their tracking ended; nothing was changed after it). V4 uses the exact Jacobian for all 200 draws, so that every draw goes through the same numerics. The emulation also records how each tracking stage ended (trigger reached, end of the 1e7 s tracking, or a solver failure); a draw whose emulation fails would be reported as failed and counted in no class, never as a draw without a short.

## Added after V4 failed (12 October; post hoc, chosen by the author)

D25. V4 amended. V4 failed under D23 (commit f9cabc7): over the 164 main-cluster draws the 2.5 and 97.5 percentiles of T_in were 146.3 and 191.4 C against 169.0 and 198.0 C, and the mean 178.8 C against 183.5 C. As the brief requires, the study stopped and the failure was reported to the author with a diagnosis, made after the fact, that showed how the verdict moves with the statistic, the cluster and the reference; the author chose to amend V4 before any case runs rather than proceed or stop. This decision was written after that diagnosis, so it is not a blind check, and wherever V4 is reported the D23 failure is reported beside it. Two changes are made, both to how the draws' band is compared with the paper's; the draws, the emulation and the classes of D23 are unchanged, and nothing is rerun (`src/v4_amended.py` reads the committed per-draw file).
(1) The statistic. The paper's band is the mean plus and minus two standard deviations of its main-cluster results: Fig. 7 is "obtained by computing the ±2σ range on the uncertain simulation results" (caption, p. 12) and shows "the range of metrics captured within two standard deviations of the mean" (p. 13); 169.0 and 198.0 C are that band as read from Fig. 7. D23 set the 2.5 and 97.5 percentiles of the draws against it, a different statistic, which unlike the paper's is set by the few draws in each tail. V4 now uses the paper's statistic for the draws as well: their main-cluster mean plus and minus two sample standard deviations.
(2) The reference. V1 found, before any draw ran, that the emulation puts the posterior-mean model's T_in at 179.25 C, 4.25 C below the paper's main-cluster mean, a location set by the heat-wait-seek details the paper does not give (D6) and shared by every draw; V1 passed because 179.25 C lies inside the band. The absolute edges of D23 count that known offset against the draws a second time. The edges are now judged relative to the emulation's own T_in of the mean model: the draws' lower and upper edges minus 179.25 C must lie within 5 C of the paper's edges minus its mean, -14.5 and +14.5 C. The mean criterion is unchanged and absolute: within 5 C of 183.5 C.
The change of reference is the one put to the author as the example of an amendment; the change of statistic was added because the reference alone fails (lower edge 18.5 C short), and it is the paper's own definition. `results/stage3_v4_amended.json` also evaluates every combination of the two changes, with the classes of D23 and with a tighter main cluster (short times inside Tukey's far-out fence of all shorts by 8500 min), so that the dependence of the verdict on each choice is on the record: no variant with absolute edges passes, and every variant with relative edges passes except the one that keeps both the percentiles and D23's classes.

## Stage 3a design, recorded with the predictions before any draw is integrated along an archived history

D26. Stage 3a (`src/stage3a.py`).
(1) Integration. Each draw is integrated exactly as Stage 1 integrated the mean model: `onset_kinetics.integrate_along` with the draw's kinetics in place of the mean model (BDF with the analytic Jacobian, rtol 1e-8, atol 1e-12, largest step 0.25 s, the archived 0.5 s series interpolated linearly, crossings read on a 0.01 s grid), one draw and one history at a time; nothing is batched across draws.
(2) Cases. The brief's nine cases (33.2 kJ with 10 and 30 s releases at 500, 200, 100 and 50 mW/K, and the median-energy case of Stage 2, 21.5 kJ, 30 s, 500 mW/K) along all three histories. For P3, which the brief defines as the Stage 1 P3 quantity, and which Stage 1 scored over the 42 two-strip cases (D11), the author chose on 12 October to compute it over the same 42 cases; the other 33 two-strip cases are therefore integrated along the hottest-node and lumped histories, the only two P3 needs. The one-strip cases are not run.
(3) Records. For each draw, case and history, the fields Stage 1 records (first times at 0.15, 1 and 10 K/min, the temperature and the dominant reaction at each, the peak rate and its time, the conversions at 900 s, the solver's step count), in `results/stage3a_onsets.csv`, with the draw's column indices.
(4) Outliers. Every draw stays in every metric. Each draw's V4 class (D23) is carried into the per-draw files, and the fractions of draws in the paper's outlier classes (delayed short, no short) and of draws whose hottest-node onset is an SEI start below 100 C are reported separately, as the paper reports its outliers beside its Fig. 7.
(5) Metrics with a node that does not start. A node without an onset by 900 s is ranked after every onset when percentiles are taken; the lead (lumped onset minus hottest-node onset) is taken over the draws whose hottest node starts, with a lumped node that does not start ranked as the longest lead, and a percentile that falls among those draws is reported as longer than 900 s minus the hottest-node onset. Draws in which neither node starts have no lead and are counted in the share with no onset anywhere.
(6) V5. Five draws, those at the minimum, lower quartile, median, upper quartile and maximum of the hottest-ramp onset temperature in `results/stage3_prediction_estimates.json` (the lower draw number on ties), are rerun on the nine cases along the three histories with the tolerances, the step limit and the evaluation grid all halved, as V2 did. Acceptance: no first time at 0.15, 1 or 10 K/min moves by more than 1 % of itself; for a first time under 1 s, by more than 0.01 s, one evaluation step. V5 runs after the predictions are public, because it integrates along archived histories.

## Stage 3b inputs and design, recorded before any 3b solve

D27. Stage 3b and 3c (`src/stage3b.py`). The sources were read on 12 October in the authors' accepted manuscripts, because the published versions are behind subscription. The manuscript of O'Regan et al. carries no page numbers, so its values are cited by table and section; the published page numbers remain to be checked.
Varied, each uniform within its range:
(a) The roll's conductivity across the layers, 0.998 to 1.38 W/mK. 0.998 W/mK is the measured jelly roll of a commercial 5 Ah Ni-rich NMC 21700 cell (3.63 V, 68.2 to 68.7 g): Chen et al., J. Electrochem. Soc. 168 (2021) 010502, Table 1, "0.998 (radial), 25.8 (axial)" (p. 27 of the accepted manuscript, WRAP 146838). 1.38 W/mK is the top of the range, 1.23 to 1.38 W/mK over 25 to 100 C, into which the two-cell study (its D3) combined in series the through-plane conductivities that O'Regan et al. (Electrochim. Acta 425 (2022) 140700) measured on the M50's electrodes and took for its separator (their Table 6, 268 to 328 K, and Table 7 at 25 C, Section 1.3.2). O'Regan et al. warn that "combining these values do not provide an accurate value for the overall cell thermal conductivity" (Section 1.3.2), which is why the measured jelly roll of Chen et al. sets the other end. The nominal, 1.3 W/mK, lies inside.
(b) The roll's conductivity along the layers, 24.9 to 25.8 W/mK: the parallel combination of O'Regan et al.'s components, 24.9 to 25.1 W/mK over 25 to 100 C (two-cell D3), and Chen et al.'s measured 25.8 W/mK (Table 1). The nominal, 25 W/mK, lies inside.
(c) The roll's specific heat, 842 to 1110 J/(kg K). O'Regan et al.'s component heat capacities (Table 6), through PyBaMM's fits as the two-cell study used them (its D3), give the jelly roll 842, 999 and 1110 J/(kg K) at 25, 60 and 100 C. O'Regan et al. print 887 J/(kg K) at 25 C as calculated (Table 7, and "An overall specific heat capacity of 887 J kg-1 K-1", Section 1.3.2) and 866 as the "Jellyroll effective heat capacity" of their simulations (Table 8). Chen et al. give 928 J/(kg K), measured (Table 1). The model's properties are constant, so the range spans every value from the two sources over 25 to 100 C, the temperatures the neighbour passes through before its onset; values at 25 C alone (866 to 928) would put every draw below the nominal, whose 950 J/(kg K) over the whole 65 g is a cell average at 60 to 100 C. In the model the roll is 56 g of the 65 g cell (the published study's cell, whose 9 g can at 500 J/(kg K) is held fixed), so a draw's roll holds 0.056 kg times its specific heat and the cell 4.5 J/K more. The nominal puts 57.25 J/K in the roll, 1022 J/(kg K), inside the range. The lumped node takes the same total heat capacity as the resolved cell in each draw, as the published study's V1a requires.
Held fixed, as the brief requires when no range is published for this cell or the solver has no such parameter:
(d) The can-to-roll contact. The published solver has no interface between the can and the roll: the can is a 0.25 mm shell on the roll's outer nodes (its D10), in perfect contact. The only published values found are for dry samples built in 18650 cans, a mean of 917 W/(m2 K) with a standard deviation of 474 across samples and temperatures (Gaitonde, Nimmagadda and Marconnet, ITHERM 2017, doi:10.1109/ITHERM.2017.7992602, abstract), not for a filled 21700 cell. Held at the solver's perfect contact.
(e) The busbar strip conductance. No published thermal conductance of a welded cell-to-busbar joint was found; the published weld contact resistances are electrical, and turning them into a thermal spread would need a model of the joint that neither study has. Held at 500 mW/K, the case.
(f) The can emissivity. No measured emissivity of a bare or a wrapped 21700 can was found; Chen et al. also assume 0.8 ("The surface emissivity is 0.8", Section 4.1, p. 15 of the accepted manuscript, after Hatchard et al.). Held at 0.8.
(g) Cell 1's energy (33.2 kJ), its release (30 s) and its archived history (one-way, as before) are the case, not the uncertainty, and are not varied.
Design. A Latin hypercube over (a) to (c), scipy.stats.qmc.LatinHypercube with numpy.random.default_rng(20261012), M = 30, written to `results/stage3_thermal_draws.csv`. Each draw runs, in a fresh process, one resolved solve with the published solver (refine 1, dt 0.1 s, 900 s, 0.5 s archive, two strips of 250 mW/K, cell 1's archived history of 33.2 kJ, 30 s, 500 mW/K) and one lumped one-way solve with the same heat capacity, both archived like the Stage 1 series (`results/stage3b_series/`). The posterior-mean kinetics are integrated along each draw's hottest-node and lumped histories with the Stage 1 driver.
V6. Before the draws, the nominal inputs through this harness must reproduce the archived series of the case. Every 3b solve's energy account must close below 1e-9 of the heat taken in. For the draws with the smallest, median and largest roll specific heat, the resolved solve is repeated with both roll conductivities at 1e4 W/mK, and its hottest node and mean must stay within 1 K of the lumped node at every archived time.
3c. Each thermal draw is paired with 7 distinct kinetic draws chosen with numpy.random.default_rng(20261012) (`results/stage3c_pairs.csv`, 210 pairs), and each kinetic draw is integrated along its thermal draw's hottest-node and lumped histories.
Decomposition at the headline case, as the brief states: the standard deviations (ddof 1, over the draws with an onset) of the hottest-node onset, the lumped onset and the lead over the 200 kinetic draws at the nominal inputs (3a), over the 30 thermal draws with the mean kinetics (3b) and over the 210 pairs (3c), and the ratio thermal to kinetic for each. An interaction is reported if the variance over the pairs exceeds the sum of the two single-factor variances by more than 20 %.

## Added after Stage 3 ran (presentation only; no result depends on these)

D28. Stage 3's figure (brief: panel (d) of the existing figure, or a second figure) is a second figure, `results/stage3_figure.png`, shown in the README, because the note must stay one page and Figure 1 already fills its width. Panel (a) shows the lead's distributions as shares per 2 s bin, so that the 200 kinetic draws, the 30 thermal draws and the 210 pairs share one axis; panel (b) shows the 30 s release, the case of P1, P2 and P4 to P6. In panel (b) a node that does not start within the run ranks after every onset, and a percentile that falls among those draws is drawn at 900 s, the run's end. To keep the note to one page with the six Stage 3 lines, its Stage 1 and 2 text is shortened (the figure caption, the method's Stage 1 and 2 items and its checks, two limits, the scorecard's wording); the full text of each stays in the README. The checks line now quotes the largest changes over all three stages (onsets moved by at most 7e-5 of themselves, energy accounts closed to 1e-11 at the published conductivities), where it quoted Stages 1 and 2 alone (4e-6 and 1.3e-12); no other number changes.

## Stage 4 (version 1.1.0) design, recorded with the predictions before any grouped run (16 October)

D29. Stage 4 (brief of 16 October; `src/v11_sensitivity.py`, `predictions_v11.md`).
(1) Submodels. The posterior's three submodels are its three supplementary files (D1, D22):
- the anode-electrolyte network (`An_Ele.csv`): electrolyte evaporation and decomposition, the anode-electrolyte reaction, the anode-binder reaction, SEI decomposition, IV and V;
- the NCM811 cathode: three sequential reactions;
- the separator: melting.

The brief's "SEI submodel" is the anode-electrolyte file, because it is the only one that holds the SEI decomposition. The file's seven reactions were inferred together, from the same DSC data, so the SEI decomposition is not varied apart from them.
(2) Grouped runs. For each submodel and each of the 200 Stage 3 draws, the kinetics are built by `bcrnn.rows_to_model` from two sources: the draw's particle of that submodel's file (its column in `results/stage3_draw_indices.csv`), and the posterior-mean rows of the other two files (`bcrnn_mean_model.json`, as Stage 1 used them). Holding two submodels at their posterior mean is the brief's choice, and Stage 3's joint draws stay the headline.

The integration is D26 (1)'s, along the hottest-node and lumped histories of `two_E33223_tau30_G0.5` and `two_E33223_tau30_G0.1`: 3 x 200 x 2 runs, each over both histories. The archives, settings and records are Stage 3a's (D26 (3)), written to `results/v11_onsets.csv`. The joint values at the two cases are Stage 3a's records (`results/stage3a_onsets.csv`), unchanged. Nothing is refitted, no new data enter, and no thermal model is run. A grouped run whose integration fails is reported as failed and counts as a draw without its outputs; it is not rerun with other settings.
(3) V7, the step 1 checks (`results/v11_checks.json`), run before the predictions:
- The three particle files match the SHA-256 of `inputs/kinetics/SOURCE.md`.
- Every draw's rows, loaded file by file, rebuild `results/stage3_kinetic_draw_parameters.csv` byte for byte (the SHA-256 of `results/stage3_draws.json`).
- For all 200 draws and the three submodels, each of the 3600 model arrays of a grouped draw equals the joint draw's on the varied file's reactions and species, and the posterior-mean model's elsewhere, exactly.
- The posterior-mean model built through the Stage 4 path reproduces Stage 1 on both cases and both histories (72 fields, largest relative difference 0).
- The five V5 draws (D26 (6)) built jointly through it reproduce Stage 3a (360 fields, largest relative difference 0).

These checks integrate only the posterior-mean model and five joint draws, whose records were already public. No grouped run was made before the predictions were pushed.
(4) Metrics and scoring are as `predictions_v11.md` defines them:
- the main-effect share, a variance ratio against the 200 joint draws;
- the rank-based repeat, a squared Spearman correlation against the joint draws on values rounded to 4 significant digits;
- the early SEI start, Stage 3's P6 quantity;
- the central half and restricted set, the dominant submodel, and the ordering, with shares closer than 0.01 tied.

(5) The central half uses the standardized Euclidean distance from the posterior mean, over the rows that vary across a file's particles. A Mahalanobis distance was not used: the anode-electrolyte file has 57 varying rows and only 100 particles, so its sample covariance is too poorly determined to rank particles by. One metric is used for all three files.
(6) Bootstrap intervals use 2000 resamples of the 200 draw numbers, drawn with `numpy.random.default_rng(20261016)` and paired across the grouped and joint runs. They are reported, not scored.

## Added after Stage 4 ran (exploratory, not pre-registered; no registered result changes)

D30. These analyses were written after the Stage 4 results, to explain why P4 failed. They are recorded in `results/v11_sensitivity.json` under "exploratory" and are not scored. Every number is computed by `src/v11_score.py` from the committed records.
(1) Modes. The joint leads at the headline case split into two modes at the largest gap of their sorted values, 30.7 to 49.7 s, as Stage 3 reported them. For each restricted set of P4 and of its secondary analyses, the record gives two numbers: the share of its draws in the long-lead mode, and the 2.5 to 97.5 % width within the short-lead mode alone.
(2) One more restriction. It takes the half of the anode-electrolyte particles whose fresh SEI's heat release per unit cell mass at 108.4 C lies closest to the posterior-mean model's. 108.4 C is the posterior-mean model's onset temperature in every Stage 1 history. The quantity is the natural log of dH times the SEI's mass fraction times the rate constant at that temperature, times the initial SEI amount, 0.15, raised to the reaction order. It stands for a measurement that pinned the fresh SEI's heat release near its mean onset.
(3) Draw by draw. Each joint draw's mode and starting reaction are set against the anode-electrolyte run of the same draw. The cathode and separator runs' onset ranges are set against the posterior-mean model's onsets.
(4) Which submodel decides the draws that differ (`src/v11_explore.py`, `results/v11_explore.json`). This was added after an independent check of the Stage 4 documents found that the draw-level differences of (3) cannot be read off the variance shares, and probed them with extra integrations.
- At the headline case, 28 draws differ between their joint draw and their anode-electrolyte run, in mode or in the hottest node's starting reaction.
- For each of them two more combinations are integrated along the same two histories, with the Stage 1 driver unchanged: the draw's anode-electrolyte and separator particles with the cathode at its posterior mean, and the draw's anode-electrolyte and cathode particles with the separator at its posterior mean.
- The record says which combination restores the joint draw's mode and starting reaction. It also gives each draw's separator melting term of the self-heating, in the fresh state at 108.4 C, against the posterior mean's.
- These are kinetic integrations along the archived histories only, with no new data and no thermal model, and nothing in them is scored.

D31. Presentation (no result depends on it).
- Figure 3 (`results/v11_figure.png`) is shown in the README; the note keeps Figure 1 alone.
- The submodels take categorical colours that Figures 1 and 2 do not use: violet, magenta and yellow, checked for colour-blind separation. Figure 1 draws the resolved mean in aqua, so blue, orange and aqua keep their meanings. A first draft gave the cathode aqua, which the independent check caught.
- The note gains its six Stage 4 lines without any change to its earlier text, as the brief requires. To keep it on one page only its stylesheet changes:
  - Figure 1 at 56 % of the text width (66 % before);
  - body text and byline at 7.4 pt (7.8 pt), line height 1.16 (1.19);
  - tables at 7.2 pt (7.6 pt), with cell padding 0.4 pt (1.2 pt);
  - page margins 6 and 5 mm at top and bottom (8 and 7 mm), heading space 2.5 pt (4 pt);
  - the credit line at 6.9 pt (7.3 pt), 2 pt below the text (3 pt).

## Added after an independent check of the Stage 4 documents (records and errata; no registered result changes)

D32. An independent check (16 October) recomputed every Stage 4 quantity from the records, without the study's scoring code. Every registered number and verdict reproduced. It found the following, recorded here.
(1) Erratum in `predictions_v11.md`, which is kept unchanged. Its background section gives the Stage 3 standard deviation of the lumped onset at 100 mW/K as 95.4 s; it is 95.3 s (95.347 s, `results/stage3a_metrics.json`). No score depends on it.
(2) The failure rule of D29 (2) was not built into the harness. A failed integration would have stopped `src/v11_sensitivity.py` rather than been recorded. No integration failed, since all 600 grouped draws completed in one pass (`results/v11_run.log`), so the rule never applied.
(3) Unspecified details:
- The distances of the central half use the particles' population standard deviation (ddof 0), which `predictions_v11.md` does not specify. With ddof 1 every central half is the same, because one scale factor per file cannot change the ranking.
- At 100 mW/K, the bootstrap intervals of the narrowing are taken over the resamples in which both widths are bounded, so they are conditional intervals. No document quotes them.
(4) Counting. D29 counts 3 x 200 x 2 = 1200 runs, one per submodel, draw and case. The README counts 600 grouped draws, one per submodel and draw, each run on both cases. Both counts make 2400 integrations.
(5) When the scoring code was written. `src/v11_score.py` was written after the predictions were pushed, while the grouped runs ran, and was committed with their results (18f184f); its rules were fixed beforehand in `predictions_v11.md`. Its exploratory parts (D30) and the per-reaction bootstrap intervals were added after the results.
(6) Wording. The documents' first wording overstated three points, now corrected:
- that the anode-electrolyte submodel carries "all" of the spread: it carries essentially all of the variance, but the separator decides the mode of a few marginal draws (D30 (4));
- the cause of the draw-level differences: the first wording pointed to the cathode at its mean, which caused none of the mode changes;
- what a measurement that decided between the lead's modes would buy: no measurement is simulated, and the central half is a parameter-space proxy for a tighter posterior.

## Stage 5 (version 1.2.0) design, recorded with the predictions before any Stage 5 run (21 October)

D33. The measurements (brief: measured targets). Fransson, Pfaff, Broche, Buckwell, Kirchner-Burles, Reid, Schopferer, Rack, Finegan and Shearing, "Exploring thermal runaway propagation in Li-ion batteries through high-speed X-ray imaging and thermal analysis", Journal of Power Sources 617 (2024) 234916, doi:10.1016/j.jpowsour.2024.234916, CC BY 4.0. It was read on 21 October through a text reader, in the Version of Record on ORA (University of Oxford) and in the copy on the document server of the National Laboratory of the Rockies, because this machine cannot download either file; the tables' averages, recomputed from their rows, match the printed ones. The rows used are in `inputs/fransson2024/m50_tests.csv`, with the quotations below in `inputs/fransson2024/SOURCE.md`.
(1) Tables 3 (M50, parallel) and 4 (M50, series) give for each test the trigger cell's maximum temperature, the neighbour's maximum, whether it propagated, the time to propagation and the failure category. Every number the brief quotes is confirmed there:
- parallel: 4 of 8 propagated, at 272 s (M50_PT_Exp4), 152 s (M50_PP_Exp1), 147 s (M50_PP_Exp2) and 275 s (M50_PP_Exp4); the table's mean and standard deviation, 212 and 72 s, recompute from the four (211.5 and 71.6 s, ddof 1);
- series: 5 of 8, at 177 s (M50_ST_Exp3), 342 s (M50_ST_Exp4), 223 s (M50_SP_Exp1), 149 s (M50_SP_Exp2) and 210 s (M50_SP_Exp3); 220 and 74 s recompute (220.2 and 74.0 s);
- the table averages of the trigger and neighbour maxima (parallel 603 ± 195 and 414 ± 290 C, series 686 ± 174 and 506 ± 256 C) recompute from the rows as well.
(2) The text. "a minimum of 150 ◦C is required in the neighboring cell in order for propagation to occur" (Section 4, read off its Fig. 3a, the distribution of the propagation onset temperature), and "the average heat rate for propagation to occur was 0.4 ◦C/s compared to 0.1 for non propagating cells", with the heat rates "calculated from measured temperatures in Section 2" (D34).
(3) Classification, followed as the paper gives it. The categories are the paper's: 1, the nail-penetration failure of a trigger cell; 2, failure in a propagating cell; 3, sidewall melting; 4, sidewall rupture of the trigger cell. Four M50 tests carry "cell movement" in place of a category (M50_PT_Exp1, which did not propagate; M50_PP_Exp1, M50_SP_Exp2 and M50_SP_Exp3, which did), because "the cell moved within the FoV" of the X-ray camera. Two carry no category (M50_PP_Exp3 and M50_SP_Exp4). Two tests that did not propagate show high neighbour maxima, 808 C (M50_PT_Exp2) and 626 C (M50_SP_Exp4). The paper does not discuss them one by one; it decides propagation from the number of temperature spikes and notes that, because cells and thermocouples moved during failure, "some outlier temperature values are still represented in the tables for consistency reasons". Both stay as not propagated, as the paper has them. So 9 of 16 tests propagated (56 %), and all nine times enter the measured range, 147 to 342 s.
(4) What the paper reports and does not report. The cells "were placed directly in contact with each other" and were "electrically connected via welded cell tabs"; the trigger cell "was penetrated around the center with a speed of 6 mm s−1 to an approximate depth of 1–3 mm"; "Thermocouples were fastened in the middle of the cell surface using Kapton tape and heat conductive glue"; each cell "was coated with black carbon paint"; and "a 15% CO2 ratio was used for fire retention". It does not report the state of charge, the tab material and dimensions, the holder's material, the ambient temperature, the nail's material and diameter, or the thermocouple type and sampling rate.

D34. Time to propagation (brief: settle the reading). The paper divides each thermocouple record into sections. Section 1 runs from the temperature increase caused by the trigger cell's thermal runaway to its maximum. In Section 2 "the trigger cell starts to cool down and the dissipated heat is conducted to the propagation cell that heats up", and it "ends at the onset of the TR in the second cells" (at the neighbour's maximum where nothing propagates). Section 3 is the neighbour's runaway. The paper never states the start and end of the time to propagation in one sentence, but its discussion of Fig. 5 reads "propagation occurred 156 s after TR in the trigger cell". The time to propagation is therefore read as the time from the trigger cell's thermal runaway, the start of Section 1, to the onset of the neighbour's runaway, the end of Section 2. The model's trigger starts to release heat at t = 0, which stands for the start of its runaway, so the model's time to propagation is the time of the propagation crossing (D35), with no shift. The other reading, the length of Section 2 alone, would subtract the time of cell 1's maximum; it is reported beside the registered one and not scored.

D35. Propagation in the model (brief: model quantities). The neighbour propagates when its hottest node (resolved) or its single temperature (lumped) first reaches 255.7 C, the lower edge of the internal-short band (the two-cell study's D2), within 900 s of the start of the trigger's release. The crossing time is interpolated linearly within the step, as in Stage 2. A run stops at that crossing, because nothing after it is scored and the model has no runaway of the neighbour as a whole; a run without it goes on to 900 s. The resolved and the lumped neighbour of a combination are separate integrations of the same cell-1 history, and each stops at its own crossing.

D36. The contact between the cans (brief, configuration 1).
(1) No published contact conductance for cylindrical cells in contact was found. Searches on 21 October (cell-to-cell contact conductance or resistance for 18650 and 21700 cells, touching cylindrical batteries, line contacts between cylinders) found single-cell radial conductivities, interfaces inside a cell, and module models that place the cells apart or give no value. So the brief's fallback applies.
(2) Three published steel-on-steel contact conductances, all from J.H. Lienhard V and J.H. Lienhard IV, A Heat Transfer Textbook, 6th edition (Phlogiston Press, Cambridge, Massachusetts, 2024), Table 2.1 (Section 2.3, p. 76), "Some typical interfacial conductances for normal surface finishes and moderate contact pressures (about 1 to 10 atm). Air gaps not evacuated unless so indicated.": stainless steel on stainless steel, 2,000 to 3,700 W/(m2 K), and, with evacuated interstices, 200 to 1,100 W/(m2 K). The table was read on 21 October in the authors' PDF of the book (ahtt.mit.edu, version 6.00), through the text of that page. Its pressures, about 1 to 10 atm, are the lightest it gives; the paper does not report the load between the cells.
- low, 200 W/(m2 K): the smallest steel-on-steel value in the table, a contact that the interstitial gas does not help (for instance through the paint layers);
- central, 2,000 W/(m2 K): with air, the low end of the range, the end a light load favours, since the cells only rest against each other;
- high, 3,700 W/(m2 K): with air, the high end.
Every Stage 5 result is conditional on this range.
(3) How the contact enters. Within the 45 degree sector of the neighbour's surface that faces cell 1 (the two-cell study's gap sector, its D6), the local conductance per unit area is h(θ) = k_air / (δ(θ) + k_air / h_c), with δ(θ) = 2R(1 − cos θ) the gap between the two cans along the line of centres at the angle θ from it, R = 10.55 mm and k_air = 0.03 W/(m K) (the two-cell study's value). On the line of contact it equals the published contact conductance h_c; away from it, it falls to the air conduction across the wedge, k_air/δ, set by the geometry. This is the conduction across an air gap of δ + Y, with the effective separation at the contact, Y = k_air/h_c (150, 15 and 8.1 µm), fixed by the published conductance instead of by roughness and paint thicknesses the paper does not report. Radiation is kept as before (D38).
(4) Integrated over the sector and the cell's length (70.1 mm), the path conducts G = 0.0503, 0.1701 and 0.2332 W/K at the low, central and high value (0.717, 2.427 and 3.327 W/(m K) per unit length), against 0.0174 W/K for the two-cell study's 2 mm gap. About half of it, 55, 52 and 51 %, crosses the band where δ < Y, which is 2.52, 0.80 and 0.58 mm wide: that is the contact. The rest crosses the wedge. The resolved neighbour takes the local h(θ) through its boundary weights, integrated with a sixth-order facet rule and normalised so that a uniform field takes exactly G (V1); the lumped neighbour takes G.
(5) Left out: the paint layers, the load between the cells and the holder, none of which the paper reports, and convection in the wedge beyond 45 degrees (D6).

D37. The tabs (brief, configuration 2). The paper says only that the cells were "electrically connected via welded cell tabs". No published dimensions and material for welded 21700 tabs were found: the welding studies found give a thickness without a width, concern 18650 cells, or join busbars. So the tabs are bracketed with the two-cell study's archived nickel-strip conductances, as the brief directs: 5 and 10 mW/K per tab, the two levels of its conductance sweep that bracket its nickel strip of 0.2 by 8 mm (its D24), and, for the central case, that strip itself over the 21.1 mm centre distance of cells in contact, 90 × 1.6e-6 / 0.0211 = 6.8 mW/K. Series: one tab, from the trigger's positive cap to the neighbour's negative base, so the neighbour's tab is at its base. Parallel: two tabs, cap to cap and base to base. A tab's heat enters the neighbour over an 8 mm disc at the centre of the end face: on the cap, the two-cell study's positive patch; on the base, the same disc, where a welded tab sits, in place of the busbar's annulus at the rim (its D7). At the central values the tabs conduct 4 % (series) and 8 % (parallel) of the contact path. Every Stage 5 result is conditional on this bracket.

D38. Radiation, room losses, trigger and ambient (brief, configurations 1, 3 and 4).
- Radiation between the cans keeps the two-cell study's grey radiosity form (its D5), with the view factor of two cylinders in contact, F = (π/2 − 1)/π = 0.1817, Incropera's parallel-cylinder formula at zero gap (0.1599 at 2 mm), and the local view factor with the centre distance 2R. The emissivity stays 0.8 although the cells were painted black, because the brief keeps radiation as before.
- Room convection, 10 W/(m2 K), acts outside the 45 degree sector (its D6), and room radiation over the whole outer area (its D5); the cells stood in a holder with no compartment around them, as in the two-cell study's open room.
- The trigger is the two-cell study's: the Databank's largest cell-body energy for the LG M50 at 100 % state of charge, 33.2 kJ (its D19), released as a triangular pulse over 10 or 30 s. The paper does not state the state of charge; 100 % is an assumption. The Databank's ten M50 nail tests at 100 % state of charge span 18.67 to 33.22 kJ (median 25.88 kJ), and the minimum and the median are run at the central case as a sensitivity.
- Cell 1 is the two-cell study's lumped node, its history one-way: the lumped pair of `lumped_two_cell.run`, explicit Euler at 0.01 s, written every 0.1 s, with the Stage 5 paths (contact, tabs, the contact view factor), as `v12_model.lumped_pair`. With the two-cell paths it reproduces all 42 archived histories character for character (V2).
- Ambient 25 C, as the two-cell study; 20 and 30 C as a sensitivity at the central case.

D39. The thermocouple (brief, configuration 6). The two probes are the surface nodes at the axial middle (z = 35.05 mm) on the line of centres: toward the trigger (θ = 0) and away from it (θ = 180 degrees). On cells in contact the face toward the trigger at θ = 0 is the line of contact itself, where a thermocouple taped to the can could not sit between the cans; both are reported, as the brief asks, and P3 and P4 are scored on both (predictions_v12.md). The mean heating rate is the probe's temperature at the propagation crossing minus its temperature 60 s before, divided by 60 s; if propagation comes earlier than 60 s, the window starts at t = 0, and the record gives its length. The probes are recorded at every step and interpolated linearly between steps. The paper's heat rates are averages over Section 2 (D33 (2), D34); the model's average from cell 1's maximum to the propagation crossing is reported beside them as exploratory.

D40. The reaction heat (brief, configuration 5). As Stage 2 (D13, D14): the reaction heat is fed back in the neighbour, one-way, with fresh reactants at t = 0 in every node and in the lumped node, under the posterior-mean kinetics and under each of the 200 Stage 3 joint draws (D22; `results/stage3_draw_indices.csv`, built through `stage3_draws.draw_kinetics`, the path Stage 3a used).

D41. The compiled reaction step (`src/v12_kernel.py`). The full factorial needs 5,248 reactive runs of the resolved neighbour (D43). The Stage 2 reaction sub-step in numpy took about 40 ms a step on the 19,355-node mesh of D42. `src/v12_kernel.py` does the same per node in numba: the same rates, sub-step counts, Heun stages, clipping, limits and BDF fallback (`reactive.LocalKinetics._implicit`, unchanged), in about 10 to 15 ms. The only difference is arithmetic order: numpy forms the sums over species with BLAS and the kernel in species order, and the two libraries' exp and log differ in the last bit. Checks before the predictions (`results/v12_checks.json`, "kernel"): on synthetic node states from 25 to 355 C (20,000, 4,000 and 1,000 nodes), the rates agree to 1.4e-14 relative and one step to 0 K in the temperatures (identical) and 1.1e-16 in the amounts, with the same nodes sent to BDF; through the Stage 5 loop in the two-cell configuration, the five Stage 2 cases reproduce Stage 2's band entries within 3.2e-14 s, its peaks within 8.0e-13 K, its step and BDF node counts and its lumped node exactly, with the series within 1.6e-12 K (V2). The numpy step stays available (`kernel="numpy"`), and V2 runs it as well. A threaded variant of the same loops (`V12_KERNEL_THREADS`) is not used.

D42. The Stage 5 mesh (`src/v12_mesh.py`). The two-cell mesh spaces its outer rings evenly, 0.49 mm apart on the can. The contact band is 0.58 to 2.52 mm wide (D36 (4)), so that spacing would put the band's peak conductance on one or two nodes. The Stage 5 mesh keeps the published generator and outer rings and changes where the nodes sit:
- the outer rings share an azimuthal spacing that starts at 0.06 mm on the line of contact and grows by 1.2 to 0.5 mm within the sector, then by 1.1 to 1.2 mm on the far side, with nodes exactly at 45 and 180 degrees;
- the inner rings are uniform at 0.9 mm (0.55 mm before), with 8 rings inside 8.2 mm (13 before);
- the axial layers grow from 0.3 mm at both ends by 1.3 to at most 3 mm (0.2 mm and 2.5 mm before), since the contact path does not vary along the cell.
It has 19,355 nodes, against 39,990. Its volume and outer area are within 0.13 and 0.05 % of the cylinder's, and its integral of the contact conductance within 2e-5 of the exact one (before the normalisation of D36 (4)). V3 halves every spacing (145,195 nodes). The two-cell mesh is kept for V2.

D43. The run design (`src/v12_run.py`).
(1) The brief's propagation share asks for 200 draws × 3 contact conductances × the tab bracket (2) × 2 releases × 2 connections = 4,800 reactive runs of the resolved neighbour. Told that this would take roughly 100 to 300 hours on this machine's two cores, the author chose the full factorial over three reduced designs (21 October), and then asked that it run on the 24 cores of his laptop. It runs in full.
(2) Five sets, run in this order:
- grid: the posterior-mean kinetics at every combination of the 3 contact conductances, the 3 tab conductances (low, central, high), the 2 releases and the 2 connections (36 runs). It holds the central case of P1 to P5 (central contact and tab, both releases and connections) and the thermal range of P7;
- sens: at the central case and the 30 s release, ambient 20 and 30 C, and the trigger energy at the Databank nail tests' minimum and median (8 runs);
- v3: at the central case and the 30 s release, every mesh spacing halved, and every step limit halved: the base step 0.1 to 0.05 s and D13's 1 K and 4 K limits to 0.5 and 2 K (4 runs);
- draws: the 200 Stage 3 draws at the central case and the 30 s release, both connections (400 runs), the kinetic spread of P7;
- fact: the factorial of the brief, 200 draws × 3 contacts × the tab bracket (5 and 10 mW/K) × 2 releases × 2 connections (4,800 runs), ordered by draw, so that a partial set is a set of whole draws.
That is 5,248 runs, each of the resolved and of the lumped neighbour. The run list's SHA-256 is 27874f55b434b92dea3d06abca49a1b5553e94fab1162b9cec016579f3c1f747 (`python3 v12_run.py list`).
(3) Each run writes one record (`results/v12_runs/<id>.json`), stamped with its start and finish (UTC); the grid, sens and v3 runs also keep their series (`results/v12_series/`). Two workers share the list by index and skip recorded runs, so an interrupted pass resumes where it stopped. Each pass runs inside a shell command of limited length; a run still going when that time runs out is left unrecorded and redone whole, with the same settings, by the next pass. The time limits use the operating system's alarm where there is one and a timer thread where there is not (Windows). A run whose integration fails, or that takes longer than 40 min (100 min on the refined mesh), is recorded as failed and left out of every share and spread, with the count reported; no run is repeated with other settings.
(4) Every run uses one thread per library (OpenBLAS, MKL, OpenMP, numba), set in the scripts before numpy and numba load, and the runs go in parallel, one process per core. Which machine ran them, and how many at a time, does not change what a run computes; it is recorded with the results. With OpenBLAS's default of two threads, its vector dot products, which the loop uses for the energy account and the mean temperature, ran about a thousand times slower beside the compiled kernel (8 ms against 5 µs), and the sparse solve three times slower. The setting does not touch the arithmetic of the temperatures, which use no BLAS call on the compiled path.
(5) The scoring rules are fixed in `predictions_v12.md` and implemented, before any Stage 5 run, in `src/v12_score.py`, committed with it.

D44. Checks before the predictions (V1, V2; `results/v12_checks.json`). None of them uses a Stage 5 history of cell 1.
- V1 (a), every boundary path of the Stage 5 cell on a uniform field against the lumped formula, at the three contact conductances: largest relative difference 2.3e-16.
- V1 (b), the brief's analytic steady case: cell 1 held at 100 C, the roll at 1e4 W/mK so that the neighbour is isothermal, contact (with no tab, the series tab or the parallel tabs) and room convection only; the resolved mean against (G T1 + hA Ta)/(G + hA): largest error 5.3e-5 of the rise (acceptance 1 %).
- V1 (c), the energy account of a passive Stage 5 run under a synthetic 10 K step of cell 1 held for 100 s, published roll: residual 8.9e-12 of the heat taken in (4.1e-9 with the roll at 1e4 W/mK, where the stiffer system carries more round-off); (d), a reactive Stage 5 run with every path off from a uniform 150 C: residual 4.5e-10.
- V2 (a), the lumped pair with the two-cell paths: all 42 histories identical to the archived files, character for character; (b), the Stage 5 class in its two-cell configuration: every array (conduction, capacities and the six boundary operators) equal to the published model's, difference 0.
- V2 (c), passive runs of the two-cell configuration through the Stage 5 loop, three cases: the archived series reproduced to 1.3e-12 K (hottest node), the lumped node exactly; Stage 1's records from the regenerated series give the same onset times to the 0.01 s reported and the same reactions, every other field within 8.0e-7 relative (a conversion at 900 s), while Stage 1's integrator takes a few more or fewer steps (4743 against 4766 at most) on series that differ at 1e-12 K.
- V2 (d), the reactive Stage 2 cases through the Stage 5 loop: with the published numpy step, the headline case gives the band entries (19.23, 20.54 and 22.00 s), the peaks, the step counts and the lumped node identical to the last digit, and the series within 8.5e-13 K; with the compiled step, all five cases give the band entries within 3.2e-14 s, the peaks within 8.0e-13 K, the step and BDF node counts and the lumped node exactly, and the series within 1.6e-12 K.
After the predictions: V3 (sets above; acceptance a change of the time to propagation under 2 %, or, where the central case does not propagate, of the peak rise of the hottest node under 2 %), the energy account of every run (acceptance 1e-9 of the heat taken in, as Stage 2), and V4, an independent read of every registered number and verdict before the note.

## Added after the Stage 5 predictions were public, before any Stage 5 run (21 October)

D45. Where the runs are made. The author asked that the runs use the 24 cores of his laptop (Intel Core Ultra 9 275HX, Windows). The commands of this study reach that laptop only through a Linux sandbox with two cores and 3 GB, so the runs go natively on Windows: Python 3.13.16 (python-build-standalone 20261020) with the packages of the machine that registered the predictions (numpy 2.5.3, scipy 1.18.1, numba 0.68.0, llvmlite 0.50.0, scikit-fem 12.0.2, pypardiso 0.4.7 with MKL 2026.1.0; `requirements_windows.txt`), started by `run_stage5_windows.cmd` and `src/v12_laptop.py`.
(1) Before any Stage 5 run there, the laptop repeats the kernel check and V2 (d), the five reactive Stage 2 cases through the Stage 5 loop with the compiled step, into `results/v12_checks_laptop/`. Acceptance, fixed here: in every case every band entry within 0.01 s and every peak (hottest node, mean and lumped node) within 0.01 K of Stage 2's records, the precision Stage 2 reported, with PARDISO as the sparse solver. If it fails, no Stage 5 run is made there.
(2) One worker per core (fewer if the memory is short, at 1 GB each), at below-normal priority. Because the laptop's cores run at unequal speeds, the workers share the run list through claim files (`v12_run.py work --queue`) instead of taking every n-th run; a stop file ends a pass. Every record carries the machine it ran on (`machine`, without the computer's name).
(3) The grid, sens and v3 sets are also run on this cloud machine's two cores, into `results/v12_runs_cloud/`. The laptop's records are the registered ones for every run, and the cloud duplicates are compared with them as a check across the two platforms. If the laptop does not complete the list, the cloud records count for the runs they cover and the cloud machine runs the rest.
None of this changes what a run computes (D43 (4)).

## Added while the Stage 5 runs were going, before any run of the draws or fact sets (21 October)

D46. Finishing sooner. On the laptop the environment check of D45 (1) passed (`results/v12_checks_laptop/acceptance.json`): in the five Stage 2 cases the band entries agree within 7.8e-14 s and the peaks within 4.3e-12 K, with the same step counts, under PARDISO. Its 16 workers, the number the memory rule of D45 (2) allowed, started at 18:08:04 UTC, and a base-mesh run takes about 400 s there. Two measures follow, neither of which changes what a run computes:
(1) More of the laptop's cores. `add_workers_windows.cmd` (`src/v12_laptop_more.py`) turns off Windows' efficiency mode for the running workers, which can hold background processes on the slower cores at lower clocks, and starts more in the same queue, as many as the idle logical processors and the free memory allow (1.3 times the largest running worker's memory each).
(2) A share for the cloud machine. Once its D45 (3) runs are done, the cloud machine makes the fact runs of draws 176 to 200 (600 runs, the end of the list), into `results/v12_runs_cloud/`. The laptop skips them through claim files placed in its run folder. Each of those 600 runs is recorded by the cloud machine, and every other run by the laptop; if the cloud machine has not finished them when the laptop runs out of work, the laptop's next start clears the claims and makes the rest.

## Added after runs failed for want of memory on the laptop (22 October; before any scoring)

D47. Runs that failed for want of memory. At 10:36:52 to 10:36:55 UTC on 22 October all 16 laptop workers failed every run they started with MemoryError or PARDISO error -2 (not enough memory): 656 runs of the fact set, draws 74 to 102, were recorded as failed within those three seconds, and the runs after them succeeded again. The cause was this study's code: `pypardiso` keeps a factorisation's memory until `free_memory` is called, and `v12_model.run_resolved` dropped its factorisations at the end of each run without calling it. On this machine 30 discarded factorisations of the base mesh took a process from 282 to 1,538 MB, and with `free_memory` it did not grow. The cloud machine's workers restart every pass; the laptop's had run for 16.5 hours.
(1) The fix frees the factorisations at the end of each run. It does not change a run's arithmetic: a grid run recomputed with it gives the same record, all 58 fields equal.
(2) These are failures of the machine, not of the integration that D43 (3) means. Their records are set aside in `results/v12_failed_memory/` (kept, with their tracebacks, and listed in `results/v12_failed_memory.json`) and the runs are made again with the same settings, by workers restarted with the fix (`restart_stage5_windows.cmd`, `src/v12_laptop_restart.py`).
(3) From now on a worker retries a run that fails for want of memory after a minute, up to 10 times, instead of recording it, and notes the retries in the record (`memory_retries`). Any other failure is recorded as D43 (3) has it.
(4) The restart asks for 24 workers, one per logical processor, as the author asked, and allows 0.6 GB of free memory for each instead of the 1 GB of D45 (2): with the fix a worker held 0.39 to 0.47 GB here. It starts fewer only if the free memory is short.
(5) The first restart (14:35 UTC) stopped the 10 workers still running; the other 6 had already ended on errors outside any run (a timer thread that could not start, Windows error 1450, MemoryError), the leak's doing. It then waited on those 6 process ids, which Windows had since given to other processes, and so started nothing. The restart now takes a process for a worker only if it was created when that worker started, and a worker treats a timer thread that cannot start, or Windows error 1450 or 1455, as a shortage of memory too.
(6) The second restart (19:13 UTC) found old workers still running, though their logs had ended: processes left hanging after the leak. A restart now gives the running workers 12 minutes to finish their current runs and then ends any that have not stopped; a run cut off this way is not recorded and is made again.

D46 (3), added 22 October at 20:10 UTC. The cloud machine finished draws 176 to 200 while the laptop, restarted at 19:51 UTC with 24 workers (D47), had not yet reached draw 166; a laptop run takes about 650 s with 24 workers running, so more of the laptop's cores bring little more speed. The cloud machine therefore also makes the fact runs of draws 166 to 175 (240 runs), on the terms of (2): claim files keep the laptop off them, and those records come from the cloud machine.

D46 (4), added 23 October at 02:00 UTC. A restart of the cloud machine stopped its runs at about 21:25 UTC, after 30 of the 240 runs of (3), and they stood until 01:55 UTC. To finish both machines at about the same time, the fact runs of draws 173 to 175 (72 runs) go back to the laptop: their claim files were moved to `results/v12_claims_returned/` on the laptop before it had reached draw 166. The cloud machine makes draws 166 to 172; every other run is the laptop's.

## Added after the Stage 5 results (23 October; presentation, records and exploratory analyses)

D48. Written after every Stage 5 run had ended and after the scoring. No registered quantity or verdict depends on it.
(1) Quoted numbers. Every Stage 5 result that the README, the note and the Zenodo description quote is computed by `src/v12_numbers.py` (`results/v12_numbers.json`) from the scored records (`results/v12_results.json`, `results/v12_runs.csv`), the run records and the series that the runs kept. The verdicts, V3 and the energy accounts are `v12_score.py`'s; the other checks are quoted from their records (`results/v12_checks.json`, `results/v12_checks_laptop/`), and the comparison of the two machines from `results/v12_merge.json`. `v12_score.py` now records the predictions commit in the scorecard, which it could not hold when it was committed before the predictions (03af770); nothing else in it changed. While the runs were going, the merge, the scoring and the figure were run on the records made so far, to test them before the records were complete. No decision after D44 changes what a run computes or how it is scored, and the registered scorecard is the one computed from the complete records.
(2) Exploratory analyses. No run propagated. The quantities that `predictions_v12.md` defines at the propagation time therefore do not exist: the mid-surface temperature and heating rate of P3 and P4, the Section 2 heating rate of D39, and the other reading of the time to propagation (D34). P1 fails, and P3 to P5 and P7 are not met, as the predictions score them. Three quantities, defined after the results, describe the runs instead. They use the series of the grid and sens sets (the v3 set keeps series too) and are not scored.
- (a) Section 2 as the paper reads it for a test without propagation, from the trigger cell's maximum (the record's time of cell 1's maximum) to the neighbour's maximum (D34): for each probe and for the lumped neighbour, the temperature rise over that interval divided by its length. The paper gives 0.1 C/s for its tests that did not propagate and 0.4 C/s for those that did, the latter over a Section 2 that ends at the neighbour's runaway.
- (b) The time that each probe and the lumped neighbour spend at or above the paper's 150 C, between crossings interpolated within the step, as in D35.
- (c) Where the hottest node is at its peak, from the node index that the series keep.
(3) Beside the paper's tables. The model's cell 1 maximum and the neighbours' maxima are set beside the trigger and neighbour maxima of Tables 3 and 4, with the tests classified as the paper classifies them (D33 (3)). This is a description, not a check: the paper's maxima include the movement of cells and thermocouples that it describes.
(4) Records.
- D47 counted the 656 runs of the first memory failure. A second, at 12:50 UTC on 22 October, before the workers had been restarted with the fix, failed 192 more runs of the fact set. The restart of 19:51 UTC set aside all 848 (draws 74 to 122) into `results/v12_failed_memory/`, listed in `results/v12_failed_memory.json`, and every one was made again. No registered record failed, and no registered run needed a retry for memory (D47 (3)).
- `src/v12_merge.py` merges the registered records under D46 (2) to (4): 4,480 from the laptop and 768 from the cloud machine. The cloud machine's 816 records, its 48 repeats of the grid, sens and v3 sets among them, stay in `results/v12_runs_cloud/`. The comparison of the 48 runs that both machines made is in `results/v12_merge.json`.
- The run folders, the workers' logs and the records set aside are committed with the results; they were left out of the repository while the runs were going. The cloud machine's series (`results/v12_series_cloud/`) are not committed: its records hold every field that the comparison uses, and the laptop's series of the same runs are the registered ones.
(5) Figure 4 (`src/make_figure_v12.py`) was written with the scoring script, before the predictions. Two changes for legibility were made while the runs were going (commit 818ccf1), once records without propagation existed: the hottest node, which hides the probe on the line of contact, is drawn over it, and the label for runs without propagation is moved clear of the markers.
(6) The note gains seven Stage 5 lines without any change to its earlier text, as the brief requires. To keep it on one page only its stylesheet changes:
- Figure 1 at 44 % of the text width (56 % before), still centred (left margin 28 %, 22 % before);
- body text and byline at 6.9 pt (7.4 pt), line height 1.12 (1.16);
- tables at 6.8 pt (7.2 pt);
- page margins 5 and 4 mm at top and bottom (6 and 5 mm).

## Added after the independent check of the Stage 5 records and documents (23 October)

D49. V4 (brief, checks): an independent read of every registered Stage 5 number and verdict, made before the note's Stage 5 lines were finalised. A separate agent, which had not seen the work being produced, recomputed the run list, the merge, P1 to P7, V3, the energy accounts and every number quoted in the README, the note's draft lines, the release notes and the Zenodo description, from the run records, the series and the measured tables, with scripts of its own and without the study's scoring code.
(1) Every registered number and verdict reproduced. The 5,248 records are exactly the run list; none failed; each is byte-identical to its source record on the machine that the merge rule names. P1 to P7, V3 and the energy accounts equal `results/v12_scorecard.json`, and V1, V2, the kernel check and the laptop's environment check reproduce from their records.
(2) It found nine discrepancies in the draft texts, none in a registered quantity. Each was corrected before the release:
- the lumped neighbour's first crossing of 150 C was taken at the first stored sample at or above it (153.5 s, quoted as 154 s); it is now interpolated within the step, as D35 does (153.49 s), and every 150 C crossing is computed the same way;
- the spread of the hottest node's peak over the 200 draws was given as "at most 0.07 K"; it is 0.073 K in each connection;
- the note and the release notes gave cell 1's maximum in the central case (548 C) without saying so; over all runs it reaches 552.5 C;
- "central contact" labelled numbers that hold for the central case, the central contact with the central tabs;
- the note's count of runs described the factorial alone;
- the Section 2 rate left out the face toward the trigger (0.56 to 1.30 C/s in the central case). Section 2 now starts at the record's time of cell 1's maximum rather than at the 0.1 s series' maximum, which moves the other rates by less than 0.01 C/s;
- the comparison of the two machines said "no other difference", though their energy residuals differ by up to 7.3e-13;
- the README called D45 to D47 decisions added while the runs were going; D45 came before any run;
- the README cited D49 before it existed.
(3) A second pass, on the corrected texts, confirmed the nine corrections and every new number, and found three more problems, corrected likewise:
- the README's list of decisions still dated D45 to the runs;
- the status bullet credited the corrected numbers and D48's rewording to bd1303b rather than 5053b2f;
- the note and the release notes gave the central case's Section 2 rates without saying so.
(4) Wording that it suggested and the study adopted:
- the V2 row names what is identical, and the run window is that of the registered runs;
- the refined V3 runs' length is given, and P6's binomial standard error (0) is reported;
- "not met, so failed" where the note and the release notes said "not met", and the Stage 5 rows of the Verification table are labelled as such;
- "if at all" beside the expectation that the model would propagate later than the tests;
- the sensitivity energies are named as the nail tests' minimum and median;
- the line of contact's Section 2 rate is given with its scope (it peaks 12 to 18 s after cell 1 in the central case, but 257 to 274 s after it at the low contact);
- D48 (1), (2) and (6) are reworded in place, as D32 did for Stage 4: the check figures are quoted from their records, the v3 set keeps series too, the Section 2 start and the 150 C crossings are as above, and Figure 1 stays centred.
(5) Errata found beside the brief:
- D46 (3) and (4) give their times as 20:10 and 02:00 UTC. They were pushed at 20:08:15 and 01:56:37 UTC (`provenance/github_activity_stage5_runs.json`), before the first runs they affect (20:08:19 and 01:56:42).
- D47 (1)'s "all 58 fields equal" counted every field of the record, the wall time, the timestamps and the machine among them.
