On 23 October the grid run `grid_mean_series_hcentral_gcentral_tau30` was made again with the final code, on the cloud machine. Its 42 other fields are identical to the cloud machine's own record of that run, made before the fix (21 October, `results/v12_runs_cloud/`). They lie within 5.2e-11 J and 9.6e-12 K of the laptop's registered record, the round-off between the two machines.


## Stage 6 design, recorded with the predictions before any Stage 6 computation (23 and 24 October)

D50. Stage 6 (brief of 24 October): why the model does not propagate where the tests did. Part A applies the paper's 150 C criterion to the archived Stage 5 runs, with no new physics; Part B runs the model with the trigger matched to the tests; Part C is recorded and not run (D57). No Stage 1 to 5 code or result is edited and no Stage 5 run is repeated: the Stage 6 code is in new files (`src/stage6_inputs.py`, `src/stage6_crossings.py`, `src/stage6_run.py`, `src/stage6_score.py`, `src/stage6_checks.py`, `src/make_figure_stage6.py`, `src/stage6_laptop.py`, `src/test_stage6.py`, `run_stage6_windows.cmd`), which import the Stage 5 model and harness (`src/v12_model.py`, `src/v12_run.py`) unchanged. The version is the author's decision (D58).

D51. Part A (brief, A1 to A3; `src/stage6_crossings.py`).
(1) The runs: all 5,248 Stage 5 records, and the histories of the 48 runs that kept their series (`results/v12_series/`: the grid, sens and v3 sets, D43 (3)). The draws and fact sets kept peaks only, so for their 5,200 runs Part A gives each history's peak, its time (except the mean's) and whether it reaches 150 C, but no crossing time.
(2) The histories: the hottest node (the hot spot); the resolved mean, weighted by heat capacity, the records' mean; the surface node at mid-height on the face away from the trigger (θ = 180 degrees, z = 35.05 mm, D39), where the paper places its thermocouple, "in the middle of the cell surface"; and the lumped neighbour. The surface node at mid-height on the line of contact (θ = 0, D39) is reported separately. The paper does not say on which side of the cell its thermocouple sat, and one taped to the can could not sit on the line of contact between the cans (D39), so the comparison uses the face away.
(3) A crossing is the first time a history reaches 150 C, interpolated linearly between the two stored samples that bracket it, as D35 and D48 (2) do: the hottest node, the mean and the lumped neighbour are stored every 0.1 s, the probes at every step. The return below 150 C is found the same way. A history reaches 150 C if its peak does. The peaks and their times are the records' (taken at every step), except the mean's time, which the records do not keep and which comes from the series.
(4) A2, from `inputs/fransson2024/m50_tests.csv` (D33 (1)): column `time_to_propagation_s` of the nine tests that propagated (Table 3: 272, 152, 147 and 275 s; Table 4: 177, 342, 223, 149 and 210 s); column `neighbour_max_C` of the seven that did not (Table 3: 71, 808, 118 and 105 C; Table 4: 128, 133 and 626 C); column `trigger_max_C` of all sixteen (164 to 832 C, median 704.5 C).
(5) A3: the measured window is 147 to 342 s, inclusive. For each history: the crossings inside it, before it and after it, and the runs without a crossing, over the grid by connection, contact, tabs and release, and in each of its 18 combinations of connection, contact and tabs; the sens and v3 sets apart.
(6) Outputs: `results/stage6_crossings.csv` (one row per record) and `results/stage6_crossings.json` (A2 and A3).

D52. The release durations (brief, B1).
(1) The paper's tables hold no time from the trigger's runaway to its maximum. Tables 3 and 4 give, per test, the trigger's and the neighbour's maxima, the outcome, the time to propagation and the failure category (D33 (1)); Tables 5 and 6 give speeds measured in the X-ray images. The paper's supplementary material (Appendix A, `1-s2.0-S0378775324008681-mmc1.pdf`, 10,240,301 bytes, SHA-256 e96713ef58e8dc13df8810034c7ee8b47fb3e2fdcdb398a5095ecdfb64dfc0ef) has, in its Figure 10, the thermocouple records of two tests. Its caption explains that each record is divided into sections, that the mean heat rate of each cell in each section is computed from them and printed in boxes beside the plot, and that Section I is "the region of TR in the trigger cell". Those rates, in C/s, trigger cell first, are recorded in `inputs/fransson2024/fig10_section_rates.csv`:
- M50_PT_Exp4, which propagated: Section I 23.97 and 3.33; Section II −1.66 and 0.36; Section III 1.12 and 11.77; Section IV −0.30 and −1.56;
- M50_PP_Exp3, which did not: Section I 37.70 and 2.22; Section II −1.43 and 0.14; Section IV −0.57 and −0.04.
The file was read on 23 and 24 October through its text layer, for the values and the caption, and as rendered, for the sections.
(2) Section I runs from the temperature increase of the trigger's runaway to its maximum (D34), so its length is the trigger's rise over it divided by its mean rate. The rise is the trigger's maximum in Table 3 (630 C for M50_PT_Exp4, 686 C for M50_PP_Exp3) less its temperature at the start. The paper does not report that temperature; it is taken as 25 C, the model's ambient, a conditional choice. The lengths are 25.24 s (M50_PT_Exp4) and 17.53 s (M50_PP_Exp3); the rendered figure ends Section I at about 25 s and 17 s. They are the range of the measured times from the trigger's runaway to its maximum, and their median, the mean of the two, is 21.39 s. The paper gives no such time for the other fourteen tests. The author chose these values on 24 October, labelled conditional.
(3) The three times are the release durations of Part B, in place of Stage 5's 10 and 30 s, which stay as the reference. They are measured on the trigger's own thermocouple, at the middle of its surface. The model's cell 1 peaks at about the end of its triangular release (9.84 s for 10 s and 28.66 s for 30 s in the Stage 5 central case), so a release of the measured length puts the model's trigger peak at about the measured time. Every Part B result is conditional on the 25 C start and on two parallel tests standing for all sixteen.

D53. The trigger energies (brief, B2). The rows are the ten LG M50 nail tests at 100 % state of charge in `inputs/databank/databank_m50_used.csv`, the rows whose cell-body energies give Stage 5's 33.2 kJ: ESRF18_Oct_Run063, Run065 and Run067, DLS19_Feb_Run045 and Run047, and SPR2021_ESTA_8B100-01_SOC_RUN194 to RUN198.
- Stage 5's energy: 33,222.9 J, the largest `Energy-Fraction-Cell-Body-kJ` (RUN195, 33.2229 kJ), as the two-cell study recorded it (D38).
- The total: column `Corrected-Total-Energy-Yield-kJ`, the energy of the whole test, which includes the fractions carried out by the ejecta (`Energy-Fraction-Positive-Ejecta-kJ` and `Energy-Fraction-Negative-Ejecta-kJ`). Over the ten rows it spans 91.19 kJ (DLS19_Feb_Run045) to 110.83 kJ (RUN197), median 107.03 kJ (the mean of RUN194's 106.45 kJ and Run065's 107.60 kJ). The median is the level; the minimum and the maximum are the bracket, conditional.
- The ejected energy left the cells in the Databank's tests. In the model each energy is released in cell 1 and raises its temperature; none of it reaches the neighbour as a separate heat flux, so it serves only to size the trigger's peak. The path by venting, ejecta and flame stays outside the model, as in Stage 5.

D54. The matched case (brief, B2), fixed before any Stage 6 history of cell 1 is computed.
(1) Cell 1's history at the central case (central contact and tabs, 25 C), in both connections, for each energy (33.2229, 107.03, 91.19 and 110.83 kJ) and each release (17.53, 21.39 and 25.24 s), computed as in Stage 5 (`v12_model.stage5_t1`, D38), and its peak.
(2) A pair of an energy level (33.2 or 107.0 kJ) and a release qualifies if cell 1's peak lies within the measured range of the sixteen trigger maxima, 164 to 832 C inclusive, in both connections. Every qualifying pair is a matched case.
(3) The central matched case is the median release, 21.39 s, at the central contact and tabs, with the energy level that qualifies there. If both do, it takes the Databank total, the level that the brief puts forward as matched to the tests; if neither does, no draws and no V3 runs are made, and the stage is reported as it stands. The draws (B3) and V3 use the central matched case.
(4) Nothing else is selected or fitted. `src/stage6_run.py trigger` makes the selection into `results/stage6_trigger.json` after the predictions are public and before any run of the neighbour; the run list's hash is then recorded.
(5) Exploratory, chosen by the author on 24 October, labelled as such and not scored: the energy that puts cell 1's peak at the median of the sixteen measured trigger maxima, 704.5 C, in series at the central case with the median release. It is found by bisection between 33.2229 and 107.03 kJ until the peak lies within 0.01 K of 704.5 C, and it is used in both connections. It is a fit to one measured number, which the brief rules out for the matched case, so it stands beside the registered sets.

D55. The run design (brief, B3 and B4; `src/stage6_run.py`).
(1) Seven configurations, in both connections: each contact (200, 2,000 and 3,700 W/(m2 K), D36) with each end of the tab bracket (5 and 10 mW/K, D37), and the central contact with the central tabs (6.8 mW/K). Ambient 25 C; the reaction heat fed back one-way (D40); the posterior-mean kinetics, except in the draws. Six sets, run in this order:
- v3: at the central matched case, every mesh spacing halved, and every step limit halved, as D43 (2) (4 runs). They come first because they take longest;
- body: 33.2 kJ at the three releases (42 runs);
- total: 107.0 kJ at the three releases (42 runs);
- bracket: 91.2 and 110.8 kJ at the central configuration and the median release (4 runs);
- peak: the exploratory energy of D54 (5) at the median release (14 runs);
- draws: the 200 Stage 3 draws (D40) at the central matched case (400 runs).
That is 506 runs, 492 of them registered. The registered list's SHA-256 (`python3 stage6_run.py list`) is ab3dbd384b040d60447e2204187765350ca905b437a3e50e8ae98b79f18b0af3 if the central matched case takes 33.2 kJ, and 500b229b6c2d9731c98ea679cea306d18fab168233741e08b4ca82e04eadd400 if it takes 107.0 kJ; the exploratory set joins it once its energy is known. The matched case is whichever of the body and total sets qualifies (D54 (2)). The reference case of the brief is Stage 5's grid (33.2 kJ over 10 and 30 s, 36 runs), read from its archive (D51) and not rerun.
(2) Each run is made as Stage 5 made its runs (`v12_run.run_one`, unchanged): the resolved neighbour stops when its hottest node reaches 255.7 C, or at 900 s; the lumped one likewise (D35). Its record adds the A1 quantities of D51 (2) and (3) for the five histories, computed from the run's own histories in every set, the draws included, and whether and when the hottest node and the lumped neighbour reach 255.7 C. The mean and the probes cannot exceed the hottest node, so within a resolved run they reach 255.7 C no earlier than it does, and the run ends there (D35); their last stored values are recorded.
(3) Records go to `results/stage6_runs/<id>.json`, and the series of every set but the draws to `results/stage6_series/<id>.npz`. The harness is `v12_run.work`'s, with its rules: a run that fails is recorded as failed and left out of every count, with the count reported (D43 (3)); one that fails for want of memory is retried after a minute, up to 10 times (D47 (3)). One change: a refined run may take 180 min instead of 100, because a refined Stage 5 run took 83 min on the laptop with 16 workers and more workers share it now. Neither changes what a run computes.
(4) Where: the author's laptop (D45), with Stage 5's Python and packages, whose environment check passed on 21 October (`results/v12_checks_laptop/acceptance.json`) and is not repeated. 24 workers at 0.6 GB each (D47 (4)), started by `run_stage6_windows.cmd` and `src/stage6_laptop.py`, running the repository at the predictions commit, copied into the laptop's run folder with `results/stage6_trigger.json`.
(5) After the runs: V3, the hottest node's peak rise above ambient, or, where the central matched case reaches 255.7 C, the time of that crossing, changing by under 1 % with every mesh spacing halved and with every step limit halved, in each connection (brief); the energy account of every run closing to under 1e-9 of the heat taken in, as Stage 5 (D44).

D56. Checks (brief, V1 and V2; `src/stage6_checks.py`) and tests.
(1) V1: the Part A crossings of three runs, fixed now (`grid_mean_series_hcentral_gcentral_tau10`, the central case; `grid_mean_parallel_hhigh_ghigh_tau30`; `grid_mean_series_hlow_glow_tau30`), recomputed from their archived series by a separate scan, sample by sample, with the bracketing samples printed, and compared with `results/stage6_crossings.csv`. Acceptance: the same crossings, within 1e-9 s.
(2) V2: the matched trigger re-derived by separate code. The Databank's energies are read again with the csv module alone; the release durations are computed from the values of D52, typed into the check; cell 1's peak comes from an implementation of the lumped pair written from D38's equations (the contact conductance by quadrature of D36's local conductance, the view factor from its formula), with Stage 5's explicit step and, as a reference for the step, a fourth-order Runge-Kutta step ten times smaller; the selection and the exploratory energy are made again. Acceptance: the same energies and durations within 1e-6, the same peaks within 1e-6 K with the same step, the same selection, and the exploratory energy within 5 J. The difference from the Runge-Kutta step is reported.
(3) Then, before the note's Stage 6 lines are written, an independent read of every registered number and verdict by an agent that has not seen the work being produced (brief).
(4) Before the predictions, `src/test_stage6.py` tested the Stage 6 code on synthetic inputs only: piecewise-linear histories, so that every interpolated crossing is exact; a synthetic function in place of cell 1's peak; synthetic records in place of the runs. It checked the crossings, the run list (506 runs, the registered part independent of the exploratory set), the selection and the bisection, the work loop, the scoring, V1, V2 and the figure. No Part A quantity, no Stage 6 history of cell 1 and no run of the neighbour in the Stage 6 configuration was computed before the predictions were public.

D57. Part C, recorded and not run (brief): for a later stage, if Part B leaves the gap. This brief predicts neither.
(1) The aged SEI: the SEI's initial amount set to zero in every node and in the lumped node, in place of the fresh value of the source paper's Fig. 2, 0.15 (`bcrnn.C0`), which stays the reference. Nothing else changes. D19 explored a milder form of the same question for Stage 1, with the SEI stored at 25 C for up to 30 days.
(2) The internal short as an event rather than a temperature: once the separator melts, the cell's stored electrical energy is released in the neighbour. The energy is 18.2 Wh, 65.52 kJ: the nominal energy in LG Chem's product specification for the INR21700 M50 (document LRB-PS-CY18.2Wh-M50, tentative, 22 September 2017, section 2.1, minimum 17.6 Wh; average voltage 3.63 V, section 2.2), read on 24 October through a text reader in the copy hosted by Conrad Electronic, and the Databank's `Cell-Energy-Wh` for these cells. The melting temperature is 138.5 C, the peak of the posterior-mean separator melting at 5 C/min (D5, `results/v0_dsc_check.json`; 139 C in the source paper's Fig. 3(e)). It is read in two ways: at the hot spot, the event when the hottest node reaches 138.5 C; in the whole cell, when the lumped neighbour (or the resolved mean) does.
(3) Left to that stage's brief: where in the resolved cell and over what time the energy is released (the measured durations of D52 are candidates); whether the separator's melted fraction, rather than a temperature, should trigger the event; and its predictions.

D58. The version (brief, setting). On 24 October the author decided that the saved Zenodo draft of version 1.2.0 (10.5281/zenodo.23589793) absorbs Stage 6 and is published once, as version 1.2.0 with Stages 1 to 6, with its description, files and date replaced. The GitHub release v1.2.0 is made on the commit that carries Stage 6. Before anything is published, the note's header carries the current date and the all-versions DOI, 10.5281/zenodo.23279455, and the note gains a Stage 6 section of at most eight lines, its earlier text unchanged.

D59. Errata in the citation of the measurements. D33, the README, `CITATION.cff` and `inputs/fransson2024/SOURCE.md` give the paper's title without its subtitle; it is "Exploring thermal runaway propagation in Li-ion batteries through high-speed X-ray imaging and thermal analysis: Impact of cell chemistry and electrical connections". The README, `CITATION.cff` and `SOURCE.md` also give three authors' initials wrongly: they are J. Pfaff, H.T. Reid and S. Schopferer (not L. Pfaff, H. Reid and T. Schopferer). Both were checked against the article's record on ORA on 24 October. `SOURCE.md` is corrected with this entry, the README and `CITATION.cff` with the release, and D33 stands as registered, corrected here.

## Added after the Stage 6 predictions were public, before any run of the neighbour (23 October)

D60. The selection of D54, made by `src/stage6_run.py trigger` at 20:12 UTC on 23 October (`results/stage6_trigger.json`), after the predictions were pushed (20:11:24 UTC) and before any run of the neighbour.
(1) Cell 1's peak at the central case. With 33.2 kJ: 536.9, 531.7 and 526.7 C in series at the 17.53, 21.39 and 25.24 s releases, reached at 17.0, 20.7 and 24.3 s (536.4, 531.2 and 526.1 C in parallel). With 107.0 kJ: 1,494.1, 1,457.6 and 1,425.0 C in series (1.0 to 1.1 K lower in parallel). The bracket: 1,263 to 1,318 C at 91.2 kJ and 1,460 to 1,534 C at 110.8 kJ. So 33.2 kJ qualifies at all three releases and 107.0 kJ at none: the matched case is the body set, and the central matched case is 33.2 kJ over 21.39 s at the central contact and tabs.
(2) The exploratory peak-matched energy is 45,149.8 J (45.15 kJ): cell 1 peaks at 704.50 C in series and 703.83 C in parallel, after 16 steps of the bisection.
(3) The run list: 506 runs, SHA-256 1c1ceb71310b6d528c3879108dad7c89047b1c3d04c1927a20c0fcf5c6ee43f1. Its registered part, 492 runs, has the SHA-256 that D55 (1) gave for this outcome, ab3dbd384b040d60447e2204187765350ca905b437a3e50e8ae98b79f18b0af3.
(4) V2 passed (`results/stage6_v2.json`): the energies and durations re-derived exactly; cell 1's peaks re-derived within 4.5e-13 K with the same step, and within 0.16 K with a fourth-order Runge-Kutta step ten times smaller; the same selection; the exploratory energy within 0.01 J.
(5) Part A was computed at 20:12 UTC (`results/stage6_crossings.csv` and `.json`), and V1 passed (`results/stage6_v1.json`): in its three runs every crossing recomputed by hand equals the file's, and every largest stored sample equals the record's peak.

## Added after the Stage 6 results (24 October; presentation and records)

D61. Written after every Stage 6 run had ended and after the scoring. No registered quantity or verdict depends on it.
(1) Records. The 506 runs ran on the author's laptop with 24 workers, from 00:02:41 to 03:52:01 UTC on 24 October (median 681 s for a run on the base mesh; the refined V3 runs took 8,358 and 8,374 s, within D55 (3)'s 180 min). They are exactly the run list of D60; none failed and none was retried for want of memory. The records, the series and the workers' logs are committed (`results/stage6_runs/`, `results/stage6_series/`, `results/stage6_logs/`). Between D60 (pushed at 20:13 UTC on 23 October) and the first run nothing was computed: the session waited for the author's approval to start the laptop's runs.
(2) Quoted numbers. Every Stage 6 number that the README, the note, the release notes and the Zenodo description quote is computed by `src/stage6_numbers.py` (`results/stage6_numbers.json`), written while the runs went, from the records; the verdicts, V3 and the energy accounts are `src/stage6_score.py`'s (`results/stage6_scorecard.json`).
(3) Figure 5 (`src/make_figure_stage6.py`), written before the predictions, changed twice, for legibility only: while the runs went (commit 6ac3b39), a run whose hot spot reaches 255.7 C ends there (D35), so it is drawn as a triangle on that line instead of joining its face away to its hot spot; after the results, a clock that never reaches 150 C is labelled so in panel a.
(4) The note gains six Stage 6 lines, at most eight as the brief allows, without any change to its earlier text; its header carries the current date and the all-versions DOI (D58). To keep it on one page only its stylesheet changes:
- body text, byline and credit at 6.5 pt (6.9 pt), line height 1.08 (1.12);
- tables at 6.4 pt (6.8 pt);
- Figure 1 at 33 % of the text width (44 % before), still centred.

## Added after the independent check of the Stage 6 records and documents (24 October)

D62. The independent read (brief, checks): every registered Stage 6 number and verdict read again before the note's Stage 6 lines were finalised. A separate agent, which had not seen the work being produced, recomputed the run list, Part A, the selection, P1 to P7, V3, the energy accounts and every Stage 6 number quoted in the README, the note's draft lines, the release notes, the Zenodo description and D60 and D61, from the raw inputs, the Stage 5 records and series and the Stage 6 records, with scripts of its own and without the study's scoring, numbers or crossings code.
(1) Every registered number and verdict reproduced. The 506 records are exactly the run list, with its two hashes (and the other outcome's of D55 (1)); none failed; each record's inputs match its id; every run ends at 900 s or within a step of its 255.7 C crossing. Part A's crossings and returns equal `results/stage6_crossings.csv` for all 48 runs with series (difference 0 s); the selection's energies and durations equal the trigger file bitwise; P1 to P7, V3 and the energy accounts equal `results/stage6_scorecard.json` in every field; `results/stage6_matched.csv` and `.json` and `results/stage6_numbers.json` reproduce. It did not integrate cell 1 again (V2 did) or run `src/test_stage6.py`.
(2) The order of work. D56 (3) and `predictions_stage6.md` put the read before the note's Stage 6 lines are written. The lines were drafted first and committed with the other draft texts (commit aaec22c), so that the read could check them, and they were finalised after it, as for Stage 5 (D49).
(3) It found no discrepancy in a registered quantity, and these in the texts and records, each corrected before the release:
- upper bounds rounded down: the largest energy residual is 8.449e-12 (quoted as 8.4e-12), now 8.5e-12; V2's largest difference with the Runge-Kutta step is 0.1627 K (0.16 K), now 0.17 K, and with the same step 4.547e-13 K (4.5e-13 K), now 4.6e-13 K;
- cell 1's peaks quoted without their scope in the note and the Zenodo description (526 to 537 C and 1,424 to 1,494 C hold for the central case; over each set's 42 runs, 520.1 to 545.0 C and 1,413.0 to 1,510.3 C);
- the bracket's cell-1 peaks were quoted over the three releases of the selection (1,263 to 1,534 C), but its runs are at the median release (1,288.9 to 1,289.9 C at 91.2 kJ, 1,494.5 to 1,495.5 C at 110.8 kJ);
- "the whole cell at times like theirs" (the note, the release notes, the Zenodo description) holds in 13 of the 50 runs in which the lumped neighbour reaches 255.7 C; in the other 37 it does so at 40.1 to 129.1 s, before the earliest test. The texts now give the count, and Part A's "whole cell at about the measured times" its scope, the central contact;
- three quoted counts (9 of 42 and 13 of 50 inside the window; the line of contact 0.01 to 0.03 s behind the hot spot) were not in `results/stage6_numbers.json`, which every quoted number should come from; `src/stage6_numbers.py` now computes them;
- the README cited D62 before it existed; D61 (4) left out that the note's credit line also went to 6.5 pt (now listed in place);
- the README's scorecard left out what `predictions_stage6.md` reports beside the verdicts: P1 on the sens and v3 sets and on all 48 runs with series, where both parts hold (sens 3 of 4 inside, v3 4 of 4, all 48 runs 18 of 32, 56.3 %, and the hot spot 34 of 34), the opposite of the grid's verdict; P2 by contact (low 0 of 1,612, central 1,714 of 2,024, high 1,612 of 1,612; the line of contact above 133 C in 3,634 runs); P4's count (0 of 6) and the bracket at the median release; P7 on the registered sets alone (46 runs, held, 31.9 to 167.9 s);
- wording: the matched case's definition now says "at the central case, in both connections", and the release notes no longer speak of one pair (three qualify); the exploratory energy's 704.5 C is in series (703.8 C in parallel); 21.39 s is the median of the two measured times, not a measured time; the README's conditions name the 25 C start and apply the release durations to Part B only; a run that ignites early takes 8 to 22 s to compute, not 10 to 20 s.
(4) Errata to D60, which stands as pushed before the runs: in (1), cell 1 peaks 0.9 to 1.1 K lower in parallel at 107.0 kJ (0.89, 1.00 and 1.09 K at the three releases), not 1.0 to 1.1 K; in (4), V2's peaks agree within 4.6e-13 K with the same step and within 0.17 K with the Runge-Kutta step (4.547e-13 and 0.1627 K).
(5) A second pass, on the corrected texts (commit 6cc799a) and the release drafts, confirmed the corrections of (3) and every new or changed number, and found three more problems, corrected likewise: the README's computing time of the runs that ignite early (8 to 22 s) was not in `results/stage6_numbers.json` (now computed there); the README's status item did not name 6cc799a; and the read's own count is eleven findings, several of them in more than one place, not eleven places. For consistency it also noted that two Stage 5 bounds were rounded down in the same way: the largest Stage 5 energy residual is 8.432e-12 (quoted as at most 8.4e-12, now 8.5e-12), and V3's largest change is 0.3104 % (quoted as at most 0.31 %, now given as 0.27 and 0.31 % with the mesh halved).

## Stage 7 design, recorded with the predictions before any Stage 7 run (26 October)

D63. Stage 7 (brief of 26 October): the ignition condition scored test by test. Part A runs the model with one trigger per test, each of the sixteen tests of Fransson et al. receiving the energy that puts cell 1 at its measured maximum, with the fresh SEI and with the aged SEI; Part B reads seven candidate ignition conditions from the Part A histories, with no new physics, and scores each against the sixteen measured outcomes; Part C runs the internal short as an event (D57 (2)) for the tests whose separator melts. No Stage 1 to 6 code or result is edited and no Stage 5 or 6 run is repeated. The Stage 7 code is in new files (`src/stage7_inputs.py`, `src/stage7_model.py`, `src/stage7_kernel.py`, `src/stage7_run.py`, `src/stage7_criteria.py`, `src/stage7_score.py`, `src/stage7_checks.py`, `src/stage7_numbers.py`, `src/make_figure_stage7.py`, `src/stage7_laptop.py`, `src/test_stage7.py`, `run_stage7_windows.cmd`), which import the Stage 5 model, kernel and harness (`src/v12_model.py`, `src/v12_kernel.py`, `src/v12_run.py`) and the Stage 6 crossing functions (`src/stage6_crossings.py`) unchanged. Every Stage 7 trigger energy is new, so no Stage 7 run coincides with an archived Stage 5 or 6 run and no Stage 7 criterion can be read from those archives; they enter only the record of what was known (`predictions_stage7.md`) and the A4 finding of D66 (3). Stage 7 is version 1.3.0 (D72).

D64. The measured tests (brief, A1; `inputs/fransson2024/m50_tests_stage7.csv`). The brief's "Table 3" holds the eight M50 tests in parallel; the eight in series are in Table 4, with the same columns, so both tables are used, as D33 used them. They were read again on 26 October through a text reader, in the copy on the document server of the National Laboratory of the Rockies (page 7 of that file). The tables' columns, as printed: "Experiments" (under "M50"); "Maximum temperature [°C]", split into "Trigger cell" and "Propagation cell"; "Propagation"; "Time to propagation [s]", a dash where nothing propagated; "Failure cat.". The file's columns are `test` ("Experiments"), `connection` (from the captions, "M50 Parallel tests" and "M50 Series tests"), `table`, `trigger_max_C` ("Trigger cell"), `neighbour_max_C` ("Propagation cell"), `propagated` ("Propagation") and `time_to_propagation_s` ("Time to propagation [s]", empty for a dash). Every value equals the Stage 5 file `inputs/fransson2024/m50_tests.csv` (D33), which stays as it is. Nine tests propagated, five in series and four in parallel, at 147 to 342 s; the propagating neighbours' maxima span 381 to 763 C; the trigger maxima span 164 to 832 C.

D65. One trigger per test (brief, A2; `src/stage7_run.py triggers`).
(1) For each test, the trigger energy whose cell-1 peak equals the test's trigger maximum, at the central case (the central contact, 2,000 W/(m2 K); the central tabs, 6.8 mW/K; 25 C) in the test's own connection, with the median release, 21.39 s (D52). Cell 1's history is Stage 5's (`v12_model.stage5_t1`, D38), unchanged. The energy is found by the Stage 6 bisection (D54 (5)): the interval is halved until cell 1's peak lies within 0.01 K of the target, at most 200 times. Its ends are 0 J and the Databank's maximum bracket, 110,833.0 J (RUN197's corrected total energy, D53), the two limits the brief names. A test whose maximum lies below the peak at 0 J (cell 1 stays at 25 C) or above the peak at 110.83 kJ is recorded as out of range and run at the nearer end, labelled; with maxima of 164 to 832 C none is expected to be.
(2) The peak-to-energy table: cell 1's peak at the central case with the median release, in both connections, for every energy from 0 to 110.75 kJ in steps of 0.25 kJ and at 110.83 kJ (445 energies in each connection).
(3) For the record, each test's energy also gives cell 1's peak at the low and high contacts (central tabs, median release) and at the central contact with the other two releases (17.53 and 25.24 s): a trigger matched at the central contact misses its target elsewhere by the contact's, or the release's, effect on cell 1's cooling, and the record shows by how much (brief, pitfalls).
(4) `results/stage7_triggers.csv` holds both tables: one row per test (`kind` `test`: its energy, the peak at the central contact and its time, the number of halvings, whether in range, the peaks at the other contacts and releases) and one row per entry of the peak-to-energy table (`kind` `mapping`). `results/stage7_triggers.json` holds the same with every halving. Both are computed after the predictions are public and before any run of the neighbour, and the run list's hash is recorded then.

D66. Part A (brief, A3 and A4; `src/stage7_run.py`).
(1) Three sets, all with the posterior-mean kinetics, the central tabs, 25 C and the reaction heat fed back one-way (D40): pertest, the sixteen tests at the three contacts (200, 2,000 and 3,700 W/(m2 K), D36) with the median release (48 runs); duration, the sixteen tests at the central contact with the 17.53 and 25.24 s releases and the same sixteen energies (32 runs); aged, as pertest with the aged SEI (48 runs). That is 128 runs.
(2) Each fresh run is made as Stage 5 made its runs (`v12_run.run_one`, unchanged): the resolved neighbour stops when its hottest node reaches 255.7 C, or at 900 s, and the lumped neighbour likewise (D35); both are driven by the same cell-1 history. An aged run is the same loop with the SEI's initial amount set to 0 in every node and in the lumped node (`stage7_model.run_resolved` and `run_lumped`, which with the fresh state and no event equal the Stage 5 loops bitwise, `src/test_stage7.py`). The SEI enters one reaction of the mean model, its own decomposition (order 0.163), so the aged SEI removes that reaction and nothing else. Every run keeps its full histories in `results/stage7_series/<id>.npz` (the hottest node, the resolved mean and the lumped neighbour every 0.1 s; the face away from the trigger, at the thermocouple's position, and the line of contact at every step; cell 1), and its record in `results/stage7_runs/<id>.json` carries the Part B quantities of D67, computed from its own histories.
(3) A4. The brief allows the aged runs at the central contact alone if an aged SEI changes nothing in the thermal histories before 138.5 C. It changes them: the mean model's SEI decomposition runs from room temperature (0.0049 K/min of self-heating at 25 C, D19), its self-heating reaches 0.15 K/min at 108.4 C on a 1 K/s ramp (`results/explore_sei_storage.json`), below 138.5 C, and it is the only reaction that does. In Stage 6's central matched case (33.2 kJ over 21.39 s, series; `results/stage6_runs/`, read and not rerun) the fresh kinetics released 280.3 J in the lumped neighbour by 900 s, 4.5 K at its 61.75 J/K, and 176.5 J in the resolved one, almost all of it SEI decomposition. So all 48 aged runs are made.

D67. Part B, the ignition conditions (brief; `src/stage7_criteria.py`, `src/stage7_score.py`). No new physics: every quantity is read from a Part A run's own histories and record.
(1) The clocks are Stage 6's (D51 (2)): the hot spot (the hottest node), the resolved mean, the face away from the trigger at mid-height (the thermocouple's position), the line of contact at mid-height (reported apart) and the lumped neighbour. For each run and clock: the peak and its time (the record's, taken at every step; the mean's time from the series), and the first crossing of 138.5, 150 and 255.7 C, interpolated linearly between the two stored samples that bracket it (D51 (3)). The hot spot's and the lumped neighbour's 255.7 C crossings are the runs' own stops, interpolated within the step (D35); a stored series can end before a sample reaches 255.7 C, so C1, C2 and C6 take the stops.
(2) The levels: 255.7 C (D35); 150 C (D33 (2)); 138.5 C, the peak of the posterior-mean separator melting at 5 C/min (D5, `results/v0_dsc_check.json`), which is a peak and not an onset.
(3) The criteria, the brief's: C1, the hot spot reaches 255.7 C; C2, the lumped neighbour reaches 255.7 C; C3, the lumped neighbour reaches 150 C; C4, the hot spot reaches 138.5 C; C5, the lumped neighbour reaches 138.5 C; C6, C1 with the aged SEI; C7, C4 with the aged SEI. Each gives, for each test and contact, a predicted outcome (the clock reaches its level within 900 s) and a predicted time (the crossing).
(4) Against the sixteen measured outcomes (D64), each prediction is a hit (both propagate), a miss (the test propagated, the criterion does not predict it), a false alarm or a correct rejection. For a hit, the time error is the predicted time minus the measured time to propagation, signed. The model's clock starts at cell 1's release and the paper's at the trigger's runaway (D34), with no shift; the figure says so. The hit rate is the hits and correct rejections over 16. A criterion that predicts propagation everywhere scores every propagated test as a hit, and so the false alarms are reported beside the hits.
(5) The scorecard is seven criteria by three contacts: the central contact is registered, the low and high contacts are the bracket (`results/stage7_scorecard.json`; `results/stage7_pertest.csv`, one row per test, contact and criterion).
(6) Reported beside it, at the central contact only, not registered: C1 to C5 with the 17.53 and 25.24 s releases (the duration set); and C4, C5 and C7 read at the 2.5 and 97.5 % points of the posterior's separator melting temperatures (D71) instead of 138.5 C.

D68. Part C, the internal short as an event (brief, Part C; `src/stage7_model.py`, `src/stage7_kernel.py`, `src/stage7_run.py`).
(1) Which runs. The brief asks for an event run "for every test and contact where C4 or C5 predicts propagation at the central contact", with the lower bracket "at the central case only", and scores P8 on the event runs at the central contact. It is read as the tests whose separator melts at the central contact: for each test where C4 predicts propagation at the central contact, one run with the event at the hot spot's 138.5 C crossing, and for each test where C5 does, one run with the event at the lumped neighbour's 138.5 C crossing. Each is made with 65.52 kJ (set event) and with 63.36 kJ (set eventlow); every run is at the central contact, the central case of the brief's A2. The other reading, event runs at the low and high contacts as well, would add runs that no prediction scores, at the cost of D69 (4); they are not made.
(2) The event: the cell's stored electrical energy, 18.2 Wh (65.52 kJ), or the specification's minimum, 17.6 Wh (63.36 kJ) (D57 (2)), released in the neighbour over the median release duration, 21.39 s, as a triangular pulse, the trigger's shape (D38), from the event's time. The event's time is the Part A run's 138.5 C crossing of D67 (1): the hot spot's for C4, the lumped neighbour's for C5, read from the pertest run of the same test at the central contact. The resolved and the lumped neighbour of an event run receive the event at the same time. All the stored energy released at the melt is a modelling choice, not a measurement.
(3) Where it goes, and the steps. In the resolved cell the event's power is shared over the roll's nodes by volume (the weights `w_vol` that hold the roll's heat capacity; the can, a shell on the outer nodes, takes none directly, D10), in the lumped cell into its one node. Each step receives the pulse's exact integral over the step, so the energy delivered equals the event's to rounding. The event's heating counts in the choice of the step as the reaction's does: D13's rule with the event's largest local heating rate added to the largest local reaction heating, so that no step heats a node by more than about 1 K from both. The kinetics, the reaction heat and the one-way coupling to cell 1's prescribed history are as in Part A, with one change from the event's time on: a node is integrated with explicit sub-steps if it needs at most 4,096 of them, instead of D13's 64 (`src/stage7_kernel.py`, the Stage 5 compiled step with that limit as an argument, which with 64 equals Stage 5's bitwise). The event carries every node through the decomposition at once, and with D13's limit the sizing run of D70 (4) sent up to 6,122 nodes at a time to the implicit integrator; near the event's peak one step took about 20 s. On that run's state at 19.6 s, where 755 nodes at 1,389 to 1,434 C were held back by the SEI's last stage, the larger limit takes 0.17 s for the step and agrees with D13's within 7e-5 K and 4e-7 in the amounts; on a state at the separator's melting, within 3e-8 K and 5e-8. The explicit sub-steps keep D13's accuracy limits (in each, every amount and every temperature-driven rate changes by less than 2e-3, the temperature by less than 0.02 K), so only the cost changes. Every step that starts before the event's time is Stage 5's, and the event's heating enters the choice of a step only within the 0.1 s before it, so an event run repeats its Part A run until then; the scoring checks this on every event run's stored history.
(4) The energy account of each run includes the event (`in_event`), and the lumped loop keeps an account as well (D70 (4)).
(5) A Part C run is not stopped at 255.7 C. It ends 120 s after its event has ended (or at 900 s if sooner), because nothing after the peak is scored, as D35 stops the Part A runs at their crossings. Recorded: each clock's peak after the event and its time, the time at which each clock reaches 255.7 C after the event, the reaction heat and the energy delivered. The scoring checks that every clock peaked at least 1 s before the run's end.
(6) Nothing in Part C is a source to a further cell: the geometry has two cells and ends at the neighbour, and cell 1's history is prescribed (one-way), so the event does not heat cell 1 either. The note says so.

D69. The run design (`src/stage7_run.py`).
(1) Order: V3's two runs as soon as the pertest run they depend on is recorded (D69 (3) and (4)); Part A, the sets pertest, duration and aged in that order (128 runs); then, once every pertest run has ended, the rest of Part C. Its list is made from the pertest records by the rule of D68 (1) and written, with its SHA-256, to `results/stage7_eventlist.json` before any Part C run other than V3's; its V3 runs are those that may already have started, and the event and eventlow sets follow in that order.
(2) The registered sets are pertest, aged and event at the central contact. The duration set, pertest and aged at the low and high contacts, and eventlow are reported beside them.
(3) V3 (brief, checks): the event run with every mesh spacing halved and with every step limit halved, as D43 (2) did, on one run fixed now: the C4 event run of M50_PT_Exp4 (the propagated test of the supplementary Figure 10) at 65.52 kJ; if C4 does not predict propagation for it at the central contact, its C5 event run; if neither, the C4 event run of M50_SP_Exp3, the hottest trigger. Each V3 run takes the base run's event time. Acceptance: the hot spot's peak rise after the event (its peak less 25 C) changes by under 1 % in each; the lumped neighbour's is reported.
(4) Where and how long. The runs are made on the author's laptop (D45, D55 (4)), with Stage 5's Python and packages, whose environment check passed on 21 October and is not repeated, by `run_stage7_windows.cmd` and `src/stage7_laptop.py`: 24 workers on one shared list, at below-normal priority, Part A and then Part C in one start. V3's two runs may start as soon as the pertest run they depend on is recorded, with the same specifications as in the Part C list, so that the refined run, the longest, overlaps the others. An event run is far costlier than a Part A run, because the event carries every node through its decomposition at once: with the Part C settings, the sizing run of D70 (4) took 747 s on one core of the cloud machine for its 142.4 s, two thirds of it in the three seconds of the neighbour's runaway, and at most 657 MB of memory (with D13's limit, 2.5 GB, D68 (3)). So a Part C run starts only if its memory slots (one on the base mesh, six on the refined mesh, 1 GB each) fit, with those of the Part C runs going, within the computer's memory less 4 GB, and as much memory is free; a worker otherwise waits a minute and looks again. A run that takes longer than 480 min on the base mesh or 1,440 min on the refined mesh is recorded as failed. Every other rule of D43 (3), D47 (3) and D55 (3) is kept: a failed run is recorded as failed and left out of every count, with the count reported; one that fails for want of memory is retried after a minute, up to 10 times. None of this changes what a run computes. Each record also carries its worker's largest memory use so far.

D70. Checks (brief; `src/stage7_checks.py`, `src/stage7_score.py`) and tests.
(1) V1: the sixteen energies re-derived by separate code from the peak-to-energy table and from the trigger maxima of Tables 3 and 4, typed into the check: for each test the table of its connection is scanned for the two energies whose peaks bracket the maximum, and the energy is interpolated linearly. Acceptance: every energy within 2 J of the recorded one (the bisection's 0.01 K is about 0.7 J; the table's interpolation adds far less), and every recorded peak within 0.01 K of its target.
(2) V2: the Part B crossings of three runs, fixed now, recomputed by hand from their archived series (each history scanned sample by sample, the bracketing samples printed, the crossing interpolated by the formula written in the check): the pertest run of M50_PT_Exp4 at the central contact, the aged run of M50_SP_Exp3 at the high contact, and the pertest run of M50_ST_Exp3 at the low contact. Acceptance: the same crossings within 1e-9 s. The runs' own 255.7 C crossings are not in the stored series, which ends at the last 0.1 s sample before the stop; for them V2 checks that every sample stored before the crossing lies below 255.7 C and that the run ended within a step of it, or, for a run that never crossed, that it went to 900 s and no stored sample reaches 255.7 C.
(3) V3 as D69 (3). V4: every run's energy account, the event's energy included, closes to under 1e-9 of the heat taken in (resolved, as D44; lumped, the new account), and every event run delivers its energy to 1e-9.
(4) Before the predictions, `src/test_stage7.py` tested the Stage 7 code on synthetic inputs only: `stage7_model` against `v12_model` bitwise with the fresh state and no event (resolved, 3 s; lumped, 120 s; cell 1 a made-up ramp to 600 C); the event's pulse, the energy it delivers and the accounts with it; the aged state; the crossings on piecewise-linear histories; the bisection, the out-of-range rule, the triggers file and V1 on a made-up peak function; the Stage 7 kernel against Stage 5's bitwise with Stage 5's limit, and an event run identical before its event with either limit; the run lists, Part C's rule and V3's early start; the memory slots; the workers' loop with a made-up run in place of the model; the scoring, V2, the figure and the quoted numbers on made-up records. Separately, to size the runs (D69 (4)), an event run on the base mesh with cell 1 held at the ambient temperature (no trigger, outside every Stage 7 configuration), 65.52 kJ from 1 s, was made twice on the cloud machine, with its lumped counterpart: first with D13's sub-step limit throughout, stopped at 21.7 s, after 43 min, once its cost was clear; then with the Part C settings of D68 (3), to 120 s after the event. The slow states of the first were saved and the two limits compared on them (D68 (3)). All of it is in `provenance/stage7_cost_test.json`, and what it shows is in `predictions_stage7.md`. No history of cell 1 for a Stage 7 trigger and no run of the neighbour in a Stage 7 configuration was computed before the predictions were public.
(5) Then, before the note's Stage 7 lines are written, an independent read of every registered number and verdict by an agent that has not seen the work being produced (brief).

D71. The separator's melting temperature over the posterior (brief, pitfalls). 138.5 C is the posterior-mean model's peak (D5). The sensitivity takes the peak of each of the posterior's 100 separator particles (`Separator.csv`, the particles Stage 3 drew from) at 5 C/min, computed as V0 computes the posterior mean's (`src/v0_dsc_check.py`, its ramp and 2 s output grid), and their 2.5 and 97.5 % points (`numpy.percentile`, linear). They are computed with the triggers, after the predictions are public, into `results/stage7_separator.json`, and C4, C5 and C7 are read at them at the central contact (D67 (6)); not registered.

D72. The version (brief, setting). Stage 7 is version 1.3.0: a further Zenodo new version of the record (all versions 10.5281/zenodo.23279455), a further GitHub release v1.3.0, `CITATION.cff` and `.zenodo.json` at v1.3.0, and the note's header at the publishing date with the all-versions DOI. The GitHub release v1.2.0 exists (published on 24 October, tag on 364ecc4). The Zenodo new version of v1.2.0 (10.5281/zenodo.23589793) is still a draft with the Stage 5 files, for the author to replace and publish; Stage 7 does not wait for it. Zenodo keeps one draft new version of a record at a time, so the v1.3.0 draft can be made only once v1.2.0 is published. The note gains a Stage 7 section of at most eight lines, its earlier text unchanged; every number it quotes comes from `results/stage7_paper_numbers.json` (`src/stage7_numbers.py`), the file from which a manuscript of Stages 1 to 7 is to be written.

D73. Housekeeping from Stage 3 (D27): the published page numbers of O'Regan et al. (Electrochimica Acta 425 (2022) 140700). They are still missing. The published version is behind subscription: the University of Warwick's record of it (WRAP 166588) gives "Restricted or Subscription Access", and the University of Birmingham's gives 19 pages and no file; the accepted manuscript (WRAP 196287, CC BY-NC-ND 4.0), which D27 read, carries no page numbers. The publisher's page was tried twice on 26 October (by the DOI and by its redirect) and returned nothing readable, so the step stops there, as the brief's rule requires. D27's values stay cited by table and section (Tables 6, 7 and 8, and Section 1.3.2), and the page numbers remain for a reader with access to the published version.

## Added after the Stage 7 predictions were public, before any run of the neighbour (26 October)

D74. The triggers of D65 and the separator's spread of D71, computed by `src/stage7_run.py triggers` from 05:48 to 05:51 UTC on 26 October (`results/stage7_triggers.csv` and `.json`, `results/stage7_separator.json`), after the predictions were pushed (05:48:11 UTC) and before any run of the neighbour.
(1) The sixteen energies span 8,976.8 J (M50_PT_Exp1, 164 C) to 54,303.7 J (M50_SP_Exp3, 832 C); each puts cell 1's peak within 0.01 K of its test's maximum, after 11 to 17 halvings, and none is out of range. The energies of the other fourteen are in the triggers file.
(2) With the same energy, cell 1 peaks 2.7 to 13.9 K higher at the low contact and 1.4 to 7.0 K lower at the high contact than at the central contact, and 1.1 to 11.3 K higher with the 17.53 s release and 1.1 to 10.7 K lower with the 25.24 s release than with the median release; the largest offsets belong to the hottest triggers.
(3) The separator's melting peak at 5 C/min over the posterior's 100 particles lies at 136.5 to 140.5 C (median 138.33 C), and its 2.5 and 97.5 % points are 136.83 and 140.09 C; the posterior-mean model's peak, computed the same way, is 138.5 C, D5's value.
(4) V1 passed (`results/stage7_v1.json`): every energy re-derived from the peak-to-energy table within 0.64 J of the recorded one, and every recorded peak within 0.0093 K of its target.
(5) The Part A run list: 128 runs, SHA-256 0495cf6db0c4b3f1555f2502e2eecafc15910fd73ada016024060e2478fef78c (`python3 src/stage7_run.py list`). The Part C list follows from the pertest records by the rule of D68 (1), and its hash is recorded when it is made (D69 (1)).

## Added after every Stage 7 run had ended and after the scoring (26 October)

D75. Written after every Stage 7 run had ended and after the scoring. No registered quantity or verdict depends on it.
(1) Records. The 186 runs ran on the author's laptop from 05:54:53 to 07:15:59 UTC on 26 October, with 23 workers: the launcher starts one per logical processor unless the free memory is short (0.6 GB each, D47 (4)), and at the start it allowed 23 of the 24 that D69 (4) asked for. They are exactly the run lists: Part A's 128 (D74 (5)) and the Part C list (below). None failed, none was retried for want of memory, and none came near its time limit (the longest, V3's refined run, took 4,193 s against 1,440 min). A Part A run that went to 900 s took 704 s (median of 70, 358 to 796 s); one that stopped at its hot spot's crossing, 14 to 29 s; an event run 544 s (median of 56, 464 to 604 s), and V3's run with the steps halved 1,133 s. The records, the series and the workers' logs are committed (`results/stage7_runs/`, `results/stage7_series/`, `results/stage7_logs/`), with `results/stage7_eventlist.json` and the launcher's `results/stage7_laptop_workers.json`.
(2) The Part C list was written by a worker at 06:30:24 UTC, once every pertest run had ended, from the pertest records by the rule of D68 (1): 58 runs, V3's two and, for each of the 14 tests whose hot spot reaches 138.5 C at the central contact (C4) and the same 14 whose lumped neighbour does (C5), one run at 65.52 kJ (event) and one at 63.36 kJ (eventlow). Its SHA-256, of its `runs` as `stage7_run.runs_hash` writes them, is 1145400e582349e5d81b22011c5684984c7e8aa5921cec8c7f6226789b0f7f8a; the list made again from the committed pertest records by `stage7_run.build_part_c` is the same, run for run. The list orders the runs test by test, each test's run at 65.52 kJ before its lower bracket, rather than the whole event set before the eventlow set as D69 (1) has it; the order changes nothing a run computes.
(3) Three corrections to the scoring script, none of which touches a verdict:
- `results/stage7_events.csv` kept one row for V3's two runs, which share a test, a contact and a criterion; its rows are now keyed by the mesh and the step as well (commit 27bef4e, while the Part C runs went, after the Part A records and V3's run with the steps halved had been scored and before any event or eventlow record was);
- the check of D68 (3), that an event run repeats its Part A run until 0.1 s before its event, was also applied to V3's two runs, whose mesh or steps differ from that run's by design; it now counts the event and eventlow runs only (the same commit);
- the same check, as first written, compared the two histories up to the event, but a Part A run whose hot spot reached 255.7 C stopped there (D35) and stored its resolved history only until then: for the 8 tests where it did, at 16.9 to 21.7 s, the C5 events come later, at 61.5 to 76.4 s, so the check failed for those 16 runs (both energies) on the length of the histories alone. It now compares the span both runs stored, the probes included, and all 56 event runs repeat their Part A run there, 16 of them until the Part A run's stop (commit 3c57255, after the Part C records were read).
(4) The workers' largest memory use, which D69 (4) has each record carry, reads 0.0 MB in every record: on Windows the reading (`stage7_run.peak_memory_mb`) passes the process handle without declaring its type, and the call fails without an error. It was for the record only; the memory gate of D69 (4), which reads the free memory, is separate and worked, and nothing a run computes depends on either. The quoted numbers give it as missing.
(5) Quoted numbers. Every Stage 7 number that the README, the note, the release notes and the Zenodo description quote comes from `src/stage7_numbers.py` (`results/stage7_paper_numbers.json`); the verdicts, the scorecard, V3 and V4 are `src/stage7_score.py`'s (`results/stage7_scorecard.json`). Blocks added to the numbers script after the results, none scored: which central verdicts the two other releases and the separator's spread change (none); the aged SEI beside the fresh, at each contact; the measured outcomes against the trigger's maximum; Part C's timing, the event's energy against the lumped neighbour's heat capacity, and the two timings of one test against each other; the cell-body shares of the Databank's ten nail tests (D53); the run times; and two exploratory blocks, written after the results: the hot spot past its Part A stop, read from the C5 event runs, which repeat their Part A runs until their events (in the 8 tests whose hot spot reached 255.7 C it peaks at 262.3 to 307.1 C and cools), and the most outcomes that a threshold on the trigger's maximum alone could get right (12 of 16, between 723 and 728 C; 6 of 8 in each connection). After the independent read it also gained the event's energies, the levels, the run counts, V1, the errors within 60 s and the design's defining constants (D76 (6) and (7)).
(6) Figure 6 (`src/make_figure_stage7.py`), written before the predictions, changed for legibility only, after the Part A records were read: the measured column's heading, "measured", ran into the next column's and now reads "Tables 3 and 4"; in panel b the line between the connections and the grid end below the legend.

## Added after the independent read of the Stage 7 records and quoted numbers (26 October)

D76. The independent read (brief, checks; D70 (5)): every registered Stage 7 number and verdict read again before the note's Stage 7 lines were written. A separate agent, which had not seen the work being produced, recomputed them from the raw inputs, the triggers file and the Stage 7 records and series, with scripts of its own and without the study's Stage 7 scoring, criteria, numbers or checks code or the Stage 6 crossing functions; for P5's first self-heating it used Stage 1's `onset_kinetics.integrate_along`, and `src/bcrnn.py` to name the reaction then dominant, with initial states of its own, as its brief allowed. It read the repository at commit e0a2265, which holds all 186 records.
(1) Every registered number and verdict reproduced. Part A's 128 ids hash to D74 (5)'s value; the Part C list, rebuilt from the pertest series by D68 (1) and D69 (3), equals `results/stage7_eventlist.json` run for run, with its SHA-256; the 186 records and series are exactly the two lists, none failed, and every record's inputs match its id and its rule. Every cell-1 peak equals the triggers file for its contact and release; all 256 stop crossings are consistent with their series; the 336 rows of `results/stage7_pertest.csv` and the 21 tallies of the scorecard are identical. P1 to P8, the sensitivities, Part C (the peaks after the event, the energy delivered, the 56 event runs repeating their Part A runs, P8), V3 (0.148 % and 0.00079 %) and V4 (3.42e-12 and 4.34e-12) equal `results/stage7_scorecard.json`, and all 1,236 numbers in `results/stage7_paper_numbers.json` (1,606 values in all) equal its own values. It did not rerun the model.
(2) Errata to the design, none touching a number. D70 (2), and the docstring of `src/stage7_checks.py`, said that a stopped run's stored series ends at the last 0.1 s sample before its 255.7 C crossing: it ends with the sample of the step that crossed, at or above 255.7 C, in all 58 resolved and 24 lumped stops, and interpolating between the last two stored samples reproduces every stop within 5.7e-14 s. So D67 (1)'s "a stored series can end before a sample reaches 255.7 C" does not happen in these runs, and C1, C2 and C6 read from the series would be the same. The docstring is corrected; D67 and D70 stand as pushed.
(3) Two parts of the scoring script did less than the decisions say, and now do what they say, with no result changed: the check of D68 (5) tested four of the five clocks and left out the line of contact (with all five, every clock of every Part C run peaks at least 120.4 s before its run ends, 120.4 to 123.1 s); and V4's verdict tested the energy accounts but not D70 (3)'s condition that every event delivers its energy to 1e-9, which it now does for the resolved and the lumped neighbour (116 deliveries, largest relative error 0).
(4) P6's reading. The rule in `predictions_stage7.md` counts an inversion "if the lower-energy test's lumped neighbour is strictly hotter, its peak higher, or, since a lumped neighbour that reaches 255.7 C stops there (D35), it reaches 255.7 C and the other does not, or both do and it does first". The scoring reads its clauses as cases: the peaks are compared when neither neighbour reaches 255.7 C and the crossing times when both do, since a stopped neighbour's recorded peak is only its stopping step's value. At the central contact, which P6 scores, only the highest-energy test of each connection reaches 255.7 C, so either reading gives no inversion and the verdict stands. At the high contact, reported beside it, comparing the stopped neighbours' recorded peaks as well would give three inversions in series, though all of them reach 255.7 C in energy order: M50_SP_Exp1 (255.916 C) against M50_SP_Exp2 (255.830 C) and M50_ST_Exp4 (255.900 C), and M50_ST_Exp1 (256.022 C) against M50_SP_Exp3 (255.916 C).
(5) In the texts, corrected before the note's Stage 7 lines were written: D75 (1) and the README's Running paragraph gave the early-stopping runs' times as 14 to 30 s (14.3 to 29.5 s, now 14 to 29 s, as the rest of D75 (1)'s sentence rounds); D75 (3) put the first scoring correction before any Part C record was scored, but V3's run with the steps halved, a Part C run, had been scored with the Part A records, and D75 (3) now says so; the README's draft called C1 the best of the seven criteria (C6, with the same verdicts, scores as well) and stated P6 at every contact without (4)'s qualification. The read also noted that the energy residuals and V1's peak offset were rounded up, as bounds (at most 3.5e-12 and 4.4e-12, within 0.0093 K, all true); the README now gives the largest values instead (3.42e-12, 4.34e-12 and 0.0092 K), and D74 (4) stands.
(6) After the read, the note gained seven Stage 7 lines, at most eight as the brief allows, without any change to its earlier text, and its header carries the date of the texts, 26 October 2026, and the all-versions DOI (D72). Every number in the lines comes from `results/stage7_paper_numbers.json`, which gained blocks for them (the event's energies and release; see (7) for the rest). With them the note no longer fits one page, even with Stage 6's stylesheet and its body text cut to 5.6 pt, so it takes two, and only its stylesheet changes: back to version 1.1.0's sizes (body text and byline 7.4 pt, line height 1.16, tables 7.2 pt, credit 6.9 pt, margins 6 and 5 mm, Figure 1 at 56 % of the text width), with two rules that keep each section's list, each table and the headline box on one page and a heading with what follows it. The releases' descriptions now call it the note, not the one-page note.
(7) A second pass of the read, on the texts at commit eaf0d68 (the README's Stage 7 section, status, checks, contents, running and sources lines, the note's Stage 7 lines and header, D75 and D76, the Zenodo description, CITATION.cff, and the release notes and finishing guide in draft), confirmed every Stage 7 number in them against its own values but these, and that the scoring script's changes of (3) change no verdict or registered number. It found, and the texts now correct:
- two values written as bounds after the first pass, "within 0.0092 K" and "at most 3.42e-12", that the largest values (0.009219 K and 3.4234e-12) exceed: the README now states them as the largest values; and its own count of the numbers it had compared, 1,236, not 1,189 as first reported (1,243 at eaf0d68, all matching);
- (3)'s clock window, which applies to the hot spot, the last clock to peak: every clock peaks 120.4 to 123.1 s before its run ends;
- central-contact results stated without their scope: in the Zenodo description and the release notes, the aged SEI's verdicts and first self-heating and the two sensitivities (at the low contact the aged SEI changes M50_PT_Exp2's C4 verdict); in the README's summary of what Stage 7 shows, the hot spot's timing and the whole cell's counts (at the low contact C4 and C7 cross as late as 216.8 and 184.0 s, at the high contact C2 predicts 9 and at the low contact C3 none); and, for clarity, the note's lines 2, 3 and 5, which now say at the central contact;
- numbers quoted that were not in `results/stage7_paper_numbers.json`: the levels 255.7 and 150 C, the 128 Part A runs, V1's 0.64 J (its 0.0092 K was there already) and the errors within 60 s of C2 at the high contact, now all in it; the three P6 pairs' 0.016 to 0.106 K, now left to (4)'s record. A third check, of these corrections and the release notes at commit e771427, found them right and found five defining constants of the design that the texts quote but the file did not hold as values: the self-heating rate, 0.15 K/min; the runs' end, 900 s; P3's window, 60 s; the DSC rate of D5, 5 C/min; and the ambient temperature, 25 C. The file now holds them, each read from where the study defines it (`definitions`);
- the note and the Zenodo description had the seven criteria read from 128 runs: they are read from the 96 runs of the sixteen tests at three contacts, fresh and aged; the other 32, with the other two releases, enter the sensitivities only;
- the README's source line put the specification's energies in sections 2.1 and 2.2: both are in section 2.1 (D57 (2)), and section 2.2 gives the average voltage;
- wording: the design errata are recorded here, not corrected in place; the release notes now give the event runs' repetition of their Part A runs over the span both stored, and the separator's 2.5 and 97.5 % points as such (the 100 particles span 136.5 to 140.5 C); this entry now names `src/bcrnn.py` among what the read used; and the README now says that the sizing run of D70 (4) was computed twice.

## Stage 8 design, recorded with the predictions before any Stage 8 run (26 October)

D77. Stage 8 (brief of 26 October): how much of the ejected energy the outcomes require. Stage 7 left the neighbour heated by conduction and radiation alone; Stage 8 asks the inverse question, test by test: what fraction f of the trigger's ejected energy, delivered to the neighbour, would the model need to reproduce each test's outcome. Part A finds that fraction by a search for each test and criterion, with its sensitivities; Part B brackets the electrical path of the parallel tests; Part C scores Stage 7's criteria at three common fractions; Part D applies the gas layer of Hoelle, Zimmermann and Hinrichsen. No Stage 1 to 7 code or result is edited, and no Stage 7 run is repeated: where f = 0 answers a case, Stage 7's pertest run at the central contact is read (`results/stage7_runs/`). The Stage 8 code is in new files (`src/stage8_inputs.py`, `src/stage8_model.py`, `src/stage8_search.py`, `src/stage8_run.py`, `src/stage8_score.py`, `src/stage8_checks.py`, `src/stage8_cost_test.py`, `src/test_stage8.py`; the quoted numbers and the figure follow the results, D84), which import the Stage 5 model, kernel and harness, the Stage 6 crossing functions and the Stage 7 model, kernel and criteria unchanged. Stage 8 is version 1.4.0 (D84).
(1) Each run is one test of Fransson et al. (D64) with its Stage 7 trigger energy E_b (D65; `results/stage7_triggers.json`: 8,976.8 J for M50_PT_Exp1 to 54,303.7 J for M50_SP_Exp3), released over the median release, 21.39 s (D52), at the central contact (2,000 W/(m2 K)), the central tabs (6.8 mW/K), 25 C, with the posterior-mean kinetics, the fresh SEI and the reaction heat fed back one-way (D40). The resolved neighbour stops when its hot spot reaches 255.7 C and the lumped neighbour when it does, or at 900 s (D35).
(2) The ejected-to-body ratio (A1). The Databank's ten M50 nail tests of D53 are rows 9 to 18 of `inputs/databank/databank_m50_used.csv` (the header is row 1). For each, r = (Energy-Fraction-Positive-Ejecta-kJ + Energy-Fraction-Negative-Ejecta-kJ) / Energy-Fraction-Cell-Body-kJ, which equals (Corrected-Total-Energy-Yield-kJ less Energy-Fraction-Cell-Body-kJ) / Energy-Fraction-Cell-Body-kJ to 2e-14: row 9 (ESRF18_Oct_Run063) 3.694372; row 10 (Run065) 2.793422; row 11 (Run067) 3.124527; row 12 (DLS19_Feb_Run045) 2.373680; row 13 (Run047) 4.018760; row 14 (SPR2021_ESTA_8B100-01_SOC_RUN194) 2.218460; row 15 (RUN195) 2.291997; row 16 (RUN196) 3.086043; row 17 (RUN197) 4.156163; row 18 (RUN198) 3.424700. Central: their median, 3.105285 (the mean of RUN196's and Run067's); bracket: the smallest, 2.218460 (RUN194), and the largest, 4.156163 (RUN197). In these rows the cell body takes 19.39 to 31.07 % of the total (Energy-Percent-Cell-Body-%), so the ejecta carry 68.9 to 80.6 %.
(3) The ejected energy, E_e = r E_b (A1): 27,875.4 J (M50_PT_Exp1) to 168,628.5 J (M50_SP_Exp3) at the central ratio; 19,914.6 to 120,470.6 J and 37,308.9 to 225,695.1 J at the bracket's ends. `results/stage8_ejected.csv` holds the sixteen and the ten rows; it is written after the predictions are public, with V1 (D83 (1)).
(4) The delivery (A2). A fraction f of E_e enters the neighbour from the start of the trigger's release (t = 0) as a triangular pulse of length tau_d, the trigger's own shape (D38): the ejecta leave with the trigger's release. tau_d is the median release, 21.39 s; 10 and 60 s are the brief's bracket, labelled conditional, since no source gives a delivery's duration. In the resolved neighbour it is a uniform flux over the half of the can facing the trigger: the lateral surface within 90 degrees of the line of contact (theta <= 90 degrees), over the whole height. Each node's share is the integral of its hat function over that region (order-6 quadrature on the lateral facets, so a facet that crosses 90 degrees is split by its quadrature points), normalised so that the half model receives half of each step's energy (D10): 2.3220e-3 m2 on the mesh, against pi R L = 2.3234e-3 m2. The bracket geometry puts the same energy on the quarter of that half nearest the cap (theta <= 90 degrees and z >= 0.75 L; the vent is in the cap, the positive end at z = L, D42), 5.809e-4 m2 against 5.808e-4 m2: four times the flux. The flux enters the outer nodes, which hold the can (a shell on the outer nodes, D10), so it heats the can and the roll's outer layer together; the cap and the base receive none in either geometry. In the lumped neighbour the same pulse enters its one node. Each step receives the pulse's exact integral over the step, so the energy delivered is f E_e to rounding; the delivery's largest local heating rate is added to the reaction's in D13's step rule (as the event of D68 (3)); and every run with a source integrates a node with explicit sub-steps if it needs at most 4,096 of them, from t = 0 (D68 (3)). The energy account of each record carries the delivery as `in_delivery` (resolved and lumped), and the record carries the geometry and the delivered energy.

D78. The search for the threshold fraction (brief, A3 and A5; `src/stage8_search.py`, `src/stage8_run.py`).
(1) For each test: f*(C1), the smallest f at which the hot spot reaches 255.7 C within 900 s, and f*(C2), the smallest f at which the lumped neighbour does, with the central contact, the central geometry, the central ratio and the 21.39 s delivery. The search assumes that delivering more never undoes a crossing; a contradiction among its points is reported as broken and ends the chain.
(2) A chain holds the fractions tried and whether each meets the criterion. Its bracket is (lo, hi]: lo the largest fraction tried that does not meet it, hi the smallest that does. f = 0 is Stage 7's pertest run at the central contact, read: where it meets the criterion, f* = 0 and no run is made (C1: eight tests; C2: two). Otherwise the first fractions are Part C's, 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3, run for every test (both neighbours in each run; Part C reads them), then 0.5 and 1.0 while the bracket still holds them, then the bracket is halved until it is at most 0.02 wide. A test that does not meet the criterion at f = 1 is recorded as above 1. f* is reported as its bracket. Part C's fractions as first points give Part C runs at exactly 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3, not the nearest ones; 0.5 and 1.0 are P3's bound and the brief's end; 0.05, P3's other bound, is the first midpoint of (0, 0.1]. So every bound a prediction names is a fraction tried or lies outside the final bracket, and "f* below x" is decided: true when the criterion is met at a fraction at or below x, false when it is not met at x or above.
(3) Runs. A chain's next run is made only once its earlier runs are recorded. C1's runs beyond the first round hold both neighbours (the lumped one recorded, not used by C2's chain); C2's hold the lumped neighbour alone (set c2). The cost, by the rules: beyond the first round, three runs for a threshold in (0, 0.3], five in (0.3, 0.5], seven in (0.5, 1] and two to find it above 1; none where Stage 7 settles the case.
(4) A5, exploratory: f*(C3), the smallest f at which the lumped neighbour reaches 150 C (D67 (1)'s crossing, read from the stored series), from f = 0 (Stage 7), the first round and C2's chain: the bracket (the largest fraction tried without the crossing, the smallest with it), and the fraction at which the lumped neighbour's peak, linear in f between those two runs, reaches 150 C (a run stopped at 255.7 C enters with 255.7 C, a lower bound of its peak).
(5) Timing is not scored at f*: at f* the crossing falls near the end of the window by construction (brief, pitfalls). It is scored at the common fractions (Part C, D81).
(6) f* is a model quantity, the fraction a conduction-and-delivery model needs, not a measurement of what reached the neighbour.

D79. A4, the sensitivities (brief; not registered). For the three tests nearest the median trigger energy (45,176.0 J), M50_PP_Exp3 and M50_ST_Exp2 (1,280.6 J away each: the median is their mean) and M50_SP_Exp1 (1,635.8 J), and the two extremes, M50_PT_Exp1 and M50_SP_Exp3, at the central contact:
(1) the delivery over 10 s and over 60 s (C1 and C2; conditional, D77 (4)); the cap-end geometry (C1; the lumped neighbour has no geometry, so f*(C2) is unchanged by construction). Each chain starts from Part A's result: f = 0 is Stage 7's run (with no delivery the variant changes nothing), then Part A's upper end, its lower end, Part A's points above, then halving to 0.02.
(2) The ratio's bracket (C1 and C2). The model receives only the delivered energy f r E_b, so the threshold in delivered energy does not depend on r, and f*(r) = f*(r_c) r_c / r exactly: Part A's points are rescaled by r_c / r, and a run at the bracket's ratio is made only where the rescaled bracket is wider than 0.02 (a bracket of 0.015625 becomes 0.0219 at the smallest ratio).
(3) A shift is the interval of f*(Part A) less f*(variant) from the two brackets. Where a variant's chain ends in Part A's own bracket, the shift is below the search's resolution; the two chains (Part A's and the variant's, each in its own configuration) are then halved on together, from that bracket, until it is an eighth as wide (sets refA and ref_), so that a shift of more than an eighth of the bracket is resolved; f* itself is reported at 0.02. "Lowered" means established lower at the finer resolution: the variant meets the criterion at a fraction where Part A does not.

D80. Part B, the electrical path in the parallel tests (brief; bracket, not registered as a value). In the eight parallel tests (Table 3: M50_PT_Exp1 to 4 and M50_PP_Exp1 to 4) the neighbour discharges through the failed cell; in series there is no such path. A fraction f_e of the stored electrical energy (65.52 kJ, D68), 0.05 (3,276 J) or 0.2 (13,104 J, 212 K in the cell before any loss), is released in the roll by volume (the weights of D68 (3)) over the trigger's release, a triangular pulse of 21.39 s from t = 0, in both neighbours, with the delivery at f as in Part A. For each parallel test and f_e, C1's chain: f = 0 first (the Joule heat alone), then Part A's upper end, its lower end, Part A's points above, then halving to 0.02; the shift is f*(Part A) less f*(f_e), as an interval. The lumped neighbour's crossings are recorded, not registered. The discharge would lower the neighbour's state of charge, but the kinetics carry no dependence on it, so the model cannot follow that loss: a stated limit.

D81. Part C, the scorecard at a common fraction (brief; read from the first round). At f = 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3, for every test, Stage 7's scorecard (D67 (3) and (4)) for C1 (the hot spot at 255.7 C, the run's own stop), C2 (the lumped neighbour at 255.7 C, its own stop) and C3 (the lumped neighbour at 150 C, the stored series' crossing): hits, misses, false alarms and correct rejections against the sixteen outcomes, the score (hits and correct rejections over 16), and each hit's signed time error, the predicted time less the measured time to propagation, the model's clock from the start of cell 1's release and the paper's from the trigger's runaway (D34). Every run is at exactly its stated fraction. Stage 7's scorecard at f = 0 is set beside it, read.

D82. Part D, the gas layer (brief; `src/stage8_model.py`, `SplitCell` and `run_resolved_split`).
(1) What the paper gives (Hoelle, Zimmermann and Hinrichsen, J. Electrochem. Soc. 170 (2023) 060516, read in full on 26 October from the copy the author supplied). Eq. 28 and 29: a thermal resistance at the interface between the can and the jelly roll that is 0 below the boiling point of DMC, R_max (T_IF less T_vap,DMC)^2 / (T_vap,EC less T_vap,DMC)^2 between the boiling points of DMC and EC, and R_max above that of EC, with T_IF the interface's local temperature. Table V: the boiling points, DMC 91 C and EC 248 C (EMC 110 C and DEC 126 C between). The text after Eq. 28: R_max = 0.03077 m2 K/W, equivalent to a gas layer of 0.8 mm at 0.026 W/(m K). Table VI (the final parameter set, variant 5), cell 2, the first cell heated by the nail-penetrated one: R_max 0.03077 m2 K/W on its heated side and 0.00962 m2 K/W on its "cold" side. The paper applies the layer on the large faces between the cells of its prismatic stack, during the heating phase before a cell's own runaway (its post-runaway resistance and its heatshield's compression concern later phases and a heatshield there is no counterpart of here, and are not applied). So the paper gives numbers, and Part D is made.
(2) How it is applied. The neighbour's roll-to-can boundary is its whole outer boundary: the lateral wall, the cap and the base. Every outer node but those of the two tab discs (the welds of the tabs to the cap and the base, D37) is given a second node, the can's, at the same place: the can's heat capacity there (its shell share, D10) and its conduction along itself move to it, with every path through the surface (the contact with the trigger, convection, radiation in and out) and the delivered flux; the roll's node keeps the roll's heat capacity, its conduction and the kinetics. Each pair is joined by its area over the paper's resistance at the interface's temperature, taken as the mean of the two nodes at the start of the step: on the half of the wall facing the trigger (theta <= 90 degrees, as the delivery's region) R_max is the heated side's, 0.03077 m2 K/W, and on every other part of the boundary (the far half of the wall, the cap and the base) the cold side's, 0.00962 m2 K/W; a node whose area straddles 90 degrees has the two in parallel, by area. Eq. 28 is applied in 64 bands of the interface temperature between 91 and 248 C (2.453 K each), each at its middle temperature, and the matrix is factorised again whenever a band changes: PARDISO's ordering and symbolic analysis are made once for each step length, since the pattern does not change, and a change of the bands costs a numerical factorisation only, which gives the same solution, bit for bit, as a factorisation from the start (tested, D83 (5)). Below 91 C the paper's 0 is taken as 1e-7 m2 K/W, so that the two nodes stay one in effect (about 0.1 K across it at the largest delivered flux). The hot spot is the roll's hottest node, so C1 is read on the roll; the can's hottest node is recorded beside it. The tab discs stay joined, so the tabs' paths are unchanged.
(3) A first form of the split, the lateral wall alone with the cap and the base joined as before, was sized (D83 (5)); there the roll's hot spot reached 255.7 C at the bottom edge of the wall, where the delivered flux entered nodes that the roll and the can still shared. That is an artefact of the form, not of the layer, and the split was extended to the whole boundary but the tab discs before the predictions.
(4) The lumped neighbour is one node, the roll and the can at once, with no boundary between them, so the layer cannot be applied to it: its crossings are unchanged by construction, and those of Part A's first-round runs at f = 0.2 are quoted for Part D.
(5) Runs: the three tests nearest the median trigger energy (D79), at f = 0.2, the central geometry and the 21.39 s delivery, on the split cell with the layer (set gas) and with the resistance at 1e-7 m2 K/W everywhere (set gasctl, the split alone).
