Recorded: the hot spot's crossing and peak with and without the layer, the change (gas less gasctl), the split's own effect (gasctl less Part A's run at f = 0.2), and the can's peak. Not registered.

D83. Checks (brief; `src/stage8_checks.py`), tests, sizing and the run design.
(1) V1: the ratios and the ejected energies re-derived by separate code: the Databank rows read with the csv module, each ratio as (Corrected-Total-Energy-Yield-kJ less Energy-Fraction-Cell-Body-kJ) / Energy-Fraction-Cell-Body-kJ (the main code uses the two ejecta columns), the median by numpy, the trigger energies from `results/stage7_triggers.json`; against `results/stage8_ejected.csv`, within 1e-9 relative.
(2) V2: the searches of C1 and C2 for three tests fixed now, M50_PT_Exp4 (the propagated test of the supplementary Figure 10), M50_ST_Exp3 (the propagated test with the second-lowest trigger) and M50_PT_Exp1 (the lowest trigger), replayed by the check's own statement of D78's rules from the stored records and Stage 7's: from f = 0, each fraction the rules call for must exist among the records, its outcome is read from the record and checked against its stored series (a crossing run stopped before 900 s with every stored sample below 255.7 C; a run that did not cross went to 900 s with every sample below), until the bracket is at most 0.02 wide. Acceptance: the same f* to the interval as the scoring's.
(3) V3: the first-round run of M50_PT_Exp1 at f = 0.1 (the lowest trigger, whose hot spot is expected not to reach 255.7 C there, so that its peak is a peak) made again with every mesh spacing halved (v3mesh, 145,195 nodes) and with every step limit halved (v3step), as D43 (2). Acceptance: the hot spot's peak rise (its peak less 25 C) changes by under 1 % in each; if the base run reaches 255.7 C, its crossing time is compared instead, with the same acceptance.
(4) V4: every record's energy accounts close with the delivered and the Joule energy in them, under 1e-9 of the heat taken in (resolved as D44, lumped as D70 (3)); every delivery equals f E_e and every Joule source f_e 65.52 kJ, to 1e-9 relative.
(5) Before the predictions, `src/test_stage8.py` tested the Stage 8 code on synthetic inputs only: `stage8_model` against `v12_model` bitwise with no source, and against `stage7_model` bitwise with one volumetric source named event (resolved and lumped; cell 1 a made-up ramp to 600 C or the ambient); the shares, their regions and areas; the energy each source delivers and the accounts with one and two sources; the split cell's matrices, vectors, areas and bands against the base model, its solver against a factorisation from the start (bitwise), the control against the base model, the layer against the control, a run cut off and resumed from its state against the uninterrupted run (bitwise), and Eq. 28; the search's rules on made-up thresholds; the run lists, every chain to its end, the restarts, the refinements and the ratio's rescaling with a made-up run in place of the model; one run of each kind through the harness on a made-up configuration (cell 1 not heated, 1 s); the scoring, V1 and V2's rules on made-up records. Separately, to size the runs, five runs with cell 1 held at the ambient temperature (no trigger, outside every Stage 8 configuration), each with a made-up delivered energy over 21.39 s through the facing half (`src/stage8_cost_test.py`; `provenance/stage8_cost_test_*.json`): 3 kJ and 20 kJ on the base mesh, both neighbours, to 900 s; 20 kJ on the split cell with the layer, in its first form and in its final one (D82 (3)), to 60 s; 3 kJ on the refined mesh, to 5 s. The final form's run was first started with a factorisation from the start at every change of the bands and stopped after 32 min, once its cost was clear, unrecorded; it was then made with the solver of D82 (2). What they show is in `predictions_stage8.md`. No run of the neighbour in a Stage 8 configuration was made before the predictions were public.
(6) The run design. The runs are made on the cloud machine (two cores, 7 GB), with the versions of D45 (Python 3.13.16, numpy 2.5.3, scipy 1.18.1, numba 0.68.0, llvmlite 0.50.0, scikit-fem 12.0.2, pypardiso 0.4.7 with MKL 2026.1.0; Linux, Intel Xeon at 2.1 GHz), by `src/stage8_run.py work`: two workers on one list that grows as the chains advance, each a background process of at most two hours, started again as they end. A worker starts a run only if enough of its time is left (5 min on the base mesh, 80 min for V3's refined run); a run still going at its worker's end is interrupted and not recorded, and made again from its start by a later worker. A run that takes longer than 105 min is recorded as failed and left out of every count, with the count reported, and a chain whose next run failed stays unfinished and is reported so. Part D's runs, which factorise their matrix again whenever a band of the interface changes and so may outlast a worker, are the exception: each keeps its whole state on disk every 5 min of wall time (`results/stage8_ckpt/`), a later worker goes on from there, and the result is what an uninterrupted run gives, bit for bit (tested, D83 (5)); such a run is recorded as failed only past 600 min over all its attempts. The list's order: the first round (48 runs), the chains' runs as they become available, Part D's six runs, V3's two. The fixed part of the list (the first round, Part D and V3, 56 runs) has the SHA-256 recorded in the README.
(7) Then, before the note's Stage 8 lines are written, an independent read of every registered number and verdict by an agent that has not seen the work being produced (brief).

D84. The version (brief, setting). Stage 8 is version 1.4.0: a further GitHub release v1.4.0, a further Zenodo new version of the record once the Zenodo record is current (all versions 10.5281/zenodo.23279455), `CITATION.cff` and `.zenodo.json` at v1.4.0, and the note's header at the publishing date with the all-versions DOI. The GitHub release v1.3.0 is a saved draft for the author to publish. The note gains a Stage 8 section of at most eight lines, its earlier text unchanged; every number it quotes comes from `results/stage8_paper_numbers.json` (`src/stage8_numbers.py`, written after the results with the figure, `src/make_figure_stage8.py`), the file from which a manuscript of Stages 1 to 8 is to be written. If the overlap of P2 appears, an exploratory line written after the results may fit the probability that a test propagates against its trigger energy from the sixteen outcomes and the f* values, labelled exploratory and with its method stated; it is not a registered result (brief, pitfalls).

## Added after the Stage 8 predictions were public, while the runs went, before any scoring (26 October)

D85. The runs' order. The lumped-only runs (C2's searches, set c2, and their sensitivities) take under a second each, but in the list's order (D83 (6)) they waited behind the resolved runs of C1's searches, which take one to five minutes each. So from 14:19 UTC a third worker took those sets only (`src/stage8_run.py work --sets ...`, labelled cloud-2core-lumped in its records). This changes the order in which the runs are made, not what any run computes or how it is scored.

D86. Two scripts, corrected before the scoring, in three places.
(1) `src/stage8_score.py` as pushed with the predictions compared each sensitivity with Part A on the two chains' final brackets at 0.02 only; where a variant ends in Part A's own bracket, D79 (3) and the P8 rule of `predictions_stage8.md` compare the refined brackets instead (both halved on to an eighth). The script now takes those where they exist, and keeps the unrefined comparison beside them in `results/stage8_thresholds.csv` and `results/stage8_verdicts.json`. The scoring was run on the partial records while the runs went, to check its outputs; no verdict was read from those runs.
(2) `src/stage8_checks.py` as pushed required, in V2, every stored sample of a run that crossed 255.7 C to lie below that level. A run's stored series ends with the sample of the step in which it crossed, as Stage 7's V2 found (D76), so the check now takes Stage 7's rule (D70 (2)): every sample stored before the crossing lies below the level and the run ended within a step of it.
(3) `src/stage8_checks.py` as pushed required, in V4, every source to have delivered its whole energy, f E_e or f_e 65.52 kJ. A run stopped at 255.7 C (D35) before its pulse ended has delivered only the pulse's integral up to its stop, so V4 now compares each source's delivered energy with that integral, which is the whole energy for a run that went past the pulse's end, and counts the runs of each kind.

## Added after every Stage 8 run had ended and after the scoring (26 October; presentation, records and the exploratory fit)

D87. Written after every Stage 8 run had ended and after the scoring: the first round's and the chains' results were scored in commit cbf9c44, before Part D's and V3's runs ended ((4)), and the complete results in a54474c. Presentation, records and the exploratory analysis. No registered quantity or verdict depends on it.
(1) The quoted numbers. `src/stage8_numbers.py` writes `results/stage8_paper_numbers.json`, which holds every Stage 8 number that the README, the note, the release notes and the Zenodo description quote. It reads the records, the scoring's outputs (`results/stage8_verdicts.json`, `results/stage8_scorecard.json`, `results/stage8_partd.json`), the checks (`results/stage8_checks.json`), the exploratory fit (`results/stage8_explore.json`), Stage 7's pertest records at f = 0, for the peaks with no delivery that the texts quote, and `results/stage7_paper_numbers.json` for the Stage 7 results they repeat. The defining constants, the checks' acceptances and the bounds of the predictions (`definitions`), and the estimates of `predictions_stage8.md` that the texts set beside the results (`predictions_estimates`), are copied into it as quoted, labelled as such.
(2) Figure 7 (`src/make_figure_stage8.py`), the brief's two panels. In (a) f\*(C1) and f\*(C2) are drawn as their brackets on a square-root scale, so that the many brackets at or near 0 stay apart, and the overlap of the two groups is marked for each criterion as a bar beside the axis. In (b), at f = 0.2, each criterion's false alarms are drawn to the right of a divider, with their number, since there is no measured time to set them against, and a propagated test a criterion misses is drawn hollow below the axis at its measured time.
(3) The exploratory fit (brief, pitfalls; `src/stage8_explore.py`, `results/stage8_explore.json`), written after Part A's results showed P2's overlap. If each test delivered a fraction f of its ejected energy, drawn afresh for every test from one distribution, and a test propagated when f reached its own f\*, a test with threshold f\* would propagate with probability P(f >= f\*). With f log-normal (median m, spread s of ln f), P = 1 - Phi((ln f\* - ln m) / s), and a test with f\* = 0 propagates for any f. (m, s) are fitted by maximum likelihood to the sixteen outcomes, each f\* taken at the middle of its bracket and, to show the bracket's weight, at its two ends; the fit is compared with one probability for every test (9/16) by the likelihood ratio, against the chi-squared distribution with one degree of freedom. For C2 at the middles: m = 0.0267, s = 4.69, a log-likelihood of -9.38 against -10.97, likelihood ratio 3.16 (p = 0.075); at the upper ends m = 0.0366, s = 4.26, likelihood ratio 3.06 (p = 0.080); at the lower ends the model cannot be fitted, since M50_ST_Exp1's lower end is 0, which would make it propagate for any f, and it did not. C1 cannot be fitted at any point of the brackets, since M50_ST_Exp1 and ST_Exp2 are met at f = 0 and did not propagate. As the brief allows, a logistic in the trigger energy alone is fitted beside it: intercept -1.511, slope 0.0428 per kJ, even chance at 35.3 kJ, likelihood ratio 0.97 against one probability (p = 0.32), a probability of 0.24 at the smallest trigger (8.98 kJ) and 0.69 at the largest (54.30 kJ). Neither is a registered result, and with sixteen outcomes neither departs significantly from one probability for every test.
(4) Part D's and V3's runs. From 15:53 UTC one worker took V3's refined run first (`--prefer v3mesh`, the option the workers' code has for the long run), so that it began with a whole worker's time, while the other went on with Part D; this changes the order of the runs, not what any of them computes. Each of Part D's three runs with the layer was cut off once at a worker's end and went on from its checkpoint (D83 (6)): 65.1 to 69.5 min over the two attempts, with 27,959 to 30,192 steps and 27,226 to 29,201 factorisations, and energy accounts closing to within 3.8e-13 of the heat taken in; the three controls took 41 to 48 s. V3's refined run took 3,638 s and its run with the steps halved 528 s; the latter had first been started near a worker's end, was interrupted there, unrecorded, and made again from its start, as D83 (6) prescribes. The last of the 230 runs ended at 18:03:03 UTC, and none failed.
(5) The note. Its Stage 8 section has eight lines, and every number in them is in `results/stage8_paper_numbers.json`, at its precision or rounded from it. The earlier text is unchanged but for the header's date, now the publishing date, 27 October 2026, beside the all-versions DOI (D84). The note stays on two pages, with its stylesheet unchanged.

## Added after the independent read of the Stage 8 records and quoted numbers (26 October)

D88. The independent read (brief, checks; D83 (7)): every registered Stage 8 number and verdict read again before the note's Stage 8 lines were written. A separate agent, which had not seen the work being produced, recomputed them from the Databank rows, the measured outcomes (`inputs/fransson2024/m50_tests_stage7.csv`), Stage 7's triggers and pertest records, and the Stage 8 records and series, with scripts of its own; it ran and imported none of the study's code. It read some of it, to explain differences or to take a definition: `src/stage8_search.py` (`state` and `restart_probes`), `relation` in `src/stage8_score.py`, V1 in `src/stage8_checks.py`, `src/stage8_explore.py` for its method, and the energy residual's definition in `src/stage8_model.py`. In its second pass, on the texts, it also read the diffs of the commits that followed the first, the command-line and import lines of `src/stage8_run.py`, `src/stage8_model.py` and `src/stage8_cost_test.py`, `spec`, `fixed_runs`, `runs_hash` and `chain_defs` in `src/stage8_run.py`, and `_outcome` and `replay` in `src/stage8_checks.py`. Its first pass read the working tree from 15:44 to 16:10 UTC, with the 222 records outside Part D and V3 and the one Part D record then made; it did not rerun the model.
(1) Every registered number and verdict reproduced. `predictions_stage8.md` and D77 to D84 are as pushed with the predictions (b8dafb4). It reproduced the ten ratios and the sixteen ejected energies. It checked every record's inputs against its id and its rule, and every outcome against its stored series: 106 resolved and 120 lumped crossings, and every run that did not cross. It reproduced all 32 brackets of Part A, A5's two values, every chain of A4 (the ratio's bracket and the refinements among them), Part B, Part C's twelve scorecards test by test, and P1 to P8 with every supporting number. V1, V2 and V4 reproduced too. In `results/stage8_paper_numbers.json`, 1,362 of the 1,370 entries it recomputes equal its values, the other 8 being the labels of (3), and so do the 178 numerical entries of the blocks added after the results (D87 (1)), with the exploratory fit refitted by its own optimiser (log-likelihoods to 1e-12).
(2) The restart rule. D79 (1) and D80, and the docstring of `restart_probes`, state a restarted chain as Part A's upper end, its lower end, "Part A's points above", then halving. The code pushed with the predictions tries every point of the grid (0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5 and 1.0) that the bracket holds, below Part A's lower end as well as above its upper end, and the runs follow it. The two differ only where a variant meets the criterion at both of Part A's ends: in M50_PT_Exp1's dur10_c1, capend_c1 and elec05_c1, whose bracket after Part A's ends was (0, 0.15], the code tried 0.1 next where the statement would have halved to 0.075, and ten runs were made that the statement would not have called for. Each bracket the code found is a bracket of the same threshold (a fraction that does not meet the criterion below one that does, at most 0.02 apart), and no verdict depends on the difference: P8 rests on f = 0.15, where the cap end meets C1 and Part A does not, and P7 on f_e = 0.2. D79 (1) and D80 stand as pushed and are corrected here: "Part A's points" are the grid's points inside the bracket. The docstring now says so.
(3) The label of eight unregistered comparisons. `relation` in `src/stage8_score.py` called "same bracket" every pair of brackets neither established lower nor higher. Eight of A4's comparisons with the ratio's bracket are of brackets that overlap without being the same, since the rescaled brackets are 0.0175 and 0.0093 wide: M50_PP_Exp3's ratiolow_c1 and ratiohigh_c1, and the ratiolow_c2 and ratiohigh_c2 of M50_PP_Exp3, ST_Exp2 and SP_Exp1. They are now labelled "overlapping, not established" in `results/stage8_verdicts.json`, `results/stage8_thresholds.csv` and `results/stage8_paper_numbers.json`, and P6 counts such a pair as a tie, as `predictions_stage8.md` defines a tie; no Part A pair is of that kind, so P6's ties are unchanged. The brackets are now written to ten significant digits, not six, so that a refined bracket such as (0.4609375, 0.4625] appears whole. No number or verdict changes.
(4) `results/stage8_thresholds.csv` did not hold the comparison at 0.02 that D86 (1) says it keeps beside the refined one, and in its ten refined rows the relation and lowering shown were the refined brackets', which it did not give. For each refined variant it now holds Part A's and the variant's refined brackets and the comparison at 0.02 (25 columns added). No number changes.
(5) Where a stored series ends. D86 (2) says that a run's stored series ends with the sample of the step in which it crossed, as Stage 7's did (D76 (2)). In Stage 8 that holds in 98 of the 229 crossings, those whose crossing step ended on a 0.1 s mark. In the other 131 (the 128 the first pass found, 90 resolved and 38 lumped, and Part D's three controls), with the sources' finer steps, the series ends with the last 0.1 s sample before the crossing, below the level (M50_PP_Exp2 at f = 0.0125 crossed at 17.064 s, stopped at 17.075 s, and its last sample is at 17.0 s, 255.19 C). The rule V2 takes from D70 (2) holds in all of them, so no outcome changes. D86 (2) stands as pushed, corrected here.
(6) V2 replayed each search from f = 0 through the fractions its bracket called for, and so left out first-round fractions that D78 (2) runs for every test: 0.2 and 0.3 of M50_PT_Exp4 for C1 and C2 and of M50_ST_Exp3 for C1, and 0.3 of M50_PT_Exp1 for C1. It now takes 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3 first, as D78 (2) does, and checks those records against their series as well. The six brackets are unchanged, and V2 passes.
(7) An erratum to the background of `predictions_stage8.md`, whose text stays as pushed. It puts f*(C2) "between 0.017 and 0.038 in eleven others (0.009 for M50_ST_Exp1)". By its own method ((255.7 C less Stage 7's lumped peak) times 61.75 J/K over E_e), the ten besides M50_ST_Exp1 lie between 0.0190 (M50_ST_Exp4) and 0.0383 (M50_SP_Exp4), so the range's lower end is 0.019, not 0.017. Its other estimates (0.42, 0.19, 0.063 and 0.009) round from 0.4162, 0.1859, 0.0625 and 0.0087. No verdict rests on them.
(8) P6's reading. `predictions_stage8.md` counts an inversion when the test with the larger trigger energy has its f* "established higher: its lower end at or above the other's upper end (both ends of a 0 are 0...)". Read literally, the bracket rule would make two tests both at 0 an inversion (0 at or above 0). The scoring reads them as equal, neither higher: the rule's "at or above" is there for brackets that share an end, since a bracket is open at its lower end, and a 0 is a point. Under the literal reading P6 would fail on 16 pairs: the 15 pairs among the six series tests met by C1 at f = 0, and M50_PP_Exp1 with PP_Exp4. The verdict, held, rests on the scoring's reading, stated here.
(9) Readings with no effect. P7's statement says "less than 0.1" and its rule "at most 0.1", and the lowering in M50_PT_Exp1 is at least 0.15. In P3, a bracket ending at 0.05 lies below 0.05 by the definition, so M50_PT_Exp4 and SP_Exp4 lie outside the band; with them it would still be 5 of 16, short of 12. P2's ranges are read as closed, and the two groups share 0 under any reading. "Within 60 s" is an absolute error of at most 60 s. D79 (3)'s "halved on together" is read as each chain halved on its own outcomes, Part A's refinement shared by the variants of a test and criterion, as the records show. A5's lower bound of 255.7 C for a stopped run's peak is not used, since no stopped run brackets a 150 C crossing, and the ratio's rescaling of a test above 1 is not defined but not needed, since no test is above 1.
(10) `src/stage8_explore.py` and its output cited D87 before D87 was written; it is now written.
(11) A second pass, on the texts in draft (the README's Stage 8 parts, the note's Stage 8 lines, D87 and this entry, the Zenodo description, CITATION.cff, the release notes, the finishing guide and the state document) and on the commits after the first pass, confirmed that those commits do what (2) to (6) say and move no registered number or verdict, and that the texts give every registered number right. It found 25 points of wording, scope and precision, now corrected in the texts:
- bounds quoted as if they were values: every crossing at the common fractions comes by 88.3 s after the release (the drafts said within 89 s); the bounds of the predictions that the texts quote (0.3, 0.05 and 0.5, 12, 0.1), the state of charge, V3's peaks and the run times are now in `results/stage8_paper_numbers.json` (`definitions`, `v3`, `runs`), and the run times are given as the records give them;
- f\*(C2) "under 0.05 in thirteen tests", where two of the thirteen are (0.0375, 0.05], met at 0.05: at most 0.05;
- the cap end called the quarter of the can nearest the cap: it is the quarter of the facing half (D77 (4)), an eighth of the lateral wall;
- the probability of propagation stated without its condition in the note, the Zenodo description and the release notes: it is conditional, as the README had it (if a delivered fraction set the outcomes, it would have had to vary from test to test);
- a verb missing in the Zenodo description and the release notes ("the seven that did not propagate would in the model from 0 to 0.1625");
- f\* called the smallest fraction that reproduces a test's outcome: it is the smallest at which the model meets the criterion, which reproduces a propagated test's outcome, while a non-propagated test's is reproduced only below it;
- in the README's summary, that every crossing at a common fraction comes minutes before any test propagated (the latest, at 88.3 s, comes 58.7 s before the earliest measured propagation, at 147 s), and that at f\* a clock crosses at its peak (in the lumped neighbour the runs at the brackets' upper ends cross up to 156 s before the lower-end runs' peaks; D78 (5)'s "near the end of the window" is the brief's wording, and the reason it gives stands: near f\* the crossing time depends steeply on f); and a ratio, "a quarter to a third", that the brackets do not establish;
- the README's header, which still said conduction and radiation only, now scoped to Stages 1 to 7; the status lines' commits (the scoring went in cbf9c44, the corrections of (2) to (6) in 900e373 and d4d9f67, before the texts); the third worker's first run, at 14:20:01 UTC (D85 says from 14:19); the modules Stage 8 reuses unchanged; and, in the release notes, M50_PT_Exp3 beside M50_PT_Exp1 at f_e = 0.05, "not significantly better" for the exploratory fit, and the six searches V2 replays (C1 and C2 for three tests);
- in the state document, the lumped-only runs' times (at most 2.6 s), the sizing run's solver (its 2,146 s are with D82 (2)'s solver; the first attempt, with a factorisation from the start at every change of the bands, was stopped at 32 min and not recorded) and the chains that needed runs; and D85's "under a second each", which holds for 93 of the 94 lumped-only runs (the longest took 2.6 s);
- in this entry, (7)'s count, the opening's account of what the read used, and four readings added to (9).
(12) A third check, from 18:06 to about 18:35 UTC, on all 230 records and series, the workers' logs, the push log, the history and the texts as committed (510d4b8 and dd77a52); besides what (1) and (11) name, it read the import lines of the Stage 8 scripts, the options and the order of `src/stage8_run.py`'s workers (`order_key` and `work`, at the head and as pushed with the predictions), the lines of `src/stage8_numbers.py` that read Stage 7's files and d4d9f67's change to `fs_text`, and ran none of them. Part D (the six records against `results/stage8_partd.json` and the blocks `part_d`, `part_d_records` and `part_d_summary`), V3 (0.515 % and 0.0021 %), V4 over all 230 records, the run counts, groups and times and the push log reproduce, and no registered number or verdict has moved since its first pass (after a54474c only the texts and the push log changed). It found 13 more points of wording, scope and precision, now corrected:
- in the README's status lines, that Part D's runs were longer than a worker's two hours (each took 65.1 to 69.5 min in all; each was cut off because it started late in a worker's time), that a54474c carried all of Part D's and V3's records (it carried the last; the others went in as they ended), and that the scripts' corrections went in 900e373 alone (also d4d9f67, here, in (11) and in the release notes);
- in item 49, that the roll cools after its peak (its hottest node falls to about 152 C by 54 s, rises again to about 161 C at 180 to 190 s and is near 106 C at 900 s; the clause is removed); and, from an optional point, that the delivered heat stays in the can (the layer holds it there, and the can radiates most of it away: 23.8 to 25.7 kJ of the 27.3 to 29.1 kJ delivered, by 900 s);
- in item 50, that Part A's thresholds would be larger with the layer: f\*(C1), shown above 0.2 in the three tests run, while f\*(C2) cannot depend on it;
- a phrase repeated in the README's running paragraph and two in the state document, and the state document's note on the header (the header's date is now the publishing date, 27 October 2026; D87 (5));
- in (11), its count (25 points, with the missing verb now listed) and 156 s for 155 s;
- in D87, a missing comma, the order of "interrupted there, unrecorded, and made again" in (4), and three constants the texts quote that the numbers file did not hold (R_floor's 1e-7 m2 K/W and the acceptances of 1e-9 and 1 %), now in `definitions`;
- in the note's line on Part D, "Eq. 28 and 29", as everywhere else;
- and, from a second optional point, (5)'s count, now over the 229 crossings of all the records.

## Stage 9, Part A0: what the paper measured, recorded before the predictions were written (29 October)

D89. Part A0 (brief of 29 October, A0.1 and A0.2). Fransson et al. (D33) were read again on 29 October, this time as rendered, with their figures: the article in the copy on the document server of the National Laboratory of the Rockies (https://docs.nlr.gov/docs/fy24osti/87353.pdf, 11 pages, 3,149,370 bytes, SHA-256 22da17dc7832bf6fb8902507cefa8afb75ba9321553a7c140c6d6543e8beccd3), and the supplementary material in the publisher's copy (https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S0378775324008681-mmc1.pdf, 5 pages, the file of D52: 10,240,301 bytes, the same SHA-256), both opened in the author's browser; the publisher's article page asked for a CAPTCHA and was not used. Pages are the journal's (1 to 11) and the supplement's (28 to 32). Values read off a plot are given as "about".
(1) The definition of propagation applied to each test. Section 4 (p. 4) says: "The propagation outcome can be determined by the temperature spikes in the thermocouple readings, where only one spike is present if no propagation occurred and two spikes visible if propagation occurred." Tables 3 and 4 give each test's outcome in their column "Propagation". A test propagated, then, when the neighbour's thermocouple shows a second spike, its own runaway. The 150 C of D33 (2) is an observation read off Fig. 3(a) (p. 5, "a minimum of 150 °C is required in the neighboring cell in order for propagation to occur"), not the definition.
(2) The thermocouple whose reading is the neighbour's maximum. Section 2 (p. 3) says: "Thermocouples were fastened in the middle of the cell surface using Kapton tape and heat conductive glue." The tables' "Maximum temperature [°C]" under "Propagation cell" is that thermocouple's maximum: the supplement's Figure 10 (p. 29) is captioned as "Plots of thermocouple measurements" of M50_PT_Exp4 and M50_PP_Exp3, and the two propagation-cell curves peak at about 610 C and about 103 C, where Table 3 gives 607 and 105 C. Neither the text nor a figure says where on the circumference the thermocouple sat. Section 2 places the cells "directly in contact with each other as in Figure 9(b) in Supplementary Materials", and that figure (p. 28) shows the holder with the two cells lying horizontally, the trigger cell above the propagating cell, the nail entering from above and the X-ray beam across them; it draws no thermocouple. The position is therefore the middle of the surface along the axis, at an angle the paper does not give.
(3) The instant from which the time to propagation is counted. Section 3.1 (p. 3) says: "The time between the temperature increase caused by the TR of the cell and the maximum measured temperature is classified as Section 1." It goes on: "This temporal range, between TR of the first cell and possible TR of the second cell, is crucial for understanding propagation behavior of the studied configurations." For a test with propagation, the second section "ends at the onset of the TR in the second cells". The supplement's Figure 10(a) starts its time axis with Section I, at the trigger's temperature rise, and puts the boundary between Sections II and III, the neighbour's onset, at about 272 s, the time Table 3 gives for M50_PT_Exp4. The time to propagation therefore runs from the start of the trigger's runaway, as its thermocouple shows it, to the onset of the neighbour's, as D34 read it from the text. The discussion of Fig. 5 (p. 6) reads "propagation occurred 156 s after TR in the trigger cell" for an example whose X-ray frames are stamped 157.04 to 158.0 s; no test in Tables 1 to 4 has 156 s, so that example is not used.
(4) What the figures show of the neighbour's surface temperature before propagation in the propagated tests. The article's figures show no temperature history: Figs. 1 and 4 to 8 are X-ray images and speeds, Fig. 2 the radiography categories, and Fig. 3 box plots of "propagation onset temperature" (a) and "propagation time" (b). In Fig. 3(a) the onset temperatures of the M50 tests lie between about 168 and 205 C (the two M50 boxes span about 171 to 201 C, median about 186 C, and about 172 to 187 C, median about 176 C). In Fig. 3(b) the box labelled "M50 - Series" holds four times, at about 147 to 275 s, which are Table 3's parallel times, and "M50 - Parallel" five, at about 149 to 342 s, Table 4's series times; the two M50 labels appear exchanged, so the M50 onset temperatures are quoted together. The one history of a propagated M50 test is the supplement's Figure 10(a), M50_PT_Exp4, whose Section II heat rate is printed as 0.36 C/s. Read off the plot, the neighbour's thermocouple rises to about 100 C within the first 3 s, at the trigger's runaway, is about 85 C at 10 s and about 110 C at the end of Section I (about 24 s), reaches about 140 C at about 45 s, and then rises steadily and slowly to about 185 C at about 270 s, with no plateau; at about 272 s it jumps (Section III, printed 11.77 C/s) to about 610 C at about 304 s. So the record shown is a steady rise, then a jump. Figure 10(b), M50_PP_Exp3, which did not propagate, shows a step to about 60 C within the first 2 s, then a slow rise to about 103 C at the end of Section II (about 310 s). The supplement's Figure 13 (p. 32) shows, for all 32 tests of both cell types, (a) the neighbour's heat rate and (b), in its caption's words, "measured temperatures in each experiment", by outcome: about 157 to 209 C where the neighbour propagated, about 101 to 161 C (and one point near 194 C) where it did not; the caption does not say which temperature each point is. No other propagated test's history is published, and the article's data statement (p. 11) reads: "Data will be made available on request."
(5) The two tests that did not propagate with high maxima. M50_PT_Exp2 (Table 3: trigger 679 C, propagation cell 808 C, "No", a dash for the time, failure category 1) and M50_SP_Exp4 (Table 4: 625 C, 626 C, "No", a dash for the time and for the category) are classified as not propagated by the rule of (1). The paper gives no reason for either test by name. Its one statement on such values (p. 5) reads: "Due to movement of cells and thermocouples during failure, some outlier temperature values are still represented in the tables for consistency reasons." Figure 13(b) of the supplement has no point of a non-propagated test above about 195 C. The two maxima are therefore taken as values that may not be the neighbour's surface temperature, and their f_max is exploratory, as the brief has it.
(6) A0.2, the model's thermocouple. The paper's position, the middle of the surface at an angle it does not give, selects no node other than Stage 6's, so the thermocouple node is the face away from the trigger of D39 and D51 (2): the surface node at the axial middle on the line of centres, on the side away from the line of contact, node 9953 of the base mesh at the central contact, at r = 10.55 mm (the can's outer surface), theta = 180 degrees and z = 35.05 mm (`v12_model.Cell2Contact.probes["away"]`; on V3's refined mesh, the node at the same place). Its history is each record's probe "away". It is the surface point at mid-length farthest from the line of contact and from the half of the can through which the delivery enters (D77 (4)). A thermocouple taped to the can could not sit on the line of contact (D39).
(7) For Stage 9 (brief, pitfalls): the thermocouple node is compared with the non-propagated tests' maxima only, and the propagated tests are read by their times only, because the model has no runaway of the neighbour as a whole (D35) and Stage 7's internal short overshot their maxima about twofold (D75). The measurements that bear on the two readings of Part C2 are M50_PT_Exp4's history and the onset temperatures of Fig. 3(a); the other eight propagated tests' histories are available from the authors on request, not in the paper.

## Stage 9 design, recorded with the predictions before any Stage 9 run (29 October)

D90. Stage 9 (brief of 29 October): the delivered fraction read from each test's time and peak, not only its outcome. Stage 8 found the fraction f* of the ejected energy at which the model first meets a criterion; Stage 9 reads a fraction from each test's measurement as well: f_t, at which the model's crossing comes at the measured time to propagation, for the nine tests that propagated (read by their times only), and f_max, at which the model's thermocouple node peaks at the measured maximum, for the seven that did not (read by their peaks only), with Stage 8's triangular delivery over 21.39 s (Part A) and with long deliveries at constant power (Part B), and asks whether those fractions separate the two groups (Part C). No Stage 1 to 8 code or result is edited, and no Stage 7 or Stage 8 run is repeated except the three of the laptop check (D95 (5)). The Stage 9 code is in new files (`src/stage9_inputs.py`, `src/stage9_model.py`, `src/stage9_search.py`, `src/stage9_run.py`, `src/stage9_score.py`, `src/stage9_checks.py`, `src/stage9_laptop.py`, `src/stage9_cost_test.py`, `src/test_stage9.py`, `run_stage9_windows.cmd`; the quoted numbers and the figure follow the results, D97), which import the Stage 5 model and harness, the Stage 7 model and kernel, and the Stage 8 inputs, model, search and harness unchanged. Stage 9 is version 1.5.0 (D97).
(1) Every input is Stage 8's (D77): each test's Stage 7 trigger E_b released over 21.39 s, the central contact and tabs, 25 C, the posterior-mean kinetics with the reaction heat fed back one-way, the ejected energy E_e = r E_b at the central ratio 3.105285, the delivery f E_e from t = 0 as a uniform flux over the half of the neighbour's can facing the trigger (the lumped neighbour receiving the same energy), the sub-step limit of 4,096 from t = 0, and C1 (the hot spot at 255.7 C) and C2 (the lumped neighbour at 255.7 C). The changes the brief names are brackets, labelled conditional: the constant power over 120, 240 and 360 s of Part B, the ratio's bracket and the aged SEI of C3.
(2) The author's answers of 29 October. The brief names "the three median tests" (M50_PP_Exp3, M50_ST_Exp2 and M50_SP_Exp1, Stage 8's A4 set) for P6, P8 and C3's aged SEI. Two of them did not propagate, so they have no measured time and no f_t, and M50_SP_Exp1's hot spot reaches 255.7 C at f = 0 already, at 21.1 s (Stage 7), so no delivery can put its C1 crossing at its measured 223 s. On the author's answers: P6 is scored on the three propagated tests whose hot spot does not reach 255.7 C at f = 0, M50_PT_Exp4, M50_PP_Exp2 and M50_ST_Exp3, the only tests where an f_t(C1) can exist; P8 and the aged SEI of C3 on the three propagated tests nearest the median trigger energy, M50_PP_Exp4 (46,860.8 J), M50_SP_Exp1 (46,811.8 J) and M50_SP_Exp2 (48,734.7 J), the three nearest both the median of the nine (46,860.8 J) and that of the sixteen (45,176.0 J); B1's resolved runs cover all sixteen tests (the brief's full factorial), not the five it names; and the runs are made on the author's laptop, started through computer access approved by the author. `src/stage9_inputs.py` checks each choice against the data it rests on.
(3) What is reused. A fraction is read from a record wherever the record's configuration and fraction coincide with the search's: at f = 0 there is no delivery, so Stage 7's pertest run (fresh SEI) or aged run (aged SEI) at the central contact serves every delivery; with the 21.39 s delivery and the fresh SEI, Stage 8's Part A runs (sets first, c1, c2, refA_c1 and refA_c2; both neighbours or the lumped one alone) serve Stage 9 at their fractions. Stage 8's sensitivity runs (other durations, the cap end, the ratio's bracket, the Joule heat, the gas layer and V3) are not reused. A record whose resolved neighbour stopped at its 255.7 C crossing gives C1 and its time but no peak of the thermocouple node beyond the stop; a peak is read only from a run that went to 900 s.

D91. A1 and A2: the induction curve and f_t (brief; `src/stage9_search.py`, `ft_state`).
(1) A1. For each of the nine propagated tests and each criterion, the crossing time t_c at f*_hi and at f*_hi plus 0.0125, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1 and 0.2, f*_hi being the upper end of Stage 8's Part A bracket (`results/stage8_thresholds.csv`; 0 where f* is 0, so that the grid is then 0, 0.0125, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1 and 0.2, and its 0, 0.1 and 0.2 are Stage 7's and Stage 8's runs). A run stops at the crossing or at 900 s. C1's runs hold both neighbours, C2's the lumped one alone.
(2) V1, before A2. Once every A1 grid is complete, t_c against f on each is written to `results/stage9_v1.json` before any A2 run is listed: every pair of neighbouring grid fractions in which the crossing comes later at the larger fraction (by more than 1e-9 s), or not at all, is flagged. The grid's monotone part is its fractions from the first up to the last before the first flagged pair.
(3) A2. The search reads the grid in order up to its first fraction whose crossing comes before the measured time t_m, hi0, within the monotone part; then bisects between f*_lo (the lower end of Stage 8's bracket) and hi0 on whether the crossing comes before t_m, until the bracket is at most 0.005 wide or a run's crossing lies within 10 s of t_m, the upper end's first and then each midpoint's (the brief). Each midpoint is rounded to 12 decimals, and a record at that fraction is reused. f_t is the fraction of the run within 10 s ("matched"), or, where the width ends the search with both ends crossing (one at or after t_m, one before), the fraction at which the crossing time, linear between the two ends, equals t_m ("bracketed"). Where the width ends it with the lower end not crossing at all, the bracket holds the threshold, the measured time is not reproduced at the search's resolution, and no f_t is reported ("threshold"). Where f* is 0 and the crossing at f = 0 already comes before t_m by more than 10 s, no fraction reproduces it ("early"). "Above grid": no grid fraction crosses before t_m; "not monotone": the monotone part holds none that does.
(4) Reported for each test and criterion: f_t with its bracket (lo, hi], f_t minus f*_hi and minus f*_lo, the crossing times at the bracket's ends, and the local sensitivity dt_c/df, from the two crossing runs (of any search in the configuration) nearest f_t, or nearest the bracket's upper end where no f_t is found. f_t is "found" when it is matched or bracketed.
(5) f_t, like f*, is a model quantity: the fraction a conduction-and-delivery model needs to reproduce the time, not a measurement of what reached the neighbour.

D92. A3: f_max (brief; `src/stage9_search.py`, `fmax_state`).
(1) The node is the thermocouple of D89 (6), the face away; its peak is read over the whole run, so every run of an f_max search goes to 900 s. Below f*_lo(C1) the hot spot does not reach 255.7 C, so a run there goes to 900 s in any case; where a search needs a fraction at which it does, its run is made with the stop at 255.7 C lifted and goes on to 900 s (the run's id carries "_ns"; the crossing is still recorded). That is the case at f = 0 for M50_ST_Exp1 and M50_ST_Exp2, whose Stage 7 runs stopped at 18.1 and 21.7 s, for the extension of (4) and for the exploratory searches.
(2) The five tests with maxima of 71 to 133 C (M50_PT_Exp1, M50_PT_Exp3, M50_PP_Exp3, M50_ST_Exp1, M50_ST_Exp2; registered). From f = 0: if the node's peak there is at least the measured maximum M, f_max = 0 and the overshoot (the peak less M) is recorded; if it is within 5 K below M, f_max = 0. Otherwise the bisection between 0 and f*_lo(C1) (Stage 8's), the upper end's run read first, until the bracket is at most 0.005 wide or a peak lies within 5 K of M; f_max is the fraction of that run, or, where the width ends it, the fraction at which the peak, linear between the bracket's ends, equals M.
(3) The two tests with maxima of 626 and 808 C (M50_SP_Exp4 and M50_PT_Exp2; exploratory, under D89 (5)): the same search run to f = 1, a bisection on (0, 1] by the same rules, its runs not stopped at 255.7 C; its upper end, 1, is run only if every midpoint falls short of M, and "above 1" is recorded if its peak does too.
(4) The extension. If the peak at f*_lo(C1) still falls more than 5 K short of M (f*_lo may be 0), f_max lies above the brief's range; the search then goes on by bisection on (f*_lo(C1), 1], as (3), and its result is reported as lying above f*(C1). It enters P4 as not below f*(C1) and P5 at its value.
(5) Whether f_max lies below f* (for each of C1 and C2): yes when f* is not 0 and f*_lo is at least f_max's upper end (the bracket's, or 0 for f_max = 0), that is, the criterion is not met at the fractions f_max allows; no when f* is 0 (the criterion is met at f = 0) or f_max's lower end is at least f*_hi; otherwise "overlapping, not established".
(6) The lumped neighbour's f_max with the 21.39 s delivery (B2's reference; exploratory, not in the brief): the same search on the lumped neighbour's peak, between 0 and Stage 8's f*_lo(C2), for all seven tests, the two high-maximum tests to f = 1.

D93. Part B: long deliveries (brief; brackets, conditional).
(1) The delivery is a constant power from t = 0 over tau = 120, 240 or 360 s (`stage9_model.Constant`), the energy f E_e, through the same facing half, the lumped neighbour receiving the same; each step receives the pulse's exact integral, and its power enters the step rule as Stage 8's pulse does. No source gives such a duration; these are the brief's brackets.
(2) For every test and duration (all sixteen, D90 (2)): f*(C1) and f*(C2) by D78's search (f = 0 Stage 7's run, which holds for every delivery; the probes 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5 and 1.0 where the bracket holds them; halving to 0.02). The search is replayed along its own path from f = 0: a fraction another search ran in the same configuration is read only where this search's path reaches it, so that f* does not move as the other searches add runs.
(3) For the nine propagated tests, f_t(C1) and f_t(C2) by D91 (3), the grid (f*_hi plus D91 (1)'s steps) read in order until its first fraction whose crossing comes before t_m; where f*(C1) is 0 (six tests), the crossing at f = 0 already comes at 16.9 to 21.1 s and no delivery can delay it, so f_t(C1) is "early" without a run. For the seven that did not propagate, f_max of the thermocouple node by D92, between 0 and f*_lo(C1) of the duration (the two high-maximum tests to f = 1, exploratory), and of the lumped neighbour between 0 and f*_lo(C2) of the duration (B2). Recorded with each: the energy f E_e and the mean power f E_e / tau.
(4) A long delivery can reproduce a time inside its own duration by choice of f, so Part B's result is the energy and power each reading requires, not the fact of a match (brief, pitfalls).

D94. Part C (brief).
(1) C1, the separation: the five registered f_max against the nine f_t(C2) (the 21.39 s delivery), the statistic the largest f_max against the smallest f_t(C2) found, with their brackets beside the values; the two exploratory f_max shown beside them; the same for C1 where A2 finds any f_t(C1).
(2) C2, the story: for each propagated test, the energy f_t E_e and mean power the near-threshold reading needs (f_t(C2) over 21.39 s, and f_t(C1) where found) and those the long-delivery reading needs (f_t(C2) and f_t(C1) over 120, 240 and 360 s), set beside what the paper shows of the neighbour's history before propagation (D89 (4)). No verdict on which the tests did: both are consistent with the outcomes, and the measurement that separates them is the neighbour's surface history before propagation. Exploratory: in one run at f_t(C2) of each delivery, rounded to 1e-6, both neighbours, the stop lifted (sets S_), the thermocouple node's temperature at the measured time and 60 s before it, beside the onset temperatures of Fig. 3(a) and M50_PT_Exp4's record.
(3) C3, not registered: the ratio's bracket (2.218460 and 4.156163 in place of 3.105285) applied to every f_t and f_max by exact rescaling, f times r_c / r (1.39975 and 0.74715), since the model sees only f r E_b; and the aged SEI (initial amount 0, D66) for M50_PP_Exp4, M50_SP_Exp1 and M50_SP_Exp2 with the 21.39 s delivery: f*(C1) and f*(C2) by D93 (2) from Stage 7's aged runs at f = 0, then f_t(C1) and f_t(C2) by D91 (3), the grid read in order.

D95. Checks (brief; `src/stage9_checks.py`), tests and sizing.
(1) V1 as D91 (2), and again from the records after the runs, against the file and the scoring.
(2) V2: the searches of A2 for C1 and C2 of M50_PT_Exp4 (the propagated test of the supplement's Figure 10) and M50_ST_Exp3 (the propagated test with the lowest trigger), and of A3 for M50_PT_Exp1 (the lowest trigger), replayed by the check's own statement of the rules from the records, found by their ids and not through the scoring's tables, each outcome read from the record and checked against its stored series (a crossing run with every stored sample before its crossing below 255.7 C; a run without one at 900 s with every sample below), each peak against the stored probe history; acceptance: the same status and bracket as the scoring's.
(3) V3: the Part B run of M50_PP_Exp3 (the first of the three tests nearest the median trigger energy) under 240 s at f*_lo(C1) of that duration, the largest fraction its hot spot stays below 255.7 C at (f*_hi with the stop lifted if f*_lo is 0), made again with every mesh spacing halved and with every step limit halved, both to 900 s; acceptance: the hot spot's peak rise (its peak less 25 C) changes by under 1 % in each.
(4) V4: every Stage 9 record's energy accounts close to under 1e-9 of the heat taken in (resolved and lumped), and every delivery equals f E_e, or the pulse's integral up to the run's end where the run ended first, to 1e-9 relative. Where two records hold the same point (D90 (3)), their crossing times agree.
(5) The laptop check: three Stage 8 runs made again on the laptop with Stage 8's code (`stage8_run.run_one`), into `results/stage9_xcheck/`: c1_M50_PT_Exp4_f0.012500000 (both neighbours, the hot spot crossing at 18.0 s), first_M50_PT_Exp1_f0.100000000 (both, to 900 s) and c2_M50_PP_Exp2_f0.037500000 (the lumped neighbour, crossing at 206.5 s); acceptance: the same outcomes, crossings and peaks within 1e-6 s and 1e-6 K of the cloud records, and every stored series within 1e-6. Stage 9's grids mix those records with the laptop's runs, so the two machines must agree.
(6) Before the predictions, `src/test_stage9.py` tested the Stage 9 code on synthetic inputs only: `stage9_model` against `stage8_model` bitwise with the fresh state and a triangular source, and against `stage7_model` bitwise with the aged state and no source (resolved, 3 s; lumped, 120 s; cell 1 a made-up ramp to 600 C); a run with the stop lifted against the stopped one up to its stop; the constant pulse, its integral over uneven steps and its largest power; every status of the two searches on made-up crossing times and peaks, the start of the bisection from f*_lo, the extension that never runs f = 1 unless needed, and the speculative midpoints of D96 (1); the relation of D92 (5); the choices of D90 (2) on the real data files; and every chain to its end with a made-up run in place of the model (717 runs, 291 chains), V1 written before any A2 run, the scoring of P1 to P8 and the result files. Separately, to size the runs, three runs with cell 1 held at the ambient temperature (no trigger, outside every Stage 9 configuration), the stop lifted, to 900 s (`src/stage9_cost_test.py`; `provenance/stage9_cost_test_*`): 20 kJ at constant power over 240 s, made to its end; 135 kJ over 360 s and 135 kJ as Stage 8's triangular pulse over 21.39 s, both stopped once their cost was clear (at 23.8 s of model time after 500 s and at 6.9 s after 1,433 s), their progress logs kept. What they show is in `predictions_stage9.md`. No run of the neighbour in a Stage 9 configuration was made before the predictions were public.
(7) Then, before the note's Stage 9 lines are written, an independent read of every registered number and verdict by an agent that has not seen the work being produced (brief).

D96. The run design (`src/stage9_run.py`, `src/stage9_laptop.py`).
(1) The runs are made on the author's laptop with the Python and packages of Stages 5 to 7, whose environment check passed on 21 October and is not repeated, by `run_stage9_windows.cmd`: one worker per logical processor (fewer if the memory is short, 0.6 GB each, D47 (4)), at below-normal priority, on one list that grows as the chains advance. The list's order: the laptop check, then every lumped-only run (each takes seconds), then the exploratory searches' runs and V3's (the longest), then Part B's and C3's resolved runs, then Part A's, then the exploratory runs of D94 (2); last, where an exploratory search's run takes hours, the midpoints of its bracket's two halves beside its next midpoint (speculative: they change neither its path nor its result, only how soon its next point is at hand, and are made only by workers that have nothing else to run). A run that takes longer than 1,440 min on the base mesh (2,880 min refined) is recorded as failed and left out of every count, with the count reported; every other rule of D43 (3) and D47 (3) is kept (claims; a run interrupted at its worker's end is not recorded). None of this changes what a run computes.
(2) Records go to `results/stage9_runs/` and series to `results/stage9_series/`, both neighbours' histories and the probes at every step, for every run; the laptop check to `results/stage9_xcheck/`; V1 to `results/stage9_v1.json`; the launcher zips them at the end, and they are committed from the zip. The list as it stands before any record (its first runs, which do not depend on any Stage 9 record) has the SHA-256 given in the README.
(3) Sizing (D95 (6)): with cell 1 at the ambient temperature, 20 kJ over 240 s took 958 s on one core of the cloud machine (18,537 steps) and crossed 255.7 C at 228.4 s, its hot spot peaking at 265.7 C at 240 s, the delivery's end; 135 kJ over 21.39 s reached 6.9 s of model time in 1,433 s, its step down to 0.0002 s, because the step rule holds the local heating near the delivered surface to about 1 K per step (D13) and the flux at that energy is about 5.4 MW/m2 at its peak (12.6 kW over 2.32e-3 m2): such a run takes hours. The exploratory searches of D92 (3) need runs of that kind, which is why their runs are listed early and their bisections take speculative midpoints.

D97. The version (brief, setting). Stage 9 is version 1.5.0: a further GitHub release v1.5.0, a further Zenodo new version of the record (all versions 10.5281/zenodo.23279455), `CITATION.cff` and `.zenodo.json` at v1.5.0, and the note's header at the publishing date with the all-versions DOI. The note gains a Stage 9 section of at most eight lines, its earlier text unchanged, which says that the propagated tests are read by their times only and the non-propagated by their peaks only (brief, pitfalls); every number it quotes comes from `results/stage9_paper_numbers.json` (`src/stage9_numbers.py`, written after the results with the two-panel figure, `src/make_figure_stage9.py`), the file from which a manuscript of Stages 1 to 9 is to be written.

## Added while the Stage 9 runs went, before any scoring (29 October)

D98. A worker stopped by a race between workers. The 24 laptop workers started at 05:00:14 UTC (D96 (1)). At 05:00:24 UTC worker 6 recorded `s9_con120_fresh_lum_M50_SP_Exp1_f0.015625000`, a lumped run of 0.06 s, and released its claim; worker 14, whose list had been read before that record was made, then took the run, made it a second time, and stopped with Windows error 5 (access denied) when it went to replace the record with its own, the error Windows gives when the file is open, most likely to another worker reading the list. Worker 6's record stands, and the run computes the same whichever worker makes it. Nothing here changes what a run computes.
(1) A worker now checks, once it holds a run's claim, that the run has not been recorded since it read the list, and if it has, lets the claim go and takes the next run. When Windows refuses to replace a record, a worker keeps the record already there if there is one, and otherwise tries again for up to 10 s (a file held for a moment by another program) before it stops (`src/stage9_run.py`, `_put`).
(2) `add_stage9_workers_windows.cmd` (`src/stage9_laptop.py --add`) leaves the running workers as they are, since stopping them would lose their runs in progress, among them exploratory runs of hours. For each worker that has stopped without its normal end (its log says neither that nothing is left to run nor that it is stopping), it starts one with the code as it now stands, numbered on from 24; while its window is open it does the same for any later one, at most 24 in all, and removes each claim whose worker is no longer running (at the end of the list such a claim would keep the others waiting; its run, if unrecorded, is made again). The first start's workers keep the code they started with, so (1) holds only for the new ones: one of the first start's workers may still make a run a second time, and its record then replaces the first with the same computation on the same machine (its own worker number and times), or, as with worker 14, it stops and is replaced.
(3) `src/test_stage9.py` tests (1) and the helpers of (2) with a made-up run in place of the model; its nine tests pass.

D99. Three scripts corrected before the scoring, and the scripts of the outputs written. While the runs went, the scoring was run on the partial records, into a scratch folder, to check that it writes its outputs, and the end-to-end test of D95 (6) was extended to every output; no verdict was read from those runs.
(1) `src/stage9_score.py` as pushed took the columns of `results/stage9_fractions.csv` from its first row. The rows of the propagated and of the non-propagated tests have different columns, so the file could not be written. It now takes every row's columns, in the order first met; no number changes.
(2) `src/test_stage9.py`'s run of every chain to its end now also makes every output from the made-up records: the scoring's files, the checks, the numbers file and the figure, in a scratch folder that sees those records beside the real Stage 7 and Stage 8 ones. Where Stage 7 or Stage 8 already holds a point, the made-up run now crosses when that record does, as a run of the model would.
(3) `src/stage9_checks.py` as pushed meant to leave the hottest node's index out of the laptop check's comparison of the series, but entered it as a difference of shapes, so the check could not pass for a run of the resolved neighbour. The index is now compared apart (the number of its entries that differ is recorded) and is not part of the acceptance.
(4) V1 compared the grids read after the runs with the file written before A2 for exact equality. A point can be held by two records, one of them made on the other machine (D95 (4)), and their crossing times agree to about 1e-13 s, not exactly (D98's two cross-checks); V1 now accepts a difference within 1e-6 s, the laptop check's tolerance, and records the largest.
(5) V4 as pushed did not check what D95 (4) states, that where two records hold the same point their crossing times agree. It now does, within 1e-6 s, and lists any pair that does not.
(6) `src/stage9_numbers.py` (every number the texts quote, D97) and `src/make_figure_stage9.py` (the two-panel figure of the brief) were written now, before the results, and are tested by (2). The nine tests of `src/test_stage9.py` pass.

## Added after the independent read of the Stage 9 records and quoted numbers (29 October)

D101. The independent read (brief, checks; D95 (7)): every registered Stage 9 number and verdict read again before the note's Stage 9 lines were written. A separate agent, which had not seen the work being produced, recomputed them from the measured tests (`inputs/fransson2024/m50_tests_stage7.csv`), Stage 7's triggers and records, the Databank rows, Stage 8's thresholds and records, and the Stage 9 records, series, cross-checks, V1's file, the workers' logs and `results/stage9_laptop_workers.json`, with scripts of its own; it ran and imported none of the study's code, and did not run the model. It read single lines of `src/stage7_inputs.py`, `src/stage8_inputs.py`, `src/stage9_inputs.py`, `src/stage9_checks.py` and `src/stage9_model.py`, for the propagation level (278.9 C times 1 less half of 0.1665, 255.681575 C) and the field that holds the crossing, and the return of `v1` in `src/stage9_checks.py`. It read the repository at dde06c5, with its 653 records, from about 10:50 to 11:30 UTC, and the block that 31ca06d added to the numbers file apart.
(1) Every registered number and verdict reproduced: every record's inputs against its id and its rule; every outcome against its stored series (292 histories of the resolved neighbour and 651 of the lumped one, and the 292 peaks of the thermocouple's node exactly); the energy accounts (at most 6.8e-12 of the heat taken in) and the 939 deliveries (to 2.4e-16); Stage 8's 32 brackets; the 18 grids of A1 and V1; all 254 searches of A2, A3, Part B and C3 (1,169 fields); the 28 runs of Part C2; the three CSV files; P1 to P8; V2, V3 and V4, the 186 pairs of records that hold the same point (on 183 points; 16 of the pairs, on 15 points, all of the lumped neighbour, a cloud record beside a laptop one) all identical in their crossing times; and the laptop check, on which the two machines' agreement for the resolved neighbour rests. Of the 4,712 entries of `results/stage9_paper_numbers.json` it recomputed, 4,711 equal its values, the other being V1's of (3), and so do the 169 of the derived block.
(2) Two bracketed f_t that straddle a jump. Over 240 and 360 s, M50_PP_Exp2's search for f_t(C1) ended "bracketed", with 0.0514 and 0.0756 interpolated between the bracket's ends as D91 (3) has it; but there the crossing does not pass through the measured time between the two ends, it jumps. Over 240 s, at f = 0.05 the hot spot peaks at 255.46 C at 35.7 s, 0.22 K short of the level, falls to 248.6 C by the measured 147 s and crosses only at 238.1 s, near the delivery's end, while at 0.053125 it crosses at 29.0 s, at that early peak; over 360 s the same at 0.0734375 (254.96 C at 35.5 s, a crossing at 246.5 s) against 30.2 s at 0.078125. No fraction there reproduces 147 s. The scoring keeps the registered status with its interpolated value and now flags a bracketed f_t whose bracket straddles such a jump (`jumps` in `src/stage9_score.py`, `across_jump` in its outputs): in the run at the bracket's lower end, the criterion's history peaks before the measured time above its value there. Of the eight bracketed f_t, these two are flagged and no other. The texts count a time as reproduced only by a matched f_t or a bracketed one not flagged: over 240 s the hot spot reproduces one time (M50_ST_Exp3), over 360 s two (M50_PT_Exp4 and ST_Exp3). P6 fails either way: M50_PT_Exp4 has no f_t(C1), and M50_PP_Exp2's is 0.0514 by the rule and none on this reading.
(3) V1's "written before A2" stood for the file's existence. The check now compares the file's time with the first A2 run's start and the last A1 run's end: all three are 05:56:10 UTC, to the second the records keep. That the file came first rests on the code: the worker that completes the grids writes it before it lists any A2 run, and an A2 chain lists no run while the file is absent (`build_runs` and `chain_state` in `src/stage9_run.py`). `maybe_write_v1` would go on if Windows refused the file's replacement; here the file exists and equals the grids, so nothing turns on it.
(4) D91 (4) takes the sensitivity dt_c/df from the two crossing runs "of any search in the configuration" nearest f_t. The scoring reads that as any run of the configuration, and so takes Part C2's runs at f_t too, which are not a search; without them seven unregistered sensitivities would differ, for instance A2's for M50_SP_Exp3, -6,853 against -7,299 s per unit f.
(5) As D91 (3) states, a grid fraction before the first that crosses before the measured time is not taken as a match, even where it crosses within 10 s after it, and the bisection does not read a grid fraction that falls inside its bracket. Two unregistered f_t would otherwise differ: M50_PP_Exp2's f_t(C2) over 360 s (0.0875, crossing 8.5 s late, against the 0.109375 found, 9.1 s early) and the aged SEI's in M50_SP_Exp2 (0.025, 6.3 s late, against 0.028125, 8.0 s early), with which P8's change there would be 0 instead of 0.0031; P8 holds either way.
(6) The two exploratory searches were labelled "extended" in `results/stage9_fractions.csv` and `results/stage9_long.csv`, the word D92 (4) gives its extension above f*_lo, which no registered search needed; they are now labelled exploratory only. Every f_max run lifts the stop, where D92 (1) asks it only of the runs that need it; no result differs.
(7) The workers' logs show 157 repeat computations of 155 lumped runs, by the first start's workers in the race of D98; every repeat agrees with the others to the logs' precision (0.1 s, 0.1 C), and each record kept equals its last.
(8) P2 now reports, as its rule says, the run nearest in time for every test, not only for those it counts: M50_PT_Exp4, ST_Exp3 and ST_Exp4 are 56.4, 81.9 and 73.2 s early at the upper ends of their brackets. M50_SP_Exp3's nearest run is Part C2's run at f_t (0.7 s early); without it the A2 run at 0.003125 (11.3 s early) would still count. The numbers file no longer gives each search's bisection runs, a field that was empty for every finished search and held the next run of the two unfinished ones (a chain keeps only the runs still to be made), and Figure 8 (b) now also draws the three tests whose C2 search ended at the threshold, at that bracket. No registered number or verdict changes.
(9) Not checked by it: the run list's SHA-256, which needs the study's code to list the runs; the push time of the predictions is taken from `provenance/github_activity_stage9.json`.
(10) A second pass, from about 11:35 to 11:50 UTC, read the commits after the first (c8d67aa and 7d90032), the code they cite (the diffs of `src/stage9_score.py`, `src/stage9_checks.py`, `src/make_figure_stage9.py` and `src/test_stage9.py`; `chain_defs`, the f_t branch of `chain_state`, `maybe_write_v1` and `build_runs` in `src/stage9_run.py`) and the texts in draft (the README's Stage 9 parts, the note's Stage 9 lines and the release notes' Stage 9 part). The commits do what (2) to (8) say and change no registered value: the scorecard and the states gain fields and change none, the tables change only the exploratory label, and its own rebuild of `results/stage9_paper_numbers.json` matches all 5,039 of its entries (6 of them descriptive strings, read rather than recomputed); the jump flag, applied to the stored series, flags the same two searches. It corrected its own count in (1), 28 for 15 points shared by a cloud and a laptop record (it had counted the cloud machine's two worker labels as two machines), and four details of this entry, now amended; every number in the drafts matched the records at the precision quoted, and it found 28 points of wording, scope and precision in them, corrected in the texts before they were committed. The estimates of `predictions_stage9.md` that the texts set beside the results are now in the numbers file (`predictions_estimates`), as quoted.

## Added after every Stage 9 run had ended, with the texts (30 October)

D100. Written after every Stage 9 run had ended, after the scoring and after the independent read; its number was left for it when D101 was written. The scoring of the first 653 records was pushed in commit dde06c5, while M50_PT_Exp2's two exploratory searches under 240 and 360 s went on ((4)), and the complete results in 9807b99. Presentation, records and the exploratory readings. No registered quantity or verdict depends on it.
(1) The quoted numbers. `src/stage9_numbers.py` writes `results/stage9_paper_numbers.json`, which holds every Stage 9 number that the README, the note, the release notes and the Zenodo description quote, but for the commits, the times of the runs' history (from git, the records and the logs; the time the laptop was closed from the author), the run list's count and hash (`python3 src/stage9_run.py list`, 01be39c) and the independent read's counts (D101). It reads the records and series (with Stage 7's and Stage 8's where they hold a point, as the scoring does), Stage 8's thresholds, the Databank's ratios, the scorecard (`results/stage9_scorecard.json`), the checks (`results/stage9_checks.json`) and the two workers files of (4). The defining constants, the checks' acceptances and the bounds of the predictions (`definitions`), and the estimates of `predictions_stage9.md` that the texts set beside the results (`predictions_estimates`), are copied into it as quoted, labelled as such; `derived` holds the ranges, counts and crossings the texts quote, read from the scoring's outputs and the records (31ca06d, 7d90032), and `runs` the counts, times and workers of (4).
(2) Figure 8 (`src/make_figure_stage9.py`), the brief's two panels. In (a) the crossing time is drawn against f - f\*_lo on a square-root scale, so that the runs near the threshold, where the crossing moves fastest, stay apart, through every run of the configuration (A1's grid, A2's bisection, Stage 8's runs and Part C2's), and each test's measured time is a short mark on its C2 line where A2 found f_t(C2), at the line's start otherwise. In (b) the fractions are drawn against the trigger energy, also on a square-root scale: a fraction found within its tolerance as a point, an interpolated one with its bracket as a bar, the three tests whose C2 search ended at the threshold hollow with that bracket, the two exploratory tests as hollow diamonds, and the two dashed lines of Part C1's comparison.
(3) The exploratory readings, written after the results; none is registered. (a) For the three tests whose C2 search ended at the threshold, the time at which the lumped neighbour peaks, short of the level, in the run at the bracket's lower end, from its stored series: 301.2 s in M50_PT_Exp4, 99.4 s in ST_Exp3 and 324.1 s in ST_Exp4. Where that peak comes before the measured time, as in M50_ST_Exp3 and ST_Exp4, a fraction inside the bracket that just reaches the level reaches it near the peak, before the measured time; in M50_PT_Exp4 the peak comes after it, so a fraction inside that bracket may reproduce the time. (b) The hot spot's latest crossing in each test, from the records (13.8 to 21.1 s), for P1's report. (c) The count of times reproduced, by a matched f_t or a bracketed one not flagged as across a jump (D101 (2)). (d) Part C1's comparison, the largest registered f_max against the smallest f_t(C2), under each long delivery (7d90032): it separates the groups under none. (e) In Part C2's runs at f_t(C2) with the stop lifted (D94 (2), exploratory by design), the thermocouple's node 60 s before the measured time and its rise over that last minute, beside its reading at the measured time; and the energies and mean powers of the README's table of the two readings, f E_e and f E_e / tau. The lumped neighbour's own f_max (D92 (6)) and the two high-maximum tests' f_max (D92 (3)) are exploratory by design, and every text labels them so.
(4) The runs. The launcher started the first start's 24 workers (D96 (1)) at 05:00:11 UTC on 29 October (the stamp of their logs; it writes the workers file, at 05:00:14 UTC, once they run), the first record began at 05:00:13 UTC, and one worker was replaced at 05:15 UTC (D98). The records were committed from the launcher's zips as the runs went: 378 in the zip written at 05:17 UTC (867e6b2), 566 in that of 07:05 UTC (38c6072), 631 at 09:39 UTC (db6ce12) and 653 at 10:44 UTC, scored in dde06c5 (867e6b2's message counts two of the three cross-checks; its tree holds all three, the third having ended at 05:16:40 UTC). The author closed the laptop at about 11:48 UTC, by his own account, while M50_PT_Exp2's two exploratory searches under 240 and 360 s went on. The first start's last record had ended at 11:37:00 UTC, 665 in all. The list, which the records alone fix, then held four runs, listed from about 11:30 and 11:34 UTC as the records before them ended: f = 1 under 240 s, and 0.99609375 under 360 s with the two speculative midpoints of that bracket's halves. A waiting worker reads the list every 10 s (`work` in `src/stage9_run.py`), so by the code they were in progress, though the logs record only finished runs; none of them was recorded. By the morning of 30 October the laptop had restarted and no worker of the first start was running. On 30 October the launcher (`run_stage9_windows.cmd`, unchanged) was started again, through the computer access the author had approved (D90 (2)): it removes the claims that stopped workers leave (`reset-claims`, at every start), and it started 24 workers at 04:33:52 UTC with the code as it stood since D98, which took those four runs, from their start, at 04:33:56 UTC; once 0.99609375 had ended, the run at f = 1 under 360 s followed. Its workers file replaced the first start's on the laptop: the repository keeps the first start's as `results/stage9_laptop_workers.json`, as D101 read it, and the second's as `results/stage9_laptop_workers_restart_20261030.json`. The last record ended at 2026-10-30 05:49:36 UTC: 670 records, none failed, 59 of them speculative (D96 (1)), 5 made after the restart. On the laptop a lumped-only run took a median of 0.16 s (at most 9.0 s); a run of both neighbours stopped at a crossing 122 s (21 to 1,125 s), one that went to 900 s 1,369 s (609 to 7,384 s), among them the exploratory searches' runs 3,977 s (1,933 to 7,384 s), since the step rule holds the local heating to about 1 K per step (D96 (3)); V3's refined run took 8,323 s and its run with the steps halved 1,437 s.
(5) The complete records. The 17 records made after the 653 of dde06c5, all of M50_PT_Exp2's exploratory f_max under 240 and 360 s (12 made before the laptop was closed and 5 after the restart), were committed in 9807b99, with the scoring, the checks, the quoted numbers and Figure 8 made again. Every registered number and verdict is as at dde06c5. Both searches end above 1: at f = 1 the thermocouple's node peaks at 747.6 C over 240 s and 660.8 C over 360 s, short of the measured 808 C (D92 (3), exploratory). V4 now covers 309 records of the resolved neighbour and 668 of the lumped one and 973 deliveries, with the same largest residuals (6.8e-12 and 4.0e-12 of the heat taken in), and 190 pairs of records on 187 points, the four added pairs each a lumped-only run beside the lumped neighbour of a run of both at the same point, all identical; V1, V2, V3 and the laptop check read no new record and are unchanged.
(6) The note. Its Stage 9 section has seven lines, every Stage 9 number in them in `results/stage9_paper_numbers.json`, at its precision or rounded from it (the predictions' commit and Stage 7's overshoot, about twofold, aside). The earlier text is unchanged but for the header's date, now the publishing date, 30 October 2026, beside the all-versions DOI (D97). With the Stage 9 section the note runs to three pages, the section and the closing paragraph on the third; its stylesheet is unchanged.
(7) Zenodo. The draft that D58 and D72 describe as version 1.2.0's (10.5281/zenodo.23589793) was published on 27 October as version 1.4.0, with v1.4.0's files and description (the record's page, read on 30 October); v1.2.0 and v1.3.0 have no Zenodo version and are GitHub releases only (published on 24 and 27 October). The README's citation paragraph now says so. Stage 9's Zenodo version is a new version of that record, its DOI reserved when its draft is made (D97).
(8) The third check. A separate agent, which had not seen the texts produced, read the texts as committed in 1c3b14b (the README's Stage 9 parts, the note, this entry, `.zenodo.json`, `CITATION.cff`, the release notes and the finishing guide) against `results/stage9_paper_numbers.json`, the scoring's outputs, the records, series and logs and the two workers files, with scripts of its own, running none of the study's code. Every registered number and verdict matched, and every quoted Stage 9 result at its precision but for the points below; no file of Stages 1 to 8 had changed. It found 13 points, corrected in the commit that adds this paragraph and, for the release notes and the finishing guide, in the release files, with no registered number or verdict changed: this entry cited D92 (4) for the exploratory search of D92 (3), and it and the README "D92's reference" for D92 (6); D101 (1)'s "254 searches of A2, A3, Part B and C3" are 254 chains, the 18 grids of A1 with the 236 searches; the README's list of entries said "three corrections to the scoring and checks scripts" for corrections to three scripts (D99); the laptop check's peaks within 7.4e-13 K are the hot spot's and the lumped neighbour's, the thermocouple's node agreeing to 6.1e-12 K; "conduction alone" stood for the runs with no delivery, which have radiation too; "only" the neighbour's history "can" tell the two readings apart, which the study did not show; numbers that the numbers file does not hold (the commits, the times of the runs' history, the run list's count and hash, which only the study's code can recompute (D101 (9)), and D101's counts) were said to come from it, and the time the laptop was closed, which no file holds, is the author's; the launcher's, the workers' and the first record's times were out of order; the release notes put M50_PP_Exp2's early peak at about 30 s (the run at the bracket's upper end crosses at 29.0 s; the early peak, at 35.7 s, is that of the run at its lower end); a test lacked its prefix; the threshold brackets' width, 0.003125, was quoted as 0.0031; the finishing guide named the licences that `.zenodo.json` does not hold; and five points of wording. The hot spot's latest crossing, 21.103 s, stays quoted as 21.1 s, the precision of its range. A short second pass on the corrections found each one made as intended and nothing else changed, and three points more, made with it: the README's closing time attributed to the author, the run list's count and hash added to the numbers file's exceptions, and the aged SEI's largest change, 0.003125, quoted as a bound in the note in full.

## Stage 10, Part A0, recorded before the Stage 10 predictions (30 October)

D102. Part A0 (brief of 30 October, A0.1 to A0.3), recorded before the Stage 10 predictions were written. The brief's rules put all of A0 first, while its P2 says only that the measured history is recorded before P2 is scored; on the author's answer of 30 October, all of A0, the digitisation included, comes first, and the predictions record what it fixed.
(1) A0.1, raw series. The Battery Failure Databank (revision 2, February 2024; D. P. Finegan et al., Journal of Power Sources 597 (2024) 234106) holds one row of summary values per single-cell test in fractional thermal runaway calorimetry (the energies through the body and the ends, the ejected mass, the heating power, the time to runaway): no two-cell or multi-cell test and no temperature history (its workbook as the two-cell study read it, `inputs/databank/SOURCE.md`, and its page, https://www.nrel.gov/transportation/battery-failure.html, read on 30 October). The article's data statement (p. 11) reads "Data will be made available on request."; the publisher lists one supplementary file, Appendix A, and the article's record in NREL's research hub links no dataset. No raw temperature series of the two-cell tests is published. The supplement's Figure 10 is, however, a vector drawing: each of its four curves is a stroked path of 7,359 to 8,307 points, 0.024 to 0.046 s apart in median, so the plotted series can be read out of the paths themselves rather than off pixels. A0.2 does that, with the brief's procedure kept: two readings, and the larger difference between them as the digitisation's uncertainty.
(2) A0.2, the history of M50_PT_Exp4.
