The file is the supplement as D52 used it (https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S0378775324008681-mmc1.pdf, 10,240,301 bytes, SHA-256 e96713ef58e8dc13df8810034c7ee8b47fb3e2fdcdb398a5095ecdfb64dfc0ef), downloaded on 30 October through the author's browser with his permission; its second page is the journal's p. 29, Figure 10. Panel (a) is "experiment 'M50 PT Exp4'"; its dashed curve, which the legend names "Propagation Cell", is the neighbour's thermocouple, and its solid curve, "Trigger Cell", the trigger's own. The tool and the procedure: `inputs/fransson2024/extract_fig10.py` reads the paths twice, by separate code and separate calibrations. The first reading uses pdfplumber's path objects, with each panel's axes fitted by least squares to its 21 vertical and 8 horizontal major grid lines, labelled by the tick labels read as text (largest residuals 6.9e-5 s and 4.0e-4 K); the second parses the figure's form XObject operator by operator with pypdf and takes the axes from the plot area's white frame as 0 to 500 s and 0 to 700 C. Each is resampled every 0.5 s from 0 to 500 s by linear interpolation between the path's points (`inputs/fransson2024/pt_exp4_history.csv`, with both readings; `inputs/fransson2024/fig10_extraction.json`). The two readings differ by at most 0.0005 K for the neighbour and 0.0014 K for the trigger: the digitisation's uncertainty. The figure's resolution is half the curves' stroke width (0.571 pt): 2.47 K and 0.45 s. The recorded uncertainty is the larger of the two, 2.47 K and 0.45 s, and no difference smaller than it is read as a result (brief, pitfalls). The paper's time axis starts at the start of Section I, the trigger's runaway (its curve rises from 28.9 C at 0 s to 128 C at 1 s), from which Table 3 counts the time to propagation (D89 (3)); the figure marks the neighbour's onset, the boundary between Sections II and III, at 271.50 s, where Table 3 gives 272 s. Section I ends at 24.16 s, at the trigger's maximum, 629.95 C (Table 3: 630 C), and Section III at 304.51 s, at the neighbour's, 606.60 C (Table 3: 607 C). The neighbour's record: 32.2 C at 0 s; a spike to 102.0 C at 3.1 s, as the trigger runs away, and back to 79.9 C at 8.4 s, which the paper does not discuss; 113.3 C at 24 s and 141.0 C at 45 s; then a steady slow rise, 153.6 C at 100 s, 171.3 C at 200 s, 187.4 C at 268 s and 197.3 C at 271.50 s; then the jump, 274.7 C at 273 s. Over the last 60 s before 271.50 s it rises by 22.8 K (28.7 K from 212 to 272 s, on Table 3's time). It first passes 100 C at 2.7 s, in the spike, and 150 C at 84.0 s.
(3) The other histories. Panel (b) is M50_PP_Exp3, which did not propagate (`inputs/fransson2024/pp_exp3_history.csv`, read the same way: the readings differ by 0.0002 K for the neighbour and 0.0023 K for the trigger, the resolution 2.46 K and 0.45 s). Its neighbour starts at 25.9 C, spikes to 66.3 C at 2.7 s, is 60.6 C at 11.2 s and rises slowly to its maximum, 102.08 C, at 309.72 s, the end of Section II (Section I ends at 15.82 s), then falls slowly. Table 3 gives 105 C for that neighbour and 686 C for its trigger, where the plotted record reaches 102.08 C and 671.41 C within its 500 s, 2.9 and 14.6 K short, more than the figure's resolution, which the paper does not explain; for M50_PT_Exp4 the plotted maxima are the table's to within 0.4 K. No other neighbour history is published: the article's figures show none (D89 (4)), and the supplement's Figure 13 shows values, not histories. Part A3 therefore has one history, M50_PP_Exp3's.
(4) A0.3, the thermocouple's position. The article's one statement of it (Section 2, p. 3): "Thermocouples were fastened in the middle of the cell surface using Kapton tape and heat conductive glue." Its other statements on the arrangement: the cells "were placed directly in contact with each other as in Figure 9(b) in Supplementary Materials" and "were aligned horizontally within the cell holder" (p. 3). Figure 9(b) (p. 28) draws the holder with the trigger above the propagating cell, the nail from above and the X-ray beam across, and no thermocouple. The height is the middle of the length; the angle is not given. The three surface nodes at mid-length (z = 35.05 mm, r = 10.55 mm) that bracket it on the base mesh: the Stage 9 node, 9953, at theta = 180 degrees, on the side away from the contact, 33.14 mm from the line of contact along the surface (21.10 mm straight); the node nearest 90 degrees from the line, 9938, at theta = 88.277 degrees, 16.25 mm from it (14.69 mm straight), inside the facing half through which the delivery of D77 (4) enters (the next node, 9939, is at 94.392 degrees); and the node next to the line, 9905, at theta = 0.326 degrees, 0.060 mm from it, the first surface node off the line (node 9904, on it, is Stage 6's "toward" probe). A thermocouple could not sit on the line itself (D39), and the paper does not say how near it one taped to the can would be, so the facing node is the nearest the mesh has.
(5) V1 (brief, checks), made now: `src/stage10_v1.py` renders the page at 1200 dpi without anti-aliasing, calibrates each panel's axes on the grid lines and the frame found in the pixels (largest residuals 0.045 s and 0.24 K) and traces the dashed curve column by column, at every whole second from 5 s to 271 s in panel (a) and to 309 s in panel (b) where a dash crosses the column: 164 and 188 seconds. At every one the pixel reading lies within the recorded uncertainty plus one pixel (0.52 K and 0.095 s), with the curve's slope times the uncertainty in time, and it is at most 3.03 and 2.45 K from the vector reading (`results/stage10_v1.json`). The first 5 s, where the two curves rise within a few pixels of each other, are left out.

## Stage 10 design, recorded with the predictions before any Stage 10 run (30 October)

D103. Stage 10 (brief of 30 October): what the one published history decides, the thermocouple's angle, and what the sixteen tests can tell a model. No Stage 1 to 9 code or result is edited. The Stage 10 code is in new files (`src/stage10_inputs.py`, `src/stage10_run.py`, `src/stage10_score.py`, `src/stage10_partc.py`, `src/stage10_checks.py`, `src/stage10_v1.py`, `src/stage10_laptop.py`, `src/test_stage10.py`, `run_stage10_windows.cmd`, with `inputs/fransson2024/extract_fig10.py` of D102; the quoted numbers and the figure follow the results, D107), which import the Stage 5 model and harness and the Stage 9 inputs, model, search, runs and checks unchanged. Every input is Stage 9's (D90 (1)): each test's Stage 7 trigger, the central tabs, 25 C, the posterior-mean kinetics with the reaction heat fed back one-way, the central ratio, the delivery through the facing half as a triangular pulse over 21.39 s or a constant power over 120, 240 or 360 s (conditional), and C1 and C2 at 255.7 C. The one change the brief names, the low and high contacts (200 and 3,700 W/(m2 K), Stage 5's bracket) for the short delivery's runs, is a bracket, conditional.
(1) Part A's runs (A1, `stage10_run.part_a_runs`): M50_PT_Exp4 with both neighbours, the stop at 255.7 C lifted, to 332 s (Table 3's 272 s plus 60 s), under each reading Stage 9 found for it (`results/stage9_states.json`): no delivery (the baseline); the 21.39 s delivery at both ends of its C2 threshold bracket, f = 0.0375 and 0.040625, at the low, central and high contacts; and the long deliveries at their f_t, C2 over 240 s (0.028125) and 360 s (0.040625) and C1 over 360 s (0.071875), at the central contact. Not registered, at the central contact: the ends of the other threshold brackets Stage 9 found for the test, C1 with the 21.39 s delivery (0.009375 and 0.0125), C2 over 120 s (0.028125 and 0.03125) and C1 over 240 s (0.05 and 0.053125). Over 120 s Stage 9 found no f_t for either criterion. Every Stage 10 run records five probes of the resolved neighbour at mid-length: the three nodes of D102 (4), away (the Stage 9 node), side and facing, and two beside them, toward (on the line) and side_out (94.4 degrees).
(2) A2, the comparison (`stage10_score.metrics`), on the record's samples every 0.5 s over the window from 0 s, the start of the trigger's release and the paper's time zero (D34, D102 (2)), to 271.50 s, the onset the figure marks: the root mean square of the model's history less the measured one, as the time average of the squared difference by the trapezoid rule; the difference at 271.50 s; each history's rise from 211.50 to 271.50 s; and the times at which each first passes 100 and 150 C, the model's over its run and the measured over its record. Every reading, contact and probe is reported; the registered comparison is at the away node and the central contact. Not registered: the same over 24.16 to 271.50 s, after Section I and its spike (D102 (2)).
(3) The scoring of P1 to P3. P1 holds if the root mean square at the away node and the central contact is below 25 K at the lower or the upper end of the short delivery's C2 bracket. P2 holds if each of the three long readings' root mean square is below the short reading's, taken as the smaller of its two ends'. P3 holds if the measured rise over the last 60 s exceeds 30 K, the short reading's rise at one of its two ends lies within a factor of two of it (from half to twice), and the rises of both 360 s readings (C2 and C1) lie outside that range.
(4) A3, not a prediction: M50_PP_Exp3's record against its Part B runs at f = 0 and at f_max at each node, over the record's 500 s: the root mean square, the difference at the end of Section II (309.72 s), and the peaks and their times.

D104. Part B, and six runs added for Part C.
(1) B1: for the five low-maximum tests (M50_PT_Exp1, PT_Exp3, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp2), f_max at the side and the facing node by Stage 9's search (D92 (2) and (4), `stage9_search.fmax_state`) on that node's peak over 900 s: f_max = 0, with the overshoot, where the peak at f = 0 is at least the measured maximum M, or within 5 K below it; otherwise the bisection between 0 and Stage 8's f*_lo(C1), the upper end's run read first, until the bracket is at most 0.005 wide or a peak lies within 5 K of M; beyond f*_lo by bisection to 1 where the peak there still falls short. The 21.39 s delivery, the central contact, the stop lifted. One run of a test serves both nodes' searches. The away node's f_max is Stage 9's (A3), not run again.
(2) B2: at each node, the largest of the five f_max against Stage 9's smallest f_t(C2), M50_SP_Exp3's 0.00158; the groups separate at that node if the former is the smaller.
(3) The scoring of P4 and P5. P4 holds if f_max is 0 at all three nodes in M50_PT_Exp3, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp1 and ST_Exp2, and M50_PT_Exp1's f_max at the facing node is below its Stage 9 value, 0.075. P5 holds if the groups do not separate at any of the three nodes.
(4) Six runs for Part C. The brief's Part C is arithmetic on numbers that exist, but one of C2's quantities, the thermocouple node's peak over 900 s with no delivery, exists for ten tests only: Stage 7's runs of the eight tests whose hot spot reached 255.7 C at f = 0 stopped there, at 16.9 to 21.7 s, and Stage 9 ran two of them again with the stop lifted (M50_ST_Exp1 and ST_Exp2, D92 (1)). The other six, M50_PP_Exp1, PP_Exp4, ST_Exp4, SP_Exp1, SP_Exp2 and SP_Exp3, are run at f = 0 with the stop lifted to 900 s, the run Stage 9 made for the two, and nothing else in Part C rests on a new run.

D105. Part C (brief), on the sixteen tests (`src/stage10_partc.py`).
(1) The table. For each test: the outcome, the connection (eight series, M50_ST and SP; eight parallel, M50_PT and PP), the measured time where it propagated and the trigger's body energy E_b. The predictors: Stage 8's f*(C1) and f*(C2), each its bracket's midpoint (0 where f* is 0), and Stage 9's fraction read from the test, f9: f_t(C2) with the 21.39 s delivery where the test propagated (its value where matched or bracketed, its threshold bracket's midpoint in M50_PT_Exp4, ST_Exp3 and ST_Exp4), f_max at the away node where it did not (the two exploratory values, M50_PT_Exp2's 0.5625 and SP_Exp4's 0.2656, included, as they are the fractions Stage 9 read). C2's further quantities: the away node's peak over 900 s with no delivery (D104 (4)) and the hot spot's crossing at f = 0 in Stage 7's runs, none in eight tests, which rank above every crossing.
(2) C1. For each predictor and each direction (fit on the eight series tests and score the eight parallel ones, then the other way round): one probability for every test and a logistic in the predictor, both fitted by Firth's penalised likelihood (the Jeffreys prior), which keeps the logistic finite where the predictor separates the training half and gives the constant (k + 0.5)/(n + 1); a predictor constant over the training half gives the constant. Scored on the other half by the mean log score (the mean of ln p of each outcome; higher is better) and the Brier score (the mean of (p - y)^2; lower is better). The difference is the logistic's score less the constant's. Its permutation distribution: 10,000 relabellings of the sixteen outcomes (numpy's default generator, seed 20261030), the predictors and the split held; the p-value is the share of relabellings at least as favourable to the logistic as the outcomes themselves, the observed one counted. The 2.5 and 97.5 % points of the distribution are the figure's band.
(3) C2. Spearman's rho (average ranks for ties) of each quantity of (1) with the outcome (sixteen), the measured time (the nine propagated) and E_b (sixteen), each with a two-sided permutation p-value from 10,000 relabellings of the second variable (seed 20261031). The connection: the difference between the series and the parallel tests' mean ranks of each quantity, with a two-sided p-value from 10,000 relabellings of the connection.
(4) C3. For each criterion (C1, C2) and delivery (21.39, 120, 240 and 360 s), the fractions read from the tests: f_t for a propagated test where its search found one (matched, or bracketed and not across a jump, D101 (2)), f_max for a registered non-propagated test, zeros included; the exploratory ones and the tests with no fraction (early, threshold, across a jump, above 1) counted apart. Reported: the ratio of the largest to the smallest non-zero fraction and the share of the sixteen whose fraction is zero; the same for the thresholds f* (Stage 8's, and Part B's under the long deliveries, at their brackets' midpoints).
(5) The scoring of P6 and P7. P6 holds if, for each of the three predictors and both directions, the logistic's mean log score exceeds the constant's by at most 0.1 and neither p-value (log score, Brier score) lies below 0.05. P7 holds if no quantity's p-value with the outcome or with the time lies below 0.05 and no connection test's does. The p-values are reported as they are, with the number of tests; no correction for their number enters the verdicts.

D106. Part D, checks, tests and runs.
(1) Part D (brief): a recorded survey, with its searches, of every published dataset found with per-test outcomes and times for cell-to-cell propagation in cells in direct contact or at a stated spacing, at least Hoelle, Zimmermann and Hinrichsen 2023, Lamb et al. 2015, Said, Lee, Stoliarov and Marshall 2019 and any multi-cell set of the Battery Failure Databank: the cell, the chemistry, the number of tests, the trigger, what is reported (outcome, time, temperatures, histories), the licence or access, and whether it could be run with this study's parameters without inventing any (an LG M50, or a 21700 NMC cell with published thermal and kinetic parameters); the one that qualifies, if any, is marked, and the survey stops there (`results/stage10_survey.md`, with `results/stage10_survey.json` for P8). It runs nothing, reads no parameter from a figure and adopts no other cell's properties. P8 holds if at least one dataset reports per-test outcomes and times for cells in direct contact, and none has a cell this study's parameters cover without an invented value.
(2) Checks (`src/stage10_checks.py`): V1 (D102 (5)); V2, the probes' nodes in every record, as `stage10_inputs.theta_ok` asserts in every run, with their distances from the line; V3, Part C recomputed by separate code (Firth's logistic by root finding on the penalised score with scipy, Spearman's rho by scipy, the permutations with another seed), acceptance 1e-10 in the scores and rho and, for the p-values, four standard errors of the Monte Carlo plus 1/N; V4, Stage 9's V4 on every Stage 10 record (energy accounts within 1e-9 of the heat taken in, every delivery exact or the pulse's integral to the run's end); and X, where a Stage 10 run holds a point that a Stage 7 or Stage 9 record holds, the histories they share agree up to the shorter run's end (acceptance 1e-6 K). Then, before the note's Stage 10 lines, an independent read of every registered number and verdict (brief).
(3) Tests (`src/test_stage10.py`, run before the predictions, all pass): a Stage 10 run equals Stage 9's run of the same point bitwise at the shared probes, the hottest node, the lumped neighbour and the energy accounts (M50_PT_Exp4, f = 0.0375, to 3 s); the probes' nodes at every contact; the history metrics on made-up histories; Part B's chains on made-up peaks; Firth's logistic against a direct maximisation of the penalised likelihood and the constant's closed form; the held-out scores, their permutation p-values and the ranks against scipy on made-up data; and the scorecard's rules on made-up results.
(4) The runs, on the author's laptop with the Python and packages of Stages 5 to 9 (`run_stage10_windows.cmd`, `src/stage10_laptop.py`): one worker per logical processor, at most 24, on one list that grows as Part B's searches advance (`stage10_run.py work`), with Stage 9's claims, its handling of a run recorded since the list was read (D98 (1)) and its time limit (D96 (1)); no speculative midpoints. Before any Stage 10 record the list holds 27 runs (Part B's five runs at f = 0, Part A's sixteen and the six of D104 (4)); its SHA-256 is in the README.

D107. The version (brief, setting). Stage 10 is version 1.6.0: a further GitHub release v1.6.0 and the Zenodo new version of the record (all versions 10.5281/zenodo.23279455), which carries Stages 9 and 10, the v1.5.0 Zenodo version having been held for it; `CITATION.cff` and `.zenodo.json` at v1.6.0; the note's header at the publishing date with the all-versions DOI. The note gains a Stage 10 section of at most eight lines, its earlier text unchanged, which says that one test's history decides between the readings for that test only (brief, pitfalls); every number it quotes comes from `results/stage10_paper_numbers.json` (`src/stage10_numbers.py`, written after the results with the two-panel figure, `src/make_figure_stage10.py`).

## Stage 10, after the runs (30 October)

D108. Written after every Stage 10 run had ended, after the first scoring and the checks' first run, and before the independent read. The records, Part C's first run, the survey and the first scoring were pushed in commit 73c5979, the checks' first run in 9b16a61.
(1) The runs. The launcher started 24 workers at 08:30:39 UTC on 30 October (the stamp of their logs; it writes `results/stage10_laptop_workers.json`, at 08:30:42 UTC, once they run), and the first records began at 08:30:41 UTC. The 27 runs of the list before any record (README, status item 14) and the three that Part B's one open search then asked for were recorded by 08:51:00 UTC, in 156 to 810 s each, none failed: 16 of Part A, 8 of Part B and the 6 of D104 (4). The 85 files of the launcher's zip (the 30 records, the 30 series, the 24 workers' logs and the workers file) were committed unchanged; the zip itself, SHA-256 25d225e08db70299c0fc97cc31ea1f1d42bcc948b7cbc3579613eed6010d895c, is not in the repository. The six records of D104 (4) were also staged from the laptop at 08:44 UTC, as they ended, so that Part C could run while Part B went on; they are byte-identical to the zip's.
(2) Part B's searches. With no delivery the side and facing nodes peak above the measured maximum, or within 5 K of it, in all five tests but one: M50_PT_Exp1's side node peaks at 61.0 C against its 71 C, so its search ran from 0 to f\*_lo(C1), 0.15 (D104 (1)): the node peaks at 159.8 C at f = 0.15, 93.4 C at 0.075 and 66.1 C at 0.0375, within 5 K, so f_max = 0.0375 (matched, 1.05 kJ). Its facing node peaks at 70.9 C at f = 0, within 5 K of 71 C, so f_max = 0 there. In the other four tests the overshoot at f = 0 is 16.6 to 60.0 K at the side node and 65.2 to 159.9 K at the facing node (13.8 to 56.3 K at the Stage 9 node).
(3) V3 and the Firth fit. V3's first run failed: the largest difference between the scoring's held-out scores and V3's own was 1.36e-4, against an acceptance of 1e-10, while each of the six log-score p-values it compared agreed within its Monte Carlo tolerance. In three of the six observed fits (f\*(C1) and f\*(C2) fitted on the series tests, Stage 9's fraction fitted on the parallel ones) the Fisher-scoring steps of `firth` in `src/stage10_partc.py` (D105) had stopped short of the penalised likelihood's maximum: near separation they zig-zag and converge linearly, by about 1.5 % a step, and for f\*(C1) their 200 steps left b at 8.833, where the maximum is at 8.840, with the score's largest component at 2.3e-4; the other two fits were off by about 1e-7. V3's root of the score and a direct maximisation of the penalised likelihood by the simplex agree with each other at that maximum to 1e-8. The estimator is D105's, unchanged; its numerical solution is now finished by Newton steps on the modified score, the gradient of the penalised log-likelihood, with its Jacobian by central differences, halving a step until the score's largest component falls, and a fit counts as converged when that component is at most 1e-10. A test of the case V3 found was added to `src/test_stage10.py`; it passes, with the others. Part C, the scoring and the checks were then run again: every fit, observed and permuted, converged; V3 passes (largest differences 4.9e-15 in the scores and 1.1e-16 in rho); the observed differences moved by at most 1.36e-4 (f\*(C1) fitted on the series tests: -0.12981 for -0.12967 in the mean log score) and the permutation bands by at most 2.7e-5; no p-value, no rank and no verdict changed. The first run's files are those of 73c5979 and 9b16a61.
(4) The quoted numbers and the figure. `src/stage10_numbers.py` writes `results/stage10_paper_numbers.json` and `src/make_figure_stage10.py` Figure 9 (`results/stage10_figure.png` and `.pdf`), both written after the results (D107).
(5) Exploratory, not registered: the comparison of Part A over parts of the window, at the Stage 9 node and the central contact, by D103 (2)'s average (`part_a_segments_exploratory` in the numbers file). From 24.16 to 100 s (the samples from 24.5 s) the short delivery's two ends lie 9.5 and 6.6 K from the record (root mean square) and the long C2 readings 57.7 and 58.1 K; from 100 to 271.50 s the short ends 8.8 and 12.0 K and the long C2 readings 8.9 and 9.1 K; over the last 60 s the short ends 4.8 and 4.4 K and the long C2 readings 3.0 and 2.1 K. The long C1 reading lies 21.4 K from the record after 100 s, and the baseline 30.7 K. So the registered difference between the readings comes from the first 100 s, the rise to 141 C by 45 s, which only the short delivery follows; after that both kinds of reading follow the record about equally, and neither has its last rise: 22.8 K in the last 60 s, of which 9.8 K in the last 3.5 s, from 187.4 C at 268 s.

## Added after the independent read of the Stage 10 records and quoted numbers (2 November)

D110. The independent read (brief; D106 (2)): every registered Stage 10 number and verdict read again before the note's Stage 10 lines were written. A separate agent, which had not seen the work being produced, recomputed them from the measured records, the Stage 10 records and series, the earlier stages' records and files and the survey, with scripts of its own; it ran and imported none of the study's code, read no line of it, and did not run the model. It read commit ca00f6a, first on 30 October from 09:08 UTC and, after the service's usage limit stopped it, from 04:12 to 05:10 UTC on 2 November, the commit and the working tree unchanged throughout.
(1) Every registered number and verdict reproduced: the 30 records' inputs against their ids, none failed, every series finite to its end; Part A's 1,920 fields (16 records at five probes) to 1e-9, and A3; Part B's 15 searches, the eight runs they need being exactly the eight Part B records; Part C's table and exact quantities to 5e-15, and its 32 p-values within D106's Monte Carlo acceptance and within 2.1 standard errors of an exact enumeration of all 11,440 relabellings; V2's 150 placements, V4's 60 energy accounts and 36 deliveries (32 whole and 4 to the run's end), and X's 24 pairs (with six Stage 9 lumped-only records at the same points, which X leaves out, also identical); and 1,900 of the 1,913 entries of the numbers file, all but 21 equal (below). P1, P4, P6 and P7 held; P2, P3, P5 and P8 failed.
(2) The held-out fit's global maximum. Firth's penalised likelihood can have two maxima: in 30 to 86 of the 255 outcome patterns of a training half, by predictor and direction. All six observed fits are at the global maximum, but in 8 patterns a fit started from zero, as D105's and D108's code did, stops at the lower one, in 0.98 % of the relabellings for f\*(C2) in each direction and 1.84 % for Stage 9's fraction fitted on the parallel tests (for f\*(C2) fitted on the parallel tests with outcomes 0, 1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 1, 1, on the predictor's own scale, the start from zero ends at b = -4.5 with a penalised log-likelihood of -6.205, where the maximum is at b = -113.7 with -5.978; on the standardised predictor the fit uses, -4.263 and -4.036, the same gap). So "every fit converged" (D108 (3)) did not make every fit D105's estimator, and V3, also started from zero, could not see it. The fit is now the global maximum (`src/stage10_partc.py`): from the start at 0 and from twelve starts on the standardised slope (plus and minus 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 and 32, each with its best intercept), the converged maximum with the largest penalised likelihood is kept; and since a fit depends only on its training half's predictor and outcomes, each is made once (1,503 distinct fits in all, 8 with a lower maximum from 0, none unconverged). Against a dense profile of the penalised likelihood (the standardised slope every 0.1 from -80 to 80, the intercept maximised at each, then the simplex from the profile's best point), the search finds the maximum in every one of the 256 outcome patterns of each of the six training halves (1,536 fits, the pattern with no propagation, which no relabelling gives, included; largest shortfall 4.0e-15 in the penalised log-likelihood; `src/stage10_profile_check.py`, `results/stage10_profile_check.json`). V3 now finds the global maximum by separate code too (roots of the score by scipy from 17 starts on the predictor's own scale, the largest maximum kept) and compares all 32 p-values, the Brier score's and the ranks' included, where it compared the six of the log score. Part C, the scoring and the checks were run again: no observed score moves (at most 6e-16); three pairs of p-values move, 0.5986 to 0.5942 and 0.6237 to 0.6198 (log and Brier score) for f\*(C2) fitted on the series tests, 0.6183 to 0.6161 and 0.6204 to 0.6182 for it fitted on the parallel ones, and 0.3550 to 0.3511 and 0.3497 to 0.3466 for Stage 9's fraction fitted on the parallel tests; the lower 2.5 % points of those three bands of the log score move by 0.045, 0.040 and 0.033 (-0.467 to -0.512, -0.216 to -0.256, -0.171 to -0.204), and the Brier bands' upper points by at most 0.006; V3 passes (largest differences 4.9e-15 in the scores and 1.1e-16 in rho, every one of the 32 p-values within its tolerance, the largest gap 0.014); no verdict changes.
(3) Corrected after the read, with no registered number or verdict changed: D108's start of the runs (the workers at 08:30:39 UTC, the stamp of their logs, the first records at 08:30:41 UTC; 08:30:42 UTC is when the workers file was written), its account of the stalled fit (b at 8.833 with the score at 2.3e-4, the other two fits about 1e-7 off: the 8.780 and 5e-3 first written were a diagnostic's after 60 steps, not the scoring's after 200), V3's first comparison (six p-values, not each), and the order of a pair; the numbers file's exploratory means by part of the window, now time averages as everywhere else (they had been plain means, up to 0.052 K away, quoted nowhere), V2's distances, now from the angles the records hold (they had used angles rounded to six decimals, 4.2e-8 mm away), and the runs' start, now the logs' stamp, the first record's start and the workers file's time apart; the history file, whose model columns now stop at each run's end instead of holding its last value to 500 s; C3's entry for M50_PP_Exp2's C1 fractions under 240 and 360 s, left out because their brackets straddle a jump (D101 (2)), now named so rather than "bracketed"; and the survey (D106 (1)): seven hosts, not four, could not be read; Read, Jones and Marco's 2 to 5 s are each neighbour's time from the penetration to 132 C at its surface, not to its runaway; the qualifying test turns on the cell, so the texts say that this study's parameters cover the cell, not that the dataset could be run without an invented value; and Lamb et al.'s report presents one result per configuration without stating how many tests were made.
(4) Recorded, not corrected: the windows that start at 24.16 s start at the first sample after it, 24.5 s, the registered one as D103 (2) built it; A3's difference at the end of Section II is taken at 309.72 s, the rounded time, where the figure's is 309.7156 s (at most 4e-4 K apart); at the facing node the five f_max tie at 0, and the scorecard names the last of them (the numbers file lists the tie); on the 0.5 s samples the scoring reads, the measured spike reaches 101.7 C and first passes 100 C at 2.8 s, where D102 read 102.0 C and 2.7 s from the path itself; and Stage 9's fraction's correlation with the trigger's energy has a p-value at 0.05 (0.0508 here, 0.0476 in the read's relabellings, 0.0493 over two million), so the texts give it as about 0.05. ca00f6a also completed the survey's row for Hoelle et al. from D82 (1) (its trigger and stack), which D108 did not record. Exploratory: 72 % of the short delivery's squared difference from the record over the window, at f = 0.0375, lies in Section I (46.3 K root mean square there), 41.2 % in its first 8.5 s, the record's spike at the trigger's runaway, and 30.5 % after it, while the record climbs from 80.0 to 113.3 C ahead of the node; for the long readings 25 to 27 % (D109 (6)).
(5) The read could not open Read, Jones and Marco's full text (its tool's requests for those pages went unanswered), so it confirmed the dataset's six tests, its 3 mm spacing, the nail and the outcomes from the abstract and the records, but not the cell's identity, the times or the maxima. The survey's account of them rests on the full text as read twice on 30 October, whose Section 2.3 names the cell: "Commercially available LG INR21700-M50 cells were used for this study." A re-read on 2 November failed (the repository's server returned errors and the publisher's page refused the fetch). Were that identification to fall, P8's second clause would rest on Read, Mathew and co-authors (2024, an unnamed cell with the M50's 4.85 Ah and 18.20 Wh) and Sun and co-authors (2025, cells named M50, no table of tests), neither counted as covered.

## Added after the independent read, with the texts (2 November)

D109. Written after the independent read and its corrections (D110), with the texts; its number was left for it when D110 was written. Presentation and records. No registered quantity or verdict depends on it.
(1) The quoted numbers. `src/stage10_numbers.py` writes `results/stage10_paper_numbers.json`, which holds every Stage 10 number that the README, the note, the release notes and the Zenodo description quote, but for the commits, the run list's count and hash (`python3 src/stage10_run.py list`, 4d6169b), the independent read's counts (D110), the values of the first runs, from the commits that hold them (V3's first difference, 1.36e-4 or 1.4e-4, at 9b16a61; the p-values before the fit was made the global maximum, at ca00f6a, from which the moves of at most 0.0044 are taken), the survey's figures from the papers it read (`results/stage10_survey.json`), the predictions' own figures (`predictions_stage10.md`, such as P5's condition of 4.9 K) and the scripts' run times ((5) and (6)), which the numbers file does not repeat. It reads the measured records, the Stage 10 records and series, the scoring's, Part C's, the survey's and the checks' files. Where the texts give a p-value of Stage 9's fraction against the trigger's energy, they give it as about 0.05 (D110 (4)).
(2) Figure 9 (`src/make_figure_stage10.py`), the brief's two panels. (a) shows the registered comparison only, the Stage 9 node at the central contact: the measured record to 290 s with the band of its 2.47 K uncertainty, and the six registered readings to 300 s, each with its root mean square difference in the legend; the strip marks the last 60 s before the onset. (b) shows, for each predictor and direction, the observed difference of the mean log scores with its permutation p-value and the 2.5 to 97.5 % band of the 10,000 relabellings, the constant at zero and P6's margin at 0.1.
(3) The note. Its Stage 10 section has seven lines, every Stage 10 number in them in the numbers file, at its precision or rounded from it (the predictions' commit aside), and it says that the one history decides between the readings for that test only (D107). The earlier text is unchanged but for the header's date, now 2 November 2026, the day the release files were made, beside the all-versions DOI; if the version is published on another day the date is set to it and the note rendered again. The note stays at three pages; its stylesheet is unchanged.
(4) Zenodo and the citation. Version 1.6.0's Zenodo version is a new version of the v1.4.0 record (10.5281/zenodo.23589793) and carries Stages 9 and 10, the v1.5.0 Zenodo version having been held for it (D107); v1.5.0 is a GitHub release only (published on 30 October, its tag at e9d1161). The README's citation paragraph says so. `.zenodo.json` and `CITATION.cff` are at v1.6.0 and add, as a reference, Read, Jones and Marco 2024 (doi:10.1016/j.powera.2024.100162), the dataset the survey marks.
(5) The check of D110 (2), from the commit. `src/stage10_profile_check.py`, committed in dff61b3 with D110, was run from that commit and wrote `results/stage10_profile_check.json`: 1,536 patterns, the largest shortfall 4.0e-15, and 8 patterns in which the start at 0 ends at the lower maximum (2 for f\*(C2) on the series tests, 2 on the parallel ones, and 4 for Stage 9's fraction on the parallel ones), the same as the run of the same computation from which D110 (2) was written. Part C run again from dff61b3 in a fresh checkout writes `results/stage10_heldout.json` and `results/stage10_rank.json` byte for byte as committed, in 157 s on one core; the README's run time for it, about 8 minutes from before each distinct fit was made once, is corrected.
(6) A third check, of the texts as committed in dff61b3 and 167fabc, from 05:36 to 06:06 UTC on 2 November: a separate agent, which had not seen them written, read the README, the note, D108 to D110, the Zenodo description, the citation file, the survey and the release notes against the numbers file, the scoring's, Part C's and the checks' files, the records, the series, the measured histories and git, with scripts of its own; it read none of the study's code and ran no model. It found no registered number or verdict wrong, and every verdict and expectation as the scorecard and the predictions have them. Corrected after it, with no registered number or verdict changed:
Precision. D108 (5)'s and the README's 9.2 K is 9.1 K (the long C2 reading over 360 s, from 100 s to the onset); M50_PP_Exp3's record is read to 2.46 K, not 2.47 K (D102 (3)); the two readings agree to 0.0005 K on the neighbours' curves and to 0.0014 and 0.0023 K on the triggers'; of the 36 deliveries 32 are whole and the 4 of the 360 s readings exact to the runs' end at 332 s; the record's 102.0 C is the maximum on the 0.5 s samples (the figure's path reaches 102.08 C at 309.72 s, D102 (3)); D110 (2)'s example is given on the predictor's own scale, and now also on the standardised one.
Scope. Section I holds the record's spike and its climb after it, not the spike alone: of the short delivery's squared difference (f = 0.0375) 41.2 % lies in the first 8.5 s and 30.5 % after it, while the record climbs from 80.0 to 113.3 C ahead of the node (D110 (4) and the README). With no delivery the far face lies below M50_PP_Exp3's record until 57.0 s, by up to 41.1 K at 2.5 s, the record's spike, and above it from 57.5 s on, so it is too warm from the first minute, not over the whole history. Stage 9's fraction does score above the constant in both directions, by 0.062 and 0.025 in the mean log score, though by less than P6's margin and not significantly, so the texts now say that nothing beats the constant by that margin or significantly, where they said that nothing beats it; and "by more than 0.062" becomes "by P6's margin of 0.1 (the largest gain 0.062)". The thresholds f\*(C2) under the long deliveries are Stage 9's (D105 (4)), not Stage 8's. Of the readings run, only the 21.39 s delivery follows the record's rise to 141 C by 45 s, which says nothing of deliveries that were not run. P5's failure was foreseen on a condition, the facing node reading within 4.9 K of the line node, which held (70.9 C at both). X compares the Stage 10 runs with Stage 7's and Stage 9's records, made on the same laptop; two Stage 10 runs also hold points of Stage 8 records made on the cloud machine (M50_PT_Exp1 at f = 0.15 and M50_PT_Exp4 at 0.0125), and agree with them to 1.3e-11 and 2.2e-12 K in the resolved fields, the lumped and the trigger's histories identical, so the texts now say that every run holding a point of a Stage 7 or Stage 9 record repeats it exactly. The survey made 26 web searches and one of the author's own files; of its nine other datasets seven are in cells in contact or at a stated spacing, the records of Hoelle et al. and of Torres-Castro et al. not establishing it. The contact between the cans takes the textbook's steel-on-steel conductances (D36) and the tabs the two-cell study's (D37), where the survey gave the two-cell study's for both. The zip of D108 (1) is not in the repository; its 85 files are.
Wording. A reader cannot resolve "the main chat": the README and the survey now say, as the Zenodo description did, that whether a further stage runs the dataset is decided separately, and every text that the survey marks the qualifying dataset and stops; "takes up three questions" replaces "settles three things"; the read's 8 patterns are 8 of the 1,536 of the six training halves.
The numbers. The quoted Stage 10 numbers that were not in the numbers file are added to it by `src/stage10_numbers.py`: the probes' angles, M50_PT_Exp1's side peaks, the readings' agreement and M50_PP_Exp3's resolution, Section I's split, where the far face lies below M50_PP_Exp3's record, the survey's counts, V3's 32 p-values and their largest gap, and V4's residuals and counts; no number already in the file changes. The values of the first runs and the survey's figures from the papers stay where they are, as (1) now says. The tests were run again at the release's code and pass (`results/stage10_tests.log`); the profile check of (5) takes about 20 minutes (1,174 s for the run from which D110 (2) was written).
(7) A short second pass of the same check, at 4d60486, from 06:19 to 06:22 UTC on 2 November, confirmed every correction of (6) but five, corrected with no registered number or verdict changed: the share of the short delivery's squared difference after the record's spike is 30.5 %, not 31 % (the check's own rounding in its first report; the shares are now given to one decimal, 41.2 % and 30.5 %, in the README, D110 (4) and (6)), and the order of the README's and D110 (4)'s clauses made the split read as the long readings'; in the README's row for the third check, "M50_PP_Exp3's 2.46 K" and "the far face's first minute" replace two phrases that misnamed them; before 57.5 s the record lies above the model from its spike on, not by its spike alone (after it the record climbs from about 61 C to 80 C by 57 s, still 37 K above the model at 20 s and 27 K at 30 s); and P5's condition of 4.9 K and the scripts' run times, quoted in the README and the release notes, are now among (1)'s exceptions. The README's row for the tests now names the scorecard's rules they test, P1 to P3's.

## Stage 11, Part A0, recorded before the Stage 11 predictions (2 November)

D111. Part A0 (brief of 2 November, A0.1 to A0.4), recorded before the Stage 11 predictions are written. Stage 11 (version 1.7.0) asks whether the kinetic posterior and the published thermal ranges could account for three numbers the manuscript rests on: the spread of the fraction a test needs, the far face's overshoot with no delivery, and the whole-cell clock's reproduced times. No Stage 1 to 10 code or result is edited; every input is Stage 10's unless the brief names a change.
(1) A0.1, the kinetic draws. Stage 3's 200 joint draws (D22; `results/stage3_draw_indices.csv`) are ranked, earliest first, by the hottest node's onset at 0.15 K/min in the registered Stage 3 case, 33.2 kJ released over 30 s with 500 mW/K of busbar (case two_E33223_tau30_G0.5, column headline_hottest_onset_s of `results/stage3a_per_draw.csv`, written by Stage 3a). No two draws tie. The brief's ranks 5, 50, 151 and 196 give: rank 5, draw 188 (6.2486 s; columns An_Ele 71, Separator 52, NCM811 3); rank 50, draw 83 (10.2308 s; 6, 15, 254); rank 151, draw 40 (16.0408 s; 37, 44, 366); rank 196, draw 184 (17.0157 s; 20, 70, 145). The brief names them the 2.5th, 25th, 75th and 97.5th percentiles by nearest rank; ranks 151 and 196 are the mirror of 50 and 5 from the top, where the nearest-rank method itself would give ranks 150 and 195 (draws 44 and 71, 16.0252 and 16.9627 s); the brief's ranks are used. The posterior-mean model's onset in that case is 11.48 s (Stage 1), between ranks 50 and 151. For the record: at that onset draws 188 and 83 start by the SEI's decomposition below 100 C (47.1 and 90.1 C), draw 40 by the SEI's at 194.5 C and draw 184 by the cathode's first reaction at 214.4 C; in Stage 3's emulation of the source paper's test (V4) draw 83 lies in the main cluster and draws 188, 40 and 184 have no short.
(2) A0.2, the thermal draws. Of Stage 3's 30 Latin hypercube draws of the roll's published properties (D27; `results/stage3_thermal_draws.csv`), the roll's mass being fixed at 0.056 kg in a fixed volume, so that its volumetric heat capacity orders as its specific heat: the smallest conductivity across the layers, draw 2 (k_radial 1.008361 W/(m K), k_axial 25.352314 W/(m K), c_roll 1030.080 J/(kg K), the cell 62.184 J/K); the largest, draw 1 (1.375499, 25.313078, 1050.285, 63.316 J/K); the smallest specific heat, draw 20 (1.177241, 25.675667, 848.616, 52.022 J/K); the largest, draw 27 (1.196191, 25.416620, 1109.080, 66.608 J/K). No draw is selected twice. The central properties, those of every stage since the two-cell study, are 1.3 and 25 W/(m K) and 61.75 J/K (0.065 kg at 950 J/(kg K)).
(3) A0.3, the tests. Stage 8's A4 set (D79) is M50_PT_Exp1 and M50_SP_Exp3, the smallest and the largest trigger energy, and the three nearest the sixteen tests' median, 45,176.0 J: M50_PP_Exp3 and M50_ST_Exp2, 1,280.6 J away each (the median is their mean), and M50_SP_Exp1. The brief's "the one nearest the median" is therefore a tie; on the author's answer of 2 November both are taken, so Parts A and B run four tests where the brief says three, and the predictions read four where the brief reads three. The four, with Stage 8's thresholds at the central ratio (`results/stage8_thresholds.csv`) and Stage 9's fractions (`results/stage9_fractions.csv`): M50_PT_Exp1 (parallel, E_b 8,976.8 J, E_e 27,875.4 J; not propagated, 71 C): f*(C1) (0.15, 0.1625], f*(C2) (0.45, 0.4625], f_max 0.075; M50_PP_Exp3 (parallel, 43,895.4 J, 136,307.6 J; not propagated, 105 C): f*(C1) (0, 0.0125], f*(C2) (0.0125, 0.025], f_max 0 (56.3 K over); M50_ST_Exp2 (series, 46,456.7 J, 144,261.2 J; not propagated, 133 C): f*(C1) 0, f*(C2) (0.0125, 0.025], f_max 0 (30.4 K over); M50_SP_Exp3 (series, 54,303.7 J, 168,628.5 J; propagated at 210 s): f*(C1) 0, f*(C2) 0, f_t(C2) bracketed in (0, 0.003125], 0.0015755 by interpolation, and Stage 9's run at 0.001575 (that value to six decimals) crossed at 209.3 s.
(4) A0.4, the runs that exist. Stage 7's pertest runs (fresh SEI, posterior mean, central properties, f = 0) of the five low-maximum tests at the low and high contacts: at the low contact all five went to 900 s, and their far-face (away) peaks are read; at the high contact those of M50_PT_Exp1 and M50_PT_Exp3 went to 900 s and are read, while those of M50_PP_Exp3, M50_ST_Exp1 and M50_ST_Exp2 stopped at the hot spot's 255.7 C (at 17.3, 15.0 and 16.3 s) and are made again with the stop lifted, to 900 s, as new runs of Part C. The far-face peaks they already fix, against the measured maxima: at the low contact M50_PT_Exp1 50.06 C (71 C, 20.9 K short), M50_PT_Exp3 100.39 C (118 C, 17.6 K short), M50_PP_Exp3 121.63 C (105 C, 16.6 K over), M50_ST_Exp1 126.96 C (128 C, 1.0 K short) and M50_ST_Exp2 120.15 C (133 C, 12.8 K short); at the high contact M50_PT_Exp1 61.88 C (9.1 K short) and M50_PT_Exp3 137.87 C (19.9 K over). At the central contact and properties the far face's f = 0 peaks are Stage 9's (A3).
(5) Part D's inputs. The nine propagated tests' f_t(C2) at the posterior mean, with the 21.39 s delivery (Stage 9 A2): matched within 10 s, M50_PP_Exp1 0.01875 (crossing 143.5 s, measured 152 s), PP_Exp2 0.046875 (143.9, 147), PP_Exp4 0.0125 (268.9, 275), SP_Exp1 0.01875 (219.0, 223), SP_Exp2 0.025 (148.5, 149), and SP_Exp3 at 0.001575, the fraction of Stage 9's run at its interpolated f_t (209.3, 210); at their threshold bracket's upper end, M50_PT_Exp4 0.040625 (215.6, 272), ST_Exp3 0.234375 (95.1, 177) and ST_Exp4 0.009375 (268.8, 342). So at the posterior mean six of the nine lie within 10 s of the measured time and seven within 60 s (M50_PT_Exp4's 56.4 s early is within 60 s; ST_Exp3's 81.9 s and ST_Exp4's 73.2 s are not); the brief's "six and nine" is read as these counts, six and seven, from the records.

## Stage 11 design, recorded with the predictions before any Stage 11 run (2 November)

D112. Stage 11 (brief of 2 November): the bands the manuscript states. No Stage 1 to 10 code or result is edited. The Stage 11 code is in new files (`src/stage11_inputs.py`, `src/stage11_run.py`, `src/stage11_score.py`, `src/stage11_checks.py`, `src/stage11_laptop.py`, `src/test_stage11.py`, `run_stage11_windows.cmd`; the quoted numbers and Figure 10 follow the results, D117), which import the Stage 5 model and harness and the Stage 7 to 10 code unchanged.
(1) Every run is a Stage 10 run (`stage9_model`'s loops, Stage 9's inputs, the five probes of D102 (4)): one test with its Stage 7 trigger, the central tabs, 25 C, the fresh SEI with the reaction heat fed back one-way, and a fraction f of the test's ejected energy (the central ratio) delivered through the facing half as Stage 8's triangular pulse over 21.39 s, none at f = 0. What Stage 11 adds is the draw: a kinetic draw replaces the posterior-mean kinetics in both neighbours (Stage 3's joint draw by its number, read from the source paper's particle files as Stage 5 read them, `stage3_draws.draw_kinetics`); a thermal draw replaces the roll's central properties.
(2) A thermal draw sets the resolved neighbour's conductivity across and along the layers and its heat capacity (the roll's 0.056 kg at the draw's specific heat, the can's 4.5 J/K held), and the lumped neighbour's heat capacity, the same total, as Stage 3b set both (D27). The trigger's history is computed at the central properties, as Stage 3b held cell 1's history fixed (D27 (g)): its energy was matched to its measured peak at those properties (D65), and the question is the neighbour's response to its own properties. The model's heat capacity is a module constant of the two-cell study's code (`lumped_two_cell.C_CELL`); it is set for the construction of the resolved cell and for the lumped neighbour's loop and restored after, whatever happens (`stage11_run.heat_capacity`).
(3) A run's id names its draw, contact, models, stop, test and fraction, so that two parts that need the same run share it. The records are `results/stage11_runs/`, the histories `results/stage11_series/`.

D113. Parts A and B, the thresholds under the draws (brief, A1, A2, B1, B2; `stage11_run.threshold_state`).
(1) For each of the four tests (D111 (3)), each criterion (C1, the hot spot at 255.7 C; C2, the lumped neighbour at 255.7 C, D35) and each draw (four kinetic at the central properties, Part A; four thermal at the posterior mean, Part B), f* is found by a search that starts from Stage 8's bracket: Stage 8's two ends are run first (or f = 0 alone where Stage 8's f* is 0); where the criterion is met at the upper end and not at the lower, the bracket is Stage 8's; where the threshold leaves the bracket, it is followed by steps of 0.05 from the end it leaves (upward from the upper end, to f = 1 at most, where a test not met at 1 is above 1; downward from the lower end, to f = 0, where a test met at 0 has f* = 0); then the bracket is halved until it is at most 0.02 wide, Stage 8's interval (D78 (2)). As in Stage 8, delivering more is taken never to undo a crossing, and a fraction met below one not met is reported as broken.
(2) f* is its bracket's midpoint (0 where it is 0), as Stage 10 took Stage 8's (D105 (1)); E* = f* E_e, the delivered energy, with the test's ejected energy at the central ratio. The posterior-mean values are Stage 8's (`results/stage8_thresholds.csv`). A2 and B2: for each test and criterion, the smallest and largest f* and E* over the four draws of each kind and the posterior mean.
(3) C1's runs hold both neighbours and stop each at its crossing (as Stage 8's); C2's hold the lumped neighbour alone. A chain reads every Stage 11 record at its point (the crossing is recorded whether or not the run stopped there), so where a C1 point at f = 0 of a low-maximum test under a thermal draw is also a run of Part C1, Part C1's run (to 900 s with the stop lifted) is listed for both.

D114. Part C, the far face (brief, C1 and C2), and the runs E3 needs.
(1) C1: for the five low-maximum tests at f = 0, the far-face (away) node's peak over 900 s with the stop lifted, under each thermal draw at the central contact (20 runs) and at the low and high contacts at the central properties: Stage 7's pertest runs where they went to 900 s (all five at the low contact; M50_PT_Exp1 and M50_PT_Exp3 at the high contact), new runs for the three whose Stage 7 run stopped (D111 (4)). Recorded: the peak, the measured maximum and the overshoot in kelvin, whether the peak exceeds the maximum, and whether f_max stays 0 by Stage 9's rule (the peak at least the maximum less 5 K).
(2) C2: M50_PT_Exp1's f_max at the far face under the thermal draw with the smallest radial conductivity (draw 2, central contact) and at the low contact (central properties), by Stage 9's search (`stage9_search.fmax_state`: from f = 0 to Stage 8's f*_lo(C1), 0.15, within 5 K of 71 C or to a bracket of 0.005), each run to 900 s with the stop lifted. To shorten these, the longest chains, the search's next two midpoints are run beside its next point (and, before the range's end is known, its first midpoint and the two after it): speculative runs, which the search reads only where its rule reaches them.
(3) E3 asks whether any thermal draw or contact puts the largest f_max of the five below the smallest f_t(C2), M50_SP_Exp3's 0.0015755 (Stage 9). Part C's runs answer it where every test's far face at f = 0 comes within 5 K of its maximum (every f_max 0: the groups separate) or where C2's search finds M50_PT_Exp1's f_max; elsewhere a test whose far face falls short by more than 5 K has an f_max above 0 that no listed run fixes. So for each case left undecided, the first such test in the brief's order (M50_PT_Exp1 first) is run at f = 0.0015755 to 900 s: if its far face then still falls short by more than 5 K, its f_max lies above that fraction and the case does not separate the groups; otherwise the next undecided test is run. These runs, added here as Stage 10 added six for Part C (D104 (4)), are E3's only additions; Part E itself runs nothing.

D115. Part D, the clock under the kinetic draws (brief, D1 and D2). For each of the nine propagated tests at its posterior-mean f_t(C2) with the 21.39 s delivery (D111 (5)), under each kinetic draw: the lumped neighbour alone, stopped at its crossing of 255.7 C or run to 900 s. Recorded: the crossing or none, its shift from the posterior-mean crossing at the same fraction (Stage 9's record there) and from the measured time. D2: under each draw, the number of the nine within 10 s and within 60 s of the measured time, beside the posterior mean's six and seven (D111 (5)).

D116. Part E, the scorecard, the checks, the tests and the runs.
(1) Part E (no runs). E1: for C2 and for C1, under each of the eight draws, the range of E* across the four tests (the largest less the smallest), the largest within-test band of E* (the largest less the smallest of a test's nine values, the eight draws and the mean, over the four tests), and their ratio. E2: for each criterion, each test's within-test factor, the largest f* over the smallest non-zero f* of its nine values, and the largest over the four tests, W, set against the spread of Stage 10's C3 under the same criterion (the ratio of the largest to the smallest non-zero fraction read from the tests, for each delivery: under C2 47.6 with the 21.39 s delivery and 42.2 to 208.1 over all four): its share is reported as ln W / ln S, the part of the spread's logarithm the within-test factor spans. E3: D114 (3), per thermal draw and per contact. E4: Spearman's rho of the trigger's body energy E_b with the outcome (sixteen tests) and with the nine measured times, with Stage 10's average ranks and two-sided permutation p-value (10,000 relabellings, seed 20261102), beside the rho of each model quantity with E_b from Stage 10's rank file.
(2) The scorecard (`src/stage11_score.py`). P1: each of the sixteen pairs of test and kinetic draw has f*(C2) with 0.5 m <= f* <= 2 m, m the posterior-mean value, or f* <= 0.025 where m = 0; a threshold above 1 or a broken chain fails it. P2: the same for f*(C1). P3: under each of the eight draws, the range of E*(C2) across the four tests exceeds E1's largest within-test band. P4: in each of the four tests whose f_max is 0 at the posterior mean (M50_PT_Exp3, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp2), the far face at f = 0 peaks above the measured maximum under at least three of the four thermal draws, and at the low contact and at the high contact. P5: in each of the four tests, the band of f*(C2) over the four thermal draws (the largest less the smallest) is narrower than over the four kinetic draws; equal bands, both zero included, are not narrower. P6: in at least two of the six tests whose time the posterior mean reproduced within 10 s, at least one kinetic draw either gives no crossing by 900 s or moves the crossing by more than 60 s from the posterior mean's. A threshold above 1 enters E1, E2, P3 and P5 as an unbounded value: a band that holds one is unbounded, so P3 then fails, and a test's thermal band is narrower than its kinetic band only if the kinetic band is unbounded and the thermal one is not, or both are bounded and the thermal one is the smaller. The brief's "three tests" read "four tests" throughout (D111 (3)).
(3) The tests (`src/test_stage11.py`, run before the predictions, all pass): a Stage 11 run at the posterior mean and the central properties equals Stage 10's run of the same point bitwise (M50_PT_Exp4, f = 0.0375, to 3 s), and its lumped-only run equals that run's lumped neighbour; each thermal draw's cell carries its heat capacity and conductivities, the trigger's history is the central one, and the module's heat capacity is restored after a run and after a failure; at the central properties the context's model is the published one, bitwise; draw 188's kinetics are Stage 3's draw of its three columns, and a short run with it closes its energy accounts and differs from the mean's; the search of D113 on made-up thresholds; every chain to its end through the work loop with a made-up run in place of the model, and from those records every output of the scoring, P1 to P6 and E1 by hand, and V3. While the code was tested, before these predictions, runs with a draw were made: short ones, to 2 and 3 s, with thermal draw 20 and kinetic draw 188 (no registered quantity), and one in full, a Part D run, M50_PT_Exp4 at 0.040625 under draw 188, whose lumped neighbour crossed at 191.0 s, against 215.6 s at the posterior mean; M50_PT_Exp4 is not among the six tests P6 counts. It is recorded here and in the predictions, and the run is made again in the list like every other.
(4) The checks (`src/stage11_checks.py`): V1, the draw selection reproduced by separate code from Stage 3a's per-case onsets and from the thermal draws' file with numpy (run before the predictions: it passes); V2, the energy accounts of every Stage 11 record under 1e-9 of the heat taken in and every delivery exact to the run's end (Stage 9's V4); V3, Part E recomputed by separate code from the run records (its own brackets from the records, the ejected energies from Stage 8's file, scipy's Spearman with the same relabellings), acceptance 1e-10; V4, three earlier records repeated at the posterior mean on the laptop through Stage 11's code (Stage 8's run of M50_PP_Exp3 at 0.0125, both neighbours stopped at their crossings; Stage 8's lumped run of M50_PT_Exp1 at 0.4625; Stage 9's run of M50_SP_Exp3 at 0.001575 to 900 s), acceptance 1e-6 s in the crossings and 1e-6 K in the peaks and in every history they share. Then, before the note's Stage 11 lines and the manuscript's sensitivity paragraph, an independent read of every registered number and verdict (brief).
(5) The runs, on the author's laptop with the Python and packages of Stages 5 to 10 (`run_stage11_windows.cmd`, `src/stage11_laptop.py`): one worker per logical processor, at most 24, on one list that grows as the chains advance (`stage11_run.py work`), with Stage 9's claims, its handling of a run recorded since the list was read (D98 (1)) and its time limit (D96 (1)). The list's order: C2's searches, the lumped-only runs (C2's thresholds and Part D), C1's thresholds, Part C1's runs, E3's runs as they become due, V4's three. Before any Stage 11 record it holds 162 runs; its SHA-256 is in the README.

D117. The version and the outputs (brief, setting and outputs). Stage 11 is version 1.7.0: a further GitHub release v1.7.0 and a Zenodo new version of the record (all versions 10.5281/zenodo.23279455), `CITATION.cff` and `.zenodo.json` at v1.7.0, the note's header at the publishing date. From v1.7.0 the note's header, the README's first line and the Zenodo description say "A pre-registered study", not "pilot". The note gains a Stage 11 section of at most six lines, its earlier text unchanged but for its header; every Stage 11 number it quotes comes from `results/stage11_paper_numbers.json` (`src/stage11_numbers.py`, written after the results with Figure 10, `src/make_figure_stage11.py`: the bands of f* per test and draw, and the far-face overshoot per draw and contact). The manuscript's own decisions follow in their entries.

## Stage 11, after the runs (2 November)

D118. Written after every Stage 11 run had ended and after the scoring and the checks. No registered quantity or verdict depends on it.
(1) The runs. 216 on the author's laptop by 24 workers (`results/stage11_laptop_workers.json`), the first started at 11:59:07 UTC and the last record written at 12:29:49 UTC (`results/stage11_logs/`); none failed. One worker, worker 21, stopped at 11:59:08 UTC after three runs, on a PermissionError while creating a claim file (its log); the other 213 runs were made by the other 23, and no record is affected (corrected after the independent read, D119 (5) and (6)). The list held 162 runs before any record (D116 (5)); the chains of D113 and D114 and the decisive runs of D114 (3) added the rest by their rules, among them four of E3's decisive runs, at the smallest f_t for M50_PT_Exp1 under the largest radial conductivity, the smallest and the largest heat capacity and at the high contact (corrected, D119 (1)). The records, their series and the workers' logs came back in the launcher's zip (`results_stage11_laptop.zip`, 457 files) and are in `results/stage11_runs/`, `results/stage11_series/` and `results/stage11_logs/` as written. While the runs went, copies of the records were read from the laptop to watch the chains end, with a helper outside the repository, and once, at 178 of the 216 records, the scoring was run on such a copy into a scratch folder to test Figure 10's script; it printed its verdicts on those partial records, which were seen. No code, rule, input or run of Stage 11 was changed after that, and the registered verdicts are those of the scoring run on the complete records.
(2) The scoring (`src/stage11_score.py`) and the checks (`src/stage11_checks.py`). P1 failed, P2 held, P3 held, P4 failed, P5 failed, P6 failed (`results/stage11_scorecard.json`). V1 to V4 pass (`results/stage11_checks.json`): the draws reproduced; the largest energy residual 6.1e-12 (resolved) and 3.6e-12 (lumped) of the heat taken in, every delivery exact; Part E recomputed to 5.6e-17; the three earlier records repeated, the cloud machine's Stage 8 record and the laptop's repeat differing by at most 1.0232e-12 K in the peaks, 2.2169e-12 K in every history they share and 8.5265e-14 s in the crossing, and the other two exactly (the values in full, D120 (1)); the histories were compared after the independent read found that the check had looked for them in the wrong folders (corrected, D119 (2) to (4)).
(3) How two verdicts read, for the texts. P1 fails on one test only, M50_ST_Exp2, under draws 188 and 83 (ranks 5 and 50): their bracket of f*(C2), (0, 0.0125], lies one step of the search below the posterior mean's, (0.0125, 0.025], so that their midpoints, the registered values, differ threefold (0.00625 against 0.01875), while the brackets themselves would allow any ratio below 1; at the brief's resolution of 0.02 the verdict rests on the midpoints, as D113 (2) fixed. P6 fails because in five of the six tests the four kinetic draws move the lumped neighbour's crossing at the same fraction by 1.5 to 56.3 s, under the 60 s it asks for, and only in M50_PP_Exp4 do the two draws whose hottest node starts latest (ranks 151 and 196) leave it uncrossed within 900 s. The agreement within 10 s is less stable than the crossings: two to four of the nine tests under the draws, against six at the posterior mean.
(4) A sentence of the brief that the result does not carry. The brief's pitfalls say: "Where P6 fails, the knife edge is the result, and the sentence is that the time a near-threshold delivery reproduces is not a stable prediction of the model but a coincidence of fraction and kinetics". P6's own statement makes the knife edge its holding ("the reproduced time sits on the knife edge Stage 9 found in f, and the kinetics move the edge"), and P6 failed: the kinetic draws do not move the edge by more than 60 s in five of the six tests. The texts therefore do not write that sentence; they state what the runs show, the 10 s agreement falling from six tests to two to four and the four tests at or near their threshold left uncrossed by the two later draws, and leave the reading of the pitfall to the author.
(5) Exploratory, not registered: f*(C1) is the posterior mean's bracket under all four kinetic draws in all four tests, the hot spot's threshold being set by conduction and the delivery at the search's resolution; under the smallest radial conductivity and the smallest heat capacity M50_PT_Exp1's f*(C1) falls to (0.1375, 0.15] and M50_PP_Exp3's to 0. M50_PT_Exp1's f_max stays at 0.075 under the smallest radial conductivity and rises to 0.09375 at the low contact; its far face lies 8.4 to 20.9 K below its 71 C in every case. E3: the fractions do not separate the groups under any thermal draw or contact bracket.
(6) The outputs written after the results: `src/stage11_numbers.py` (`results/stage11_paper_numbers.json`), the file from which every Stage 11 number in the texts is written, and Figure 10 (`src/make_figure_stage11.py`, `results/stage11_figure.png` and `.pdf`).

D119. The independent read (brief, checks; D116 (4)): every registered Stage 11 number and verdict read again, before the note's Stage 11 lines and the manuscript's sensitivity paragraph were written, by a separate agent with scripts of its own from the 216 raw records, Stage 3's, Stage 7's, Stage 8's, Stage 9's and Stage 10's files and the decisions (its scripts and outputs in the session's scratch folder, not in the repository). It found no discrepancy in a registered number or verdict: the draws, every record's inputs and accounts, the 64 chains and their 216 band entries, the 35 rows of Part C1, C2's two f_max, the 36 rows of Part D and their counts, E1 to E4, P1 to P6 (the same six verdicts) and the 881 entries of `results/stage11_paper_numbers.json` agree, and its own reconstruction of the run list before any record gives the README's hash. What it found, and what was done:
(1) D118 (1) counted three of E3's decisive runs; the records hold four, the fourth under the largest radial conductivity (the far face of M50_PT_Exp1 at 60.19 C, 10.8 K short of its 71 C). Corrected in place.
(2) D118 (2) gave the repeats' crossing difference as 8.6e-14 s; it is 8.5265e-14 s. Corrected in place.
(3) D118 (2) gave 1.1e-12 K as the repeats' agreement; that holds for the peaks (1.0232e-12 K), and the histories the cloud machine's record and its laptop repeat share differ by up to 2.2169e-12 K. Corrected in place.
(4) V4 never compared the histories: `src/stage11_checks.py` looked for the earlier series in `results/stage8_runs/` and `results/stage9_runs/`, which hold no series. The check now reads them from `results/stage8_series/` and `results/stage9_series/`: the largest difference is 2.2e-12 K, against the acceptance of 1e-6 K, and V4 passes as before.
(5) D118 (1) did not say that worker 21 stopped after three runs (a PermissionError while creating a claim file, at 11:59:08 UTC). Added in place; no record is affected.
(6) D118 (1) gave 12:29:59 UTC as the last record's time; that is a worker's last line, and the last record was written at 12:29:49 UTC. Corrected in place.
(7) V2's "duplicate_points" read n = 0 because the check was called without the point list, so the points held by two records were not compared. The check now compares them: 24 points are held by two records (22 for the lumped neighbour, 2 for the resolved), and every pair agrees exactly.
(8) E2's factor for M50_SP_Exp3 under C2 was left empty where D116 (1)'s words, the largest over the smallest non-zero value, give 1.0 (one non-zero value, 0.00625 under the largest heat capacity). The scoring and V3 now give 1.0; the largest factor, 5.0 (M50_PP_Exp3), and the shares are unchanged.
(9) Cosmetic: the numbers file stored the draws' integers as decimals; it now stores integers.
Readings the checker recorded, none changing a number:
(a) D113 (1) and (3): a chain reads every record at its point, so a run made for another part can change which fractions it asks for next. In M50_PT_Exp1's C1 chain under the smallest radial conductivity this added runs at 0.075 (with the stop) and 0.13125, which D113 (1)'s sequence alone never calls for; the chain closed at (0.1375, 0.15], as the sequence gives, because the run at 0.1375 had started before the one at 0.13125 ended, and in the other order it would have closed at (0.13125, 0.15]. A registered value could therefore depend on the order of runs; here none did: all 64 chains give identical results by both readings.
(b) P1 rests on the midpoint rule at the search's resolution (D118 (3)); P6's margin is 3.7 s (M50_SP_Exp1's largest shift, 56.29 s, against the 60 s it asks for); at the low contact M50_ST_Exp1's far face peaks 1.04 K below its maximum, so its f_max stays 0 by Stage 9's 5 K rule while P4's strict "above the maximum" fails there, as the outputs read it.
(c) E4's time test drew from the same generator as its outcome test; a separate generator with the same seed gives 0.8761 for 0.8773. By full enumeration the p-values are 0.2105 (2,408 of 11,440 relabellings) and 0.8801 (319,368 of 362,880), both within the Monte Carlo error of the registered values; `src/stage11_numbers.py` now records them.
(d) The two late-onset draws' uncrossed runs in Part D are not cut off by the run's end: the lumped neighbour peaks at 226.7 to 240.8 C between 82.4 and 294.9 s and cools; recorded in the numbers file.
The scoring, the checks and the numbers were run again after (4), (7), (8) and (9); every verdict and every registered number is unchanged.

D120. The Stage 11 texts (brief, outputs), written after the independent read (D119) from `results/stage11_paper_numbers.json`, `results/stage11_scorecard.json` and the decisions.
(1) D118 (2) gave the repeats' agreement as "within 1.1e-12 K in the peaks, 2.3e-12 K in every history they share and 8.5e-14 s". After D119 (2) the last of these was a bound that its own value, 8.5265e-14 s, exceeds, the error D101 (8) records for Stage 9. D118 (2) now gives the three largest differences in full (1.0232e-12 K, 2.2169e-12 K and 8.5265e-14 s), and the texts give them as largest values, not bounds; the same holds for the energy residuals (6.1288e-12 and 3.5567e-12, quoted as the largest, 6.1e-12 and 3.6e-12).
(2) From v1.7.0 the note's header, the README's first line and the description in `.zenodo.json` say "A pre-registered study" (brief); `.zenodo.json` had said "A pre-registered pilot study". The two-cell study's own title, which contains the word, is cited as its record gives it, and the folder of its repository keeps its name in the README's commands.
(3) The note gains a Stage 11 section of five items; no earlier line of the note changes, and it stays three pages. The README gains the Stage 11 section (items 68 to 73, the scorecard and Figure 10), the status items, the verification rows, the contents, the running commands and the run times; `.zenodo.json` gains Stage 11 in the summary, the results, the scorecards, the checks, the limits and the files, at v1.7.0.
(4) Wording kept to the files: the bands are called bands over four draws a side, never posterior intervals; Stage 3's 200-draw band of the onset lead is the posterior band the study has (brief, pitfalls). P6's knife edge did not appear in five of the six tests, and the texts say so rather than the pitfall's expected knife edge (D118 (4)). E4's p-values are the registered permutation values, 0.21 and 0.88, which the exact enumeration of D119 (c) gives to the same two places.

D121. The manuscript (brief, Part M): its form, its references and checks, the separate reviewer's read, and the changes that read led to.
(1) Form (M1). LaTeX, elsarticle in its preprint option, single column, line numbers on, figures embedded and kept as files (`manuscript/figures/`, by `src/make_manuscript_figures.py`; tables by `src/make_manuscript_tables.py`). After the changes of (6): abstract 198 words; five highlights of 79 to 84 characters; seven keywords; main text 7,450 words by `manuscript/word_count.py` (Introduction to Conclusions, without the abstract, figures, tables, declarations and references); eight figures and four tables; the supplement 20 pages. Three candidate titles are in `manuscript/titles.md`, the first preferred; the choice is the author's.
(2) References (M1, M6). 25 entries in `manuscript/references.bib`, each verified on the page `manuscript/references_verified.json` names, in one numeric style. Gaitonde et al. 2017 stays out (its pages could not be verified). The two-cell study is cited by its v1.0.0 Zenodo record, read on 2 November, whose own title contains the word "pilot": it is cited as published, and the text says it is the author's own record, not peer reviewed. The study's archive is cited by its all-versions DOI with the version named (1.7.0); a version's own DOI exists only once its Zenodo draft is made, and can replace it then. The contact conductances are cited at Table 2.1 of the textbook's 6th edition, where D36 (2) read them on 21 October; Bergman et al.'s textbook was verified from a library catalogue record and a bookseller's listing. The comparators' numbers and conditions are in `manuscript/data/comparators.md` and `comparators.csv`, from the pages read; several papers could be read in their abstracts only, which those files and the text respect.
(3) The claims and their scope (M2). The brief's claim that "conduction and radiation alone ignite the neighbour in no run across the posterior and the published thermal ranges" is stated for body energies up to the Databank's largest, 33.2 kJ, with the cans in contact, under every kinetic draw: no Stage 5 or Stage 6 run varied the roll's thermal properties, with each test's fitted trigger conduction alone takes the hot spot to 255.7 C in eight tests (Stage 7), and in the two-cell study's busbar geometry the hot spot reaches it at 19.2 s (Stage 2). "The whole-cell clock predicts when but rarely whether" is stated as "can match the times but predicts few propagations"; "the local criterion predicts which tests propagate better than any other but never when" as "gets the most outcomes right but almost none of the times" (C6, its aged-SEI counterpart, ties at 11 of 16, and with the 240 and 360 s deliveries C1 reproduces one and two of the nine times); "followed by a short delivery near its threshold and by no long one" as "followed most closely by a short delivery near its threshold" (after 100 s the readings follow the record about equally). The foreseen count follows the rule stated in the supplement (S2): 21 of Stages 1 to 10's 32 failures and one of Stage 11's four, 22 of 36, with 14 not foreseen; the stages' own texts label 17 of the first 21.
(4) The numbers check (M7). `manuscript/numbers_check.py` traces every number of the main text, its tables and the supplement to a numbers or results file, with the unit changes the texts use, and lists up to four matching files for each; after (6), 1,050 numbers are found and none is missing (`manuscript/numbers_check.log`, run with `--supplement`). The quantities the manuscript states that no stage registered (the spread in energy, the trigger energies against the Databank's, the unforeseen count) are computed by `src/manuscript_numbers.py` into `results/manuscript_numbers.json` under "after_review", which also records, with its source, the 178 records of D118 (1).
(5) The reviewer's read (M7). A separate agent that had not seen the drafting read `main.pdf` and `supplement.pdf` as committed in e400a1a, as a reviewer for the journal, with scripts of its own and none of the study's code, and checked the manuscript's claims against the results files (`manuscript/review_report.md`, kept as written). It recommended major revision: the study sound and reproducible, the headline claims stated more broadly than the Results, the spread between tests fragile and resting on fitted triggers larger than the calorimetry, numbers attributed to the wrong node, quantity or condition, and comparator statements beyond their sources. Every finding was checked against the files before any change, and every number it gave was confirmed.
(6) Changed after the read:
- the abstract, the highlights, the Introduction's statement of the findings, five headings and the Conclusions, scoped as in (3), with D109 (6)'s "by the registered margin" wherever nothing beats one probability for every test, and the draws' "at most fivefold" stated for the non-zero thresholds of four tests (under the heat capacity's ends M50_ST_Exp2's falls to 0 and SP_Exp3's rises from 0);
- the spread defined: the largest non-zero fraction over the smallest, four tests at zero, M50_SP_Exp3's the smallest in every reading (0.0013 to 0.0031) and PT_Exp1's or ST_Exp3's the largest, PT_Exp1 and SP_Exp3 being two of the four tests Fransson et al. mark "cell movement"; 22- to 64-fold in energy; f*(C2) itself 73- to 109-fold among the tests that need a delivery; the fourth highlight names the share, not the energy;
- the trigger: the fitted body energies exceed the Databank's largest body energy for this cell (33.2 kJ) in 13 of the 16 tests, and the ejected energies its largest ejected energy (89.3 kJ) in 14; the eight tests in which conduction alone ignites the hot spot are those with the largest fitted triggers (46.5 to 54.3 kJ); stated in the Methods, the Results and the Limits;
- numbers attributed to the wrong node or condition: the side and facing overshoot is 16.6 to 159.9 K (it read 13.8); the delays the clock reproduces run 147 to 275 s (it read 342); the reaction heat moves the band entry by at most 0.02 s, from 19.24 to 19.23 s (it read 0.01 s); 108.4 C holds at the posterior mean (over the draws the onset runs from 45.2 to 216.6 C, and 37 draws start with a cathode reaction); at 107 kJ the lumped neighbour crosses too, at 40 to 180 s; M50_PP_Exp4's lost crossing is named beside the 56.3 s;
- the gas-layer bracket: in its runs the can reaches 1,886 to 1,924 C, so it shows the layer's direction, not its size;
- the record (Section 2.10): the Stage 11 run made before the predictions while the code was tested (D116 (3)) and the partial scoring whose verdicts were seen (D118 (1)); the convergence checks belong to the stages that changed the configuration, Stages 10 and 11 running Stage 9's; the separate reads, from Stage 5 on, were made by another instance of the same model, an internal check and not an independent review; the AI model, its developer, the interface and the dates of use; Section 3.9 says "predicted" where it said "expected", since several of those predictions were recorded as uncertain or as able to fail, and names Stage 4's prediction as the central half of the anode-electrolyte posterior;
- the comparators: the Databank's 19 to 31 % marked as computed from its rows; Feng et al.'s 75 % as one of four design changes that stop propagation in their model; Said et al.'s chemical heat as computed and the flaming heat as from their tests in air; the Torres-Castro comparison as for a different cell and stack; the opening sentence as calorimetry of cylindrical cells, without a common basis; Mishra and Jain cited for radiation and convection only; the gas layer as formed by the vaporising electrolyte; `references_verified.json` reconciled with `comparators.md` for Read, Jones and Marco and with D82 for Hoelle et al., and Torres-Castro et al. added to `comparators.md`;
- the pack-model paragraph framed as implications that no run tested, without "the local criterion for whether", which the held-out scores do not support;
