- the declarations: the generative-AI declaration covers the writing (the text, and the scripts that draw the figures and tables), the research uses are in Section 2.10, and no generative image tool was used; the CRediT statement lists the author's own roles. Both are drafted for the author's approval;
- clarity: a paragraph of terms in the Methods; Fransson et al. named where "the paper" stood; the four reports a test should give listed as four; Table 1 gains Fransson et al.'s failure category; the Limits gain the trigger's fit, the sidewall ruptures, the "cell movement" tests, the contact conductance's source and the unreported angle, the submodels' independence, the SEI's decomposition at 25 C (exploratory) and the rank of the draws in another geometry; the supplement cites the four works of its survey table and the decisions through D121.
- outside the manuscript: README item 73 says "non-zero"; the note's and `.zenodo.json`'s Stage 11 lines say which tests each part ran; the README's Stage 11 introduction says "with low maxima".
Not changed, with the reason: the Results' order, which the brief sets (one subsection per claim, in the claims' order); a nomenclature table (a paragraph of terms instead, inside the word limit); the version DOI (made only with the Zenodo draft); a sensitivity case with the trigger held to the Databank's energies (new runs, a stage of its own with its own predictions); Figures 3b and 5b (their clustering far from the line of equality is what they show); the corresponding e-mail (added at submission, not in the public repository); and the sentence naming the module study as the next question, which the brief requires.

D122. Part A0 (brief of 3 November, A0.1), recorded before the Stage 12 predictions are written. Stage 12 (version 1.8.0) holds the trigger's energies to what calorimetry measured for this cell: the body energy capped, and the ejected energy fixed. No Stage 1 to 11 code or result is edited.
(1) The cap. The largest body energy among the ten LG M50 nail tests of D53 (rows 9 to 18 of `inputs/databank/databank_m50_used.csv`, the header being row 1) is RUN195's, row 15 (SPR2021_ESTA_8B100-01_SOC_RUN195), Energy-Fraction-Cell-Body-kJ 33.2229118228995; it is also the largest of the file's seventeen rows. Stages 5 and 6 used it as 33,222.9 J, the two-cell study's rounding (D38, D53), and Stage 12 caps the trigger there, so that its runs share points with Stage 6's.
(2) The fixed ejected energy. Each nail test's ejected energy is Energy-Fraction-Positive-Ejecta-kJ plus Energy-Fraction-Negative-Ejecta-kJ: row 9 (ESRF18_Oct_Run063) 76.2513, row 10 (Run065) 79.2322, row 11 (Run067) 77.2313, row 12 (DLS19_Feb_Run045) 64.1598, row 13 (Run047) 75.0361, row 14 (RUN194) 73.3780, row 15 (RUN195) 76.1468, row 16 (RUN196) 83.4493, row 17 (RUN197) 89.3378 and row 18 (RUN198) 84.7238 kJ. Their median, the mean of the fifth and the sixth in order, Run063's and Run067's, is 76,741.25 J (76.74125365663527 kJ); the smallest is Run045's 64.16 kJ and the largest RUN197's 89.34 kJ, the one D121 quotes.
(3) The eight tests whose hot spot reaches 255.7 C with no delivery (C1 at f = 0, central contact, Stage 7; `results/stage7_paper_numbers.json`, part_b.C1.central.predicted_tests), with their fitted body energies (`results/stage7_triggers.csv`): M50_ST_Exp2 46,456.7 J, SP_Exp1 46,811.8, PP_Exp4 46,860.8, SP_Exp2 48,734.7, ST_Exp4 48,805.7, PP_Exp1 51,085.4, ST_Exp1 51,105.7 and SP_Exp3 54,303.7 J (46.5 to 54.3 kJ); six in series and two in parallel (M50_PP_Exp4 and PP_Exp1). The other eight tests' fitted energies run from 8,976.8 to 43,895.4 J.
(4) The four Stage 11 tests with their Stage 8 brackets at the posterior mean and the central contact (`stage9_run.fstar8`) and their ejected energies r E_b (`results/stage9_fractions.csv`): M50_PT_Exp1, f*(C1) (0.15, 0.1625], f*(C2) (0.45, 0.4625], 27,875.4 J; M50_PP_Exp3, (0, 0.0125] and (0.0125, 0.025], 136,307.6 J; M50_ST_Exp2, 0 and (0.0125, 0.025], 144,261.2 J; M50_SP_Exp3, 0 and 0, 168,628.5 J.
(5) The nine propagated tests' Stage 9 searches with the 21.39 s delivery (A2, C2; `results/stage9_states.json`, `results/stage9_fractions.csv`): matched in M50_PP_Exp1 (0.01875, 2,974.4 J), PP_Exp2 (0.046875, 5,962.4 J), PP_Exp4 (0.0125, 1,819.0 J), SP_Exp1 (0.01875, 2,725.6 J) and SP_Exp2 (0.025, 3,783.4 J); bracketed in SP_Exp3, (0, 0.003125], 221.5 and 198.7 s at its ends, interpolated to 0.0015755 (265.7 J); at the threshold in PT_Exp4, (0.0375, 0.040625], ST_Exp3, (0.23125, 0.234375], and ST_Exp4, (0.00625, 0.009375]. The registered f_max of the non-propagated tests: M50_PT_Exp1 0.075 (2,090.7 J), and 0 in PT_Exp3, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp1 and ST_Exp2; PT_Exp2's and SP_Exp4's are exploratory.
(6) What the records already hold. With E_b capped, a run of Stage 7's form at f = 0 depends on the test only through its connection: the release (21.39 s), the tabs, the contact, the kinetics and the SEI are common to every test, and nothing else of the test enters a run with no delivery. So the eight tests fall on two points per contact, series and parallel. Stage 6 holds both at the central contact, at the posterior mean, the central tabs, 33,222.9 J and 21.39 s (`results/stage6_runs/body_mean_series_hcentral_gcentral_tau21.39_E33.22.json` and `..._parallel_...`, its central matched case, D54 (3)): neither neighbour reaches 255.7 C; the hot spot peaks at 192.16 C (series) and 191.90 C (parallel), the lumped neighbour at 159.76 and 161.75 C. At the high contact Stage 6 holds both connections with the low and the high tabs, not the central ones: the hot spot peaks at 226.14 to 226.75 C and the lumped neighbour at 175.52 to 178.76 C, and neither crosses.
(7) The delivery of Stages 8 to 11 is a triangular pulse of energy f E_e over 21.39 s through the facing half (D77 (4)); a run depends on f and E_e only through their product, the delivered energy. A fraction of a fixed E_e therefore names the same runs as a fraction of r E_b at the same energy: the thresholds and the fractions read from the tests keep their energies, and only their expression as a fraction changes.
(8) The cap is a sensitivity, not a correction (brief, pitfalls): the fitted triggers reproduce the measured trigger peaks, which the tests support; the capped trigger reproduces the calorimetry's largest body energy, which it does not reach in the tests' peaks (Stage 6: 526.1 to 536.9 C at 33.2 kJ over the measured releases, against measured peaks of 164 to 832 C).

D123. Stage 12's design (brief of 3 November), recorded with the predictions (`predictions_stage12.md`) before any Stage 12 run. The code is in new files (`src/stage12_inputs.py`, `src/stage12_run.py`, `src/test_stage12.py`; after the runs, the scoring, the checks and the quoted numbers); no Stage 1 to 11 code or result is edited.
(1) Part A. One run per point of D122 (6): series and parallel, at the central contact (A1) and at the high contact (A2), with the central tabs, the posterior mean, the central properties, the fresh SEI, 33,222.9 J released over 21.39 s and no delivery, both neighbours with Stage 10's probes, to 900 s with the stop lifted (`stage11_run.run_one`). Each of the eight tests is scored from its connection's run; the brief's eight runs per contact would be eight copies of two runs. The two central-contact runs repeat Stage 6's records of the same points with the Stage 9 to 11 loops, and check X compares them.
(2) B1. For each of the four Stage 11 tests and each criterion, f* as a fraction of the fixed ejected energy E_fix = 76,741.25 J. The search starts from Stage 8's bracket, taken as the energies it holds, (lo r E_b, hi r E_b], halves it in energy until its width is at most 0.02 E_fix (1,534.8 J), and reads each new point from a run: C1's runs hold both neighbours and stop at their crossings, C2's hold the lumped neighbour alone (as in Stage 11). A zero stays zero. f*, as a fraction of E_fix, is the bracket's midpoint energy over E_fix, and E* = f* E_fix is reported beside Stage 8's E* (its bracket's midpoint times r E_b). A run is specified by its fraction of r E_b at the energy asked for, so that it names the same point as any earlier record at that energy.
(3) B2. For each of the nine propagated tests, f_t(C2) as a fraction of E_fix. A search Stage 9 ended matched stays matched (its run came within 10 s); a bracketed or threshold one continues from Stage 9's bracket by Stage 9's bisection (a crossing before the measured time moves the upper end; none, or a later one, the lower), until the width is at most 0.005 E_fix (383.7 J) or a run comes within 10 s of the measured time. A bracketed result is interpolated as in Stage 9 (`stage9_search.interp_t`). Lumped neighbour only, stopping at its crossing.
(4) Part C and the scoring. Part C1: the spread of the fractions read from the tests with the 21.39 s delivery by Stage 10 C3's rule (f_t(C2) where matched or bracketed, the registered f_max of the non-propagated tests, the two exploratory maxima apart, the zeros counted apart), each as a fraction of E_fix; with one E_fix for every test, the spread of the fractions equals the spread of their energies. It is reported beside Stage 10's 22- to 64-fold (D121), with the share of zeros and the tests at its ends with their categories. P1 to P4 are scored as `predictions_stage12.md` states.
(5) Tests before the predictions (`src/test_stage12.py`, 24 checks, all pass): the cap and the fixed energy from the file; the eight tests' points; the specifications, Part A's central series point having Stage 6's trigger inputs; the searches on made-up outcomes; and one lumped-only run with Stage 12's specification at a Stage 8 point (M50_PT_Exp1 at 0.4625), whose crossing, 56.75 s, repeats Stage 8's exactly. No Stage 12 run was made.
(6) Checks (brief). V1: the cap and the fixed energy recomputed from the file by separate code (the csv module and a median written out). V2: every record's energy account (within 1e-9 of the heat taken in) and delivery (the pulse's integral to the run's end). V3: Part C recomputed by separate code from the records and Stage 9's files (within 1e-12). X: Part A's central-contact runs against Stage 6's records of the same points: no crossing in either, and the peaks of the hot spot, the lumped neighbour and the trigger within 1e-6 K. Then an independent read of every registered Stage 12 number and verdict.
(7) The runs: on the cloud machine (two cores, PARDISO), by two workers of `stage12_run.py work`. The list before any record holds ten runs, Part A's four and the first steps of three B1 and three B2 searches (SHA-256 d18bed85c5c0c3fbdebcfeb06f1e760c8a5b64644cb8e6d387699bf606e4bfac); later runs follow from the records by (2) and (3).
(8) The version (brief, setting): Stage 12 and the manuscript revised for the Journal of The Electrochemical Society are version 1.8.0.

D124. Stage 12's runs and results (brief of 3 November; `predictions_stage12.md`, SHA-256 be6e9f21d5ec0875202d53e06a82f2ee835f3ada83dc5b8843778ea232ae3b23, committed in 981c1e6 and pushed at 09:30:26 UTC, 34 s before the first Stage 12 run started at 09:31:00 UTC; `provenance/github_activity_stage12.json`). Scored by `src/stage12_score.py`, checked by `src/stage12_checks.py`, quoted from `results/stage12_paper_numbers.json` (`src/stage12_numbers.py`).
(1) The runs. Ten, on the cloud machine by two workers, 09:31:00 to 09:37:18 UTC: Part A's four (177 to 188 s each) and six of Part B. The list grew by none beyond its ten: B1's three searches each reached 0.02 E_fix with one halving (M50_PP_Exp3's C1 and C2, M50_ST_Exp2's C2), M50_PT_Exp1's two brackets stood (0.0125 r E_b is 0.0045 E_fix) and the three zeros stood; in B2, M50_SP_Exp3's and M50_ST_Exp4's first runs came within 10 s of the measured times (209.4 s against 210 s; 337.6 s against 342 s), M50_PT_Exp4's crossed at 241.9 s, 30.1 s before the measured 272 s, leaving (0.0375, 0.0390625] of r E_b, 0.0025 E_fix wide, with no crossing at its lower end, and M50_ST_Exp3's Stage 9 bracket was already 0.0021 E_fix wide.
(2) Part A (P1, P2). No run meets C1 or C2 within 900 s. Central contact: the hot spot peaks at 191.90 C (parallel) and 192.16 C (series), 63.5 to 63.8 K short of 255.7 C; the lumped neighbour at 159.76 to 161.75 C; the far face at 133.37 to 135.96 C. High contact: the hot spot at 226.39 to 226.69 C, 29.0 to 29.3 K short; the lumped neighbour at 175.92 to 177.40 C; the far face at 140.15 to 142.42 C. With their fitted triggers (46.5 to 54.3 kJ, 1.40 to 1.63 times the cap) the same eight tests met C1 at 16.9 to 21.7 s (central) and 14.3 to 16.3 s (high; Stage 7). P1 holds; P2 holds. Check X: the central runs repeat Stage 6's records to 9.2e-12 K.
(3) B1, as fractions of E_fix = 76,741.25 J, with E* = f* E_e beside Stage 8's: M50_PT_Exp1 f*(C1) (0.0545, 0.0590], E* (4.181, 4.530] kJ, and f*(C2) (0.1635, 0.1680], (12.544, 12.892] kJ, both Stage 8's; M50_PP_Exp3 f*(C1) (0, 0.0111], (0, 0.852] kJ against Stage 8's (0, 1.704], and f*(C2) (0.0333, 0.0444], (2.556, 3.408] kJ against (1.704, 3.408]; M50_ST_Exp2 f*(C1) 0 and f*(C2) (0.0235, 0.0352], (1.803, 2.705] kJ against (1.803, 3.607]; M50_SP_Exp3 0 and 0.
(4) B2. Matched in seven: M50_PP_Exp1 0.0388 of E_fix (2,974.4 J), PP_Exp2 0.0777 (5,962.4 J), PP_Exp4 0.0237 (1,819.0 J), SP_Exp1 0.0355 (2,725.6 J) and SP_Exp2 0.0493 (3,783.4 J), Stage 9's; SP_Exp3 0.0034 (263.5 J; bracketed in Stage 9) and ST_Exp4 0.0154 (1,184.0 J; at the threshold in Stage 9). At the threshold in two: M50_PT_Exp4 (0.0607, 0.0632], (4.657, 4.851] kJ, and ST_Exp3 (0.1557, 0.1578], (11.950, 12.111] kJ. P3: the seven span 22.6-fold (M50_SP_Exp3's 263.5 J to M50_PP_Exp2's 5,962.4 J); holds. P4: M50_PT_Exp1's f_max, 0.0272 of E_fix (2,090.7 J), is 7.9 times M50_SP_Exp3's f_t(C2), 0.0034; holds.
(5) Part C. With one ejected energy for every test, a test's fraction is its delivered energy over E_fix, so the spread in fraction is the spread in energy: 22.6-fold over the twelve tests with a fraction (the seven f_t(C2) and the five registered f_max), from M50_SP_Exp3 (263.5 J; marked "cell movement") to M50_PP_Exp2 (5,962.4 J; Fransson et al.'s category 2); four of the sixteen need none (PT_Exp3, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp2), as in Stage 10. Stage 10's spreads of the same readings: in energy, 22.4-fold at 21.39 s and 21.8- to 63.8-fold over the four deliveries; as fractions of r E_b, 47.6-fold at 21.39 s and 42.2- to 208.1-fold. Counted at their brackets, the two threshold tests would widen Part C's spread to 45.4- to 46.0-fold. The thresholds f*(C2) as fractions of E_fix (B1's brackets for its four tests, Stage 8's restated by D122 (7) for the other twelve) vary 14.0-fold by their brackets' midpoints, from M50_PP_Exp4's (0, 0.0237] to M50_PT_Exp1's (0.1635, 0.1680], against Stage 10's 73.0-fold as fractions of r E_b; the propagated tests' reach 0 to 0.1599 and the others' 0 to 0.1680, and f*(C1) 0 to 0.0589 against 0 to 0.0590, so the groups still overlap in both.
(6) Checks (`results/stage12_checks.json`), all pass. V1: the cap, 33,222.9118 J (RUN195), and the median, 76,741.2537 J, recomputed from the Databank's columns by name. V2: ten records; every account closes within 1.2e-12; the seven deliveries equal the pulse's integral to the run's end within 1.4e-16, six whole and one cut at its C1 stop (M50_PP_Exp3's resolved run at 0.00625 r E_b, stopped at 19.0 s with 830.7 of 851.9 J released, the triangle's share at that time); every fraction of E_fix times E_fix equals the record's delivered energy. V3: Part C, P3, P4, Stage 10's ranges and the fixed thresholds of both criteria recomputed from the records and Stages 8 to 10's files. X: as in (2).
(7) What Stage 12 changes. The headline stands: with the trigger held to the calorimetry's largest body energy, conduction and radiation meet neither criterion in any of the eight tests at either contact, and with the ejected energy held to the calorimetry's median the fraction a test needs still varies 22.6-fold. What it qualifies: at 21.39 s the spread in fraction falls from 47.6-fold (fractions of r E_b) to 22.6-fold (fractions of E_fix), the spread in energy, and the thresholds' from 73.0- to 14.0-fold; the larger spreads were in part the ejected energy's scaling with the fitted trigger. Every outcome but the two high-contact runs was fixed by Stages 6 and 9's records before the runs, as the predictions file says. Counts: Stage 12 committed 4 predictions, 4 held; through Stage 12, 74 committed, 38 held, 36 failed.

D125. The independent read of Stage 12 (brief of 3 November, Checks: "the independent read of every registered Stage 12 number and verdict"), made on commit fcc43f4 by a separate instance of the model that had not drafted the stage's texts, from the raw files (the ten records, Stages 6 to 10's files, the Databank, the tests' table and the push log), without importing the scoring, the checks or the numbers script. Verdict: agree; every registered number and verdict reproduces to rounding, and P1 to P4 hold. It found:
(1) `results/stage12_paper_numbers.json`, regenerated after fcc43f4 once Stage 12's four predictions were added to `manuscript/data/predictions_by_stage.csv`, counted all 74 rows of that file as Stages 1 to 11 (74 instead of 70; overall 78 committed and 42 held instead of 74 and 38). `src/stage12_numbers.py` now counts the rows of Stages 1 to 11 only; no text had used the wrong counts.
(2) D124 (3) writes "E* = f* E_e" beside fractions of E_fix: it means E* = f* E_fix with f* the fraction of E_fix, equal to f E_e with f the fraction of r E_b; the numbers are right. D123 (2) names f* as a bracket's midpoint; the scorecard and D124 give the brackets.
(3) D124 (5)'s group ranges of f*(C2) as fractions of E_fix follow its rule, B1's brackets for the four Stage 11 tests and Stage 8's restated for the other twelve. The f_t(C2) searches of Stage 9 and B2, made in the same configuration with C2's 900 s, narrow three of the twelve: M50_ST_Exp3 to (0.1557, 0.1578] (no crossing at 0.23125 r E_b, a crossing at 0.234375), M50_PT_Exp4 to (0.0607, 0.0632] and M50_ST_Exp4 to (0.0123, 0.0154]. With them the propagated tests' range ends at 0.1578 rather than 0.1599; the midpoints' 14.0-fold spread and the overlap are unchanged. The texts keep the rule's figures, since Table 5 of the manuscript restates Table 4(a), Stage 8's brackets, at the same energies.
(4) The propagation level is 255.68 C in the code (`stage7_inputs.LEVEL_PROP_C`, D35), quoted as 255.7 C. The scoring now takes the code's value (the shortfalls are unchanged at their precision, 63.5 to 63.8 K and 29.0 to 29.3 K); M50_ST_Exp4's B2 run crosses with a lumped peak of 255.688 C, above the level and below its rounded quotation.
Also confirmed: the predictions file's hash and its push 34 s before the first run; the run list rebuilt from the ten records' specifications hashes to d18bed85, the registered list, and the searches' rules ask for no further run; V1's cap, median and range (64.16 to 89.34 kJ); the eight tests and their fitted energies; every Part A peak and shortfall; Stage 7's fitted crossings at f = 0 (16.94 to 21.69 s central, 14.30 to 16.26 s high); check X; V2's residuals and the one delivery cut at its stop; every B1 and B2 bracket, end moved and stopping rule; P3's 22.63 over seven tests and P4's 0.027243 against 0.003433; Part C's twelve fractions, ends, zeros and the 45.35 to 45.97 with the threshold tests counted; Stage 10's spreads (47.60 and 42.16 to 208.12 in fraction, 22.44 and 21.81 to 63.78 in energy, 73.0 for the thresholds); and the counts, 74 committed, 38 held, 36 failed.

D126. The manuscript's second revision, for the Journal of The Electrochemical Society (brief of 3 November, Part M), before the author's pass. The journal's instructions were read on 3 November 2026 from its page "Welcome to the ECS Publishing Journey" (last modified 7 October 2026); `manuscript/submission/checklist.md` sets the manuscript and the files against them item by item.
(1) M1, the form. Line numbers removed. The title has 15 words, hyphenated words counted as one ("Neither conduction nor a fixed delivered fraction reproduces sixteen two-cell propagation tests: a pre-registered study"), with two alternatives in `manuscript/titles.md`. The journal line names the journal. Sections are unnumbered (`\setcounter{secnumdepth}{0}`), and every reference to a section was taken out of the text, as the journal asks. The corresponding author is marked with a superscript z and the e-mail is a red placeholder, since it is not committed to the public repository. Hyperlinks are disabled and the references' journals carry CAS abbreviations. The manuscript and the supplement use the Latin Modern fonts, so every text font in the PDFs is Type 1 rather than a bitmap.
(2) M2, Stage 12 written in. The Methods' section on Stage 11's bands gains the capped and the fixed cases in two sentences, and the table of symbols E_fix. The conduction result gains the capped runs; the criteria result gains the seventh time Stage 12's narrower search matched (M50_ST_Exp4's). The fraction result gains Part C's paragraph, and the Limits gain theirs: the cap and the fixed energy are sensitivities, not corrections. The Discussion gives the delivered energy as a share of the measured ejected energy. The counts become twelve stages and 74 predictions. The abstract (198 words) says that the conduction result holds in any test at the calorimetry's largest body energy, and gives the spread in energy. The brief's "Table 3", the fractions table, is Table 4 now that the symbols table is Table 1; its readings as fractions of E_fix stand as a table of their own, Table 5, because the three parts together overran the page. The supplement gains a section on Stage 12 with its table, Stage 12's four rows in the predictions table, its row in the energy table and D122 to D124 in the decisions list; its title matches the manuscript's.
(3) M4, the figure files. Each main figure is at the full width, 17.1 cm, as TIFF (300 dpi, LZW) and as PDF; the smallest lettering is 8.73 pt, 2.25 mm in capital height (`manuscript/submission/figures/sizes.json`). None is at the one-column width the journal prefers wherever possible, because every figure has panels side by side or a table, which at 8.26 cm would put the lettering under 1.6 mm. The graphical abstract is drawn from the results files (`src/make_graphical_abstract.py`): Fig. 5a's thresholds f*(C2) against the trigger's energy with the range the two groups share, and Fig. 7a's history with no, a short and a long delivery; 16 by 7 cm at 300 dpi, its caption beside it.
(4) M5, the Word copy (`manuscript/main_for_author.docx`, `src/make_author_docx.py`), by pandoc from the source as compiled. Citations and cross-references are resolved from `main.aux`, the references come from `main.bbl`, captions are numbered as in the PDF, the simple formulas are written as text with italics, sub- and superscripts so that they can be edited in Track Changes, and the figures come from the TIFF files; the instruction for the pass heads it. Its numbers against the PDF's text, compiled with the figures as frames: 1,337 against 1,338, the one difference a unit's exponent (10^-11) that the PDF's text extraction reads as a number (`manuscript/main_for_author_numbers.txt`).
(5) M6, the declarations. The Acknowledgments carry the conflicts of interest, the funding, the author's contributions (CRediT, drafted for his confirmation), the data availability (the all-versions DOI and version 1.8.0) and the use of generative AI. The AI use is also stated in the Methods, each naming the tool, the model, the provider, the period and the use. The sentence that the author revised the text and that no finding or interpretation was accepted without his validation is a red placeholder until his pass.
(6) M7, `manuscript/submission/` (`src/make_submission_files.py`). It holds the manuscript as one PDF and as one .tex file, which builds alone with the figure PDFs to the same text; the supplement; the figures; the graphical abstract; `Highlights.docx` (the journal asks for a Word file named Highlights; the five highlights rewritten without jargon or acronyms, 78 to 83 characters); the keywords; the cover letter draft (the main chat's text, aligned with the manuscript's scoped claims and with Stage 12); the suggested reviewers (the main chat's seven); and the checklist.
(7) Checks. `manuscript/numbers_check.py` traces every number of the text, the tables and the supplement to a numbers or results file, with Stage 12's files added: 1,267 found, none missing. Word count: abstract 198, main text 8,268.

D127. The submission files finalised in the main chat on 4 November 2026, before the author's pass. (1) The corresponding author's e-mail entered in the address line (it is public on the repository's README). (2) The two placeholder sentences of the generative-AI declaration (Methods, Acknowledgments) replaced by what is true of the work as it stands: the author set each stage's questions and inputs, read its results and its texts, decided what the next stage asked, and reviewed the paper before its submission; the author directed the work stage by stage and takes full responsibility for its content. The journal's policy, read again on 4 November 2026 ("Use of Generative Artificial Intelligence"), says that where AI tools generate text "authors must critically review and revise the material"; the sentence that the author revised the text is written only after the author's pass over `manuscript/main_for_author.docx`, which is regenerated from this source. (3) "Supervision" removed from the CRediT roles, which read oddly for a sole author. (4) Reference [20] at version v1.8.0, the version the data statement names (it read v1.7.0; `references.bib`, `main.bbl`, `supplement.bbl`). (5) The elsarticle footer ("Preprint submitted to Journal of The Electrochemical Society" on the manuscript, "Preprint submitted to Elsevier" on the supplement) removed by redefining the title page style, in both sources. (6) The straight double quotes of "not at all" in the predictions table (`tables/s2_predictions.tex`) set as LaTeX quotes. (7) Built with XeLaTeX and the Latin Modern OpenType fonts, because the main chat's machine has no Type 1 Latin Modern; the committed sources keep the pdflatex preamble, and the single-file source builds to the same text; 36 pages (the figures fall on different pages), the text compared word by word with the 3 November PDF, the only differences being the edits above. `numbers_check.py`: 1,267 found, none missing; abstract 198 words, main text 8,282. The cover letter final with five suggested reviewers whose e-mails were taken from their institutional pages or the CVs posted there (Stoliarov, Ezekoye, Jain, Coman, Feng); the checklist updated.

## Stage 13 (version 1.9.0, on the branch stage13): the mechanism of each threshold

Brief 22 asks whether the two thresholds of the manuscript have simple mechanisms: the local one (f*(C1), the hot spot of the resolved neighbour reaching the level T_L = 255.68 C) as conduction into a skin, and the whole-cell one (f*(C2), the lumped neighbour reaching T_L) as an energy balance to an ignition temperature. It builds reduced models of both from the archive's own inputs, states their numbers before any new run, and scores ten predictions against new runs of the resolved and the lumped neighbour. Nothing of Stages 1 to 12 is edited; the new code is in `src/stage13_*.py`, which imports the earlier modules unchanged; nothing under `manuscript/` is opened. D128 and D129 are kept for the submission's record, and Stage 14 (on its own branch) writes D150 onward.

D130. Part A0: the model's facts, the archived values as context, and the classical results.
(1) The facts the reduced models use, each with the file it is read from. The roll conducts 1.3 W/(m K) across its layers and 25 W/(m K) along them (`lumped_two_cell.py` of the published two-cell study, under `external/`, K_RADIAL and K_AXIAL; the tensor k_z I + (k_r - k_z) e_r e_r in `src/v12_model.py`, Cell2Contact). Its volumetric heat capacity is the cell's heat capacity less the can's over the cell's volume, (61.75 - 4.5) J/K over pi R^2 L = 2.336e6 J/(m3 K) (v12_model, rho_cp_roll, which divides by the mesh's volume; the exact volume is used here and differs by 0.03 %), so that the effusivity across the layers is e = sqrt(k_r rho c) = 1,743 J/(m2 K s^0.5) and the diffusivities are 5.565e-7 m2/s across and 1.070e-5 m2/s along the layers. The can is 0.25 mm of steel at 15 W/(m K), entering as a shell conductance k t = 3.75e-3 W/K over the outer facets, and its 9 g at 500 J/(kg K), 4.5 J/K, are spread over the outer area A_cell = 5.346e-3 m2, a heat capacity per area C_s = 841.7 J/(m2 K) (lumped_two_cell, T_CAN, K_CAN, M_CAN, CP_CAN, A_CELL; v12_model, c_shell). The contact and the air wedge give, within 45 degrees of the line of contact, h(theta) = k_air / (2 R (1 - cos theta) + k_air / h_c) per unit area, with k_air = 0.03 W/(m K) and h_c = 200, 2,000 or 3,700 W/(m2 K) at the low, central and high contact; over the cell this is 0.050, 0.170 and 0.233 W/K, and at the central contact the band in which the gap is narrower than k_air/h_c is 2.2 degrees either side of the line (v12_model, h_local, g_contact, contact_split). The tabs add 6.8 mW/K each at the central level, one at the base in series and one at each end in parallel (v12_model, tabs). The room takes 10 W/(m2 K) outside the 90 degree sector facing the trigger, eps sigma (T^4 - Ta^4) with eps = 0.8 over the whole can, and the trigger's radiation arrives as eps^2 sigma F (T1^4 - Ta^4) with F = 0.1817 over the lateral area, shared over theta as its local view factor (v12_model, F_TOUCH, f_local_touch, the Cell2Contact weights). The delivery is the archive's: a flux uniform over the lateral faces of its region, theta <= 90 degrees (area pi R L = 2.323e-3 m2) or, for the cap-end quarter, also z >= 0.75 L (`src/stage8_model.py`, surface_weights), released from t = 0 as a triangle over tau_d with its peak 2E/tau_d at tau_d/2 (`src/stage7_model.py`, Event, which is Stage 8's Pulse and Stage 9's Triangular) or at a constant power (`src/stage9_model.py`, Constant). The lumped neighbour's balance is C dT/dt = G (T1 - T) + eps^2 sigma F A_lat (T1^4 - Ta^4) - h A_conv (T - Ta) - eps sigma A_cell (T^4 - Ta^4) + Q + P, with C = 61.75 J/K, G the contact and wedge plus the tabs (0.1837 W/K in parallel and 0.1769 W/K in series at the central levels), h A_conv = 0.0418 W/K, Q the reaction heat and P the delivery (stage9_model, run_lumped). The level is T_L = 255.68 C, the lower end of the two-cell study's onset band (lumped_two_cell, T_ONSET_BAND; v12_model, LEVEL_PROP). The rate law is r_j = exp(ln A_j + b_j ln T - E_j/(R T) + sum_s n_sj ln c_s), each reaction stopping when a reactant is exhausted, and the heat is M sum_j h_j r_j for the cell's mass M = 65 g (`src/bcrnn.py`, Kinetics; `inputs/kinetics/bcrnn_mean_model.json`). The heats per kg of cell, h_j, are -16,856 (electrolyte evaporation, which absorbs heat), 21,374 (electrolyte decomposition), 59,195 (anode and electrolyte), 14,948 (anode and binder), 219,828 (SEI decomposition, of an initial 0.15), 22,737 and 16,734 (reactions IV and V of the decomposition products), 41,034 (the first cathode reaction, cat1), 8,806 (cat2), 44,053 (cat3) and -1,063 J/kg (the separator's melting); the first cathode reaction's whole heat in the 65 g cell is 2,667 J, an adiabatic rise of 43.2 K. Its order in its reactant is 1.342 and its activation energy 220.2 kJ/mol. The trigger is the archive's: the two-cell pair of v12_model.lumped_pair releasing the test's body energy E_b over a triangle of 21.39 s, tabulated every 0.1 s and read by every neighbour one-way (v12_model, stage5_t1).
(2) The archived values, as context and never scored. `src/stage13_archive.py` writes every archived run of Stages 8 and 9 (900 records) with its fraction and the hot spot's and the lumped neighbour's peak and crossing time to `results/stage13_archive_runs.csv`, the sixteen f*(C1) and f*(C2) brackets and the five tests' duration, region and ratio brackets to `results/stage13_archive_brackets.csv`, and the three readings of the brief, checked against those records, to `results/stage13_readings.json`. The first reading (the local threshold is conduction into a skin) is reproduced. PT_Exp1's hot spot rises 150.78 K at f = 0.1 and 220.29 K at f = 0.15, peaking at 14.59 and 14.27 s against two thirds of the pulse, 14.26 s, at theta = 26.3 degrees and z = 30.6 mm; the line through the two reaches the level's rise, 230.68 K, at f = 0.1575; the run at f = 0.1625 crosses at 12.45 s, 28.9 degrees from the line of contact, with 0.132 J of reaction heat in the half model; at 60 s the rises at f = 0.2 and 0.25 are 173.84 and 210.55 K, the line crossing at f = 0.2774, and the run at 0.275 peaks at 255.45 C. With 1.2284 Q''/(e sqrt(tau)) for the half-space (item (3)), the 21.39 s runs imply e = 2,170 (from the ratio at f = 0.15) to 2,293 J/(m2 K s^0.5) (from the slope), against 1,743 for the roll as the resolved neighbour holds it and 1,810 with the whole cell's heat capacity over its volume (the brief's "about 1,790" lies between); the ratio of the rise per unit f between 21.39 and 60 s is 1.893 against sqrt(60/21.39) = 1.675. The second reading (the whole-cell threshold is an energy balance to an ignition temperature) is reproduced apart from one range: with the separator molten, the first cathode reaction releases 48.2, 136.7 and 372.6 W at 230, 240 and 250 C; the lumped neighbour's loss at 230 C is 20.5 W with the trigger at the neighbour's own temperature and 59.9 W with it at 25 C (parallel link; 58.5 W in series), against "23 to 62 W" in the brief; PT_Exp1's refined threshold is 12.849 to 12.892 kJ against C times 205 K = 12.659 kJ, and the runs just below it peak at 224.2, 230.3, 240.9 and 250.7 C; for PP_Exp3 the step of 851.9 J between f = 0.0125 and 0.01875 raises the lumped peak by 13.77 K against 851.9 J / C = 13.80 K (the next step, 213 J, raises it by 11.2 K, the reaction then adding its own heat). The third reading (E* is a difference) holds only in part: among the parallel tests whose trigger alone does not propagate, the needed delivery falls by 0.210 J per joule of body energy between PT_Exp1 and PT_Exp3, by 0.291 between PT_Exp1 and PP_Exp3 and by 0.440 between PT_Exp3 and PP_Exp3, so that the slope is not one number; "about 0.22 J" is the weakest pair's.
(3) The classical results. None of the four could be read at its source from this session: the half-space under a surface flux (Carslaw and Jaeger's text), Semenov's balance (his 1928 paper), the ignition of a reactive solid by a surface heat flux, and the consumption of the reactant in thermal explosion (Frank-Kamenetskii's treatment) are in books and journals behind their publishers' pages. Each is therefore derived here and none is cited.
(a) A half-space x > 0 of conductivity k and volumetric heat capacity rho c, at zero initially, receives a flux q(t) at x = 0. In Laplace variables the bounded solution of k T'' = rho c s T is T = A exp(-x sqrt(s/alpha)) with alpha = k/(rho c), and -k T'(0) = q gives A = q/(e sqrt(s)), e = sqrt(k rho c). Since 1/sqrt(s) is the transform of 1/sqrt(pi t), the surface temperature is T_s(t) = (1/(e sqrt(pi))) times the integral from 0 to t of q(t') / sqrt(t - t') dt'. For a ramp q = a t' the integral is (4/3) a t^(3/2), and the triangle of energy Q'' per area over tau is the slope 4Q''/tau^2 times the ramps at 0, -2 at tau/2 and +1 at tau. Between tau/2 and tau the derivative of t^(3/2) - 2 (t - tau/2)^(3/2) vanishes where t = 4 (t - tau/2), at t = 2 tau/3, and the peak is T_pk = (16 Q'' / (3 e sqrt(pi) tau^2)) ((2/3)^(3/2) - 2 (1/6)^(3/2)) tau^(3/2) = 1.2284 Q''/(e sqrt(tau)). A surface layer of heat capacity C_s and a linear loss h (T_s - T_b) change the transform to q / (C_s s + e sqrt(s) + h), which A1 inverts (D131 (1)).
(b) A body at a uniform temperature T with heat capacity C, a heat release Q(T) and a loss L(T) obeys C dT/dt = Q - L. Its rest points are where Q = L: one where Q' < L' is stable (a small excess cools back), one where Q' > L' is unstable (a small excess grows, since the release then outruns the loss). Above the unstable point Q > L and T rises for as long as the reactants last. That point is the ignition temperature T_ign of B1, with L the lumped neighbour's loss and the trigger at a stated temperature (D132).
(c) In a solid heated through its surface the temperature falls from the surface's T_s over the heated depth d ~ sqrt(pi alpha t). A rate of activation temperature T_a = E/R falls by a factor e within the first T_s^2 / T_a of that fall, so the layer that reacts is d T_s^2 / (T_a (T_s - T_0)) deep, and its heat per area is the volumetric release at T_s times that depth. The solid ignites locally when this heat becomes comparable with the flux it absorbs. With a strong flux over a short time the surface reaches any fixed level before the reacting layer can matter, which is reading 1's 0.13 J at 21.39 s; with a weaker flux over a longer time the layer has time to add its heat before the level, which is why P3 asks whether the resolved threshold at 120 s falls below the conduction model's.
(d) A reaction of order n with an adiabatic rise Delta T_ad consumes its reactant as it heats. In units of the temperature over which its rate grows e-fold, R T^2 / E, the rise is B = Delta T_ad E / (R T^2). For B large the reactant is almost whole when the runaway starts and Semenov's criterion is sharp; for B of a few, a sizeable share is consumed during the slow approach to the unstable point, the runaway can end before a given level, and more than the balance point is needed. For the first cathode reaction at 218.5 C (D132), Delta T_ad = 43.2 K and R T^2 / E_eff = 10.5 K, so B = 4.1: this is why archived lumped runs pass T_ign and peak at 241 and 251 C without crossing, and why B2's rule asks for the reaction's heat left as well as the ignition temperature (D133).

D131. The reduced models of the local threshold, A1 and A2.
(1) A1, the closed form (`src/stage13_skin.py`). A half-space with the roll's properties across its layers (e = 1,743 J/(m2 K s^0.5)) carries the can at its surface as a layer of heat capacity C_s = 841.7 J/(m2 K) and no resistance (the can's conductance across its 0.25 mm, 60,000 W/(m2 K), is left out), receives the triangular flux of energy E'' = f E_e / A per unit area (A the region's area, E_e the test's ejected energy) and loses heat linearly, h (T_s - T_b), from its starting temperature T_b. The loss coefficient h is the surface's to first order: the local contact or wedge coefficient h(theta) at the hot spot's angle (or room convection beyond 45 degrees) plus radiation as the secant from T_b to the level, eps sigma (T_L^2 + T_b^2)(T_L + T_b). The surface's rise is then the inverse of q(s) / (C_s s + e sqrt(s) + h), written with the roots a and b of C_s p^2 + e p + h = 0 and the functions exp(x^2 t) erfc(x sqrt(t)) (the module's docstring has the impulse, step, ramp and triangle forms; the roots are complex conjugates when 4 C_s h > e^2, within the contact band). Its peak is u_pk = Phi(gamma, beta) E''/(e sqrt(tau)), with gamma = C_s/(e sqrt(tau)) for the can and beta = h sqrt(tau)/e for the loss, and the threshold is f*_A1 = (T_L - T_b) A e sqrt(tau) / (E_e Phi). For gamma = beta = 0, Phi = 1.2284 at t = 2 tau/3 (D130 (3)(a)); the can delays and lowers the peak, Phi(0.1045, 0) = 1.0999 at 0.711 tau, and the loss lowers it further. A1 is one dimensional and does not find its own hot spot: it takes the hot spot's angle and its temperature with no delivery from A2 (item (2)), both of which enter only as small corrections (the loss, a few per cent of the rise at 21.39 s; T_b, 10.7 K above 25 C at 21.39 s for PT_Exp1). The pure skin, with T_b = 25 C and no loss, is reported beside it. The hand case is PT_Exp1 at 21.39 s over the facing half: E'' = 1.1998e7 J/m2 per unit f, gamma = 0.1045, the hot spot (from A2) at 26.3 degrees where h(theta) = 13.6 W/(m2 K), T_b = 35.7 C, radiation's secant 14.3 W/(m2 K), so beta = 0.0740, Phi = 1.0563, a rise of 1,572.7 K per unit f, a peak at 14.96 s and f*_A1 = 0.13986; the pure skin gives 0.14086 (Phi = 1.0999 at 15.21 s).
(2) A2, the cross-section conduction model (`src/stage13_section.py`). Linear conduction in r and theta at mid-height in the half cross-section, per unit length, with no reaction and no axial direction: the roll's two conductivities and its heat capacity, the can as a surface layer with its heat capacity and its in-plane conductance, the contact and the wedge to the trigger's temperature history (the exact integral of h(theta) over each face), room convection beyond 45 degrees, radiation to the room as the code holds it (eps sigma (T^4 - Ta^4), explicit), the radiation received from the trigger shared as its local view factor, and the delivery's flux, uniform over the region's faces (theta <= 90 degrees, or every theta for the whole surface; the eighth and the cap-end quarter are axial and lie outside A2). It is stepped as the resolved code steps (implicit in conduction and the contact, the trigger at the end of the step, explicit radiation, the delivery's energy exact over each step) with 0.01 s to 60 s and tau_d/1000 (between 0.01 and 0.1 s) after, on 41 radial and 74 azimuthal cells: the first radial cell is 0.02 mm deep and the cells grow by 1.15 inward to 0.4 mm; the first azimuthal cell at the line of contact is 0.03 mm of arc, growing by 1.15 to 0.4 mm within 45 degrees and to 1 mm on the far side, with faces at 45 and 90 degrees. The trigger's conduction enters by superposition: the run with the trigger and no delivery, T_cond, with radiation as the code holds it, and the response to the whole ejected energy with the trigger's terms removed, T_unit, whose radiation is the secant of the code's radiation along the threshold's own solution, eps sigma ((T_cond + f T_unit)^4 - T_cond^4) / (f T_unit), found by iterating on f (from the tangent, converging to 1e-12 in about six passes). At convergence T_cond + f* T_unit is the nonlinear model's own solution at f*, which a direct run at f* checks (its surface peak equals T_L to within 1e-9 K in every case, and no interior node is hotter than the surface). The threshold is f*_A2 = min over the surface's nodes and steps of (T_L - T_cond)/T_unit, which also gives the hot spot's angle and time; where T_cond alone reaches T_L, f*_A2 = 0. The discretisation's effect, from a grid 1.5 times finer in both directions and half the steps, is +0.046 % (0.156826 to 0.156898) at 21.39 s and +0.037 % (0.41450 to 0.41465) at 120 s for PT_Exp1. The hand case is PT_Exp1 at 21.39 s: T_cond at the hot spot is 34.63 C, T_unit 1,409.5 K per unit f, so f*_A2 = (528.83 - 307.78) / 1,409.5 = 0.15683, at 26.3 degrees and 14.19 s.

D132. B1, the ignition temperature T_ign (`src/stage13_tign.py`). T_ign is where the cell's reaction heat Q(T) equals the lumped neighbour's loss L(T; T1), exactly as run_lumped holds it, with the trigger at a stated temperature T1: the last temperature below T_L at which Q - L changes sign from negative to positive, so that between T_ign and T_L the reaction outruns the loss. The state is the fresh one with the separator's melting complete (sep = 0): the electrolyte, the lithiated anode, the binder and the first cathode reaction's reactant at 1, the SEI at 0.15, every intermediate at zero. With the posterior-mean kinetics, T_ign is 218.48 C with the trigger at the neighbour's own temperature (no loss through the link, so the same in series and in parallel) and 230.38 C with the trigger at 25 C (parallel link; 230.12 C in series). The carrying reaction is the first cathode reaction, 73 % of the heat at 218.5 C (the anode and electrolyte 19 %, the SEI 12 %, the electrolyte's evaporation -4 %) and 84 % at 230.4 C; the effective activation temperature T^2 d ln Q/dT is 23,094 and 24,983 K, so R T^2/E_eff = 10.5 and 10.1 K, a factor e every 10 K; at T_ign the first cathode reaction's whole heat, 2,667 J, is still there by construction (the reaction's heat left along an actual path is B2's, D133). Over the 200 posterior draws T_ign is computed after the predictions are pushed, since P7 states its span.

D133. B2, the two-body model of the whole-cell threshold (`src/stage13_twobody.py`).
(1) Definition. Two temperatures joined by the link, each with its losses, and the trigger's release and the delivery as the archive defines them. The trigger T1(t) is the archive's pair, the trigger releasing E_b over 21.39 s next to an inert, undelivered neighbour (v12_model.lumped_pair, tabulated every 0.1 s as every archived run reads it); as in the archive the delivered neighbour does not act back on it. The neighbour's inert temperature Ti(t) obeys the lumped neighbour's equation with the reaction switched off and the delivery P(t). Along it, without integrating the kinetics: the first cathode reaction's remaining fraction is the closed form of its own rate law along the path, c = (1 + (n - 1) I)^(-1/(n - 1)) with I the integral of its rate constant k(Ti(t)) and n = 1.342; and the self-heating before ignition is taken to first order, C d theta/dt = Q(Ti, c) - Lambda theta, with Q the reaction heat along the inert path (B1's state, cat1 at c) and Lambda = G + h A_conv + 4 eps sigma A_cell Ti^3 the loss's slope there, so that Tc = Ti + theta is the neighbour's temperature to first order in the reaction's heat. No reaction term feeds back into its own rate.
(2) Closed form. With the radiation linearised, Ti is the inert response T_cond(t; E_b) plus E times the delivery's response, so that reaching an ignition temperature at the time of the peak gives E* = C (T_ign - T_cond,pk(E_b) - theta_pk) / eta, eta being the share of the delivered energy still in the neighbour at the peak (one less the share the link and the room have taken). This is the reading's E* = C (T_ign - T_peak,cond) plus losses, and its derivatives give C2's conditioning; B2's numbers are the ODEs' (radiation as the code holds it), not this approximation.
(3) The crossing rule, fixed here before the predictions. Write M = Q(Tc, c) - L(Tc; T1(t)) with B1's Q and L and the trigger at its temperature of the moment. The neighbour is at or above its ignition temperature when M >= 0 and dM/dT > 0. At such a time the first cathode reaction's heat still left is Q_left = 2,667 J times c, and the part of it needed to carry the neighbour to T_L against its losses is need = s_bar C (T_L - Tc) + s(Tc) C Q/(dQ/dT), where C (T_L - Tc) is the sensible heat to the level, C Q/(dQ/dT) = C R T^2/E_eff is the loss during one adiabatic induction time at the balance point (where the loss equals Q and the induction time is C/(dQ/dT)), s(T) is the first cathode reaction's share of the heat at T and s_bar its mean over [Tc, T_L] (four-point Gauss rule); the rest of the climb's heat comes from the other reactions, which are fresh (D132). The neighbour crosses if Q_left >= need at some such time, or if Tc itself reaches T_L. The threshold E* is the least delivered energy for which it crosses (bisection to 1e-13 of the bracket); it is zero where the case crosses with no delivery.
(4) The rule's first form, and why it changed. Before this entry was written, B2 was computed in a first form without the self-heating theta and with the whole climb's need taken from the first cathode reaction (Q_left >= C (T_L - T) + C Q/(dQ/dT)), against four archived thresholds at the central contact. It gave PT_Exp1 12,604 J (archived 12,849 to 12,892 J) and ST_Exp3 12,340 J (11,627 to 12,273 J), but 8,864 J for PP_Exp3 (archived 1,704 to 3,408 J, refined 2,556 to 3,408 J) and 5,808 J for SP_Exp3, whose trigger alone propagates in the archive. The first form left out two heats that the model's own rate law holds: the other reactions' share of the climb (27 % of the heat at 218.5 C) and the reaction's heat before the balance point, which is large when a strong trigger holds the neighbour near 200 C for minutes. The registered form adds both, each from the rate law and the model's constants; nothing was adjusted to a value, and the comparisons with archived thresholds remain explanatory (B3), never scored. The registered form gives 12,507 J for PT_Exp1, 3,270 J for PP_Exp3, zero for SP_Exp3 and 11,604 J for ST_Exp3.
(5) Two implementations (V1): classical Runge-Kutta with 16 steps per 0.1 s and the fifth-order Dormand-Prince weights with 10 steps per 0.1 s, each with the delivery's corners as step ends, each finding the largest margin on the cubic Hermite interpolant of its steps (the edges of the admissible set by bisection, its interior maxima by golden-section or ternary search), written separately. On the four cases above they agree to 2e-13 of E*.

D134. The resolved searches (Part A4, `src/stage13_run.py`). Every run is the archive's resolved neighbour as Stage 8 ran it: the test's trigger, the central contact and tabs, 25 C, the posterior-mean kinetics, the fresh SEI, the reaction heat fed back one-way, the Stage 5 mesh, the triangular delivery from t = 0 through the search's region, the stop at T_L and the 900 s window; the step control and the explicit sub-steps (4,096) as Stage 8's. The ten new searches are PT_Exp1 over the facing half with deliveries of 5, 40, 120 and 360 s; ST_Exp3 and PT_Exp3 at 10 and 60 s; and PT_Exp1 at 21.39 s over the whole lateral surface and over the eighth of the facing half nearest the cap (theta <= 90 degrees, z >= 0.875 L, area pi R L/8 = 2.904e-4 m2). The whole and the eighth take new weights, each facet clipped by the region's planes and each node's hat function integrated exactly over the clipped part (`stage13_inputs.surface_weights`); the half keeps the archive's own weights. A search starts at A2's f*, registered with the predictions (for the eighth, which A2 cannot hold, at the facing half's flux per area plus 25 %), steps away by 3 %, doubling the step until the threshold is bracketed, then halves until the bracket is no wider than 4 % of its midpoint. The three archived references are refined by the archive's own rule, the midpoint of the bracket, to the same width: PT_Exp1 (0.15, 0.1625] (one halving), ST_Exp3 (0.075, 0.0875] (two) and PT_Exp3 (0.025, 0.0375] (four). A search that has not ended after 12 runs stops and is reported. A5: for each new search, the run at its bracket's upper end again with the reaction switched off, without the stop, to min(900 s, 2 tau_d + 150 s), and the difference in the hot spot's peak. Fractions are rounded to nine decimals as the archive's.

D135. The lumped searches (Part B4). Every run is the archive's lumped neighbour as Stage 8 ran it, with the trigger history of its contact level (the pair's link changes with the contact) and its body energy. The seventy searches are the sixteen tests at the low and at the high contact (200 and 3,700 W/(m2 K)) with the 21.39 s triangle; the sixteen at the central contact with triangular deliveries of 120 and 360 s (triangles, so that the ratio with A4's thresholds compares like shapes); and PP_Exp3, ST_Exp2 and PT_Exp3 with the trigger's body energy 2,000 J lower and 2,000 J higher (their f stays referred to the archived E_e). A search starts at B2's E*, registered with the predictions, steps away by 3 %, doubling until bracketed (a step that would fall below 20 J runs E = 0 instead, and a search whose E = 0 run crosses ends at zero), then halves until the bracket is no wider than 2 % of its midpoint or 20 J. A search that has not ended after 40 runs stops and is reported.

D136. The checks, with their acceptance fixed before the runs. V1: A1, A2 and B2 each computed by their two implementations, the thresholds agreeing to 1e-8 (A1 on every case, A2 on PT_Exp1 at 21.39 s and ST_Exp3 at 60 s, B2 on PT_Exp1, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp3 and SP_Exp3 at the central contact and on PT_Exp1 at the low contact). V2: the resolved neighbour with the reaction switched off, PT_Exp1 over the facing half at 21.39 s and f = 0.15625 (the archived bracket's midpoint), against A2 at the same f: the hot spot's temperature (the resolved model's largest node temperature, A2's largest surface temperature) within 2 % of the resolved rise above 25 C at every recorded time (every 0.1 s) from 1 s to 3 tau_d (64 s), the window in which the local threshold is decided; the largest difference over the whole 900 s is reported beside it, since A2 has neither the ends nor the tabs. V3: the lumped neighbour with the reaction switched off against B2's inert temperature Ti, PT_Exp1, ST_Exp3 and PP_Exp3 at the central contact with the 21.39 s triangle at B2's E*, within 0.5 K at every 0.1 s over 900 s. V4: every new run's energy account closes to 1e-6 of the heat taken in, and its delivered energy equals E to 1e-9. V5: the hand cases of D131 and D133 (2). V6: the new regions' weights sum to their areas within 0.1 % (half -0.058 %, whole -0.033 %, eighth -0.018 %; `results/stage13_v6.json`). Then a separate agent, which has not seen the runs being made, reads every registered number and verdict.

D137. The registration. `src/stage13_reduced.py` wrote every reduced number the predictions state or the searches start from to `results/stage13_reduced.json`; `predictions_stage13.md` states the ten predictions with their expectations and scoring rules, the run lists' hash and the reduced file's hash, and is committed and pushed before any Stage 13 run of the resolved or the lumped model.

D138. Part C's definitions, fixed before the runs. (1) C1, the map in the Fourier number. For each delivery tau the Fourier number across the layers is Fo = alpha_r tau / a^2, with alpha_r = 5.565e-7 m2/s and a = R = 10.55 mm, the depth sqrt(alpha_r tau) to which the delivery's heat has spread in units of the radius, squared. The regimes are named by that depth alone: a skin where the heated depth is under a third of the radius (Fo < 1/9), through-heated where it reaches the radius (Fo >= 1), and between them intermediate. The manuscript's deliveries are then: the 10 s pulse, Fo = 0.050, a skin; the 21.39 s pulse, 0.107, a skin at the regime's edge; the 60 s pulse, 0.300, intermediate; the 120 s power, 0.600, intermediate; the 240 s power, 1.20, through-heated; the 360 s power, 1.80, through-heated. C1 reports for PT_Exp1 the ratio f*(C2)/f*(C1) of the runs' midpoints at 21.39, 120 and 360 s (the 21.39 s f*(C2) is the archive's refined bracket) against Fo, with the reduced models' ratio B2/A2 at every duration of `results/stage13_reduced.json` beside them. (2) C2, the conditioning. From B2 at the central contact with the 21.39 s triangle, for each of the sixteen tests, the derivatives of E* by central differences: in the trigger's body energy E_b (plus and minus 500 J, the trigger's history recomputed), in the contact conductance h_c (plus and minus 5 %, in the pair and in the neighbour alike), in the neighbour's heat capacity C (plus and minus 1 %, the trigger's history held) and in the ignition temperature (the balance M taken at Tc - dT with dT = plus and minus 1 K, D133 (3)); a derivative across a zero of E* is reported as one-sided. The error each implies with the manuscript's brackets is the bracket's width in energy (f*(C2) brackets of 0.0125 in f, times the test's E_e) divided by the derivative: the error in E_b, h_c, C or T_ign that would move E* by one bracket. C2 also reports how much of the spread of f*(C2) B2 reproduces from the triggers' energies alone: the ratio of B2's largest to smallest nonzero f* over the sixteen tests with the 21.39 s triangle (against Stage 10's 73.0) and with the constant powers of 120, 240 and 360 s (against Stage 10's 79.0, 91.25 and 108.75; in each the archive's smallest nonzero value is the midpoint of its first bracket, 0.00625), and the same at one ejected energy (Stage 12's f_fixed, the delivered energy over its fixed ejected energy of 76.7 kJ, whose ratio of the end midpoints is 14.0).

D139. The runs. The registration was pushed at 12:52:23 UTC on 16 November (fe10115). The lumped searches ran on the cloud machine from 12:52:45 to 12:53:36 UTC: 340 runs for the seventy searches and V3's three runs, none failed; every search ended by its width rule, in 1 to 11 runs, thirteen of them at zero (the run with no delivery crossed). The steps before bracketing did not always follow D135's wording. The driver takes each step from the search's earliest run, which it finds by the run's start time; that time is recorded to the second, and the lumped runs take 0.02 to 1.7 s, so a search's first runs shared a second and the driver took the lowest of them instead (`src/stage13_run.py`, where a note now says so). In the 43 searches whose first two runs both crossed, the third and later steps down were therefore taken from the lowest value so far, not from B2's value: B4_low_PT_Exp2 ran 9,305.6, 9,026.4 and then 8,484.9 J, where the wording gives 8,747.3 J. The independent read emulated the code from the records' times and reproduced all 340 search runs (D143), so the records are the registered code's and are kept. Every bracket is still a non-crossing below a crossing of the registered width, so the departure changes where a bracket lies, not whether it holds the threshold, and a search that followed the wording would have ended within the two brackets' half-widths together. That moves no verdict: P8's smallest margin is 108 J at the recorded midpoints and 87 J after the largest such shift; P10's slopes for PP_Exp3 and ST_Exp2 move by at most 27 and 20 J per kJ, against 456 and 435 J per kJ and P10's upper end of 300 (PT_Exp3's searches followed the wording); P9's ratio at 360 s moves by at most 0.017, against 0.938 and the 1.5 it had to stay below. The thirteen resolved searches follow D134 exactly. The two resolved workers started with the lumped searches were stopped with the cloud machine a few minutes later, before any resolved run had finished: a worker detached from the session's shell does not outlive the session's turn. They were restarted at 03:35 UTC on 17 November from the same records, under the session's own background runner, with a limit of 100 minutes after which a worker starts no new run (`stage13_run.py work --max-minutes`; nothing else in the driver changed, and the claim left by the stopped run was removed). Because each search's next run is read from its records, the restart neither repeated nor skipped a run. The 46 resolved runs, 35 for the thirteen searches, ten for A5 and one for V2, ran from 03:36 to 04:59 UTC on 17 November on the cloud machine's two cores, 40 to 620 s each, and none failed. The searches ended in 1 to 7 runs; the eighth took seven, its start (the facing half's flux per area plus 25 %) lying 12 % below its threshold.

D140. The whole-cell threshold: B1 over the draws, B3 and B4, and P7, P8 and P10. (1) B1 over the 200 posterior draws (`results/stage13_tign.csv`), and the first cathode reaction's heat left along B2's paths. At B2's central-contact thresholds the thirteen heat-limited tests other than ST_Exp3 are decided 133 to 217 s after the start, where the margin of the crossing rule peaks at zero with the neighbour at 218.9 to 219.4 C on the first-order path and 2,352 to 2,376 J of the first cathode reaction's 2,667 J left (10.9 to 11.8 % consumed, after 7.5 to 8.2 K of self-heating); ST_Exp3, also heat-limited, is decided at 21.4 s at 213.2 C with 2,647 J left; PT_Exp1, whose threshold the ignition sets, reaches its balance point at 21.3 s at 224.0 C with 2,604 J left and its margin 401 J above zero (`src/stage13_twobody.py`, Case.summary, at the registered thresholds). Over the draws: with the trigger at the neighbour's own temperature T_ign has a mean of 218.43 C, a median of 218.58 C and a central 95 % from 215.26 to 221.39 C, a span of 6.14 K (all 200 between 214.71 and 222.39 C); with the trigger at 25 C (parallel link) the central 95 % is 227.72 to 233.87 C. The first cathode reaction carries the heat at T_ign in every draw. P7's span clause holds by a factor of six; its first clause, T_ign of the posterior-mean kinetics, 218.48 C (the mean over the draws is 218.43 C), was known at registration and is not counted. At that T_ign the first cathode reaction gives 73.3 % of the reaction heat (57 to 85 % over the draws, each at its own T_ign). (2) B3, explanatory: against the sixteen archived f*(C2) brackets at the central contact B2 lies inside eleven (PT_Exp3, PT_Exp4, PP_Exp2, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp2, ST_Exp4 and SP_Exp1 to SP_Exp4). Outside are PT_Exp1 (12,507 J against the refined 12,849 to 12,892 J, 2.7 % below), ST_Exp3 (11,604 against 11,627 to 12,273 J, 0.2 % below), PT_Exp2 (3,484 against 1,685 to 3,370 J), PP_Exp4 (1,978 against 0 to 1,819 J) and PP_Exp1 (150 J against zero). B2's ignition time, when its first-order path first reaches the balance point, comes 35 to 137 s before the archived crossing in fourteen of the fifteen tests with a crossing time, the climb from T_ign to 255.7 C taking that long near the threshold; in PP_Exp1, which crosses with no delivery in the archive while B2 needs 150 J, it comes 7.8 s after. (3) B4: the lumped neighbour's thresholds against B2 (`results/stage13_twobody.csv`). At the low and the high contact all 32 lie within the larger of 10 % and 300 J of B2: P8 holds, 32 of 32 against the 28 it needed. That tolerance, not the brackets' width, is B2's agreement: B2 lies inside none of the 23 nonzero brackets, 104 to 415 J beyond the nearer end (in one, PT_Exp3 at the high contact, exactly at the lower end), while the brackets are 18 to 190 J wide. At the low contact B2 lies 249 to 482 J above the lumped midpoint in the fourteen tests whose trigger is the stronger, and 276 and 442 J below it for PT_Exp1 and ST_Exp3, the two weakest; at the high contact nine tests cross with no delivery in both, and the others agree within 52 to 433 J. With the triangular 120 and 360 s deliveries at the central contact, explanatory, B2 lies within 350 J of the lumped midpoint in 30 of the 32 and within 631 J in all (PT_Exp1 and ST_Exp3 at 360 s, 631 and 489 J above); by P8's measure, the larger of 10 % and 300 J, 29 of the 32 agree, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp4 and SP_Exp2 at 120 s lying 346, 317 and 307 J above. (4) P10 fails: across the 4 kJ between the shifted triggers the needed delivered energy falls by 456 J per kJ for PP_Exp3 (3,834 to 3,894 J at E_b - 2 kJ, 2,023 to 2,056 J at E_b + 2 kJ), 435 J per kJ for ST_Exp2 and 393 J per kJ for PT_Exp3, against 150 to 300. B2 had stated 435, 418 and 367 J per kJ at registration. The brief's "about 0.22 J per joule" is the mean slope between PT_Exp1 and PT_Exp3 (D130 (2)); the local slope near a given trigger is about twice that.

D141. The local threshold: A3, A4 and A5, and P1 to P6. (1) A3, explanatory (`results/stage13_skin.csv`): A2 lies inside every one of the sixteen archived f*(C1) brackets at 21.39 s, including the eight tests whose trigger alone carries the hot spot to the level (A2 gives zero for exactly those eight: PP_Exp1, PP_Exp4, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp2, ST_Exp4 and SP_Exp1 to SP_Exp3). Over the five tests' duration brackets it lies inside PT_Exp1's at 10 and at 60 s and PP_Exp3's refined bracket at 60 s, gives zero with the archive for ST_Exp2, SP_Exp1 and SP_Exp3, and lies 10.7 % below PP_Exp3's refined bracket at 10 s (0.00419 against 0.0046875 to 0.00625), so that it lies inside 25 of the 26 archived brackets in all; it gives PP_Exp3's pattern, a shorter delivery raising the threshold (0.00419 at 10 s against 0.00315 at 21.39 s) and a longer one lowering it (0.00245 at 60 s). A1 agrees with A2 to 0.05 % at 5 s, 3.8 % at 10 s and 10.8 % at 21.39 s, and falls to 0.73 of A2 by 120 s: the one-dimensional skin holds while the heated layer is thin and the spreading along the layers (25 W/(m K)) is short, and departs as that spreading reaches the delivered region's edge and the line of contact. Where the hot spot sits within the contact band (the strong triggers), A1 does not apply. (2) A4, the new resolved searches (`results/stage13_runs/`, 35 runs with the reaction, none failed). PT_Exp1 over the facing half: 5 s (0.077051, 0.079363], 40 s (0.211222, 0.217754], 120 s (0.402065, 0.414500], 360 s (0.612180, 0.633050]; the archived 21.39 s bracket refined to (0.15625, 0.1625]. ST_Exp3: 10 s (0.057397, 0.059119], 60 s (0.124402, 0.128250], 21.39 s refined to (0.078125, 0.08125]. PT_Exp3: 10 s (0.029951, 0.030850], 60 s (0.032917, 0.033904], 21.39 s refined to (0.0265625, 0.02734375]. PT_Exp1 at 21.39 s over the whole lateral surface (0.263893, 0.271809] and over the eighth nearest the cap (0.027444, 0.028180]. In every search from 5 to 120 s and over the whole surface, A2's value is one end of the final bracket: the first 3 % step from it already bracketed the threshold, so the resolved threshold lies within 3 % of A2 for Fourier numbers from 0.025 to 0.60; the three refined references contain A2's value (PT_Exp1 and ST_Exp3) or lie 0.57 % above it (PT_Exp3). At 360 s (Fo = 1.80) the resolved threshold lies 10.5 % below A2 (midpoint 0.62262 against 0.69566), and the crossings there come late, at 210 to 317 s. (3) A5, the reaction switched off at each new search's upper end: up to 120 s the reaction decides at most the last 1.14 K. At the upper ends of the 40 and 120 s searches the hot spot without the reaction peaks 1.14 and 0.78 K below the level (254.55 and 254.90 C), the runs with the reaction having held 4.6 and 21.0 J of reaction heat in the half model when they crossed; at 5 and 10 s (PT_Exp1, ST_Exp3 and PT_Exp3) and over the whole surface it crosses without the reaction, peaking 4.1 to 5.5 K above the level, and over the eighth 0.28 K above. With the stronger triggers at 60 s the hot spot sits on the line of contact and peaks without the reaction 0.68 K below the level (ST_Exp3) or 1.47 K above it (PT_Exp3). At 360 s the reaction decides the crossing: without it the hot spot peaks at 236.75 C, 18.9 K below the level, on the surface at 45 degrees and 238.8 s, while with it the run crosses at 316.5 s on the cell's axis at mid-height (r = 0, not the surface), with the neighbour's mean temperature at 240.1 C and 2,297 J of reaction heat in the half model. The crossing at 360 s is a runaway of the reaction inside the cell, not the delivery's conduction (the crossing node's radius is now a column of `results/stage13_skin.csv`). V2's run (PT_Exp1, 21.39 s, f = 0.15625, no reaction) peaks at 253.58 C against 254.52 C with the reaction at the same f, so the reaction adds 0.94 K at 21.39 s. The trigger's part, from A2 with the trigger held at 25 C (`results/stage13_reduced_post.json`, explanatory): the trigger's conduction lowers PT_Exp1's local threshold by 0.1 % at 5 s, 3.5 % at 21.39 s, 11.1 % at 120 s and 14.2 % at 360 s, and with the stronger triggers it lowers it by up to 22 % (ST_Exp3, 60 s) and 62 % (PT_Exp3, 60 s), moving the hot spot onto the line of contact; with the trigger at 25 C the needed energy is one number for every test at a given duration (2,992 J at 10 s). The spreading's part is A1 against A2 (item (1)). (4) The predictions. P1 holds: at 5 s the resolved midpoint, 0.07821, lies 1.55 % from A1's 0.07701. P2 holds: at 40 s A2's 0.21775 lies 1.52 % from the midpoint 0.21449. P3 fails: at 120 s the resolved midpoint, 0.40828, lies 1.50 % below A2's 0.41450, not more than 5 % (the whole bracket is within 3 % of A2). P4 holds: for ST_Exp3 and PT_Exp3 at 10 and 60 s A2 lies within 1.48 to 1.52 % of the four midpoints, and in all four it gives the direction of change from 21.39 s (ST_Exp3 falls at 10 s and rises at 60 s, PT_Exp3 rises at both, as A2 had it), each pair of brackets apart. P5 fails: over the whole lateral surface the threshold in delivered energy is 1.681 times the facing half's (midpoints 0.26785 and 0.15938), outside 1.8 to 2.2; A2 had stated 1.683. P6 holds: over the eighth nearest the cap the threshold per unit area is 1.396 times the facing half's (0.027812 times 8 against 0.159375), above 1.25.

D142. Part C: the two thresholds together. (1) C1, the map (`results/stage13_map.csv`). For PT_Exp1 the ratio f*(C2)/f*(C1) of the runs' midpoints is 2.897 at 21.39 s (Fo = 0.107; f*(C1) the refined (0.15625, 0.1625], f*(C2) the archive's refined (0.4609375, 0.4625]), 1.160 at 120 s (Fo = 0.600) and 0.938 at 360 s (Fo = 1.80), so P9 holds: the ratio falls and is below 1.5 at 360 s. The reduced models' ratio B2/A2 is 5.91, 4.24, 2.86, 2.07, 1.64, 1.13, 0.91 and 0.87 at 5, 10, 21.39, 40, 60, 120, 240 and 360 s (Fo 0.025 to 1.80); B2 at 5, 10, 40, 60 and 240 s was computed after the registration for this map (`results/stage13_reduced_post.json`). Where both exist the reduced ratio lies 1.2, 2.2 and 7.0 % below the runs' (2.86 against 2.90, 1.13 against 1.16, 0.87 against 0.94). Named by D138's rule, the manuscript's 10 and 21.39 s pulses are skin deliveries (the 21.39 s at the edge), the 60 s pulse and the 120 s power intermediate and the 240 and 360 s powers through-heated; the map's values are for triangular deliveries of those durations, not for the manuscript's constant powers. In the skin regime the local threshold is conduction (A5, D141 (3)) and lies well below the whole-cell one (2.9 times in the runs at 21.39 s; 4.2 and 5.9 times in the reduced models at 10 and 5 s); through-heated, the two meet and the local crossing becomes the reaction's runaway. (2) C2, the conditioning (`results/stage13_conditioning.csv`). B2's derivatives of E* at the central contact with the 21.39 s triangle: in the trigger's body energy, -0.368 to -0.435 J per J in the thirteen other tests with a nonzero threshold, all heat-limited, whose margin is decided 133 to 217 s after the start while the trigger's conduction is still raising the neighbour (D140 (1)), and -0.098 for PT_Exp1 and -0.033 for ST_Exp3, whose thresholds are decided near the end of the delivery (21.3 and 21.4 s), before that conduction has added much (ST_Exp3's trigger peaks at 282.0 C, above T_ign, PT_Exp1's at 164.0 C, below it); in the logarithm of the contact conductance, -1,998 to -6,132 J in those thirteen and +181 and -66 J for PT_Exp1 and ST_Exp3; in the logarithm of the neighbour's heat capacity, 12,285 to 27,381 J; in the balance point alone (D138), 59.3 J per K for PT_Exp1, whose threshold the ignition sets, and zero for the heat-limited tests, whose threshold the cathode reaction's heat left sets. Because that zero says only that moving the balance point alone moves nothing, a shift of the whole rate law in temperature was added after the registration as an explanatory derivative (every rate taken at T - dT, which moves T_ign by 1.09 dT with the trigger at the neighbour's own temperature): -40.7 to -102.3 J per K in the heat-limited tests (a slower reaction leaves more of the cathode reaction's heat for the climb) and +72.9 J per K for PT_Exp1. The error each implies with the manuscript's brackets, the width of one f*(C2) step (0.0125 E_e, 348 to 2,108 J) over the derivative: 3.3 to 4.8 kJ of the trigger's body energy in the thirteen (3.6 kJ for PT_Exp1, 19.6 kJ for ST_Exp3); 32 to 61 % of the contact conductance in the thirteen (193 % and 980 % for PT_Exp1 and ST_Exp3); 2.8 to 7.3 % of the heat capacity; 4.8 to 20.1 K of the rate law's temperature (5.9 K of the balance point for PT_Exp1). PP_Exp1's derivatives in E_b, h_c and C are one-sided, taken on the side away from zero, because its E* of 150 J falls to zero at E_b + 500 J, h_c + 5 % and C - 1 %: D138 (2) registered this, the first version of `src/stage13_conditioning.py` took the central differences across the zero, and the independent read found it (D143; the table's column one_sided names them). So one bracket of the manuscript spans several kilojoules of a trigger's body energy that the tests do not measure, 32 % or more of an uncertain contact, or, in the thirteen, a shift of the kinetics about three times the posterior's 95 % span of T_ign (6.1 K): the tests constrain the model less than the spreads of f*(C2) suggest. The spread itself (`results/stage13_spread.json`): with the triggers' energies alone B2's f*(C2) spans 475-fold over the fifteen nonzero tests at 21.39 s (PT_Exp1's 0.449 against PP_Exp1's 0.00095, 150 J), and 639-, 944- and 771-fold with the constant 120, 240 and 360 s powers, against the archive's 73.0, 79.0, 91.25 and 108.75; at Stage 12's one ejected energy (76,741 J) it spans 83-fold against the archive's 14.0. B2 reproduces more than all of the archived spread: each archived ratio is set at its low end by a first bracket, (0, 0.0125], whose midpoint stands in for every test between zero and one step, and B2's by the one test it places 150 J from zero. The ratio of a difference near its zero measures how near the zero the smallest value falls, not a property of the tests. (3) C3, the sentences, are in `results/stage13_sentences.md`, not in `manuscript/`: two for the manuscript's revision and one each for Professors Deng, Ghoniem, Battiato and Sapsis.

D143. The checks and the independent read. V1 holds: A1's two implementations agree to 1.8e-10 at worst over its 22 cases, A2's to 6.1e-13 (PT_Exp1 at 21.39 s, ST_Exp3 at 60 s), B2's to 2.2e-13 (five cases, SP_Exp3 zero in both). V2 holds: with the reaction off, PT_Exp1 at 21.39 s and f = 0.15625, A2's hot spot lies within 1.83 % of the resolved rise at every 0.1 s from 1 s to 64 s (the largest at 1.0 s); the peaks are 254.88 C at 14.19 s (A2) and 253.58 C at 14.2 s (resolved), and over the whole 900 s the largest difference is 8.0 % of the rise, at 900 s, where A2 has neither the ends nor the tabs. V3 holds: B2's inert temperature lies within 0.0035, 0.0094 and 0.104 K of the lumped neighbour with the reaction off (PT_Exp1, ST_Exp3, PP_Exp3), against 0.5 K. V4 holds: all 389 runs close their energy accounts to 3.0e-12 of the heat taken in, and every delivery that ended before its run did is exact. V5 holds as the code ran it but checks less than D136 registered: `results/stage13_checks.json` holds Phi(0, 0) = 1.2284236 against the exact 1.2284236 and the balance at T_ign (-4.6e-14 W), and its pass flag rests on Phi(0, 0) alone. The independent read checked D131's hand case for PT_Exp1 against `results/stage13_reduced.json` and found it as stated (f*_A1 = 0.13986, f*_A2 = 0.15683); D133 (2) states a closed form and no number, and nothing checks it. V6 holds (D136). The independent read: a separate agent, which had not seen the runs being made, read the repository on 17 November after the results commit (6ebb116) with read-only scripts of its own. It reproduced from the files every registered number, every final bracket, the ten verdicts and V1 to V6, and confirmed the registration (fe10115 holds `predictions_stage13.md` and `results/stage13_reduced.json`, whose SHA-256 is 7b115703... in the commit and on disk; neither they nor D130 to D138 changed after it; the run lists' hash, e018365e..., rebuilt independently), the 389 records (46 resolved, 343 lumped, none failed) and their parameters, every search's start, the refined references' 1, 2 and 4 halvings, the width rule in all 83 brackets, the scores to all digits, V4's worst residual (2.99e-12) and that nothing under `manuscript/` changed. It found twenty discrepancies. Each is corrected here, in the README, the note or the sentences, and none moves a verdict: (a) the lumped searches' steps in 43 of the seventy (D139); (b) A3's count, 25 of 26 archived brackets, not 22 of 23, the three 21.39 s brackets that Stage 13 refined having been left out (D141 (1), `src/stage13_numbers.py`); (c) 10.7 %, not 10.6 % (D141 (1)); (d) the 360 s crossing on the cell's axis, not on the line of contact, with the crossing node's radius now in `results/stage13_skin.csv` (D141 (3)); (e) 2,992 J, not 2,993 J, and the last 1.14 K, not the last kelvin (D141 (3)); (f) PP_Exp1's derivatives, taken across zero, now one-sided as D138 (2) registered, which moves D142 (2)'s ranges in the thirteen tests (dE*/dE_b from -0.365 to -0.368 at its small end; one bracket from 3.3 to 5.4 kJ to 3.3 to 4.8 kJ of body energy, from 33 to 32 % of the contact conductance at its small end and from 2.8 to 9.4 % to 2.8 to 7.3 % of the heat capacity; dE*/dln C from 27,104 to 27,381 J at its large end); (g) ST_Exp3's trigger, which peaks at 282.0 C, above T_ign, not below it (D142 (2)); (h) 1.2 to 7.0 % below the runs, where "within 7.0 %" was exceeded by 7.03 % (D142 (1)); (i) V5's scope (above); (j) a first name in D144, now "the author"; and, in the README, the note and the sentences, the count of resolved runs (46, not 71), B2's agreement (P8's tolerance, not the brackets' width), T_ign as the point where the whole reaction heat overtakes the loss (the first cathode reaction giving 73.3 % of it), the values computed after the registration (now marked), the search runs' durations (49 to 620 s; the 40 s run was an A5 run), the "32 new searches" (the low and the high contact), P7's first clause (T_ign of the posterior-mean kinetics, not T_ign's mean), the lumped runs' times and the symbols E'', tau and e, and alpha, tau and a (now defined). `predictions_stage13.md` uses E_b (the trigger's body energy), tau (the delivery's duration), theta (the angle round the can from the line of contact), z (the distance along the cell, the cap at z = L) and L (the cell's length) without defining them; it is registered and is left unchanged, and D130 to D138 use the symbols in those senses.

D144. The texts and the files. `results/stage13_skin.csv`, `stage13_tign.csv`, `stage13_twobody.csv`, `stage13_map.csv`, `stage13_conditioning.csv`, `stage13_scorecard.json`, `stage13_paper_numbers.json` and `stage13_sentences.md`; the figure `results/stage13_figure.pdf` (and `.png`): (a) the local threshold against the delivery's duration (A1, A2 and the runs), (b) the delivered energy at the whole-cell threshold against the trigger's body energy at the three contacts (B2 and the runs), with T_ign over the 200 draws inset, (c) the ratio of the two thresholds against the Fourier number. A note section of six numbered lines (`note/neighbour_onset_note.md`, before "What it means for the thesis"; the earlier text unchanged) and a README section. `manuscript/data/predictions_by_stage.csv` lies under `manuscript/`, which this stage does not open: its Stage 13 row (ten committed, seven held, three failed) is left to the main chat. Nothing is merged, tagged or released; the expected version is 1.9.0, at the author's word from the main chat.

## Stage 14, Part A0, recorded before the Stage 14 predictions (16 and 17 November)

D150. Part A0 (brief of 16 November, A0.1 and A0.2): the rig read again, and what the calorimeter counts. Stage 14 asks, for the rig of Fransson et al. (D33), what physics allows each path by which the trigger's ejecta or its circuit can heat the half of the neighbour's can that faces it (the facing half of D77 (4)), as a lower and an upper bound from published inputs alone. No Stage 1 to 12 code or result is edited; Stage 14's files are new (`src/stage14_*.py`, `results/stage14_*`, `predictions_stage14.md`). It is worked on the branch stage14 from main at bd2c9ad; D128 to D149 are left to the submission's record and to Stage 13.
(1) The copies read on 16 November. The article, as in D89 (the copy on the document server of the National Laboratory of the Rockies, journal pages 1 to 11), through a text reader. Its supplementary material, the file of D52 (10,240,301 bytes, SHA-256 e96713ef58e8dc13df8810034c7ee8b47fb3e2fdcdb398a5095ecdfb64dfc0ef), downloaded by the author and read here through its text layer and as rendered (pages 28 to 32). Reference [20] of the article, Fransson et al., J.
