Energy Storage 71 (2023) 108088, in the authors' manuscript (`Buckwell_Sidewall-breach_manuscript.pdf`, 13 pages, SHA-256 dbf0cbce6dcff891ac212b4be93f8472ec11e091ecbcb62ced487ed4d8ec5650), downloaded by the author. Reference [19], Pfaff et al., J. Synchrotron Radiat. 30 (2023) 192-199, the chamber's paper, through a text reader in the laboratory's copy (https://docs.nlr.gov/docs/fy23osti/82686.pdf). The values that enter a bound are rows of `results/stage14_inputs.csv` (D152).
(2) A0.1, the rig.
- Orientation. The cells were "aligned horizontally within the cell holder [20]" and "placed directly in contact with each other as in Figure 9(b)" (p. 3). The supplement's Figure 9(b) (p. 28), a photograph of the holder, labels the trigger cell above the propagating cell, with the nail above the trigger; the archive's reading (D89 (2)) stands.
- The holder. The article does not name its material (D33 (4)). The chamber's paper does: "The basic structure is an aluminium frame", with "inserted 3D-printed inlets" that isolate the cells electrically, each fixture touching a cell over "only 2 mm" (Pfaff et al. p. 194). Figure 9(b) shows two upright end plates joined by threaded rods on a base, each with a tall window lined by a black insert, in which the two cells' ends sit one above the other. What touches the neighbour: the trigger, along the line of contact; the inserts, at its two ends; the welded tabs (p. 3); and its thermocouple with tape and glue (p. 3). The inserts' material and shape are given nowhere. The 2023 study's photograph of the holder (manuscript p. 5, Fig. 1(a), Molicel P42A cells; its caption, pp. 5-6, notes "a small air gap" between the sidewalls in one radiograph) shows the same arrangement, with a taped block under the lower cell; whether the M50 tests had such a block is not stated.
- The enclosure and its atmosphere. The chamber (Pfaff et al. pp. 192-193) is heat-resistant and gas-tight, of 343 l, with three 6 mm stainless-steel sidewalls, aluminium windows for the beam, an inlet for air or inert gas, an exhaust pipe to an extraction unit and a CO2 bottle purged into it to suppress fire. In the M50 tests "a 15% CO2 ratio was used for fire retention" (p. 3), and "there is no compartment enclosing the two cells" (p. 6). Fire was seen in the rig: "a large amount of gas and fire is released in this instant" (p. 6, the category 1 example); "fire and gas are ejected from the vent region of the cell" (p. 7, the category 3 example, a sidewall melt, which the paper reports only in P42A cells); "gas and fire release through the vent opening" (pp. 7-8, the category 4 example). No sentence ties a fire to an M50 test by name (the second reading, D152 (3)); the bounds' upper ends allow the gas to burn and their lower ends do not assume it. The supplement's Figure 14 (p. 32) shows thermal images of the "gas, fire and explosion evolution" for each category, on a scale that ends at 500 C.
- Where the vent points. The article does not say which end of a trigger vented in which test. The supplement's Figure 12 (p. 31), a drawing, puts the two positive ends on the same side in parallel and on opposite sides in series, as the tabs require. The cells of the Databank, "LG 21700-M50 (BV)", had a bottom vent (3); whether the commercial cells of the tests had one is not stated.
- The thermocouples were "fastened in the middle of the cell surface using Kapton tape and heat conductive glue" (p. 3), at an angle not given (D89 (2)).
- What the text says of each test's ejection. The article gives each test's category (Tables 3 and 4, p. 7) and no account of its ejection. Of the tests together: "Rupture only occured in trigger cells, where the rapid initiation will lead to a fast accumulation of gas" (p. 5); "Sidewall breaches were observed in P42As only, while ruptures only occurred in trigger cells" (p. 10); in the trigger, material is "pushed towards the venting region of the battery (to the left in the frame)" (p. 4). The supplement's Figure 11 (p. 30) shows a sidewall melt, a sidewall rupture and a cell moved out of the field of view, without naming the tests.
- The failure categories (p. 4, and the Fig. 2 caption, p. 5): 1, "Trigger cell failures where cell disintegration initiates to the left of the nail"; 2, failures in a propagating cell; 3, sidewall melting; 4, "Cases where sidewall rupture occurred as a result of TR (trigger cells)". "In 4 of the M50 tests, the cell moved within the FoV" (p. 4). Among the sixteen M50 tests (D33 (3)): category 4 in M50_PT_Exp3, PT_Exp4 and ST_Exp2; 1 in PT_Exp2, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp3 and ST_Exp4; 2 in PP_Exp2, PP_Exp4 and SP_Exp1; "cell movement" in PT_Exp1, PP_Exp1, SP_Exp2 and SP_Exp3; none in PP_Exp3 and SP_Exp4.
- The test names (p. 3): "'PT' stands for 'Parallel Trigger', meaning the cells are connected in parallel and the X-ray FoV cover the trigger cell"; PP, parallel with the propagating cell in the field of view; ST and SP, the same in series.
- The parallel circuit: "As the neighboring cell discharges through the trigger cell, this dumps its current in the already failed cell" (p. 2); the supplement's Figure 12 (p. 31) draws it. No current or voltage history is reported.
(3) A0.2, the Databank and its calorimeter. The ten nail rows of D53 (`inputs/databank/databank_m50_used.csv`) were read again in the workbook (`battery-failure-databank-revision2-feb24.xlsx`, SHA-256 18a6b0ed0bbc881295ef9c832e4dd1937b653702ec6481834e3c6ee50c86a7c4, downloaded by the author), with the columns the earlier file lacks. Every row is "LG 21700-M50 (BV)", with "Bottom-Vent-Yes-No" Yes, 18.2 Wh and an open-circuit voltage of 4.164 to 4.189 V; the failure mechanism is "Top and Bottom Vent" in five rows, "Top Vent" in four and "Bottom Vent" in one, and no row has a breach. Body, positive-end and negative-end energies (kJ): Run063 20.64, 2.77, 73.48; Run065 28.36, 38.60, 40.63; Run067 24.72, 36.06, 41.17; Run045 27.03, 6.29, 57.87; Run047 18.67, 0.49, 74.55; RUN194 33.08, 68.02, 5.36; RUN195 33.22, 74.68, 1.47; RUN196 27.04, 83.06, 0.39; RUN197 21.50, 89.33, 0.01; RUN198 24.74, 84.16, 0.56. The ejected energy, positive plus negative, spans 64.16 to 89.34 kJ, median 76,741.25 J (D122 (2)). The mass that left the cell body (pre-test mass less post-test body mass) is 33.51 to 48.85 g, of which 12.13 to 22.81 g was not recovered.
What the calorimeter counts. The ReadMe defines the ejecta fractions as energy "released through solids, liquids, and gases ejected" from the positive end (button end) and from the negative end (bottom), and the corrected total as the yield "corrected for both conductive heat loss and also for the heat associated with unrecovered cell mass"; the "Theoretical energy associated with unrecovered mass" is "accounted for in the Corrected Total Energy Yield and in the energy fractions/percents". The method is in the calorimeter group's own study, read in the manuscript of its Part 1 (Walker et al., whose cells include the LG 21700-M50): "The maximum theoretical temperature change of the unrecovered mass is approximated" from the baffles' temperatures and multiplied by a specific heat (manuscript p. 7), a sensible heat. In the ten rows it comes to 0.13 to 0.32 kJ per gram of unrecovered mass. The calorimeter's baffles, copper mesh and end caps capture particles and arrest flames (Finegan et al. 2024, p. 2), and "the amount of excess oxygen is expected to be less inside the enclosed volume of the FTRC than if the mass was ejected into an open volume" (p. 7). So the Databank's ejected energy is the heat the ejecta gave the calorimeter, including what burned inside it in limited oxygen, plus an estimated sensible heat of the gas and smoke that escaped: the heat of burning the escaped gas in air is not in it. Walker et al., J. Power Sources 415 (2019) 207, named by the brief, is behind a subscription and was not read; its method is taken from the Part 1 manuscript, which describes the same calorimeter.

D151. A0.3, the tests' needs (`src/stage14_needs.py`, `results/stage14_needs.csv`). Nothing is run. For each of the sixteen tests, the delivered energy the model needs, read from Stages 8, 9 and 12, each with the delivery through the facing half of D77 (4) (a run depends on f and E_e only through their product, D122 (7)):
(1) with the 21.39 s triangular delivery: for the nine propagated tests, f_t(C2) from Stage 12's B2 (`results/stage12_fixed_ejecta.csv`), matched in M50_PP_Exp1 (2,974.4 J), PP_Exp2 (5,962.4 J), PP_Exp4 (1,819.0 J), ST_Exp4 (1,184.0 J), SP_Exp1 (2,725.6 J), SP_Exp2 (3,783.4 J) and SP_Exp3 (263.5 J), the seven matched times of the brief, and at the threshold in PT_Exp4 (4,656.9 to 4,851.0 J) and ST_Exp3 (11,950.0 to 12,111.5 J); for the seven that did not propagate, the registered f_max at the far face (`results/stage9_fractions.csv`): PT_Exp1 2,090.7 J, 0 in PT_Exp3, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp1 and ST_Exp2, and the exploratory PT_Exp2 75,814.9 J (808 C) and SP_Exp4 32,667.0 J (626 C);
(2) with a constant power over 120, 240 and 360 s (Stage 9's Part B, `results/stage9_long.csv`), the same readings as recorded there (PT_Exp2's exploratory searches under 240 and 360 s have no registered value);
(3) the thresholds f*(C1) and f*(C2) of Stage 8 (`results/stage8_thresholds.csv`) in joules, as Stage 12's B1 narrowed them where it searched (`results/stage12_fixed_ejecta.csv`).
A need is the energy delivered uniformly over the facing half within its duration; a path that delivers to a patch, or over minutes, meets a different threshold (Stage 13 is working out how the threshold depends on place and duration), and each bound below names the reading it should be compared with.

D152. A0.4, the survey and its second reading (`results/stage14_survey.md` and `.json`, `results/stage14_inputs.csv` with its source list `results/stage14_inputs_sources.csv`, `src/stage14_survey.py`, `src/stage14_inputs.py`).
(1) The searches. Forty-three, written and committed before any was run (c8172e2, pushed at 11:13:23 UTC on 16 November as the branch's first push, by GitHub's activity log), then run on 16 November with the web search tool in standard mode; the candidates each returned are in the JSON. One search was added afterwards, to find the full reference of a resistance study that a page found under search 39 cites. The shell of this machine reaches none of the publishers, so web sources were read through a text reader, which answers questions about a page; the supplement, the 2023 manuscript, the Databank's workbook and Lienhard's textbook were downloaded by the author into a folder this chat can read, and were read here in full. Walker et al. (2019) and Abbott et al. (2022) are behind subscriptions: not read (Abbott's values come from its vendor's summary, labelled so).
(2) What the survey found and did not find. Every value that enters a bound, and every value a statement of Part B relies on, is a row of the inputs table, with its source, page, words, conditions and whose it is (this cell, this rig, other cell, relation, model, limit), and the bound it enters, or "survey"; the survey's record also names values that enter neither (corrected after the independent read, D162 (6)). What no source gives, for any cell of this format: a radiant fraction of a vent flame; a heat flux or deposited energy at a neighbouring cylindrical cell's place; a gas temperature measured at a 21700 vent; a 21700 vent velocity; the share of vent gas that passes a neighbour, or a velocity or temperature beside it; the share of the ejecta that leaves through a breach, a breach's opening or its jet's velocity; the temperature of ejected solids, or the mass of solids and melt found on a neighbour; the material of the holder's inserts or any temperature of the holder; a current or voltage of parallel cells during a neighbour's failure, or a nail short's resistance. The largest values that enter the bounds are measured on other cells: the heat released in open air (136.5 kJ, a 4.8 Ah 21700 NMC cell), the flame temperature (1500 C, a module, cited by Chen et al. 2020), the vent velocity (398.5 m/s, an LG MJ1 18650 cell) and the longest combustion (about 5 min, a 21700 NCA cell); each widens a range and sets no value for this cell.
(3) The second reading (check V1). A separate agent, which had not seen the survey made, opened every one of the 89 rows at the place it cites (the 80 rows written first and nine rows of Lienhard's Table A.6 added while it read), recomputed the Databank's rows from the workbook, and wrote `results/stage14_v1_second_reading.csv` and `.md` on 16 and 17 November (it stopped at a usage limit and resumed from its saved state). It found 71 rows agreeing, 17 needing a correction and one that cites no source (chi_rad, a limit by definition). The corrections, all applied in `src/stage14_inputs.py` before the predictions (the inputs table records each row's verdict in a column `second_reading`): the shortest published runaway is Gillich et al.'s measured 1.131 +- 0.291 s, not the bound "lower than 1.5 s", so the time bounds start at 0.84 s; Vendra et al.'s Table 2 prints test 2's total heat release as 136.5 kJ though its printed parts sum to 127.3 kJ (tests 1 and 3 add up), and since the source does not say which number is wrong, the upper bound keeps the printed 136.5 kJ, the larger; the sentence of p. 7 that reports fire at the vent describes a P42A sidewall melt, and no sentence ties a fire to an M50 test by name (D150 (2)); Vendra et al.'s anode is "carbon", not graphite; the Sandia page gives the M50's electrolyte share without a citation, for "similar M50 cells"; Lugaresi et al.'s 50 to 150 kW/m2 is the first flaming event of most cells, the 26650 cells' later peaks reaching 350 to 500 kW/m2; Chen et al. (2023) count 2 ruptures of 6 at 6 mm/s and report 1 of 5 at 0.5 mm/s and 4 of 5 at 100 mm/s; Karp and Sica's combustion energies are calculated for complete combustion, not measured; and nine locators (the chamber, pp. 192-193; the holder, p. 194; the categories, p. 4; the 2023 caption, pp. 5-6; Csernyei and Straatman's Eq. 16, p. 302; the pages of Karp and Sica, of Mier et al.'s slides and of NASA's slides). Two pages remain unconfirmed: Pfaff et al.'s rest on one reading, and Popp et al.'s p. 172 cannot be checked because the web copy has no page numbers; Sharp et al.'s sentence lies on the eighth or ninth page of a copy without page numbers. No value that enters a bound changed but the time bounds' lower end.

## Stage 14 design, recorded with the predictions before any bound is computed (16 and 17 November)

D153. The bounds (brief of 16 November, Part A). Every number below is a row of `results/stage14_inputs.csv` (D152), named by its key; the code is `src/stage14_bounds.py`, run only after `predictions_stage14.md` is pushed.
(1) What a bound is here. For each path, the energy it delivers to the facing half (A_f = pi R L = 2.3234e-3 m2, R = 10.55 mm, L = 70.1 mm) has a lower and an upper bound, and so has the time over which it arrives. An input recorded only on other cells enters with its range, the envelope of every value recorded on this cell and on others, and the upper bound takes the end that makes it largest; no value narrows a range. A share or fraction that no source gives enters at 1 in the upper bound and at 0 in the lower. No source shows any path acting on the neighbour with a measured amount, so every lower bound is 0. The upper bounds are given for the energy delivered within 21.39 s (the model's delivery), within 120, 240 and 360 s (Stage 9's long deliveries) and in total. A bound is what the published inputs cannot be made to exceed; it says nothing of what happened in a test.
(2) The energy the ejecta can release outside the trigger in this rig. E_avail = db_Eej_max + vendra_Qt_max = 89,337.78 + 136,500 = 225,837.78 J. The first is the largest ejected energy of the ten calorimetry tests of this cell (D150 (3)): the heat of the ejecta, with what burned in the calorimeter's limited oxygen, but not the heat of burning the escaped gas in air. The second is the largest total heat release of an NMC 21700 cell burning in open air (Vendra et al., another cell: 96.2 to 136.5 kJ, from oxygen consumption with a correction for the oxygen the cathode releases), which covers that burning; in this rig the gas burned (D150 (2)). The sum counts twice what the calorimeter already burned, which an upper bound allows. Everything the trigger can release, its body included, is E_global = db_Etotal_max + vendra_Qt_max = 110,833.03 + 136,500 = 247,333.03 J.
(3) When a path is determinable. A path's upper bound is determinable when it follows from quantities that sources give for the path's carrier and from shares and fractions that sources give, that geometry fixes or that cannot exceed 1. The carrier of A1 to A4 is the ejecta, gas, liquid and solids, whose energy calorimetry measured (E_avail); that of A6 is the neighbour's stored charge (db_stored_energy). A path whose relation needs a quantity that no source gives and that is not a share (a material's conductivity, a contact's conductance, the heat a part receives) is not determinable; the cap that energy conservation alone sets (E_global) is stated beside it, not as its bound.
(4) A1, radiation from the jet or flame of a cap-end vent. The flame is taken anywhere outside the cells beyond the end plane of the trigger's venting end (z >= L for the positive end; the same at z <= 0 for the negative end, where this cell also vented in calorimetry); a flame between the end planes is gas that passes the neighbour, (5). Two terms, and the bound is the smaller:
- energy: U1E = absorptivity x chi_rad x w_sup x E_avail, with absorptivity and chi_rad at 1 (no source gives either for these surfaces and flames: rows absorptivity, chi_rad) and w_sup the largest share of an isotropic emitter's directions whose first surface is the facing half, over every emitter position in that region. The ray tracing (`src/stage14_view.py`; 2,000,000 directions per point, seed 14) gives w on a grid: on the trigger's axis beyond its end at 0, 0.5, 1, 2, 5 and 10 R; over the trigger's end face and the column in front of it, at the points of a polar grid of the face (radius 0 to R in steps of R/4, angle in steps of 30 degrees) at 0.01 R, 0.1 R and R beyond the plane; and on points that approach the facing half's end edge, at angles of 0 to 90 degrees from the line of contact in steps of 15 degrees, radially 0.3, 0.1, 0.03 and 0.01 R outside the can and 1e-6 R beyond the end plane. An emitter at the edge sees the facing half in a quarter of all directions (the tangent plane fills a quarter of the sphere; the semi-infinite cylinder's share arcsin(R/rho)/(2 pi) tends to 1/4 as rho tends to R), so w_sup is the larger of 1/4 and the largest value on the grid. The first two parts of the grid are reported, not used: they say what a flame held on the vent's axis, or in front of the trigger's end, would give.
- rate: U1R(t) = absorptivity x sigma x T_flame_max^4 x A_f x F_end x t, with T_flame_max = 1500 C (1773.15 K) and F_end the cosine-weighted share of the facing half's view that leaves through the end plane z = L without meeting a cell (ray tracing, 4,000,000 rays, seed 15): the most the facing half can receive from a black flame at the largest published flame temperature filling everything beyond that plane. With both ends venting, the rate term doubles; that is reported beside.
Total: min(U1E, U1R(t_flame_max = 300 s)); within a window tau, min(U1E, U1R(tau)). Time: 0.84 to 300 s (the lower end of t_vent_min, 1.131 +- 0.291 s for an 18650 cell's runaway, and t_flame_max); the shortest time in which U1E could arrive at the rate term's power is reported. Radiation reflected by the cells or the holder is not counted (the holder's shape and surfaces are not given; the cells are painted black). Where it lands: on the facing half near the venting end, so it should be set beside a local reading (C1) and the long deliveries rather than the uniform 21.39 s reading.
(5) A2, heat from vented gas that passes the neighbour: gas between the end planes, beside the facing half, burning or not. The brief's "convection" is taken with the radiation of that gas included, so that no exchange with gas beside the neighbour falls between A1 and A2. The share of the vent gas that passes: buoyancy lifts hot gas away from the neighbour, which lies below the trigger, and the vent jet leaves along the axis away from the cells; but gas can gather in the two wedges beside the line of contact, whose roof is the trigger, and no source measures the share, the velocity or the temperature there, so the share is at most 1. Two terms, and the bound is the smaller:
- energy: U2E = share x E_avail = E_avail;
- rate: U2R(t) = [h_max (T_gas_max - T0) + sigma T_gas_max^4 (1 - F_trig)] x A_f x t, with T_gas_max = T_flame_max, T0 = 25 C, F_trig the cosine-weighted share of the facing half's view that ends on the trigger (ray tracing, as F_end), and h_max = Nu k / D at the film temperature (T_gas_max + T0)/2 = 1035.65 K, D = 2R, Re = u_vent_max D / nu with u_vent_max = 398.5 m/s. Nu is Churchill and Bernstein's (Lienhard eqn (7.65)); for 40,000 < Re < 400,000, where eqn (7.65) "underpredicts most of the data by about 20%", the larger of 1.2 times (7.65) and eqn (7.68). The properties are air's (Table A.6), interpolated linearly at the film temperature, and hydrogen's (Table A.6) at 1000 K, its highest row; h_max is the larger of the two (hydrogen is 22 % of the vent gas of Vendra et al.).
Total: min(U2E, U2R(300 s)); within tau, min(U2E, U2R(tau)). Time: 0.84 to 300 s. Where: anywhere on the facing half; a delivery over the whole half compares with C2 and the uniform reading, one confined to the wedges with C1.
(6) A3, the jet of a sidewall breach. A breach on the trigger's sidewall at the angle phi from the line of contact (phi = 0 faces the neighbour), at mid-length. A narrow jet along the outward normal meets the neighbour if 2R |sin phi| < R, that is |phi| < 30 degrees, and then strikes the facing half at an angle from the line of contact between 0 and 60 degrees: a sixth of the circumference sends such a jet onto the facing half. A jet that spreads strikes over a wider range: for each phi from 0 to 180 degrees in steps of 5 degrees and each cone half-angle beta of 0, 5, 10, 15, 30, 45, 60 and 90 degrees, the ray tracing (1,000,000 directions uniform in the cone, seed 16) gives the share s(phi, beta) of the cone whose first surface is the facing half; s_max(phi) = the largest over beta (no source gives a breach jet's spread). The energy the jet carries is at most E_avail (no source gives the share of the ejecta that leaves through a breach; the Databank's ten tests had none), and the share of it given to the surface at most 1. So U3(phi) = s_max(phi) x E_avail in every window that can hold the whole runaway: the shortest published runaway lasts about 1.1 s (1.131 +- 0.291 s, an 18650 cell; about 2 s in Sharp et al.'s nail tests), so the energy can arrive within 21.39 s. No rate term is determinable: a breach's opening, its jet's velocity and its distance from the neighbour have no source, and Csernyei and Straatman's relation for jets on a cylinder (row csernyei_jet) does not reach a jet that starts against the can. Time: 0.35 s (fr2023_breach_time) to 300 s. Where: a patch around the point struck; C1. The share of events in which a breach occurs (Part C): 3 of 16 tests in this rig (category 4), 0 of 10 in calorimetry, 3 of 3 and 0 in Read et al. with deep and shallow nails (this cell), 2 of 6 and 1.1 to 5.2 % on other cells.
(7) A4, solids and melt on or against the facing half. Gravity: the trigger lies above. From a breach on the trigger's lower half (|phi| < 90 degrees), melt that falls straight down lands on the facing half, since the neighbour spans the same width; from the upper half it runs down the trigger and leaves it at x = +-R, which meets the neighbour only at 90 degrees: half of the circumference, against a sixth for a narrow jet (6). From a cap-end vent, a solid in free flight that leaves the end face moving away from it cannot come back past the end plane (gravity acts across the axis), so it can reach the facing half only by rebounding off the holder or the chamber, by being carried back by the gas, or as melt that runs down the trigger's end face; no source gives these amounts. The solids and melt are part of the ejecta, so U4 = E_avail, in every window (no source gives the solids' temperature, the mass that stays on the neighbour or its contact, so no rate term is determinable). Time: they arrive with the ejection, 0.84 to 300 s; how long they take to give up their heat is not given. Where: patches; C1.
(8) A5, conduction through the holder and fixtures beyond the contact and the tabs the model has. Not determinable by (3): the holder's frame is aluminium and its 3D-printed inserts touch each cell over about 2 mm per fixture (rig_holder), but the inserts' material, their shape and the holder's temperature are given nowhere, and nothing measures the heat the holder receives from the trigger's can or from the flames. The measurement that would close it: the inserts' material (its conductivity) and their contact with the cells, or a thermocouple on the holder at the neighbour's ends during a test. Beside it, E_global = 247,333.03 J, what energy conservation alone allows.
(9) A6, the neighbour's own discharge into the shorted trigger (parallel tests, 8 of 16). The heat released inside the neighbour while it discharges through the trigger's short and the tabs. Its energy term: the stored energy at full charge, db_stored_energy = 65,520 J (18.2 Wh), all of which is released inside the neighbour if the outer path has no resistance; the reversible heat of the cell reaction, which no source gives for this cell, is left out. Its rate term at 25 C: V_ocv_max^2 x t / R_dc_min = 4.189^2 t / 0.024 W, the power the neighbour dissipates when the outer path is a short, with the datasheet's lowest DC resistance at 25 C (30 +- 6 mOhm) and the largest open-circuit voltage of the ten calorimetry tests; the resistance falls as the cell warms (about fivefold from 10 to 45 C, row R_temperature) and no source gives it above 45 C, so the rate term binds only a neighbour at 25 C and is reported beside, conditional. The registered bound is the energy term in every window. Time: the discharge's duration is not given. Where: in the roll, by volume, not through the facing half: it compares with Stage 8's Part B (3,276 and 13,104 J, 5 and 20 % of the stored energy, released in the roll over 21.39 s, D80).
(10) What would narrow each bound (Part A asks for it; a column of the bounds table): A1, the flame's position and extent beside the cells during venting (a side view in visible light), its temperature, or a radiometer at the facing half's place, and the radiant fraction of such flames; A2, the gas's temperature and velocity in the wedges beside the line of contact during venting (a thermocouple or a heat-flux gauge there), or the share of the vent gas that passes; A3, where breaches face and how much of the ejecta leaves through them, and the jet's velocity and opening; A4, the mass and temperature of the debris and melt found on the facing half after a test, or a heat-flux gauge there; A5, the inserts' material and contact, or a thermocouple on the holder at the neighbour's ends; A6, the current between the cells in a parallel test (a shunt in a tab) and the neighbour's resistance at its temperature.
(11) The ray tracing's counts and seeds are fixed here: point shares with 2,000,000 directions (seed 14 for the grid, a fresh draw for each point from one generator), F_end and F_trig with 4,000,000 rays (seed 15), the breach shares with 1,000,000 directions (seed 16). The standard error of each share is reported beside it.

D154. Parts B and C (brief; `src/stage14_tests.py`, arithmetic only).
(1) B1. For each of the sixteen tests and each path, the path's upper bound in the window of each need (21.39 s; 120, 240 and 360 s where a long-delivery need is recorded) against that need (D151): "reaches" when the bound is at least the need's upper end, "reaches part" when it lies between the need's ends, "does not reach" below its lower end; a need of 0 is reached by any bound and is recorded as "needs nothing". A6 is set against the parallel tests only (in series there is no such path). A3 is set against every test, with its category beside it; A5 is "not determinable".
(2) B2. The nine propagated tests ranked by their need at 21.39 s (its upper end); the three that need the most, with their categories; the needs of the three tests in category 4; whether those that need the most are those whose category allows a path whose bound within 21.39 s reaches the largest need (A3 needs a trigger rupture, category 4; A1, A2 and A4 act wherever the trigger vents, that is in every test); and, at each duration, whether any test needs more than the sum of every determinable upper bound.
(3) B3. For M50_PT_Exp2 (808 C) and M50_SP_Exp4 (626 C), which did not propagate: for each path, whether its source can be hotter than the reading (the published flame and gas temperatures for A1 and A2, up to 1500 C; molten copper in the ejecta for A4, Sharp et al.; the jet of a breach for A3, which neither test's category shows) and whether it delivers to a patch, so that a surface thermocouple could read that far while the roll stays below its onset. Stated as possibilities, not built on; the paper's own remark that cells and thermocouples moved (D89 (5)) is set beside.
(4) Part C, the module table (`results/stage14_module_table.csv`): one row per path with the carrier, the lower and upper bounds of the delivered energy within 21.39, 120, 240 and 360 s and in total, the bounds of the time, where on the neighbour it delivers and which reading it compares with, whether it is determinable, and the share of events in which it acts where a source gives one (A3: 3 of 16 in this rig; A6: the 8 parallel tests of 16; for A1, A2 and A4 the calorimetry's ten tests all vented, and the rig's text reports fire without a count). No shape is asserted inside a bound.

D155. Scoring of P1 to P6 (`predictions_stage14.md`; `src/stage14_score.py`, `results/stage14_scorecard.json`). P1 holds if A1's total upper bound is below 0.02 x 76,741.25 J = 1,534.825 J. P2 holds if the sum of the upper bounds within 21.39 s of the determinable paths among A1, A2 and A5 is below 5,962.4 J (M50_PP_Exp2's need at 21.39 s, the largest of the seven matched times); P3 if the same sum exceeds 263.5 J (M50_SP_Exp3's). P4 holds if A3's upper bound for a breach whose jet faces the neighbour (the largest over phi) within 21.39 s exceeds 5,962.4 J. P5 holds if at least one of A1 to A6 is not determinable by D153 (3). P6 holds if A6's registered upper bound in total exceeds 1,000 J. Beside each, unscored: P1 within 21.39 s and with the flame held on the vent's axis or in front of the trigger's end; P2 and P3 with the totals; P6 within 21.39 s with the rate term at 25 C. A failure counts as foreseen if `predictions_stage14.md` records it as fixed by the design.

D156. Checks (brief). V1, the second reading of every input by a separate agent, is part of A0 (D152 (3)). V2: every bound recomputed by separate code from the inputs table alone (`src/stage14_v2.py`), which imports nothing of the study's and traces its own rays with its own intersection code and its own seeds; acceptance: every bound within four standard errors of the ray tracing's shares (propagated to the bound), and every term that uses no ray tracing within 1e-9 relative. V3: the units of every relation and one hand case per path, written in DECISIONS after the bounds. V4: the ray tracing against known configurations (`src/test_stage14.py`: the solid angle of a disc from its axis at three heights, a long cylinder seen from its end plane at three distances, and the crossed-strings view factor of two touching cylinders, 2 (pi/2 - 1)/pi = 0.3634 from the facing half), run before the predictions and again with the bounds; acceptance: four standard errors, plus the finite length's share where the configuration is infinite. V5: Part B recomputed by separate code from `results/stage14_bounds.csv` and `results/stage14_needs.csv` (`src/stage14_v5.py`); acceptance: every verdict identical. Then an independent read of every registered number and verdict by a separate agent that has not seen the work produced, before the note's and the README's Stage 14 lines are written.

D157. Code, files and what is not done. `src/stage14_needs.py` (A0.3), `src/stage14_inputs.py` (writes the inputs table and its source list), `src/stage14_view.py` (the ray tracing), `src/test_stage14.py` (V4's known configurations: run on 16 November before the predictions, all seven cases within their allowance), and, after the push, `src/stage14_bounds.py`, `src/stage14_tests.py`, `src/stage14_score.py`, `src/stage14_v2.py`, `src/stage14_v5.py` and `src/make_figure_stage14.py` (one figure, `results/stage14_figure.png` and `.pdf`: each path's bounds as bars on a logarithmic energy axis, with the sixteen tests' needs marked). Outputs: `results/stage14_survey.md` and `.json`, `results/stage14_inputs.csv`, `results/stage14_needs.csv`, `results/stage14_bounds.csv`, `results/stage14_tests.csv`, `results/stage14_module_table.csv`, `results/stage14_scorecard.json`. No model run is made, nothing is added to the model, and no registered result changes. No merge into main, no tag, no release and no Zenodo version until the author says so from the main chat, which merges Stage 13 first.

## Stage 14 results, recorded after the bounds were computed (17 November)

D158. Part A, the bounds (`src/stage14_bounds.py`, run after `predictions_stage14.md` was pushed in commit 4dcc174d1ab09324d503a01ef8ef7996adf49baf at 03:42:37 UTC on 17 November; `results/stage14_bounds.csv` and `.json`, `results/stage14_raytrace.json`). The ejecta can release at most E_avail = 225,837.78 J outside the trigger in this rig; everything the trigger can release is at most E_global = 247,333.03 J. Every lower bound is 0.
(1) A1. The share w of an emitter's directions whose first surface is the facing half: on the trigger's axis beyond its end, 0 at the end face, 0.00334 at 0.5 R, 0.00364 +- 0.00004 at 1 R (the largest on the grid; finer scans find 0.0037 to 0.0038 near 0.85 R, D162), 0.00204 at 2 R, 0.00050 at 5 R and 0.00012 at 10 R; in front of the trigger's end face, at most 0.1217 +- 0.0002 on the grid, at the rim 30 degrees from the line of contact and 0.01 R beyond the plane, where the trigger's rim overhangs the wedge beside the line of contact (nearer the plane and the line of contact the share rises toward 1/4: 0.2272 at 2 degrees and 1e-4 R, D162); toward the facing half's end edge, from 30 to 75 degrees, 0.12 to 0.14 at 0.3 R from the can and about 0.18 at 0.1 R, and from 15 to 75 degrees, about 0.21 at 0.03 R and 0.2277 +- 0.0003 at 0.01 R, rising toward 1/4; at 90 degrees, the facing half's border, about half as much; at 0 degrees, and at 15 degrees farther than 0.03 R, nothing, because those points lie in front of the trigger's end face. So w_sup = 0.25. The facing half's view: F_trig = 0.35566 +- 0.00024 (the infinite cylinders' 0.3634, less what the open ends take) and F_end = 0.32234 +- 0.00023 through each end plane. The energy term is 0.25 x 225,837.78 = 56,459.4 J; the rate term 419.78 W (839.56 W with both ends venting). The bound: 8,979.1 J within 21.39 s, 50,373.8 J within 120 s, 56,459.4 J within 240 s, within 360 s and in total, the energy term binding from 134.5 s on. Time: 0.84 to 300 s. Beside it, not the bound: a flame held on the vent's axis would deliver at most about 0.0037 to 0.0038 of 225,837.78 J, 843 to 849 J (1.1 % of 76.74 kJ; the grid's 0.0036355 gave 821.0 J), and one held in front of the trigger's end face up to a quarter of it, as anywhere beyond the plane (the grid's 0.1217 gave 27,490.0 J; D162). This bound counts direct radiation from one end; the independent read showed that the stage's own rule asks for more (D162).
(2) A2. Air at 1035.65 K: Re 64,605, Nu 196.4 (1.2 times eqn (7.65), 163.6, against eqn (7.68), 185.0), h 646.6 W/(m2 K); hydrogen at 1000 K: Re 9,965, Nu 52.46 (eqn (7.65)), h 1,143.6 W/(m2 K), the larger. Convective flux 1.687 MW/m2, radiative 0.361 MW/m2; rate term 4,758.2 W. The bound: 101,778.8 J within 21.39 s, and the energy term, 225,837.8 J, from 47.5 s on. Time: 0.84 to 300 s.
(3) A3. A narrow jet strikes the facing half from a breach less than 30 degrees from the line of contact, a sixth of the circumference (at exactly 30 degrees it grazes the neighbour, and the code's rounding counted the grazing ray as a strike: a spreading jet gives about 0.50 there, D162); from 35 to 85 degrees only a spreading jet does, a hemispherical spray best, with 0.435 of its directions at 35 degrees falling to 0.025 at 85; from 90 degrees on, nothing. For a breach facing the neighbour s_max = 1: 225,837.8 J in every window. Time: registered as 0.35 to 300 s; no source gives a breach jet's shortest duration (D162).
(4) A4: 225,837.8 J in every window, melt from a breach falling onto the facing half from half of the circumference. A5: not determinable; energy conservation alone caps it at 247,333.0 J. A6: 65,520 J in every window, the rated energy, with the excess of the open-circuit voltage and the reversible heat left out (D162); beside it, at 25 C the rate term is 731.2 W and gives 15,639.4 J within 21.39 s. Stage 8's bracket, 3,276 and 13,104 J, lies inside the bound.

D159. Parts B and C (`src/stage14_tests.py`; `results/stage14_tests.csv`, `results/stage14_partb.json`, `results/stage14_module_table.csv`).
(1) B1, within 21.39 s: A2, A3 and A4 reach the need of each of the twelve tests that need something (four need nothing: M50_PT_Exp3, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp2). A1 reaches nine and does not reach three: ST_Exp3 at its threshold (11,950.0 to 12,111.5 J) and the exploratory peaks of PT_Exp2 (75,814.9 J) and SP_Exp4 (32,667.0 J). A6 reaches five of the six parallel tests that need something; it does not reach PT_Exp2 (75,814.9 J against 65,520 J), and it compares with the roll's heating, not with a delivery through the facing half. Over 120 s, A1 reaches all but PT_Exp2 (96,874.6 J); over 240 and 360 s, all but SP_Exp4 (76,863.6 and 115,295.4 J), PT_Exp2 having no registered value there; A6 over 120 s misses PT_Exp2 again.
(2) B2. The three tests that need the most within 21.39 s: ST_Exp3 (12,111.5 J; category 1; series), PP_Exp2 (5,962.4 J; category 2; parallel) and PT_Exp4 (4,851.0 J; category 4; parallel). The paths whose bound within 21.39 s reaches the largest need are A2, A3, A4 and A6, but in that test, a series test without a rupture, only A2 and A4 can act. A2 and A4 act in every test, so no category shuts the large paths out; A3 needs a trigger rupture, which of the three only PT_Exp4 had; A6 needs a parallel test. The categories do not single out the tests that need the most: the largest need is a category 1 test, and of the three trigger ruptures only PT_Exp4 propagated, while PT_Exp3 and ST_Exp2 did not and need nothing at their peaks. No test needs more than E_avail at any duration: the largest need is 115,295.4 J (SP_Exp4's exploratory peak over 360 s). The arithmetic sums of the determinable bounds (within 21.39 s, 562,433.5 J in series and 627,953.5 J in parallel) count E_avail more than once and exceed what energy conservation allows, 247,333.0 J.
(3) B3, stated as possibilities and not built on: for PT_Exp2 (808 C; category 1; parallel) and SP_Exp4 (626 C; no category; series), a flame or hot gas at up to 1500 C touching the thermocouple or radiating onto its patch (A1, A2), or hot solids and melt resting on it (A4), could heat a surface thermocouple that far while the roll stays below its onset; a breach's jet (A3) could too, but neither test's category shows a rupture; A6 heats the roll by volume and cannot raise one surface reading far above it; A5 is not determinable. The paper's remark that cells and thermocouples moved (D89 (5)) stands beside.
(4) Part C, the module table: for each path its carrier, the lower bound (0), the upper bounds within 21.39, 120, 240 and 360 s and in total, the bounds of the time, where on the neighbour it delivers and which reading it compares with, whether it is determinable, the share of events in which it acts where a source gives one, and what would narrow it.

D160. Scoring (`src/stage14_score.py`, `results/stage14_scorecard.json`). P1 failed: A1's total bound is 56,459.4 J against 1,534.8 J (within 21.39 s, 8,979.1 J); held on the vent's axis, a flame would give at most about 849 J, below the line. P2 failed: A1 and A2 within 21.39 s sum to 110,757.9 J against 5,962.4 J (A5 not determinable; in total 282,297.2 J). P3 held: the same sum exceeds 263.5 J. P4 held: 225,837.8 J within 21.39 s. P5 held: A5 is not determinable under D153 (3) as written; the independent read showed that the rule sets A5 apart from A3 and A4 by its wording, and read as they are, A5 would be bounded by 247,333.0 J and P5 would fail (D162). P6 held: 65,520 J (within 21.39 s at 25 C, 15,639.4 J). Each verdict is the one `predictions_stage14.md` recorded as fixed by the design, so both failures were foreseen. Stage 14: six predictions, two failed.

D161. Checks V2 to V5 (D156). V2 (`src/stage14_v2.py`, `results/stage14_v2.json`): separate code from the inputs table alone, with its own intersection code, samplers and seeds, reproduced every bound within its allowance and, for each of the 37 breach angles, the largest share over the cones within four standard errors (the independent read compared all 296 shares, D162) (w_sup 0.25 in both; F_end 0.32248 against 0.32234; F_trig 0.35585 against 0.35566; on the axis 0.00358 against 0.00364; in front of the end 0.1220 against 0.1217; at the edge 0.2279 against 0.2277; h_max 1,143.6 W/(m2 K) in both). V4 (`results/stage14_v4.txt`): the seven known configurations rerun with the bounds, all within their allowance. V5 (`src/stage14_v5.py`, `results/stage14_v5.json`): the 372 verdicts of B1 recomputed by separate code, all identical, and B2's sums agree. V3, the units and one hand case per path: A1, sigma T^4 A_f F_end in W/(m2 K4) x K4 x m2 = W: 5.670374419e-8 x 1773.15^4 = 560.52 kW/m2, x 2.32338e-3 m2 = 1,302.3 W, x 0.3223365 = 419.78 W, x 21.39 s = 8,979.1 J. A2, h = Nu k / D in W/(m K) / m: hydrogen at 1000 K, Re = 398.5 x 0.0211 / 8.438e-4 = 9,965; the factor 0.62 Re^0.5 Pr^(1/3) / [1 + (0.4/Pr)^(2/3)]^(1/4) = 0.62 x 99.82 x 0.8772 / 1.1428 = 47.51; [1 + (Re/282,000)^(5/8)]^(4/5) = 1.0979; Nu = 0.3 + 52.16 = 52.46; h = 52.46 x 0.460 / 0.0211 = 1,143.6 W/(m2 K); with 1,475 K, 1.687 MW/m2. A3, for a breach 20 degrees from the line of contact the narrow jet travels t = R [(2 cos 20 - 1) - (4 cos^2 20 - 3)^(1/2)] = 0.1499 R and strikes the facing half at (0.3933 R, 0.9194 R), 23.2 degrees from the line of contact, on the neighbour's surface (radius 1.0000 R). A4, 89,337.78 J + 136,500 J = 225,837.78 J. A5, 110,833.03 J + 136,500 J = 247,333.03 J (the cap beside it). A6, 18.2 Wh x 3,600 s/h = 65,520 J; V^2/R in V2/Ohm = W: 4.189^2 / 0.024 = 731.16 W, x 21.39 s = 15,639.4 J.

## Stage 14 after the independent read (17 November)

D162. The independent read (`results/stage14_independent_read.md`) and what followed (`src/stage14_corrections.py`; `results/stage14_bounds_corrected.csv`, `results/stage14_tests_corrected.csv`, `results/stage14_module_table_corrected.csv`, `results/stage14_corrections.json`).
(1) The read. A separate agent that had not seen the work produced recomputed, from the inputs table and with its own code, every registered number and verdict: its own ray tracer in another frame reproduced the 181 emitter points and the 296 breach shares within sampling error; an exact quadrature gave F_trig = 0.355663 and F_end = 0.322168 (the study's 0.35566 and 0.32234); the study's scripts reproduced their committed outputs byte for byte; the needs are what the earlier files hold; the 372 verdicts of B1 and P1 to P6 come out as registered. It found seventeen points, taken up below.
(2) Four registered bounds are not what D153 (1) asks of an upper bound. They are corrected beside the registered ones, whose files stay as computed.
- A1 left out reflected radiation and counted one venting end. D153 (1) puts a fraction that no source gives at 1; no source gives the reflectivity of the holder's aluminium frame, and with it at 1 the holder's shape, which no source gives either, would decide what reaches the facing half. So only the caps that need no geometry remain: a passive surrounding cannot raise radiance, so the facing half's irradiation is at most sigma T^4 over its whole view, 1,302.3 W, and the energy at most E_avail. Corrected A1: 27,856.4 J within 21.39 s, 156,277.1 J within 120 s, and 225,837.8 J from 173.4 s on. Direct radiation from both ends (this cell vented at both in five of the ten calorimetry tests, and Sharp et al. report both vents opening) would give 17,949.0 J within 21.39 s.
- A2's radiation left out the rays that end on the trigger, though gas gathered in the wedges lies along them, and its convection applied Churchill and Bernstein's mean over the whole circumference to one half. With the whole view, and a half's mean taken at most twice the whole's (the other half's is not negative; no source gives the half's own), the rate term is 9,140.5 W: 195,515.9 J within 21.39 s, and E_avail from 24.7 s on. This holds for a cross flow; a jet aimed at the facing half is A3's case.
- A3: at exactly 30 degrees the code's rounding counted a grazing ray as a strike (a spreading jet gives 0.5005 there); and 0.35 s, the interval between two breaches of a P42A cell, is not the shortest duration of a breach's jet, which no source gives.
- A6: the rated 18.2 Wh is delivered at a terminal voltage below the open-circuit voltage; the excess at open-circuit voltage and the reversible heat of the cell reaction, which no source gives for this cell, are left out, and the bound holds with them left out.
(3) Under the corrected bounds four verdicts of B1 change, all for A1 and all from "does not reach" to "reaches": M50_ST_Exp3 within 21.39 s, PT_Exp2 within 120 s, and SP_Exp4 within 240 and 360 s. Within 21.39 s A1 then misses only the two exploratory peaks (32,667.0 and 75,814.9 J). P1 to P6 come out as registered, P1 and P2 failing by more (A1 in total 225,837.8 J; A1 and A2 within 21.39 s 223,372.3 J) and P3 holding by more.
(4) P5 rests on the wording of D153 (3). A3 and A4 count as determinable because what they carry is at most a share of a measured energy; the holder too can pass to the neighbour at most a share of what the trigger releases, which calorimetry measures (E_global = 247,333.0 J), and A4's own relation needs quantities that no source gives as much as A5's does. Under the rule as written A5 is not determinable and P5 holds, as the predictions recorded beforehand; read as A3 and A4 are, every path is determinable, A5 at 247,333.0 J, and P5 fails. Both readings are reported. The finding behind them does not change: no source gives the inserts' material or their contact, and only energy conservation bounds the holder.
(5) The readings beside P1 were the largest values on the grid, not maxima. On the vent's axis, a scan in steps of 0.05 R from 0.5 to 1.5 R with 4,000,000 directions per point gives at most 0.00373 +- 0.00003 (843 J; the read found 0.00376 +- 0.00002 at 0.85 R, 849 J): about 1.1 % of 76.74 kJ, still below P1's line. In front of the trigger's end face the share rises toward 1/4 at the rim beside the line of contact (0.2272 at 2 degrees and 1e-4 R beyond the plane, 0.2244 at 5 degrees; the read found the same), so a flame held in front of the end face is bounded no better than one anywhere beyond the plane. D158 (1), D160 and the figure now say so.
(6) Wording and records, corrected in place in D158 to D161: B2's list of the paths that reach the largest need now says that only A2 and A4 can act in that test; B2's sums are replaced by the statement that no need exceeds E_avail (the largest is 115,295.4 J); "0.026 at 85" is 0.025; V2 compared the largest share over the cones at each breach angle, not every share; Pr^(1/3) is 0.8772; the predictions' commit, 4dcc174, is named in D158. Three values that statements rely on (Sharp et al.'s both vents, molten copper in 90 % of their LG 21700 tests, and 30 to 54 g ejected) are now rows of the inputs table with their pages, read once, after the read. The cell's length stays the model's 70.1 mm rather than the datasheet's upper end of 70.15 mm, because the needs are the model's for its own facing half (a difference of 0.07 %).
(7) The corrected module table (`results/stage14_module_table_corrected.csv`) is the one for a sampler: A1 and A2 at their corrected bounds, A3 and A4 at E_avail, A5 not determinable (or 247,333.0 J read as A3 and A4 are), A6 at 65,520 J in parallel tests, every lower bound 0.

## Stage 15: the resolved neighbour's own runaway (Brief 26; Part A0 recorded before the predictions)

D163. The merges, made in the main chat on 18 November 2026 on the author's word. Stage 13 (branch stage13, head 7ae5124) is version 1.9.0, Stage 14 (branch stage14, head 026b9c0) version 1.10.0 and Stage 15 (branch stage15, head 88970f6, taken from stage13) version 1.11.0. Main was fast-forwarded to Stage 13, Stage 14 was merged into it and then Stage 15. Each branch had appended its sections at the same place in README.md, DECISIONS.md and note/neighbour_onset_note.md; every section is kept whole, in stage order, and no result file of any stage is changed. Changed in the texts: the three headings name their versions instead of their branches; the README's opening says which version is archived; its status list gains items 17 to 20; Stage 15's totals drop the words about Stage 14's branch (98 committed, 56 held, 42 failed, with Stage 14's six, as before). Not done: no row for Stages 13 to 15 is added to `manuscript/data/predictions_by_stage.csv`, which belongs to the submitted manuscript and ends at Stage 12 (74 committed, 36 failed); the running totals are in the README. The note is rendered again with the Stage 13 to 15 lines. D164 to D169 stay free. D128 and D129 stay kept for the record of the submission.

D170. Stage 15 (brief of 17 November): what the resolved neighbour itself does after its hot spot crosses. The manuscript judges the neighbour by two conditions, its resolved model's hottest node reaching 255.68 C (C1, the local criterion) and its lumped model reaching it (C2, the whole-cell clock), and says neither alone is a model of the neighbour's runaway; no stage has asked whether the resolved cell as a whole runs away, because a search run stops at C1. Stage 15 reads that from the archive's continued runs, builds a reactive cross-section to predict it (Part A), and makes new three-dimensional runs (Part B). The work is on branch `stage15`, taken from `stage13` at 7ae5124 because `main` holds neither Stage 13 nor Stage 14 (Stage 15 needs Stage 13's cross-section model and nothing of Stage 14). No earlier code or result is edited: the Stage 15 code is in new files, `src/stage15_inputs.py`, `stage15_archive.py`, `stage15_a0.py`, `stage15_model.py`, `stage15_section.py`, `stage15_reduced.py`, `stage15_run.py`, `stage15_partb.py`, `stage15_predictions.py`, `stage15_score.py`, `make_figure_stage15.py` and `src/test_stage15.py` (the scoring and the figure were written before the runs and are used after them), importing the earlier modules unchanged. Nothing under `manuscript/` is opened. Entries start at D170 (D163 to D169 are kept for the merges); the expected version is 1.11.0, and nothing is merged, tagged or released until the author says so from the main chat.
(1) A0.1, the archive's continued runs (`src/stage15_archive.py`, `results/stage15_archive_table.csv`). A continued run is a resolved run whose stop at the hot spot's 255.68 C was lifted ("stop": false) with the reaction on: 199 records, 126 of Stage 9 (the f_max searches of D92 and D93 (3), 96 runs, the story runs of D94 (2), 28, and V3's two), 30 of Stage 10 (the 16 history runs of D103 (1), which end at 332 s, the 8 runs of Part B's f_max searches, D104 (1), and the 6 no-delivery runs of D104 (4)), 39 of Stage 11 (the far-face runs of D114 and one run of V4, D116 (4)) and 4 of Stage 12 (Part A's capped trigger, D123 (1)). They were made on the author's laptop (195) and the cloud machine (4). For each the table holds the record's fields (stage, test, kinetics, the roll's properties, the contact, the SEI, the delivery and its energy, the hot spot's crossing and peak, the far face's peak, the reaction heat, the lumped twin's crossing, peak and reaction heat, the run's end) and, read from the stored series, the mean temperature's crossing of 255.68 C, its peak and the time of its peak, its value at the run's end, and the start of the far face's jump (D171 (2)). Every stage's stored series carries the mean (Stages 11 and 12's as well as 9 and 10's). Groups: 151 central (the posterior-mean kinetics, the central properties and contact, the fresh SEI, the base mesh and steps); 27 thermal variants and 11 contact variants of Stage 11 (their kinetics are the posterior mean too) and Stage 10's 4 at the low and high contacts; Stage 9's two discretisation checks; Stage 12's 2 capped-trigger runs at the central contact and 2 at the high. The variants are kept out of the central readings. In 92 of the 151 central runs the mean reaches 255.68 C, and in none of the 48 variant runs.
(2) V1 (brief, checks), made now: the mean's crossing and peak of all 199 runs read again by separate code (`_reader_b`: the series read from the npz archive's raw arrays through the standard library's zipfile and numpy's format reader, a plain loop, the crossing measured back from the upper sample) against the first reader (numpy on the loaded arrays): they agree to 0 s and 0 K in every run (acceptance 0.1 s and 0.01 K; `results/stage15_v1.json`). The record's own peak of the mean, taken at every step where the series is sampled every 0.1 s, lies within 0.010 K of the series' peak.
(3) The brief's five readings, redone from the table (`results/stage15_readings.json`); every number the brief gives is reproduced, with one correction of form in reading 5:
1. At the delivery at which the whole-cell clock reproduces a test's measured time, with the 21.39 s delivery (Stage 9's story runs, D94 (2)): in the six matched tests (M50_PP_Exp1 2,974 J, PP_Exp2 5,962 J, PP_Exp4 1,819 J, SP_Exp1 2,726 J, SP_Exp2 3,783 J, SP_Exp3 266 J) the lumped twin crosses at 143.5 to 268.9 s and releases 22.5 to 22.8 kJ of reaction heat; the resolved cell's hot spot crosses at 9.3 to 16.3 s and peaks at 307.6 to 466.9 C, its mean peaks at 184.7 to 204.5 C between 205 and 266 s and is 108 to 116 C at 900 s, and it releases 0.6 to 2.1 kJ. No mean reaches the level. M50_PT_Exp4 at 5,045 J (Stage 10's tri21_C2_hi): the lumped twin crosses at 215.6 s, the resolved mean peaks at 190.6 C at 209 s, and the run ends at 332 s. Reproduced.
2. With no delivery, the eight tests whose trigger alone takes the hot spot over the level (M50_PP_Exp1, PP_Exp4, ST_Exp1, ST_Exp2, ST_Exp4, SP_Exp1, SP_Exp2, SP_Exp3; Stage 9's two runs and Stage 10's six, D104 (4)): the hot spot crosses at 16.9 to 21.7 s and peaks at 262.3 to 307.1 C, the mean peaks at 169.5 to 186.2 C and the cell releases 0.40 to 0.67 kJ. None runs away; six of the eight propagated in the rig. Reproduced.
3. With 240 s of constant power the mean crosses the level at 243.5 s in M50_PP_Exp1 (3,966 J; lumped twin 146.7 s; measured 152 s), 219.6 s in PP_Exp2 (7,552 J; 154.0; 147), 221.8 s in SP_Exp2 (5,675 J; 143.8; 149), 263.2 s in PT_Exp4 (6,597 J; 171.4; 272) and 207.9 s in ST_Exp3 (16,633 J; 176.9; 177); with 360 s at 243.1, 186.9, 253.9, 283.2 (PT_Exp4, 8,926 J) and 205.5 s. It does not in PP_Exp4, SP_Exp1 and SP_Exp3 at their matched energies, nor in any 120 s run at a matched energy. M50_PT_Exp4's 240 s run at 6,209 J ends at 332 s with the mean at 241.2 C, its peak, still rising. Reproduced. (For M50_PT_Exp4 the 240 and 360 s energies of 6,597 and 8,926 J are the upper ends of Stage 9's C1 readings, D103 (1), not matched C2 energies; at its C2 reading over 240 s, 3,493 J, the mean peaks at 189.8 C.)
4. With no delivery, in the ten tests on record whose lumped twin does not itself run away, it peaks 6.3 to 53.7 K above the resolved mean (M50_PT_Exp1 67.8 against 61.5 C; PT_Exp4 183.0 against 158.1 C, to 332 s; ST_Exp1 233.2 against 179.5 C). By 900 s M50_ST_Exp2's lumped twin has taken 12,458 J through its link, the tabs included, where the resolved cell has taken 11,122 J through its contact and tabs. Reproduced.
5. M50_PT_Exp4's record (D102 (2)) reads 141.0 C at 45 s and 197.3 C at 271.5 s. In the 5,045 J run the model's far face reads 132.5 C at 45 s and 181.3 C at 271.5 s, 16.0 K low, and is falling (by 0.07 K/s over the last 10 s). In the ten runs of reading 3 whose mean crosses, the far face reads 212.4 to 225.2 C twenty seconds before the mean crosses; in the five in which its rise first slows below 0.5 K/s, it reads 227.5 to 237.7 C when its jump begins. Reproduced with the rates taken over centred 2 s differences on the 0.5 s grid of the measured record (D171 (2)); with backward differences on a 0.1 s grid the same five read 228.2 to 238.6 C.

D171. A0.2 and A0.3 (brief), fixed before any new run.
(1) The event. C8, the neighbour's own runaway: the first time within 900 s at which the resolved neighbour's mean temperature reaches 255.68 C (T_L, the level of C1 and C2, D35). The mean is the resolved model's stored Tmean: the sum of C_i T_i over the sum of C_i over the mesh's nodes, C_i each node's heat capacity, the roll's volumetric heat capacity ((61.75 - 4.5) J/K over the mesh's volume) times the node's volume weight, plus the node's share of the can's 4.5 J/K by its outer-surface weight (`v12_model.Cell2Contact.C`). The half model holds half the cell, so by symmetry its mean is the whole cell's. It is the stored heat over the heat capacity, the quantity the lumped neighbour's single temperature stands for, so the two are compared on one footing. E*(C8) is the least delivered energy for which C8 happens, for a stated delivery (its shape, duration and region, with the test's trigger, the contact, the kinetics and the SEI held). A reading at a measured time follows Stage 9's rule for f_t (D91 (3)) read on C8 in place of the crossing. A Stage 15 run stops at C8, the crossing taken linear in the mean within its step; the hot spot's crossing (C1) is recorded and does not stop it, and the lumped twin runs to 900 s.
(2) Recorded beside C8 and not scored: (a) the time at which the resolved cell's reaction heat passes 11.3 kJ, half of what the lumped twin releases when it runs away (22.5 to 22.8 kJ, D170 (3) 1); (b) the start of the far face's jump. The far face is the surface node at mid-length opposite the line of contact (theta = 180 degrees, Stage 9's thermocouple node, D89 (6)). Its rate of rise is r(t) = [T(t + 1 s) - T(t - 1 s)] / 2 s on the measured record's 0.5 s grid; from the first time r reaches 0.5 K/s, the first later time it falls below 0.5 K/s ("its rise first slows"), and then the first later time it reaches 1 K/s, before C8: the far face's reading there is the jump's start. Where there is no such time before C8, the reading 20 s before C8 stands in: the brief names one such case, the rise never slowing below 0.5 K/s; the two others, the rise slowing and not reaching 1 K/s again before C8, or never reaching 0.5 K/s, take the same stand-in, fixed here before any new run (the cross-section's run at the upper end of M50_PT_Exp4's E*(C8) bracket is one, D174 (6)). The brief states the rule in words ("over 2 s differences"); the centred differences on the 0.5 s grid are the form that reproduces its reading 5 to the decimal, and are taken (`stage15_archive.far_face_jump`). Applied to M50_PT_Exp4's record from the end of Section I (24.16 s, D102 (2)), the rule finds the record's rise slowing below 0.5 K/s at 42 s and its jump beginning at 267.5 s at 186.5 C. P5's reference is the brief's, 197.3 C, the record at 271.50 s, the onset the figure marks; the rule's own reading of the record lies 10.7 K below it, so a model reading must clear the brief's reference by that much more than a reading of both by one rule would ask.
(3) A0.3, what is measured. The nine propagation times and the sixteen neighbour maxima: Tables 3 and 4 of Fransson et al. 2024 as D64 read them (`inputs/fransson2024/m50_tests_stage7.csv`, through `stage8_inputs.tests`), with D89's reading of the time zero (the trigger's runaway) and of the thermocouple's place (the middle of the cell's length; its angle is not reported, D102 (4)). M50_PT_Exp4's history: D102 (2)'s reading of the supplement's Figure 10 (`inputs/fransson2024/pt_exp4_history.csv`, every 0.5 s, uncertainty 2.47 K and 0.45 s). Stage 10's rule for scoring a run against it (D103 (2), `stage10_score.metrics`): on the record's samples every 0.5 s from 0 to 271.50 s, the root mean square of the model's far-face history less the record (the time average of the squared difference by the trapezoid rule), the difference at 271.50 s, each history's rise over the last 60 s before it, and the times at which each first passes 100 and 150 C. A Stage 15 run that reaches C8 before 271.50 s is scored to C8.

D172. A0.4 and A0.5 (brief).
(1) The repeats. A0.4 asks for both neighbours' accounts against time in the same run, which the archived records hold only at the run's end. Stage 10's no-delivery runs of M50_PT_Exp4 (to 332 s), PP_Exp3 and ST_Exp2 (to 900 s) were therefore made again through `stage15_model` with the accounts kept every 0.5 s, on this machine, before the predictions; their configurations and outcomes are archived records, and the repeats are not Stage 15 runs of any registered configuration. Two more repeats sized the runs: Stage 10's M50_PT_Exp4 run with 6,597 J over 240 s, stopped at C8, and its run with 5,045 J over 21.39 s, to 332 s (`stage15_run.py repeats`, `results/stage15_repeats/`). Every repeat's stored histories (the mean, the hottest node, the trigger, the lumped twin and the probes) lie within 2.9e-10 K of the laptop's records, the full runs' accounts within 3.2e-13 of their totals, and the stopped run's C8, 263.227 s, within 1.1e-10 s of the archived series' crossing (`results/stage15_accounts.json`).
(2) A0.4, the accounts (`src/stage15_a0.py`). The lumped twin's link carries its tabs with its contact (one conductance, G = g_bus + g_link = 0.184 W/K in parallel, 0.177 W/K in series); its tabs' share of the link's heat is g_bus / G (7.4 % in parallel, 3.8 % in series) exactly, since both see T1 - T. Both neighbours have the same heat capacity, 61.75 J/K, so the twin's excess over the resolved mean is the difference of their stored heats over it. At 250 s, near the twin's peak (249.9, 248.0 and 252.8 s), the lumped twin has taken 2,052, 2,183 and 2,309 J more through its contact than the resolved cell through its contact and wedge (M50_PT_Exp4, PP_Exp3, ST_Exp2: 9,876 against 7,824 J, 10,463 against 8,280 J and 10,995 against 8,686 J), and 73, 80 and 41 J less through its tabs; it has lost 477, 540 and 584 J more by convection and radiation, being hotter, and released 90, 176 and 249 J more reaction heat; it has stored 1,591, 1,738 and 1,934 J more, 25.8, 28.1 and 31.3 K. The excess is the contact: the same conductance draws more heat into one temperature than into a cell whose contact patch heats towards the trigger's temperature while its mean lags (the resolved cell's node on the line of contact reaches 262 C by 29 s in M50_ST_Exp2, D104's record, where its mean is 60 C). The excess intake builds through the trigger's hot phase (1,085 to 1,271 J by 60 s, 35 to 36 % of the resolved cell's intake by then) and is largest by 250 s; by 900 s the hotter twin has lost most of it again (106 and 138 J stored above the resolved cell in PP_Exp3 and ST_Exp2). Radiation from the trigger is the same for both (the record's one term).
(3) A0.5, sizing (`results/stage15_sizing.json`). In the archive the 199 continued runs took a median of 644 s on the laptop where the mean did not cross (107 runs) and 3,759 s where it did (92 runs), all of them the laptop's, with 24 workers at once; in the runs that cross, a median of 13.1 % of the steps (7.5 to 70.9 %) came before the crossing, so a run stopped at C8 costs about what a run without C8 costs. What the solver records: `max_sub` is the largest number of explicit sub-steps a node may take in the reaction step (64 by default, raised to 4,096 from the delivery's start in every run with a delivery, D68 (3), D90 (1)); a node that would need more is left to the implicit integrator, `max_implicit_nodes` is the largest number of such nodes in one step, `steps_with_max_sub` the number of steps made with the raised limit, and `implicit_node_steps_with_max_sub` the number of nodes left to the implicit integrator over those steps. In 30 continued runs, all without a delivery and so with the limit of 64, up to 812 nodes in a step went to the implicit integrator; in no run with a delivery did any node need more than 4,096 (implicit_node_steps_with_max_sub is 0 in all 199). One run repeated two steps at half their length (DT_ACCEPT). No archived continued run reached a limit beyond which its reaction step was not computed as designed: the implicit integrator is the reaction step's own fallback (D13), not a cap, and the step's floor, 0.1 s / 2^16, is not recorded. On this machine (two cores) the repeats took 114 s (M50_PT_Exp4 to 332 s, no delivery), 250 and 251 s (900 s, no delivery), 258 s (5,045 J over 21.39 s, to 332 s) and 211 s (6,597 J over 240 s, to C8 at 263.2 s), 2.2 to 3.7 times faster each than the laptop's records of the same runs. Part B's list (D175) is about 30 runs of 4 to 10 minutes each and V4's four, of which the two on the refined mesh (82,309 nodes against 19,355) take about an hour each by the nodes' ratio: some 3 hours on this machine's two workers. The container carries the list, and the laptop is not asked for.

## Stage 15 design, recorded with the predictions before any new three-dimensional run

D173. Part A, the reactive cross-section (brief; `src/stage15_section.py`). Stage 13's cross-section model of the mid-height section (D131 (2), `stage13_section.Section`: conduction in r and theta with the roll's 1.3 and 25 W/(m K) across and along the layers, the can as a surface layer of 841.7 J/(m2 K), the contact and air wedge to the trigger, convection of 10 W/(m2 K) beyond 45 degrees, radiation to the room and from the trigger, and the delivery as a uniform flux over the facing half), with the archive's kinetics integrated in every cell of the roll and their heat fed back. Nothing else is new. (1) Each roll cell carries the three-dimensional model's reacting mass per unit volume, the cell's 0.065 kg over its volume (v12_model's m_node), and its own heat capacity; the can's nodes carry none, as in the three-dimensional model the can adds heat capacity and no reacting mass. (2) The reaction step is the three-dimensional model's (`stage9_model.run_resolved`): the compiled sub-steps of `v12_kernel.FastKinetics` (up to 64 explicit sub-steps per cell, then the implicit integrator), from the delivery's start `stage7_kernel.FastKineticsMax` with 4,096; the level k of each step (the base step over 2^k) by `reactive.choose_level`'s rule on the largest local reaction heating (at most 1 K per step, at most one level coarser than the previous step, aligned to the base grid); a step repeated at half its length while its reaction sub-step heats any cell by more than 4 K, down to 2^-16 of the base step. (3) The base step is Stage 13's fixed schedule, 0.01 s and then 0.1 s, switching at 30 s (Stage 13 switched at 60 s; 30 s covers the 21.39 s pulse, the trigger's fast heating and the hot spot's peak, and saves a fifth of a run's cost; the three-dimensional model's base step is 0.1 s). The level only refines it, so with the reaction off the run is `Section.run` step for step: V3 holds, the two agreeing to 7.4e-13 K (M50_PT_Exp1, 3,000 J over 21.39 s, 30 s, `src/test_stage15.py`). (4) What it stands for. The section is per unit length of the half section; the whole cell's energies are 2 L times its own (L = 70.1 mm), its mean is the heat-capacity-weighted mean over the roll's cells and the can's nodes, the section's stand-in for Tmean, and its far face is the can node nearest 180 degrees. It has neither the ends (the caps with their convection and radiation), nor the tabs, nor the hot spot's spread along the cell: it is a cell infinitely long under mid-height's conditions. A run takes 17 to 71 s on one core of this machine (median 30 s). (5) The kinetics' shift (B4) and the aged SEI enter as in the three-dimensional runs (D176 (1)); the kinetic draws are Stage 11's (D111 (1)).

D174. Part A's results, before the predictions (`src/stage15_reduced.py`, `results/stage15_cross_section.json`; every run's summary in `results/stage15_xs/`). The 675 cross-section runs were made on this machine by two workers from 10:43 UTC on 17 November to 05:07 UTC on 18 November, 5.9 hours of one core's time; the work stopped at 12:42 UTC on 17 November, when its background job reached its time limit, and resumed from the records at 04:05 UTC on 18 November (each run's record is written as it ends, and a run with a record is read, not made again).
(1) A1 (explanatory): each of the 151 central continued runs of A0.1 made again in the cross-section with its test's trigger and its delivery, to the archived run's end. The outcome, C8 within the run's span or none, is the same in all 151: 92 with C8 (58 of them crossing within 30 s, at energies of 15 to 118 kJ, and 10 after 100 s), 59 without. Where both cross, the cross-section's C8 time lies 5.5 s before to 34.5 s after the archived one (mean 1.0 s); where neither does, its mean peaks 20.7 K below to 3.8 K above the archived mean's peak (mean 1.9 K below), 14.7 s earlier to 32.9 s later, and its far face 22.4 K below to 3.0 K above. What the missing ends and tabs cost is read from the split by connection. In the eight series tests (one tab, to the negative end) the cross-section's mean peaks within 0.7 K below and 3.3 K above the archive's (20 runs), and its late C8 times lie 5.5 s before to 1.3 s after (4 runs): the ends' losses and the one tab nearly cancel. In the eight parallel tests (both tabs) the three-dimensional cell takes heat through its tabs (938 J by 332 s with no delivery in M50_PT_Exp4, 1,085 J by 900 s in PP_Exp3, D172 (2)) that the cross-section has no way to receive; there its mean peaks 2.8 K lower on average (39 runs, at most 20.7 K lower, M50_PT_Exp4's 6,209 J over 240 s at 332 s, 220.5 against 241.2 C), and its late C8 times come 7.8 to 34.5 s later (6 runs). The model so stands for the three-dimensional cell's outcome everywhere the archive has looked, and leans late and cool in the parallel tests by what their tabs carry.
(2) A3: the cross-section misses the outcome in none of the 151 runs, not more than one in ten; it stands for the three-dimensional cell, P1 is kept, and Part B's searches are centred on its E*(C8).
(3) A2, E*(C8) with the 21.39 s delivery (each from 8,000 J, by factors of 1.5 until a run with C8 lies above one without, then halving until the bracket is at most 1 % of its midpoint, 8 to 10 runs a search): M50_PT_Exp1 10,781 J, PT_Exp2 6,354, PT_Exp3 8,594, PT_Exp4 7,062, PP_Exp1 4,542, PP_Exp2 6,854, PP_Exp3 6,229, PP_Exp4 5,604, ST_Exp1 4,431, ST_Exp2 5,604, ST_Exp3 10,281, ST_Exp4 5,042, SP_Exp1 5,521, SP_Exp2 5,069, SP_Exp3 3,528 and SP_Exp4 7,104 J. They span 3.5 to 10.8 kJ, 3.1-fold, where the whole-cell clock's thresholds span 0 to 12.7 kJ (zero in two tests). In the fourteen tests with strong triggers (body energies E_b of 31.6 to 54.3 kJ) the cell's own runaway needs 1.08 to 6.16 times the clock's energy (twelve tests), or 4.5 and 3.5 kJ where the clock needs none (M50_PP_Exp1 and SP_Exp3); in the two with weak triggers (M50_PT_Exp1, E_b 9.0 kJ, and ST_Exp3, 16.6 kJ) it needs 0.85 times it: a weak trigger leaves the delivery, concentrated in the facing half, to start the runaway, and the lumped twin's advantage at the contact (D172 (2)) is then small.
(4) A2 with constant power over 120, 240 and 360 s, the nine propagated tests (each search from 6,000 J by the same rule, 8 to 10 runs; the energy is the whole delivery's): M50_PT_Exp4 6,820, 6,398 and 7,055 J; PP_Exp1 3,927, 3,531 and 3,927; PP_Exp2 6,539, 6,117 and 6,773; PP_Exp4 5,016, 4,578 and 5,078; ST_Exp3 12,410, 13,553 and 15,240; ST_Exp4 4,453, 4,016 and 4,453; SP_Exp1 4,953, 4,516 and 5,016; SP_Exp2 4,453, 4,016 and 4,484; SP_Exp3 3,010, 2,677 and 2,990 J. In eight of the nine the 240 s delivery needs the least, 0.76 to 0.91 times the 21.39 s pulse's E*(C8), against 0.85 to 0.97 times over 120 s and 0.85 to 1.00 times over 360 s; M50_ST_Exp3, whose trigger is weak, needs more the longer the delivery, 1.21, 1.32 and 1.48 times the pulse's. Near its threshold the cell runs away late: at the upper end of the E*(C8) bracket, C8 comes at 287 to 508 s in 34 of the 36 brackets of the nine tests (four deliveries each), and at 114.0 and 190.2 s in M50_ST_Exp3 with the pulse and over 120 s.
(5) A2 at the measured times: the delivered energy at which C8 falls at each propagated test's measured time t_m, by Stage 9's rule for f_t (D91 (3)) read on C8. The grid f8_hi + 0, 0.0125, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1 and 0.2 (fractions of E_e; f8_lo and f8_hi the ends of the E*(C8) bracket) is read in order up to its first point whose C8 comes before t_m; then the bisection between f8_lo and that point runs until the bracket is at most 0.005 E_e wide or a run's C8 lies within 10 s of t_m. Stage 9's check that the times fall along the grid (D91 (2)) cuts nothing: in all 36 searches C8 comes earlier at every larger energy run. Of the 36 (nine tests, four deliveries), 26 are matched, 9 end at the threshold and one is above the grid (the table of `predictions_stage15.md`). With the 21.39 s pulse the five matched energies, M50_PP_Exp1 6,036 J, PP_Exp2 7,649, SP_Exp1 5,965, SP_Exp2 6,495 and SP_Exp3 4,582 J, are 1.08 to 1.33 times their E*(C8), and 1.28 to 2.19 times the energies at which the whole-cell clock gives the same times (D170 (3) 1), 17.2 times in M50_SP_Exp3 (266 J). Where C8 comes before a long delivery ends, the cell has had only part of it by then: in the 26 matched searches the energy delivered by C8 is 4,064 to 8,116 J, and 13,844 J in M50_ST_Exp3 over 240 s; within each of the seven tests matched under two or more deliveries it changes by 6.1 to 12.7 %, so the cell's own runaway at a measured time asks for about the same energy delivered by then, whatever the delivery's duration. In eight of the nine thresholds C8 comes after t_m at f8_hi and before it at the bracket's upper end, the rule's last run, 109 to 453 J higher: t_m is reached by an energy inside that bracket, within the rule's resolution (0.005 E_e, 258 to 758 J in these four tests) of the threshold, where the rule's width ends the search and it reports the threshold (M50_PT_Exp4 with the pulse: C8 at 310.6 s with 7,083 J and at 188.5 s with 7,440 J, against 272 s). In M50_ST_Exp3 with the pulse C8 comes at 114.0 s already at f8_hi, before its 177 s, and no delivered energy reproduces the time. M50_ST_Exp3 over 360 s is above the grid: up to f8_hi + 0.2 (25,628 J) its C8 comes no earlier than 196.4 s.
(6) The cross-section's values for Part B and for C2, made with the same model and stated in the predictions. M50_PT_Exp4 with the 21.39 s pulse: at the upper end of its E*(C8) bracket (7,083 J) C8 comes at 310.6 s, and the far face, whose rise slows below 0.5 K/s at 39.5 s and stays under 0.47 K/s up to C8, reads 240.1 C twenty seconds before it (D171 (2)'s stand-in); at 1.05 E*(C8) (7,416 J) C8 comes at 192.3 s with the far face's jump starting at 233.8 C, and at 1.15 E*(C8) (8,122 J) at 121.5 s, at 230.2 C. The pulse and the hold (B3 (b)'s rule from 10 W): C8 at 344.2 s with 10 W and at 265.9 s with 12.5 W, matched at 12.5 W, where Part B's search starts. M50_PT_Exp4 at 5,045 J over 21.39 s: no C8 under the four draws (the mean peaks at 189.0, 188.5, 190.5 and 189.0 C) nor with the aged SEI (185.5 C); with the rate laws shifted, no C8 at 5, 10, 15 and 17.5 K, and C8 at 316.2 s at 20 K, the least shift. M50_PT_Exp4 with 240 s of constant power: no C8 at 6,209 J (the mean peaks at 220.5 C) nor at 5,821 J (211.4 C), where the archived three-dimensional run of 6,209 J, which ended at 332 s, had its mean at 241.2 C and rising (D170 (3) 3), the cross-section's largest departure from the archive (D174 (1)). With no delivery (for C2), at the low, central and high contacts: no C8 in any of the 48 runs; the mean peaks at 47.9 to 132.7 C, 60.3 to 185.9 C and 62.3 to 199.2 C, and the hot spot reaches the level in none, 8 and 13 of the sixteen tests (at the central contact the same eight as the archive's three-dimensional runs, D170 (3) 2).

D175. Part B's registered list and the predictions (brief; `src/stage15_partb.py`, `predictions_stage15.md`). Every run is M50's resolved and lumped neighbours with the test's archived trigger, the central contact and tabs, 25 C, the posterior-mean kinetics and the fresh SEI unless stated, the delivery from t = 0 through the facing half, Stage 10's five probes; the resolved neighbour stops at C8 or goes to 900 s, its hot spot's crossing recorded without a stop; the lumped twin goes to 900 s; the sub-step limit is 4,096 from the delivery's start. Each next run is read from the records.
(1) B1: for M50_PT_Exp4, PP_Exp2, SP_Exp3 and ST_Exp3 (fitted triggers of 40.0, 41.0, 54.3 and 16.6 kJ) with the 21.39 s delivery, runs at the cross-section's E*(C8) less 5 % and plus 5 %; then, while both reach C8 or neither does, one further step of 10 % of that value on the side needed (down from the lowest run with C8, or up from the highest without), until a run with C8 lies directly above one without. The bracket is (the highest without C8, the lowest with C8], 10 % of the cross-section's value wide, and its midpoint is the three-dimensional E*(C8). A chain that reaches 5 % of the cross-section's value going down, or 2.95 times it going up, without a bracket ends there.
(2) B2: M50_PT_Exp4 with constant power over 240 s at f = 0.05 (6,209 J; the archived run of that point ended at 332 s with its mean at 241.2 C and rising) and at f = 0.046875 (5,821 J), each to C8 or 900 s.
(3) B3: (a) B1's M50_PT_Exp4 run at the upper end of its bracket (the run that reaches C8) is scored against the measured history by Stage 10's rule at the far face (D171 (3)), with the far face's reading at the start of its jump; nothing more is run for it. (b) The pulse and the hold: f = 0.040625 (5,045 J) over 21.39 s, the delivery that follows the history best (Stage 10), then a constant power P from 21.39 s until the run ends. P starts at the cross-section's value on the 2.5 W grid, 12.5 W (D174 (6)); then steps of 2.5 W, down while C8 comes more than 10 s before 271.5 s and up while it comes more than 10 s after it or not at all, until C8 lies within 10 s of 271.5 s ("matched"), or two neighbouring powers bracket it ("bracketed": C8 more than 10 s early at the higher, more than 10 s late or none at the lower), or 16 runs are made. The delivery's shape is a choice and is reported as one; what the history and the far face then do is the test, and the search does not fit them.
